The guided operating-point card reports the solved D1/D2 currents, then asks the learner to increase only R2 to 680 Ω. A focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verifies both currents after the first run, stale-result messaging, and that the D2 current falls while D1 remains higher after rerun. Uses the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild.

Capacitor discharge lesson (2026-10-12): The example now uses two explicit rails between vertically oriented capacitor/resistor terminals, avoiding the short that occurred when a wire crossed the capacitor's second pin. Focused Firefox/ngspice coverage checks both connected wires and verifies solved values at 10 ms, stale-result prediction, and the slower discharge after doubling R1. A domain regression checks branch-current propagation onto both rails using known component currents. No WASM rebuild.

Example discovery follow-up (2026-10-12): The 35-example catalog now has topic filters for basics, diodes/LEDs, AC/reactive circuits, switching, and advanced topics. Filters compose with title/description search and expose their selected state to assistive technology. Focused Firefox UI regressions verify topic counts, parallel-LED discovery, combined filtering, and the full catalog.

Parallel RLC resonance lesson (2026-10-12): Added a wired parallel L/C tank fed through a source resistor and damped by a load resistor. The AC lesson calculates the resonance from the current L and C values and guides users to move the peak by changing either component. Domain coverage verifies topology and the logarithmic sweep; Firefox/ngspice verifies the output peak near 1.59 kHz is above −1 dB and at least 15 dB above the 100 Hz response. All 28 unit groups and typecheck pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Current-source schematic follow-up (2026-10-12): Replaced the current-source example's repeated global labels with an explicit output link and a three-segment grounded return loop. The focused Firefox/ngspice workflow confirms the 1 V operating point and correctly directed 1 mA current flow on all four wire segments; a domain regression protects the loop's current propagation. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Transformer schematic follow-up (2026-10-12): The AC step-up and transient winding-current examples now show nine explicit source, primary, secondary, and load wire segments. VIN/VOUT and the necessary ground references remain labeled for probing and the shared return. Focused Firefox/ngspice workflows confirm the 2:1 AC response and both transient winding-current animations; all 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Controlled-source schematic follow-up (2026-10-12): The VCVS and VCCS example now draws four explicit wire segments from each controlled output around its opposite terminal to the load. Control-input and ground labels preserve the intended shared nets. The focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verifies all eight segments, the +2 V/−1 V outputs, and the correct current direction through both loads; all 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Inverting-amplifier schematic follow-up (2026-10-12): The lesson now draws explicit input, inverting-feedback, amplifier-output, and load wires instead of connecting those paths through repeated global labels. Power rails and ground remain labeled. The focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verifies all 15 wire segments render and the output remains approximately −1 V; all 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

RC filter schematic follow-up (2026-10-12): The paired low-pass and high-pass AC lessons now use nine explicit input, series-element, output, and grounded-return wire segments apiece, with VIN/VOUT labels retained as probes. Firefox/ngspice verifies both cutoff lessons still solve after the 1 kHz-to-500 Hz component edit; all 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Responsive canvas guidance (2026-10-14): The schematic toolbar now shows concise, mode-specific instructions instead of an overlong line that overflowed the canvas at desktop and phone widths. The default visible hint preserves left-to-right containment versus right-to-left crossing selection; the full gesture explanation remains available on hover, and mode changes are announced through a polite status region. A Firefox regression verifies the status fits at 1280 px and 390 px, stays one line high, and updates when Wire mode is selected.

### M2 — Project system and deeper simulation

**M2.1 `.elsim` specification.** Versioned manifest and ZIP layout; schema validation, migration, asset hashes, relative paths, safe extraction limits, and deterministic serialization. Archive v2 stores canonical project JSON with raw-DEFLATE compression when beneficial, falling back to stored entries; the reader preserves v1 stored-archive compatibility. User model source remains in optional project-v2 `modelAssets` with SHA-256 and unknown redistribution status. Project v2 also persists selected trace IDs, normalized horizontal and value ranges for transient, AC, noise, and DC sweep plots, detached waveform-window bounds, trace colors, component-voltage probe definitions, schematic trace references, and each plot panel’s independent second-cursor coordinate per analysis. The v8 schema now describes these optional panel fields alongside runtime validation. The reader and writer now permit a project JSON entry up to 49 MiB inside the 50 MiB total archive limit, matching v1’s inline storage of bounded imported models, symbols, and schematic source; a 21 MiB source-provenance archive round trip is tested. Chromium now downloads an actual `.elsim` ZIP containing unknown-license model/symbol assets, original `.asc` source, and the built-in two-level hierarchy, opens the archive in the app, and verifies the source contents, nested sheet relationship, and per-instance parameter override survive. Project schema v8 persists hierarchy and component-definition IDs; schemas v1–v7 remain migration inputs. Acceptance: round trip preserves connectivity, layout, models, plots, and hierarchy; malformed archives cannot read arbitrary local files. See `PROJECT_FORMAT.md` for current format and limits.

**M2.2 Analysis expansion.** AC, noise, and DC source sweeps are implemented for independent voltage and current sources; noise analysis selects a labeled non-ground output net and independent input source, resolves label aliases to the netlist’s canonical node name, and runs ngspice’s spectral-density plot, and displays output/input-referred noise against frequency with the input-referred unit derived from ngspice vector metadata (V/√Hz for a voltage input, A/√Hz for a current input). The local WASM runtime confirms the current-source vector type; raw-parser tests cover both unit cases, and Firefox regressions verify voltage- and current-input noise plots and cursor units. Connectivity and netlist tests verify grounded labels are rejected and secondary labels resolve to the emitted node name. Unit-aware controls, netlists, and imported directives preserve the source quantity; LTspice export work remains deferred to the separate interoperability goal. DC sweep fields now switch between volts and amperes with source selection, reset to safe quantity-specific defaults when changing source type, retain ranges between sources of the same type, and reject explicit V/A suffix mismatches before simulation or netlist export; a browser regression checks that a voltage range cannot silently become an ampere range. AC sweep point controls now distinguish total points for linear sweeps from points per decade or octave in both field labels and validation guidance. Single-source `.dc` imports now require a placed independent voltage/current source, quantity-matched numeric limits, a nonzero step, and a step direction toward the stop value, and at most 10,000 points; invalid or oversized sweeps remain in original-source provenance with line-specific warnings. Two-source `.dc` imports now execute the primary axis and run 2–10 bounded secondary-source cases as separately labeled ngspice result series; exact secondary values are retained when a step does not land on the stop value; users may edit them into nonuniform exact lists, and LTspice export reconstructs the original range. Domain, export, and pinned-WASM tests cover the non-dividing-step case; the Chromium regression imports, edits, and runs the three-case flow. Per-source AC magnitude and phase are available for voltage and independent current sources and round-trip through LTspice AC attributes. Resistors, lamps, and potentiometers can independently opt out of ngspice thermal and flicker noise through `noiseEnabled: false`, which emits native `NOISY=0` on each generated resistor branch; omission preserves the default. Project validation, v8 schema, netlist, and inspector-save regressions protect the behavior; paired existing-runtime WASM noise analyses verify disabling noise drives the entire spectrum to zero while the default remains nonzero. Parameter stepping supports up to three distinct passive values, scalar DC sources, numeric parameters on selected imported device `.MODEL` cards, and represented waveform fields, with at most ten combinations and separate result series. Sweep bounds are parsed as bounded numeric scalars; validated expression defaults are retained, and supported sweep targets accept either a numeric range or an explicit value list, parsed in the target field’s units. The editor uses ngspice `altermod` and labels each trace with component and parameter. Chromium browser regression runs an eight-case three-parameter transient sweep through the ngspice worker, sweeps a voltage source from -1 V through the valid signed range, and verifies all combination labels. A user-owned BJT model can be assigned to Q1, its numeric BF parameter selected and stepped from 10 to 200, and both labeled result series are verified in the browser ngspice worker; the WASM fixture separately confirms the solver changes collector voltage. PULSE fields include initial/high levels, delay, rise/fall, width, and period; PWL voltage/current point levels use one shared scale while retaining point times. Every represented SINE, EXP, and SFFM field can be selected for voltage and current sources, including offset, amplitude, frequency, timing, phase/damping, time constants, and modulation values. WASM and Chromium tests confirm frequency sweeps; unit and browser tests cover timing and phase target generation. PWL values can now be stepped independently by point index as well as through a shared scale; unit and browser regressions cover individual voltage and current-source points; Firefox runs a two-case current-source PWL point sweep through ngspice and verifies both separate current traces. Independent current sources support scalar DC, seven/eight-argument PULSE levels/timing with optional finite cycle counts, bounded scalar-pair PWL tables, SINE, EXP, and SFFM waveforms, including LTspice import/export. Current-source waveform values use ampere-specific fields; Firefox verifies native current-source SINE editing and transient simulation, while SINE, EXP, and SFFM fixtures verify numeric WASM load responses. The PWL editor displays a scaled voltage/current preview for valid point tables and updates it as points change. It accepts pasted rows or local CSV/TSV/text files with time/value columns and an optional header, validates point count, units, and ordering with line-specific errors, and replaces the table only after the full input validates; unit tests cover both quantities and malformed input, and Firefox verifies paste and file import. The properties inspector scrolls within its workspace column on desktop so long source editors remain reachable above the status bar; Firefox verified the bulk-paste interaction end to end. The PWL inspector validates current units and point ordering; project-archive and LTspice round-trip tests cover persistence, and ngspice WASM verifies positive and negative current into a resistive load. Transient `MAX`, `MIN`, `PP`, `AVG`, `RMS`, `INTEG`, and trigger-to-target `DELAY` measurements support voltage, device-current, and independent current-source vectors and retain sweep-case labels. Current-source measurements are calculated from the exact solved raw samples because ngspice does not emit `.meas` scalars for the `@i[current]` parameter; averages/RMS use trapezoidal time integration, and threshold crossings are interpolated. Initial conditions support labeled-node and capacitor voltages, inductor current, diode-family junction voltage, BJT terminal voltages, and MOS/JFET/MESFET/VDMOS terminal voltages. Device-specific `IC=` values automatically enable transient UIC and are validated against the matching placed component; dedicated Firefox and existing-runtime WASM regressions cover the D, Q, M, J, and Z device forms. LTspice `.ic V(node+,node-)=scalar` maps when the two named nodes match a placed capacitor's pin order; reversed pairs invert the voltage, and unmatched pairs warn. Linear VCVS (`E`), VCCS (`G`), CCCS (`F`), and CCVS (`H`) devices use ngspice primitives. Current-controlled sources select a voltage source as their current sensor; the inspector explains the reference direction. WASM fixtures check F/H output values and branch-current vectors against known load values and verify an inductor starts at the requested current. A schematic B source supports voltage or current output with an allowlisted expression grammar, validated node/component references, and arithmetic, conditional, and common math functions; WASM fixtures check both output modes. LTspice table() calls with literal strictly increasing breakpoints and up to 256 point pairs are translated to ngspice PWL with endpoint clamps. LTspice if(), buf(), and inv() calls translate to ngspice ternary expressions using a 1e-12 difference margin to keep nominal 0.5 V false under ngspice solve roundoff, limit(x,low,high) maps to min(max(x,low),high) for ordered bounds, mod(x,y) and the % operator map to tested truncation-based sgn/floor/abs arithmetic, arcsin/arccos/arctan map to ngspice asin/acos/atan, atan2() expands to quadrant-aware atan()/pi logic, and cbrt() expands to a sign-preserving pow(); WASM operating-point checks cover Boolean behavior at 0.4/0.5/0.6, atan2 quadrants and axes, positive and negative cube roots, and positive and negative remainders. An 11-point WASM DC sweep verifies interpolation, endpoint clamps, and threshold behavior. Table functions in raw imported model-library text remain untranslated and diagnosed. Native voltage/current source PWL supports bounded scalar time/voltage or time/current point lists (2–256 points), LTspice `REPEAT FOREVER` translated to the pinned ngspice voltage-source `r=0` extension, and finite `REPEAT FOR N` blocks expanded to at most 32 ngspice points. Finite repeats require matching first/last voltage values so the cycle boundary is continuous; the project retains the repeat count and LTspice export restores the repeat syntax. Import, netlist, export round-trip, and numeric WASM tests cover the feature; the source inspector exposes the finite count with a point-limit-aware maximum and reports a mismatched cycle boundary inline; Chromium verifies editing, correction, and running a finite repeat through the browser ngspice worker. LTspice voltage/current `PWL FILE=...` tables now import from selected plain-text companion files, preserve their data and source filename in project state, and export as equivalent inline points. Tables are limited to 32 points; repeated file-backed tables, discontinuous finite boundaries, nested repeat blocks, and wider behavioral-dialect coverage remain unsupported. ngspice has no native `.step` directive; the worker uses explicit `alter`/`run`/`write` control commands, following the [ngspice user manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). Acceptance: numeric comparison against pinned ngspice reference circuits, with documented tolerances and unsupported directives.


Controlled-switch initialization (2026-10-24): Voltage- and current-controlled switch inspectors expose ngspice’s native ON/OFF initial-state flag. It defaults to OFF for existing projects and also seeds schematic hysteresis playback. Unit tests cover both device forms and invalid values; Firefox checks both inspectors and saved project state. An existing-runtime browser WASM transient fixture verifies that ON and OFF produce different startup states for SW and CSW devices inside their hysteresis bands with UIC. If an ordinary operating-point solve overrides the selected state, the inspector directs users to the existing Skip initial operating point control. No WASM rebuild is required.


AC-only resistor value (2026-10-24): A resistor can set a positive optional AC resistance while retaining its ordinary value for transient and operating-point analyses. The property is schema-validated, exposed in the resistor inspector with explicit AC-only guidance, persisted in projects, and emitted as ngspice’s native `AC=` instance option. A focused Firefox workflow verifies editing, saving, and netlist generation; an existing-runtime WASM fixture checks the resulting 1/3 divider response. This follows the [ngspice resistor instance syntax](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf), which documents `AC=val` and requires it to be nonzero. No WASM rebuild is needed.

Imported `.step param` supports declared root-sheet parameters specified as numeric START/STOP/INCREMENT ranges, explicit numeric `list` values (including non-arithmetic lists), and aligned dec/oct log ranges, with at most ten total simulation cases. The importer accepts the exact ten-value boundary for linear and logarithmic ranges and rejects an eleven-value range with a diagnostic. Discrete imported values remain editable in the parameter-stepping panel, and the browser ngspice worker applies each value with `alterparam`, `reset`, and `run`; domain/import tests, a numeric WASM RC fixture, and a Chromium workflow verify the behavior. Unaligned log ranges receive a diagnostic.
Native transient measurements include peak-to-peak (`pp`) and time integration (`integ`) for voltage and current, alongside maximum, minimum, average, RMS, and propagation delay. Time integration uses solved waveform samples with trapezoidal integration and supports clipped time windows and parameter-sweep cases; current integrals report coulombs and voltage integrals report volt-seconds. Component-current aliases such as `I(R1)` resolve to ngspice internal device-current vectors. The project and worker schemas include both operations and their units. Focused Firefox/ngspice tests verify a roughly 5 V RC excursion and about 50 µC transferred into the capacitor during its charging half-cycle. Unit tests cover vector aliases, current-source fallback, clipped time windows, voltage/current units, sweeps, and generated netlist handling. No WASM rebuild is required.

AC bias and common-emitter example (2026-10-12): Explicitly DC-mode voltage and current sources retain their DC value alongside AC magnitude and phase in AC-analysis netlists. This lets nonlinear devices establish their intended DC operating point before small-signal analysis. Voltage- and current-source netlist regressions pass. The Examples library adds a wired, AC-coupled NPN common-emitter amplifier with a resistor bias divider, emitter stabilization, collector/load resistors, and labeled input/output. Firefox/ngspice verifies output gain exceeds input by at least 3 dB and the 1 kHz output phase is between −150° and −190°. All 28 unit groups and typecheck pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Noise integrated totals (2026-10-24): Noise runs now capture ngspice’s native `onoise_total` and `inoise_total` values from the paired `noise2` plot, retain per-case totals for parameter sweeps, and show output RMS plus input-referred RMS beside the spectral-density graph. Input-referred units follow the selected independent voltage or current source. The existing local runtime needs no rebuild; unit, worker-protocol, and focused Firefox tests cover single runs, swept runs, voltage inputs, and current inputs.

Transient integration choice (2026-10-24): The advanced solver settings now let users retain ngspice's default trapezoidal integration, explicitly select trapezoidal, or choose Gear integration for transient analysis; Gear's maximum order can optionally be set from 2 through 6. The UI explains that Gear's extra numerical damping can suppress solver ringing and also hide real fast behavior. Both settings are project-persisted, schema-validated, emitted only for transient netlists, and covered by netlist and Firefox/ngspice runtime regressions using the existing WASM build. No WASM rebuild or Pages run.

AC operating-point control (2026-10-24): Advanced solver settings can opt into ngspice `NOOPAC` for AC analysis, which can solve linear circuits with ideal LC loops that fail during a preliminary DC operating-point solve. The UI explains that ngspice still performs an operating point when nonlinear devices are present. The opt-in is schema-validated and emitted only for AC runs; unit and Firefox/ngspice regressions use the existing runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages run.

Imported device-model expression sweeps (2026-10-24): Validated braced and single-quoted `.MODEL` parameter expressions appear as sweep targets with explicit numeric bounds; model defaults remain unchanged. Firefox verifies `altermod` against the existing browser ngspice runtime; unit tests cover labels, values, and validation.

Discrete values across parameter sweeps (2026-10-24): Exact numeric lists now apply to passive component values, source waveform fields, imported model and IC parameters, global parameters, and supported secondary DC sources. Field units and positivity constraints are retained; up to ten total combinations still apply. Domain tests cover passive and frequency lists, and Firefox runs a discrete resistor list combined with capacitor/source sweeps.

**M2.3 Hierarchy.** Implemented for native ELSim project sheets and reusable blocks: project schema v8 provides independently editable nested sheets, ordered ports mapped to named nets, reusable child-sheet X instances, numeric, bounded SPICE-expression, or double-quoted string block parameter defaults and per-instance overrides, `{PARAM}` component values, recursive `.SUBCKT` generation, and cycle/pin-map validation. Chromium verified loading and simulating a hierarchical example, opening a specific block instance, probing its internal midpoint at 2.5 V, and probing `RLOAD1` at 5 mA without losing the parent result. Sheet navigation from an instance retains its instance path, including when returning to an ancestor through sheet tabs; a repeated-X2 nested-path regression verifies that the chosen instance is not replaced by the first instance in the definition. Direct root navigation clears the path. Labeled internal nodes and component currents resolve to ngspice scoped vectors (for example `v(xload.mid)` and `i(@r.xload.rload1[i])`), exposed ports resolve to their parent net, and generated `.save` directives retain these internal vectors. Domain tests verify distinct node/current vectors for repeated instances. WASM examples compare repeated instances against flattened references across voltage/current vectors. The built-in “Nested reusable divider” example now guides learners through simulation, opening X2, opening nested XCORE, probing RCORE current, and returning to the root. A matching WASM fixture verifies 2.5 V and 3.333 V divider outputs and 3.333 mA inner current. Port placement and direction are stored per ordered port and used by block geometry. Ports can now be moved up or down in the interface editor; existing parent wires, junctions, and labels follow the stable port ID so reordering changes pin numbers without changing which named signal each existing connection represents. Domain coverage verifies wire remapping, and Firefox covers reordered instance pin names. Interface edits now propagate ordered pins, side/direction metadata, and parameter removals to every instance; wires, junctions, and labels attached to surviving pin coordinates are remapped when geometry changes. While instances exist, deleting pins is constrained to the end of the ordered list to avoid shifting a wire onto a different signal; removing the final port requires removing instances first. Sheet settings now offer an inline block-parameter form with name/value validation, duplicate-name rejection, and editable numeric, SPICE-expression, or double-quoted string defaults that preserve per-instance overrides; removing a parameter clears its overrides. Domain tests cover remapping and stale-override cleanup. Chromium browser regression verifies interface editing, wire-anchor movement, duplicate rejection, parameter creation/default editing, and override cleanup. Per-instance block values now validate as bounded numeric SPICE values or basic arithmetic expressions while editing: invalid drafts remain visible with an inline error but are not saved; a browser regression verifies a corrected override is persisted and appears in the generated X-instance netlist. Circuit checks recognize declared `{PARAM}` passive and current-source values on the active sheet and continue warning on undeclared names; they also validate passive and independent-source values across reachable child sheets using each sheet’s own parameter scope, report undeclared names against a selectable top-level block, and have electrical-check regressions for declared and missing nested parameters. Child-sheet duplicate-reference reports use unique sheet-qualified issue IDs and select the corresponding visible top-level block instance. Behavioral-source node, component, and parameter references are validated in each reachable child sheet’s own scope; reports select the visible block, and unit tests cover declared and missing child parameters. CCCS/CCVS checks also resolve their controlling voltage-source reference within the child sheet and select the visible block when it is missing. Child transistor beta and imported semiconductor model checks now use the same sheet scope and selectable parent-block reporting. Floating terminal checks now cover reachable child sheets, preserve pin names, and select the containing top-level block; a domain regression covers an isolated nested resistor. Same-net short checks and MOSFET bulk/source body-bias advisories also run in each child sheet with scoped connectivity and selectable block reports. Child connectivity diagnostics now include conflicting net labels and orphan wires, with namespaced issue IDs and selection of the containing block; regressions verify both findings. Missing-ground guidance searches only instantiated sheets, identifies a voltage source inside a reachable child sheet, and selects its visible block when no root source exists. Hierarchical clipboard entries now carry the required nested sheet definitions; cross-project paste remaps their IDs, keeps child references and instance overrides intact, promotes a copied definition to the destination root when its source parent is absent, and reuses matching definitions within the same project. Clipboard closure also carries the transitive imported model libraries and custom symbol assets required by a copied user IC, deduplicating by content and remapping conflicting asset IDs; Firefox verifies cross-project paste of a user IC with a transitive library dependency and custom symbol. Unit tests cover nested remapping, same-project reuse, ID collisions, missing definitions, imported-asset closure, and copied user ICs inside child sheets with symbol-ID collision remapping. Firefox verifies copying X2 from the nested-divider example into a fresh project. It also found and fixed toolbar status text wrapping over sheet controls at the 854 px browser viewport. LTspice `.asc` import now applies safe `SpiceLine PARAMS:` overrides to both reusable child-sheet instances and user-library `.SUBCKT` instances when each parameter is declared; unsupported or undeclared overrides remain preserved and produce source-line warnings. Importer tests verify expression bounds, netlist generation, and separate nested-instance overrides after export/reimport. Reusable child sheets can now be duplicated with all nested definitions copied as an independent subtree; sheet, port, parameter, part, wire, and label IDs are regenerated, nested part settings are deep-cloned, and nested block references point at the copied definitions. The duplicate opens immediately in a sibling sheet named “Copy of …” with a collision-safe numeric suffix, and undo removes the copied subtree. Domain tests validate nested ID/reference remapping and Firefox verifies creation and undo. Acceptance: a nested two-level circuit simulates equivalently to a flattened reference; repeated block instances remain distinct.
Circuit checks now inspect duplicate SPICE references within every reachable sheet definition, while allowing the same reference name in separate sheets. A domain regression covers duplicates inside a reused child definition and a same-named component at the root.

Hierarchy status audit (2026-10-12): native reusable-sheet hierarchy meets this milestone’s acceptance; the focused Firefox/ngspice nested-versus-flattened workflow verifies both repeated parameterized instances. LTspice-specific import compatibility remains deferred with the LTspice work.
Hierarchy verification follow-up (2026-10-12): The Firefox nested-versus-flattened ngspice workflow now avoids trying to right-click probe labels at the canvas edge or outside the canvas after a plot window opens; it fits the schematic before retrying those labels. The focused acceptance run passes against the existing local runtime and checks both differently parameterized block instances against their flattened voltage-divider results. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Hierarchy selection restoration (2026-10-14): Adding a child sheet now snapshots the current parent selection before opening the blank sheet, so returning to the parent restores its selected parts, wires, and labels just like ordinary sheet navigation. Focused Firefox coverage verifies the starter resistor remains selected after returning from a new child sheet; TypeScript and the regression pass.

**M2.4 Custom symbols/models.** Partial: user-selected text libraries containing complete `.SUBCKT` blocks, top-level `.MODEL` cards, and shared `.PARAM`, `.FUNC`, or `.GLOBAL` records can be parsed and stored locally in project data. `.FUNC` declarations inside imported `.SUBCKT` blocks are retained with lexical visibility: descendants inherit parent definitions, local definitions can shadow them, and sibling subcircuits remain isolated. It resolves `.INCLUDE` and file-form `.LIB` references only against explicitly imported project-local assets; it never reads arbitrary local paths. Control/execution directives, top-level circuit elements, malformed pin declarations, and unmatched blocks are rejected. The UI displays imported cards, file name, and unknown redistribution status; SHA-256 is verified on project open and required before simulation. The editor parses each imported library once per model-asset revision and shares those parsed cards with the component picker and dependency analysis; a domain regression verifies card/default and resolved-dependency parity, and Firefox imports a plain user library, adds its X instance, and checks ordered pins and parameter defaults in the inspector. Imported subcircuits can be added as schematic `X` instances; instance pin geometry and inspector rows follow declared SPICE order. User-imported symbols with the same pin count may be paired with subcircuits by `SpiceOrder` even when `PinName` labels differ; the picker exposes each ordered model-to-symbol label pairing, and Firefox verifies placement retains the model’s declared pin order. Circuit Checks now flags a placed external `X` instance when no imported `.SUBCKT` definition matches its model name, including instances in reachable child sheets, and selects the visible parent block. The parser reads numeric and bounded arithmetic defaults from a subcircuit `PARAMS:` header, the inspector permits numeric or arithmetic per-instance overrides, rejects invalid drafts without saving them, and the netlister emits valid values as `X... PARAMS:` plus project-owned model definitions. A pinned WASM fixture verifies a dependent model default and a per-instance parameter override independently. D, NPN, PNP, NJF, PJF, NMOS, and PMOS model cards can be selected on matching built-in parts; hand-written NJF/PJF import tests verify D-G-S symbol mapping, and WASM fixtures verify NJF and PJF operating-point/current outputs and a negative-gate NJF cutoff. Chromium imports a user-supplied NJF library and matching LTspice J symbol/schematic, verifies the selected model in the inspector, and runs the transient in browser ngspice. A hand-written NMOS card fixture verifies custom-model drain voltage and current, while other fixtures confirm a 1N4148-style card's 0.679 V operating point, a four-pin subcircuit's 10 V output, and a GAIN override's 15 V output. Netlist source maps retain the imported library's filename identity and original card line. The inspector maps ngspice model errors to that file and line and offers a replacement path validated against current model and pin references. The browser worker now reads actionable errors from stderr, preserves ngspice’s separate “line no.” location line for source mapping, and explains when an imported model calls an unavailable behavioral function; a pinned-WASM Chromium workflow verifies the library filename, original line, and advice. Model-library replacement now compares the dependency graph before/after and blocks newly missing transitive cards or newly recursive chains required by placed parts; tests also verify compatible changes and allow pre-existing unrelated missing references. Imported library dependency analysis resolves `.SUBCKT` calls and D/Q/J/M/S/W/Z model references across project libraries, identifies missing definitions with source lines, and reports recursive subcircuit chains in the import panel. Resolved dependency edges protect transitively referenced libraries from removal while an imported IC that uses them remains placed. Analysis is advisory for unused imported definitions; users can still simulate circuits that do not instantiate an incomplete library model. No vendor model is bundled. Portable `.elsim` export pauses when imported libraries, symbols, or original `.asc` source are included; it names each file, marks redistribution rights unknown, and requires a separate Save project file action after the warning. Chromium verified Cancel and confirmed local archive download with a user-provided test model; Playwright regression covers the same warning and download paths. The `uplim`/`dnlim` ngspice `lta` activation check now joins `+` continuation records, matching the dialect diagnostics; a regression covers functions and comments on continuation lines. Static dialect checks identify XSPICE `A` devices as requiring compiled `.cm` code-model modules, which this browser build omits and ELSim does not import; the warning retains the physical source line and explains that the required module cannot be loaded. Imported LTspice B-source expressions now retain a dialect marker so `^` is translated as Boolean XOR and `**` remains exponentiation, without changing native ngspice `^` behavior. The supported schematic B-source subset also maps `pwrs(x,y)` to sign-preserving ngspice arithmetic and `xor(x,y)` to threshold-aware Boolean logic; `int(x)` truncates toward zero, `square(x)` maps to multiplication, `hypot(x,y)` uses scale-normalized ngspice arithmetic, and imported `u(x)` maps to a strict positive-value step because ngspice returns 0.5 at zero while LTspice returns 0. Signed-power, truncation, square, hypot, unit-step boundary, and XOR truth cases are verified in WASM. Local importer/netlist tests and a browser WASM workflow verify the dialect paths. Imported raw-library `u()` expressions retain their source and receive a line-specific diagnostic for the 0 versus 0.5 zero-boundary mismatch. Imported model libraries, symbols, and root/child `.asc` sources let users record a source URL, a license reference, and rights-review notes; fields round-trip in `.elsim`, are bounded by schema validation, and remain explicitly unverified while redistribution rights stay unknown. Browser coverage records metadata for imported assets and verifies that rights remain unknown. Chromium verifies that a single multi-file LTspice import can load an `.asc`, matching `.asy` symbols, and a user-provided `.lib` together, resolve the `X` instance, and run it in browser ngspice to produce `V(out) = 2 V`. New project-original example assets at `examples/user-opamp/` show a five-pin imported op-amp macromodel and document its finite-gain, 50 Ω output stage, ideal rail clamps, and omitted bandwidth/slew/bias/offset behavior. A separate pinned-WASM fixture and Chromium workflow import its symbol, schematic, and model, confirm ordered pins `INP INM VPOS VNEG OUT`, and solve the loaded feedback circuit at approximately 1 V. This closes the op-amp import acceptance example without treating the teaching model as a physical device or vendor model. Schematic compatibility diagnostics apply the importer’s `.step` case-count and logarithmic alignment limits, including acceptance of exactly ten values; invalid ranges are reported before simulation. Additional static checks now cover LTspice statistical/selection function mismatches in raw model libraries (`flat`, one-argument `gauss`, `mc`, and `select`), with line-specific diagnostics that can open and focus the original imported library line in the editor; reports reveal warnings progressively in groups of 20 so later source lines remain reachable in large libraries. Pinned-WASM regressions verify the unsupported forms. Native ngspice `.IF/.ELSEIF/.ELSE/.ENDIF` blocks are now preserved in imported model libraries; mutually exclusive branches may define the same device-model name when type/channel match, and the picker exposes only parameters common to every variant and identifies defaults that differ between branches, so parameter sweeps do not imply a single baseline value. Parser coverage checks top-level and subcircuit-scoped variants, duplicate and incompatible definitions, forbidden directives, malformed branch order, and unmatched blocks. The existing-runtime WASM fixtures verify ngspice selects the configured diode variant and produces the expected 4.602852 V output for both top-level and subcircuit-scoped conditional model cards. Imported libraries also preserve native top-level ngspice `.TEMP` cards and report that their setting applies circuit-wide; a non-default project temperature is emitted afterward so the project choice takes precedence. Parser/netlist tests, existing-runtime WASM fixtures, and a Firefox import-and-simulate workflow cover both inherited and overridden temperatures. Standalone resistor, capacitor, and inductor parts can select imported native `.MODEL R/C/L` cards; an explicit instance value overrides the model nominal parameter, while a blank value uses the model’s `R`, `CAP`, or `IND` default when declared. Model-family and default-value diagnostics run before simulation, netlists emit native instance syntax, and project asset tracking retains the source library while a passive model is selected. Unit coverage verifies all three model families and asset retention; Firefox verifies model selection and generated netlist output. A focused fixture on the existing pinned WASM runtime confirms R default/override operating-point behavior and the parsed C/L nominal parameters without rebuilding WASM. Duplicate parameter names in native `.MODEL` cards now use the final case-insensitive value in inspector metadata, matching ngspice while preserving the original card for simulation; parser coverage and an existing-runtime WASM comparison fixture verify this. Broader native ngspice dialect coverage remains active; rights review is deferred unless the project distribution scope changes, as recorded under M0.2. Acceptance: user-supplied op-amp IC model works in a fresh browser session; missing/incompatible models yield actionable diagnostics.

M2.4 standalone library terminator coverage (2026-10-24): A user library may end with ngspice’s deck-level `.END`; the library parser strips that final record and rejects any later non-comment content, so it cannot terminate ELSim’s generated circuit early. A parser regression and focused Firefox import/netlist workflow verify the source file imports and the generated deck contains only ELSim’s final `.end`. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; the existing local WASM was reused, with no rebuild or Pages workflow.

M2.4 native singular `.OPTION` cards (2026-10-24): ngspice accepts singular `.OPTION` cards as well as `.OPTIONS`; imported model libraries now preserve either spelling and issue the same circuit-wide solver-settings guidance. Both forms are rejected inside `.SUBCKT` rather than silently ignored. Parser tests cover preservation, source-line warnings, and scope validation; Firefox imports `.OPTION reltol=.005` and successfully simulates with the existing local WASM runtime. All 38 unit groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual, §11.1](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

M2.4 repeated global `.PARAM` assignments on one source line (2026-10-24): Duplicate names on the same top-level card now produce one conflict entry with the shared filename/line, rather than being silently collapsed. A parser regression verifies case-insensitive duplicate detection and a single source location; all 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native `.PARAM` identifier punctuation (2026-10-24): Duplicate-name analysis now recognizes ngspice parameter identifiers containing documented `!`, `#`, `$`, `%`, `[` and `]` characters after their initial letter. Parser tests verify case-insensitive conflicts across libraries for each supported punctuation form. This follows the [ngspice manual §2.11.1](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf); no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 `.PARAM` identifier validation (2026-10-24): Imported top-level and subcircuit-local `.PARAM` declarations now reject malformed identifiers and ngspice-reserved words such as `sqrt`, `defined`, and `time` during import, with the original source line. Tests cover built-in functions, reserved operators, scope, punctuation, and valid custom names. Identifier and reserved-word rules are specified in the [ngspice manual §§2.11.1, 2.11.6](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 continued global `.PARAM` conflict analysis (2026-10-24): Conflict analysis now uses parsed logical top-level parameter cards, so assignments introduced on `+` or ngspice backslash continuation lines are checked across imported libraries. Diagnostics point to the physical line where the card begins; local subcircuit parameters remain excluded. Parser tests cover continued duplicates and reserved-name validation. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and `git diff --check` pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 repeated parameter on one continued card (2026-10-24): Case-insensitive duplicates split between the first physical line and a `+` continuation now produce a conflict while retaining just one Review location at the card start. A parser regression covers this same-line logical declaration case; all 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass.

M2.4 native punctuation in `.FUNC` identifiers (2026-10-24): The imported-library parser and bounded parameter-expression tokenizer accept ngspice identifier punctuation in custom function names and arguments, so a function such as `twice!` with argument `x$` can be declared, resolved through a project-local include, and used by an IC parameter default. Domain coverage checks declaration, signature resolution, and expression validation; a focused Firefox/ngspice workflow checks a subcircuit-local punctuation-named function with the existing WASM build. This follows the [ngspice manual §§2.11–2.12](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 ambiguous project-wide `.FUNC` signatures (2026-10-24): The initial library parse now preserves a custom function name with a sentinel arity when unrelated imported files define that name with different argument counts. This keeps model-card expressions parseable; validation for a placed model still uses the exact signature resolved through its include chain. A regression covers an included one-argument helper alongside an unrelated two-argument definition, including a `.MODEL` expression and subcircuit default. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and `git diff --check` pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 duplicate `.FUNC` guidance (2026-10-24): ngspice has one global function namespace and does not overload a `.FUNC` name by argument count; a model call can therefore resolve to the wrong declaration or fail with a parameter mismatch even when only one definition is in that model's include chain. The imported-library conflict panel explains this limitation and keeps both filename/line locations visible. The worker now turns ngspice's parameter-mismatch failure into guidance to check for duplicate `.FUNC` names, rename one definition, and rerun. A focused Firefox/ngspice regression verifies model import, both source locations, the native failure, and the displayed recovery hint. These collisions remain unsupported until ELSim can safely isolate or rewrite function namespaces. All 780 domain assertions, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox/WASM test pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native `.FUNC` identifier validation (2026-10-24): Function names and arguments now use the same alphabetic-starting identifier rules as ngspice `.PARAM`, including the documented punctuation suffixes and rejecting reserved function/parameter words. Parser and Firefox/ngspice tests cover invalid leading underscores and reserved names plus punctuation-named functions using the native `= {expression}` form continued onto a `+` line. Rules follow the [ngspice manual §§2.11.1, 2.11.6, 2.12](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf).

Native lamp model support (2026-10-23): Lamps can now select user-imported ngspice R model cards in the Properties inspector, matching their documented resistor-based electrical model while keeping the result-driven glow animation. Netlist generation emits the selected model and allows its R parameter to provide the nominal resistance when the lamp value is blank. Component compatibility, explicit-value/default-value netlist generation, and a Firefox import/place/select/netlist workflow pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Native potentiometer model support (2026-10-23): Potentiometers can select user-imported ngspice R model cards. The chosen card is attached to both internal resistor branches, and its `R` parameter can provide the total resistance when the nominal value is blank; explicit component values still take precedence. Compatibility, generated branch values/model references, default-value handling, and incompatible-model diagnostics are covered. A focused Firefox import/select/edit/netlist workflow passes. A pinned solver fixture on the existing ngspice WASM runtime verifies the imported model on both split branches, a loaded 25% wiper output of 4.285714 V, and the expected upper/lower branch currents. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Passive model compatibility registry (2026-10-23): R, C, and L components now declare their ngspice model family in the shared component compatibility function alongside other built-in devices. The inspector model picker and electrical model checks consume this single mapping instead of maintaining separate passive fallbacks. Unit coverage confirms matching R/C/L cards, potentiometer R compatibility, and rejection of a mismatched card; TypeScript and all unit groups pass. No WASM rebuild is needed.

Imported device-model inspection (2026-10-23): When a built-in part selects a user-imported `.MODEL`, the Properties inspector offers a collapsed, keyboard-accessible summary that initially shows 16 parsed defaults, searches all model parameters by name or default value, and reveals further results in batches of 50, while noting that the original source card remains authoritative. A Review action opens the imported file at the original `.MODEL` line, connecting model values to their source without making users search the library list. The Firefox potentiometer workflow verifies `R=10k` and that Review focuses the correct source line.

Imported-model replacement follow-up (2026-10-23): replacing a library now rebinds placed subcircuits to the updated content-addressed component definition after dependency checks, while still validating the model’s ordered pins and parameters. Source and rights notes remain attached only when the replacement text is identical; changed model text clears those notes and tells the user to record the new source. Cancelling the file picker clears pending replacement state, and opening the ordinary import action explicitly clears it too, so the next library import cannot accidentally replace the wrong asset. Unit coverage and a focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verify provenance, rebinding, cancellation, and subsequent ordinary import against the existing runtime.

Imported subcircuit parameter defaults and instance overrides now accept ngspice’s documented deterministic math functions (`sqrt`, trigonometric/hyperbolic functions, `abs`, rounding, `pow`/`pwr`, `min`/`max`, `sgn`, and `ternary_fcn`) with bounded expression length, nesting, token count, and arity validation. Unsupported or stochastic calls remain rejected. The model inspector describes the math-expression support; Firefox verifies an imported `gain={max(sqrt(4),1)}` default and saves/netlists a `sqrt(9)` override. The existing local WASM fixture independently solves the default as 20 V and the override as 15 V without rebuilding the runtime. Unit coverage checks supported functions, nested calls, arity, unknown names, and rejected syntax.

Native ngspice string-valued subcircuit parameters are now accepted as bounded, single-line double-quoted literals (up to 64 characters); expressions, embedded quotes, and control characters remain validated separately. Imported model defaults and instance overrides retain the quotes and whitespace in the generated `X... PARAMS:` card, and the inspector identifies quoted text as a supported parameter value. Unit tests cover parsing, safety validation, project validation, and netlist generation; Firefox verifies editing and persistence, while an existing-runtime WASM workflow confirms ngspice still solves the model with a quoted default and override. No WASM rebuild was required.

Included-library `.FUNC` parameter support (2026-10-14): Libraries are parsed in two passes so an imported `.SUBCKT` parameter default can call a function declared in a project-local included library. Project validation and netlist generation share the collected signatures, while instance overrides still require the function to be available from the model library’s include chain. Firefox verifies importing the helper and model files, displaying the model parameter, and generating both `.FUNC` and `.SUBCKT` records; domain tests cover library analysis, include scoping, project validation, and netlisting. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment was needed.

Imported primitive `.MODEL` parameter metadata now retains bounded expressions that call `.FUNC` helpers declared in the same library or its project-local include chain. Project validation rejects a model card whose helper is declared only in an unrelated library. Domain tests cover the parsed parameter and include-scope rejection; Firefox imports the model before its helper, verifies the missing-include report clears after helper import, and confirms both `.FUNC` and the original `.MODEL` card reach the generated netlist. Parameter stepping accepts explicitly entered numeric bounds for validated braced or single-quoted expression defaults on imported device models and imported IC/reusable-block instance parameters. Numeric start/stop bounds are required; the original expression remains the unswept default for other instances.

Imported `.MODEL` helper-function solver check (2026-10-23): The existing-runtime ngspice WASM fixture compares a user NPN model with `BF={SCALE(5)}` from an explicitly imported `.include` library against an otherwise equivalent `BF=10` reference model; their operating-point outputs match. A focused Firefox workflow imports both user-owned libraries, selects the model, verifies the generated records, runs the circuit, and confirms the collector trace. TypeScript, the one-fixture WASM regression, and the focused Firefox test pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Imported primitive-device assignments now retain syntactically safe unresolved or wrong-family model names during project validation so the user can open the project and receive a Circuit Checks finding instead of losing the workspace to a starter-project fallback. The checker reports the expected SPICE card family for D, NPN, PNP, NJF, PJF, NMOS, PMOS, and SW devices; netlist validation still blocks incompatible model cards. Chromium UI coverage verifies missing- and wrong-family-model guidance plus affected-part selection, and project/netlist tests cover retention, safe model-name syntax, and preflight. Voltage-controlled switch projects now validate SW assignments during open/save round trips.

Cross-browser UI pass (2026-10-14): The 66 `@ui-smoke` browser workflows pass in Firefox, including the missing/wrong-family model guidance. The production preview and Vite development server were both used by the Firefox configuration; no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment was needed.

Full Firefox compatibility pass (2026-10-14): `npm run test:e2e:compat` passes all 132 workflows that exclude only engine-tagged suites. This includes wire and mixed-selection editing, copy/paste, archive round trips, model import/diagnostics, hierarchy editing, keyboard/focus behavior, and plot controls. No failures were found and no WASM rebuild was needed.

Fresh-session verification (2026-10-12): Firefox opens a `.elsim` archive containing the imported educational op-amp model in an isolated browser context, confirms the model asset and symbol are restored, and simulates the circuit to the expected 1.5 V output using the existing local WASM runtime. The focused `@engine-smoke` regression passes without rebuilding WASM.

The imported-model browser now searches subcircuit names, source filenames, ordered pins, parameter names, and device-model names/types. Both subcircuit and device-model lists render 50 matches initially and reveal further groups on request. Imported-symbol matches are capped at 50 and follow the subcircuit search, so large libraries do not create unbounded model-card or symbol-match button lists. A Chromium regression imports 52 subcircuits and 52 device models, verifies both caps and search paths, and checks that a matching two-pin symbol lists 50 matches before narrowing to one IC.

Imported-asset dependency guidance (2026-10-12): When placed ICs require a model library, or an IC uses an imported symbol, the library panel now explains why its remove control is disabled and what must be removed first. A focused UI smoke workflow checks the transitive model-library case.

Direct model-include recovery (2026-10-12): A native model dependency report with unresolved project-local `.include`/`.inc` files now opens the multi-file model picker directly. Users can select the referenced files together; ELSim still imports only user-selected text files and never reads paths from model source. Firefox UI coverage imports a parent `.SUBCKT`, follows its missing include, adds the child library, and confirms the dependency report clears. Unit tests, typecheck, and the focused Firefox workflow pass without rebuilding WASM.

Included-asset retention (2026-10-12): The required-model set now follows resolved include references recursively, including libraries that provide only shared `.PARAM`/`.FUNC` definitions and nested include files. Removing an imported IC's shared definition file can no longer silently break its simulation. A domain regression checks a two-level include chain and confirms unrelated libraries remain removable.

Model library file management (2026-10-12): Every imported library now has one remove control in its provenance card, including include-only files with no `.MODEL` or `.SUBCKT` cards. The control is disabled when a placed IC or another included library depends on the file; unused files can be removed directly. Firefox verifies both protected two-level include dependencies and deletion of an unrelated model file.

Shared model-library solver verification (2026-10-13): The Firefox engine workflow for two imported project-local libraries now checks that an `.include` resolves to the selected shared library, `.PARAM`, `.FUNC`, and `.GLOBAL` records appear in the generated netlist, and ngspice completes the circuit run with those records loaded. The focused case passes against the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Deep model-dependency traversal (2026-10-13): Recursive `.SUBCKT` cycle analysis now uses an explicit depth-first frame stack and an active-name index instead of JavaScript recursion and a linear search of the current path. A 12,000-definition cycle regression verifies stack safety and the reported cycle endpoints. All 30 unit groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or browser-server test is needed for this domain-only change.

Generated IC symbol pin layout (2026-10-14): Users can expand a model’s “Customize generated symbol pins” editor and choose left, right, top, or bottom for each ordered pin before creating a symbol. The generator retains SPICE pin order and validates that every pin receives one supported side; the generated `.elsym` becomes a project asset and the placed instance stores those exact pin offsets. The layout controls are mounted only when expanded and live in a lazy-loaded module. Unit tests cover default inference, custom coordinates/order, and invalid overrides; the focused Firefox importer workflow verifies a custom op-amp layout. All 31 unit groups, TypeScript, production build, and the focused Firefox test pass. The build stays within the initial gzip download budget (215.0/245 KiB); Vite’s advisory main-chunk warning remains by 0.26 kB. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Project-local ngspice `.LIB filename section` references (2026-10-14): Imported libraries can now refer to another user-imported file and named section. The parser distinguishes file references from `.LIB section` wrappers, omits the external directive from generated netlists, and resolves only safe project-local filenames. Dependency diagnostics identify missing/unselected sections; included asset closure retains the referenced file. Firefox imports a two-section library, selects the referenced section, verifies only its records are flattened into the netlist, and runs ngspice. Unit coverage verifies quoted references, unsafe paths, unselected sections, dependency retention, and generated netlists. All 34 domain-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild was needed; M2.4 remains active for broader library dialect and symbol coverage.

Terminal `.END` in imported model libraries (2026-10-14): Native model files may end with the same `.END` deck terminator as standalone circuits. The importer now strips a final `.END` before combining model text into the project netlist, while rejecting content after it and occurrences inside `.SUBCKT` or `.LIB` blocks. A netlist regression verifies ELSim emits only its own final terminator; the Firefox/ngspice library-section workflow now imports and simulates a source file containing `.END`. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Native global solver cards in imported libraries (2026-10-14): Project-local model libraries preserve ngspice `.OPTIONS`, `.IC`, and `.NODESET` cards as top-level records. The importer warns that `.OPTIONS` changes circuit-wide solver behavior, `.IC` changes transient initialization depending on UIC, and `.NODESET` supplies circuit-wide starting guesses; it rejects `.IC` and `.NODESET` inside a `.SUBCKT`. Parser tests cover preservation, warnings, and scope errors; Firefox verifies the warnings and generated netlist; an existing-runtime WASM fixture confirms `.IC V(out)=4` starts the transient near 4 V. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M2.4 remains active for broader library-dialect coverage.

Native lossy transmission-line dependencies (2026-10-14): Imported-IC dependency analysis now resolves ngspice `Y` single-lossy transmission-line references to `TXL` cards and variable-width `P` coupled-line references to `CPL` cards, including optional `LEN` instance syntax. Circuit Checks reports wrong model families for both prefixes. Unit tests cover fixed and variable conductor counts, missing and cross-library resolution, family mismatches, and the Firefox UI report selects the affected IC. This extends M2.4 native model-library coverage; solver support and user-authored model semantics remain ngspice’s responsibility.

Imported `.GLOBAL` solver coverage (2026-10-24): The Firefox workflow now imports and places a subcircuit with a global `VIN` rail, then runs the starter circuit and verifies the generated deck retains `.GLOBAL VIN` alongside the placed X instance. An existing-runtime ngspice fixture separately checks the numeric effect: a subcircuit resistor connected to a top-level 5 V global rail and a 1 kΩ load must solve to 2.5 V. Both focused paths pass without rebuilding WASM. Broader M2.4 library compatibility remains active.

M2.4 `.GLOBAL` comma guidance (2026-10-24): A direct probe against the existing ngspice runtime showed `.GLOBAL VCC, VSS` can parse while leaving `VCC,` as a different hierarchical node (`x1.vcc`); it does not behave like the whitespace-separated global names. The importer continues to reject this unsafe spelling and now explains that names must be space-separated. A parser regression protects the actionable error; no engine rebuild is needed.

M2.4 large-library section picker (2026-10-24): The single and batch model-library section dialogs now filter sections by name without changing selection state, so users can find a needed section in a large library without losing already checked sections. Filtered lists are computed once per render; selected and visible section counts remain clear when selected items are hidden; no-match feedback is announced in the dialog. The focused Firefox UI regression passes filtering in both dialogs, no matches, clearing the filter, and preserving/counting selected sections. TypeScript, all 39 domain-test groups, and the production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 imported model search work (2026-10-24): The component picker now builds normalized searchable text for imported subcircuits and device models only when their library-derived arrays change; typing filters cached text instead of rebuilding model names, filenames, pin labels, parameter names, and device families on each keystroke. Firefox verifies IC searches by model name, pin name, and parameter name, device-model searches by name, family, and filename, and retains the existing symbol pin-mapping flow. TypeScript passes; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 punctuation-bearing helper functions (2026-10-24): Imported ngspice `.FUNC` reachability now recognizes the documented identifier characters `!`, `#`, `$`, `%`, `[` and `]` (in addition to letters, digits, `_`, and `.`). A regression follows a B-source call through two helpers named with `#` and `!` and reports an unsupported `sqr()` at the helper source; another ensures an unreachable punctuation-named helper does not create a false compatibility warning. The Firefox UI regression confirms reachable warnings link to the correct imported library line and unreachable helpers stay quiet. All 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Identifier characters follow the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

### M3 — LTspice interoperability (deferred by owner)

**Status: paused for a separate future goal.** Do not count M3 items as active development work or resume LTspice-specific import/export changes during the current app goal. Preserve the existing compatibility notes, fixtures, and matrix for the later goal. Continue general native ngspice support under M2.4 when it benefits ELSim independently of LTspice compatibility.

**M3.1 Compatibility matrix and fixtures.** Catalog supported `.asc`, `.asy`, directives, primitives, parameters, and model dialect features. Use original hand-written fixtures or files whose redistribution is permitted. Acceptance: every supported syntax item has a fixture; known unsupported items have a diagnostic.

The compatibility matrix records scalar independent `I` sources and transient PULSE, SINE, EXP, SFFM, inline PWL, repeated PWL, and selected-file PWL support, with quantity-specific import, netlist, export, and fixtures. Forever current-source PWL repeats use ngspice's `r=0` extension; finite repeats expand to bounded point tables and require equal endpoint currents. The maintained [LTspice compatibility matrix](LTSPICE_COMPATIBILITY.md) records the current `.asc`, `.asy`, component, hierarchy, and model-dialect subset with fixture references and unsupported behavior. Fixtures are hand-written in `tests/ltspice-symbol.test.cjs` and `tests/ltspice-schematic.test.cjs`; no LTspice sample or artwork is bundled. Per-record coverage and real-world compatibility remain incomplete. Model preflight has tested diagnostics for limit functions handled by ngspice `lta`, PSpice VSWITCH cards outside the supported numeric subset, LTspice B-source `R=`/`P=`, behavioral Laplace options, raw imported-library LTspice `table()`, `idt()`, `if()`, and a further verified set of unavailable LTspice B-source functions, plus the LTspice/ngspice `^` operator semantic mismatch. The behavioral-source WASM fixture verifies ngspice’s numerical `^` and `pow()` results alongside `min()`, `max()`, and `uramp()`. Warnings on `+` continuation records now point to the physical line containing the unsupported option, and `$` inside quoted text is preserved when removing inline comments. The test compiler now preserves the `src/` module tree expected by the CommonJS suites; unit tests cover continuation attribution and quoted comment markers, and `npm test` passes. The WASM fixture suite also passes; native parity was skipped because no pinned native ngspice executable was supplied. This is not complete dialect validation; see the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf) and [LTspice behavioral-source reference](https://ltspicehelpmanual.azurewebsites.net/barbitrarybehavioralvoltageorcur.htm).

**M3.2 `.asy` and `.asc` importer.** Parse symbols, pin order, placements, wires, labels, directives, hierarchy references, and model paths. The bounded importer retains exact original root `.asc` text and its safe source filename in `importedSchematicSource`; child source files are retained in `importedChildSchematicSources`. `.asy` and model text remain as provenance-bearing assets. Project validation bounds and checks these extensions, and `.elsim` round trips preserve them. `.asc` `IOPIN` records match a same-coordinate `FLAG` to create ordered input/output/inout sheet-port metadata; unmatched, duplicate, and invalid names produce warnings. Parent imports link matching child `.asc` files to no-prefix block `.asy` symbols, validate ordered pins against child ports, and create native reusable sheet blocks; repeated instances share a definition. Nested child sheets are recursively imported with remapped IDs and model references, while ancestry filtering prevents circular imports. Unmatched child files are reported and retained. Voltage and current source `PWL FILE=...` references resolve against selected text companion files; supported 2–32-point tables are parsed into project data with filename provenance and exported as inline point lists, while missing/ambiguous/malformed data receives an import warning. LTspice `.func NAME(args) {expression}` declarations are retained in the exact source archive and inlined into behavioral B-source calls with bounded expansion and cycle detection (up to 32 definitions, eight arguments, 256 characters per function body, and 16 expansion levels). Function calls outside B-source expressions remain unsupported. LTspice `B` sources using `V={expression}` or `I={expression}` map to ELSim behavioral parts when syntax, declared `.param` names, and node/instance references pass the safe behavioral-expression validator. Literal monotonic `table(x, x0, y0, ...)` lookups with 2–256 point pairs are translated to clamped ngspice `pwl()` expressions. `if(x,y,z)` calls map to ngspice ternary syntax while preserving LTspice’s `x > 0.5` condition threshold; inverse-trig aliases `arcsin`, `arccos`, and `arctan` map to `asin`, `acos`, and `atan`. Variable table breakpoints and file-backed tables remain unsupported. Undeclared parameters and unsupported expressions are skipped with a warning while the original `.asc` remains preserved. Chromium and Firefox workflows import a parameter-backed voltage B-source schematic and solve it through the browser ngspice worker; a pinned WASM operating-point fixture also checks the expected 6 V output. The browser workflow imports a schematic and companion waveform file, verifies persisted points, and simulates through ngspice. Broader LTspice text and device semantics remain incomplete; the tested generated `.asc` subset is tracked in M3.4 and [LTSPICE_EXPORT.md](LTSPICE_EXPORT.md). .param declarations import numeric scalars and bounded braced arithmetic expressions with parameter references, parentheses, unary signs, and `+ - * / ^`; function calls, comparisons, and other syntax remain preserved with an explicit warning. Imported arithmetic is retained verbatim in the project `.param` and generated netlist. Domain tests verify safe-expression parsing, rejection of executable/unsupported syntax, and emitted netlist text. A pinned ngspice WASM operating-point fixture verifies that a dependent `{RBASE * 2}` parameter produces the expected 2 kΩ resistance, 1.6667 V divider output, and 1.6667 mA current. Chromium and Firefox browser regressions both import supported dependent arithmetic parameters, verify that unsupported function/comparison expressions still warn, and confirm the original `.asc` source remains preserved. Common `.save V(named_node)` and `.save I(placed_component)` directives restore persisted plot selections; `.ic V(named_node)=scalar`, `.ic V(node+,node-)=scalar` across a matching placed capacitor, and `.ic I(inductor)=scalar` initialize transient simulations; reversed capacitor terminal order preserves polarity by inverting the stored initial voltage. `.meas tran` / `.measure tran` scalar MAX/MIN/AVG/RMS and trigger/target DELAY measurements map to ELSim transient results for labeled-node voltage or placed-component current. Scalar MAX/MIN/AVG/RMS measurements now import, persist, simulate, and export optional FROM/TO time windows; the editor validates nonnegative SPICE time bounds and their order, current-source windows use clipped/interpolated sample integration, and a pinned WASM fixture plus Chromium worker workflow verify numeric results. Unsupported qualifiers and vector forms outside the supported labeled-node or placed-component set still warn. Trigger/target `RISE=n`, `FALL=n`, and `CROSS=n` counts from 1 through 9999 import, persist, edit, generate matching ngspice/LTspice directives, and calculate source-current delays from the requested crossing sequence; optional count fields default to one for older projects. Export emits matching scalar and trigger/target directives, with importer, exporter, schema, engine-independent waveform, and WASM fixtures covering the supported mapping. Capacitor and inductor `SYMATTR SpiceLine IC=scalar` maps to the corresponding transient element initial condition while retaining the original attribute; wrong units and unsupported accompanying `SpiceLine` options warn instead of being silently applied. Domain and Chromium tests verify the imported capacitor starts at 2 V. `.tran ... uic` maps to skip-initial-operating-point, while unsupported `startup` source ramp behavior warns; unresolved or incompatible directives also warn. Chromium verifies imported initial conditions, saved traces, and scalar measurements with ngspice. Untranslated component `SYMATTR` records are retained per part in the bounded `extensions.ltspice.attributes` namespace, validated by the v7 project model, preserved through archive round trips, and reported as losses by LTspice export. This provides source material for future loss-aware export without silently applying stale source lines to edited schematic data. Acceptance: imported ordinary schematics preserve topology and visible placement; report missing symbols/models and dialect mismatches; unknown source text survives project save/open; linked nested child sheets retain ordered ports and generate valid X instances. Voltage/current seven/eight-argument `PULSE(...)`, `SINE(...)`, `EXP(V1 V2 TD1 TAU1 TD2 TAU2)`, and `SFFM(VO VA FC MDI FS)` sources import into editable ELSim fields with quantity-specific units, validate and netlist for transient analysis, and export back to LTspice. SFFM retains LTspice field order and uses the common ngspice five-parameter form. Optional pulse cycle counts round-trip into ngspice and stop both source types after the requested pulses; archive, netlist, round-trip, and numeric WASM regressions cover voltage/current waveforms. Chromium browser interaction coverage for sine, exponential, and SFFM editing is configured but could not be run in this restricted socket environment. Pulse cycle-count editing is covered by the engine-independent Playwright regressions.

The imported `.asy` symbol reader now parses bounded `POLYLINE` geometry and preserves `TEXT` as literal, inert artwork in the symbol preview. Directive-like text is never executed. Parser tests cover point lists, malformed pairs, the 256-point limit, text alignment, and size bounds. Model-library import now supports named `.LIB` sections: schematic references select matching sections automatically, otherwise a section picker stores the chosen names while preserving original library bytes and hashes. Only selected model definitions reach the solver and generated LTspice deck. Unit, project-archive, export, and browser tests cover filtering, selection, and repair diagnostics; see [LTSPICE_COMPATIBILITY.md](LTSPICE_COMPATIBILITY.md).

**M3.3 Model compatibility assistant.** Identify unsupported proprietary/encrypted models, LTspice-specific constructs, and ngspice dialect differences. Offer only transformations backed by tests; never silently rewrite a model. Acceptance: user sees exactly which model or directive prevents simulation and can supply a compatible replacement.

Current slice: failures mapped to generated model-library lines report the imported filename and original source line, and the inspector offers a replacement action. Replacements retain project-local provenance and are accepted only if validation confirms that all instantiated subcircuits and selected device models remain available and pin/type compatible. The importer recognizes LTspice's encrypted-file marker and refuses protected or binary/non-UTF-8 content with an actionable request to obtain a plain-text model from its author/vendor; it does not attempt decryption. Imported libraries are scanned for `uplim`/`dnlim` and tanh variants; these trigger ngspice's limited `lta` translation mode. A pinned-WASM fixture verifies the upper-limit function result. `limit()` on native schematic B sources now translates to `min(max(x,low),high)` for ordered bounds, and generated-netlist tests verify the translation. Raw imported model libraries remain unchanged and receive a source-line diagnostic because the pinned WASM returns 0 V for `limit(4,0,3)` rather than LTspice's 3 V clamp, both with and without `lta`. `VSWITCH` cards with exactly numeric `VON`, `VOFF`, `RON`, and `ROFF` values now translate in the generated netlist to ngspice `SW` models using equivalent `VT`/`VH`; the imported file remains unchanged. Extra, conditional, or expression-based cards remain diagnosed as unsupported. A focused Firefox test exercises model selection, generated syntax, and both switch states in the existing WASM engine. This conversion is a deliberately narrow PSpice subset, not broad LTspice compatibility. The assistant flags tested PSpice-style `AKO:` inheritance and dimension-selected MOSFET bins outside ngspice-native BSIM3 levels 8 and 49. The default pinned WASM mode rejects the tested AKO card; a separate probe succeeds with `ngbehavior=psa`, which ELSim does not enable because it changes interpretation of the whole deck. Imports preserve these cards unchanged and report physical source lines; ELSim does not expand inheritance or choose bins. The compatibility report also covers B-source R=/P=, behavioral Laplace and tuning options, and checks retained root and child `.asc` sources for missing `.include`/`.inc`/`.lib` companions, untranslated `.option` settings, unsupported `.save` vectors, and unhandled dot directives, with original filename/line and next steps. Missing-library findings link to the file picker and clear when the matching file is imported; Chromium covers that repair loop. The WASM suite confirms cbrt, atan2, mod, pwrs, LTspice arc-function aliases, noise, smallsig, and unary `~` are rejected in raw imported model-library text by the current runtime; native schematic B sources translate cbrt(), atan2(), inverse-trig aliases, limit(), mod(), and the `%` operator; it also confirms an accepted ngspice `!` expression returns a different value from LTspice at the documented 0.5 Boolean threshold. The checker distinguishes the `~` parse failure, `!` semantic mismatch, and `!=` inequality without rewriting imported source. Behavioral function dialect warnings now require a B-source `V=` or `I=` expression, avoiding false positives in unrelated `.param`, `.model`, and passive-device text; unit regressions cover both missed/observed cases. Unit and pinned-WASM regressions verify source attribution, rejection, and numeric divergence. Chromium verifies that an imported library displays the filename, line, and suggested action for unsupported functions. This remains targeted detection, not complete dialect validation.
