**M3.4 Export design.** The format mapping, portable ZIP layout, hierarchy rules, licensing gate, loss categories, and verification gates are specified in [LTSPICE_EXPORT.md](LTSPICE_EXPORT.md). Implemented: deterministic pure file-tree generation; source/symbol mappings for the supported component subset; recursive child-sheet and port output; referenced model-library inclusion with SHA-256 checks; typed blockers/losses; machine-readable manifest; human-readable loss report; preview dialog; deterministic bounded ZIP download; Windows device-name-safe paths; and explicit unknown-license acknowledgement for included model files. Fixed-state switches export as `1m`/`1T` resistors matching the current ELSim `Ron`/`Roff`, with an explicit warning that LTspice receives no toggle control. Validated behavioral voltage/current sources now export as generated LTspice `B` symbols and preserve `V={...}` / `I={...}` expressions, including declared sheet `.param` references; node/component/parameter references and the allowlisted expression grammar are checked, `.param` directives are emitted per sheet, `^` is blocked because LTspice treats it as XOR while this ngspice engine uses exponentiation, and direct pass-through is reported as a review warning. Hand-written export/reimport fixtures cover both output modes and a shared parameter. Linear CCCS/CCVS devices export their voltage-source sensor in Value and gain/transresistance in Value2, with hand-written export/reimport round-trip coverage. Diagonal wires are emitted as diagonal WIRE records and split at pins, labels, endpoints, and explicit junctions; round-trip fixtures verify connected and unconnected crossings. Capacitor voltage initial conditions export as two-node `.ic V(...)` directives; unnamed nets receive deterministic flags at capacitor terminals. Inductor current initial conditions export as `.ic I(Lref)=current`. Round-trip fixtures check generated capacitor labels, restored capacitor IC polarity/target, and inductor IC values. Generated `.MODEL` cards and sheet `.PARAM` declarations are emitted on the sheet that owns their instances/declarations, including nested semiconductor devices. Declared root parameter sweeps and scalar resistor/lamp/capacitor/inductor/voltage-source/current-source sweeps export as LTspice `.step param NAME list ...` directives. Component sweeps substitute a generated `.param` into the target value; regular ranges expand to their selected points. Unit tests round-trip uneven global lists and R/C/L/current-source component sweeps. Numeric imported device-model parameter sweeps now emit generated `.param`/`.step` controls and clone the affected `.MODEL` card for only the swept instance, preserving the original card for other instances and preserving `.LIB` section scope by inserting the clone beside its source card. Reimport recognizes the generated model card and restores the component/parameter sweep; a netlist regression verifies the model expression and no structural diagnostics. Multiline model cards now preserve their physical continuation lines in the generated clone; export blocks if the swept assignment is split across physical lines or duplicated. An ngspice WASM fixture verifies that generated `.MODEL` parameter expressions load and respond to a model alteration. Waveform-field sweeps are now covered as well. A separate regression verifies a default diode model in a two-level child sheet. Malformed/missing assets and unsafe/colliding output paths remain blockers. Domain tests compare nested hierarchy connectivity after reimport and validate ZIP entry names/path limits; Chromium manually verified starter preview and a downloaded ZIP with `unzip -t`; Playwright covers included unknown-license models and unsupported-device blockers. Remaining release gate: open and simulate representative generated fixtures in LTspice itself and record version/date/results; broader format parity, true compression, and additional device mappings remain future work. Acceptance: exported supported projects open in LTspice and preserve circuit topology; unsupported ELSim animation/UI data is clearly omitted and reported.

Export interoperability update (2026-10-11): LTspice hierarchical `SpiceLine PARAMS:` overrides now import into matching declared child-sheet parameters with bounded scalar/arithmetic validation; nested export/import regression verifies distinct overrides survive. Unsupported values remain preserved and produce a source-line warning. LTspice PULSE, SINE, EXP, and SFFM numeric-field sweeps export through generated `.param`/`.step` values, import back into matching source-field sweep controls, and produce runnable ngspice netlists. Indexed PWL point-value sweeps keep point times literal. Shared PWL value-scale sweeps now use LTspice's `VALUE_SCALE_FACTOR`, restore as ELSim scale sweeps for voltage and current sources, and pass export/import/netlist domain regressions. Numeric imported device-model parameter sweep export now round-trips through an instance-scoped model clone. Multiline `.MODEL` continuation records round-trip when the target assignment remains on one physical line; split/duplicated assignments remain explicit export blockers.

### M4 — Distribution and performance

**M4.1 Browser release.** Partial: Supported browser matrix: Chromium and Firefox only. GitHub Actions contains one workflow: GitHub Pages deployment. It builds and deploys the engine-free editor preview under `/ELSim/`; no test suites or WASM compilation run in Actions by owner request. Engine-dependent Playwright cases carry an explicit `@ngspice` tag; UI-only suites exclude that tag, while smoke and full engine commands are available locally. Simulation requires a developer-built local WASM runtime and is not available on the hosted preview. When those assets are absent, the toolbar explains that editing and saving remain available and provides local build instructions; a Firefox UI regression fakes missing assets, checks the run guidance, and confirms resistor editing still works. Keep Pages for personal editor preview; do not use Pages as a test environment or poll deployments during feature work. Accessibility coverage: automated Firefox checks verify the 2 px focus outline and at least 3:1 contrast on component cards, search and analysis controls, inspector/PWL fields, context-menu actions, expandable summary controls, schematic parts/wires/net labels, the keyboard-opened Examples dialog, and waveform controls. Remaining: review unsampled focus states and perform manual screen-reader testing; browser automation does not establish assistive-technology usability. Continue browser/solver verification locally when deliberately requested. Acceptance: clean deployment on Chromium and Firefox for local project editing; simulation runs in local development when the user has built the runtime.

Accessibility review slice (2026-10-12): The UI smoke suite audits accessible names on all visible keyboard-focusable controls, including non-button SVG and role/tabindex controls, in the main workspace, Examples dialog, quick tour, schematic context menu, and 390 px phone layout. Firefox passes this DOM-level audit; after a real transient solve it also covers the interactive waveform workspace using the existing local runtime. Result playback controls expose a named group when transient results are available; Firefox verified that group in a real simulation using the already-built local runtime. The expanded name check passes in Firefox; button-name coverage previously passed in Chromium as part of the UI suite. The Component library, Schematic workspace, and Properties inspector now expose named landmarks, asserted by the accessible-controls smoke workflow. The Firefox control-name audit now also selects a resistor and checks all visible inspector inputs, buttons, and keyboard controls, in addition to the unselected workspace state. A separate Firefox check opens a newly created reusable-block settings dialog and verifies its visible controls and dialog keyboard controls are named. Large imported-model dependency and conflict reports use named navigable regions rather than live status announcements; a UI smoke regression checks the missing-transitive-dependency report. Existing contrast, focus-loop, keyboard-navigation, and reduced-motion regressions remain part of the local suite. Transient, AC, and DC-sweep charts are keyboard-focusable interactive groups: Left/Right moves one sample and Home/End jumps to the visible range edges, with a visible focus outline and usage guidance in each accessible name. Focused Firefox workflows verify keyboard cursor movement in all three analyses. The transient playback slider now exposes the current simulation time as `aria-valuetext` in seconds instead of announcing only an internal sample index; the Firefox current-animation workflow verifies that text updates when scrubbing. AC and DC cursor readouts use polite atomic live regions that announce the active frequency/sweep coordinate and selected trace values. Transient visible readouts remain non-live to avoid autoplay announcements; a separate polite live region announces time and up to five trace values after keyboard cursor movement. Focused Firefox AC/DC workflows verify their live-region content. Imported user ICs now include ordered model pin names in their accessible component name even without a custom symbol. When a custom symbol is present, its `SpiceOrder` pin names are announced instead; up to eight pins are listed with an overflow count. A focused Firefox import-and-place flow verifies both cases, including differing model and symbol pin labels. Broader screen-reader and non-button control review remains open.

Example-replacement focus follow-up (2026-10-13): Choosing “Open example” replaces the list with a confirmation prompt, which previously removed the focused button and left keyboard focus on the document body. The prompt now becomes a named alert dialog with its replacement explanation associated as the description and focuses the safe Cancel action; Cancel restores focus to the same example’s newly rendered button. Five focused Firefox workflows pass for both focus transitions, alert-dialog semantics, lazy lesson loading, picker filtering, nested-example replacement, and portable export. TypeScript passes; the last production bundle was 208.4 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against 245 KiB before this small semantic markup change. Manual screen-reader testing and unsampled accessibility states remain open under M4.1. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Plot viewport rerun verification (2026-10-13): The focused Firefox/ngspice plot workflow now captures the fully persisted range after zoom and drag-pan, then reruns the unchanged circuit and verifies that both the range and selected traces remain unchanged. This closes the tested rerun case for the user's saved waveform setup; edits that change topology still use the existing probe-reconciliation logic. The test passes with the existing local WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Thevenin beginner lesson (2026-10-13): The loaded-divider example now derives its open-circuit voltage and output resistance from the editable divider values and explains the equivalent source to a beginner. At the default 10 V, 1 kΩ/1 kΩ divider, it derives a 5 V source with 500 Ω output resistance, predicts 3.333 V with a 1 kΩ load, and updates the prediction to 4.762 V when the load changes to 10 kΩ. The example catalog advertises the Thevenin concept. A focused Firefox/ngspice workflow checks the initial explanation, stale edited-value prediction, and solved output/current after rerun; all 30 unit groups and the production size-budget build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Theme contrast guard (2026-10-12): the visible-controls Firefox smoke test now calculates rendered WCAG contrast ratios for body text, component names/subtitles, the Run button, and toolbar text. Each normal-text sample must meet 4.5:1. The focused Firefox test and TypeScript check pass; this representative token check does not replace a full contrast review of every state, overlay, warning, or focus indicator.

Wire editing accessibility follow-up (2026-10-12): endpoint handles are now keyboard-focusable controls with coordinate-aware names, arrow-key instructions, and a polite live announcement after movement; Firefox verifies the focus flow and announcement text.

Mobile waveform controls (2026-10-12): floating plot title bars are 44 px high and their close targets are 40×40 px. The Firefox waveform-window workflow checks both dimensions at 390 px after verifying keyboard move and resize behavior; it uses the existing local engine.

Floating waveform windows are now keyboard movable and resizable as well as pointer operated. Their title bar is a named, focusable group: arrow keys move it 10 px and Shift+arrow keys resize it by 20 px, respecting the same viewport and minimum-size bounds as pointer resizing. A focused Firefox workflow opens a real waveform, moves it, resizes it, and verifies the updated bounds using the existing local engine; no WASM rebuild was needed. Broader screen-reader and non-button control review remains open.

Waveform drag-pan follow-up (2026-10-13): Transient, AC, and DC sweep plot gestures now batch accumulated pointer deltas to one horizontal range update per animation frame and flush the pending movement on release/cancel. This bounds update frequency during high-rate pointer input; no timing gain is claimed. The focused Firefox plot interaction regression passes, with its cursor announcement assertion aligned to the engineering time units shown by the UI. No WASM rebuild was run.

Waveform focus-indicator review (2026-10-13): A Firefox/ngspice browser check now verifies keyboard-visible focus rings on a floating waveform title, interactive plot, and zoom button. Each has a solid outline at least 2 px wide and at least 3:1 contrast against its rendered surface. This covers these waveform states only; manual screen-reader review and broader state coverage remain open.

Current Pages-path slice: Vite accepts a validated absolute `VITE_BASE_PATH`, and application entry, engine availability checks, worker module/WASM lookup, and service-worker registration use that base. GitHub Pages uses the standard engine-free build under `/ELSim/`; it does not install Emscripten, compile ngspice, or package any runtime. The local Vite server can serve an ignored runtime produced by `tools/build-ngspice-wasm.sh`. Pages is not used as a test environment.

Dialog accessibility follow-up (2026-10-12): The existing UI smoke audit now also checks keyboard-reachable control names in the generated-netlist and Circuit Checks dialogs, alongside the editor, inspector, Examples, quick tour, shortcut reference, sheet settings, context menu, and phone layout. The focused audit passes in Chromium and Firefox. This is a DOM-level accessible-name check, not a substitute for a full screen-reader review; no ngspice or WASM build is needed.

UI health pass (2026-10-13): Reviewed the main editor interaction/render paths, waveform plot lifecycle and resize handling, cursor stores, wire animation loop, and clipboard selection flow. No new reproducible defect or safe additional optimization was identified in this pass. The Firefox `@ui-smoke` suite passes all 55 cases, including clipboard, mixed selection, responsive layout, keyboard focus, and waveform-window focus; all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production bundle check also pass. Existing safeguards include viewport-culling and memoized schematic layers, indexed wire hit testing, frame-batched plot panning, and shared cursor subscriptions. This is a focused UI health pass, not a claim that every UI state has been exhaustively reviewed; manual screen-reader testing and broader assistive-technology review remain open.

Inspector accessibility follow-up (2026-10-12): The focused Firefox accessible-name audit now selects the starter voltage source, capacitor, and ground as well as the resistor and checks every visible keyboard control, button, and form control in each inspector. All four inspector states pass; the full browser suite and ngspice build were not needed.

PWL editor accessibility follow-up (2026-10-12): The “Add point” action no longer exposes `aria-invalid`, which is reserved for invalid form controls; count-validation messages remain announced and associated with the action. The focused waveform-editor browser workflow asserts the button has no invalid state.

Runtime startup race fix (2026-10-12): Run stays disabled until the browser confirms both local ngspice runtime files are available, preventing an early click from reaching a failed dynamic module import. The button explains that the runtime check is in progress; after a confirmed missing runtime, existing setup guidance remains available. Focused Firefox regressions cover both pending and missing-runtime states.

**M4.2 Desktop evaluation.** Initial recommendation recorded in [DESKTOP_EVALUATION.md](DESKTOP_EVALUATION.md): keep the PWA primary; prototype Tauri 2 around the same web UI and ngspice WASM worker; keep Electron as a WebView-compatibility fallback; defer native ngspice until measurements justify it. The document compares packaging and engine paths, bounds the shell security interface, and lists performance, signing, updater, licensing, and three-OS validation gates. This is a design recommendation, not a platform prototype or desktop release. Remaining: clean Windows/macOS/Linux builds, actual WebView/engine workflows, installer and performance measurements, signing/notarization, update policy, and exact desktop ngspice license review.

**M4.3 Large-circuit performance.** Partial: `npm run benchmark:performance` generates named resistor-chain projects and wire-grid projects with independent two-endpoint segments at 100/500/1,000/5,000 parts, a repeated two-level hierarchy at 100–5,000 root instances, plus one-million-sample plot vectors; it reports repeatable median/p95 connectivity, netlist, hierarchy, extrema-reduction, raw-parse, and structured-clone estimate costs. When a local ignored engine build exists it also measures cold-worker ngspice operating-point solve, raw bytes, and vector sizes. Invariants check pin counts, exactly two endpoints per wire, hierarchy child definitions, no netlist errors, N0 = 5 V, and a bounded 902-point plot result. `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md` records the 2026-10-10 i7-12700F baseline and working budgets, plus the current-model wire benchmark run from 2026-10-12. A 64-pixel spatial broadphase reduced the 5,000-section chain to 48/54 ms p95 for connectivity/netlisting, within the 500 ms target; 1,000-section p95 is about 8/8 ms, within the 75 ms target. Its 5,000-section one-point OP solve takes 39 ms, raw parsing p95 is 4.8 ms, and a Node clone estimate for 40 kB of vectors is 6.8 ms. The historical 5,000-resistor/4,900-wire/34,300-segment fixture exposed quadratic wire-to-net mapping; the one-pass index reduced connectivity p95 from 610 to 140 ms and netlisting from 633 to 161 ms while retaining exact topology. The current independent-endpoint fixture has 4,900 wires/segments; its same-host p95 timings are recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. The two-level fixture's 5,000 repeated instances produce 15,000 root pins; connectivity p95 is 139 ms and netlisting p95 is 218 ms, within the 500 ms domain budget. A same-host repeat measured 200/431 ms p95 for hierarchy connectivity/netlisting and 170/208 ms for the historical routed-wire fixture; see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). Per-netlist sheet-ID maps replaced repeated linear hierarchy lookups; the next run measured hierarchy netlisting at 299 ms p95 (about 31% below the immediately preceding 431 ms run), with identical unit/WASM validation; benchmark variance is documented and this is not treated as a stable guaranteed gain. Pin/label attachments, T-junctions, explicit crossings, and junction behavior retain their exact geometry predicates; a long-segment overflow regression covers fallback behavior. Connection-dot rendering now uses a 64-unit spatial broadphase rather than comparing all pins/endpoints to every wire. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire fixture emits the same 9,800 dots with 16.035 ms median / 41.183 ms seven-run max; see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). Numeric topology and solver outputs remain exact; no approximations were introduced. Browser SVG rendering and selection latency have a Playwright baseline in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md` for 100/500/1,000/5,000 parts in Chromium and Firefox. The 5,000-part sheet loads in about 3.4–4.2 s. Per-part selected/focus subscriptions and memoized part/net-label layers, followed by viewport-based symbol-detail rendering, reduced 5,000-part selection median from 531.7 to 142.1 ms in Chromium and 308 to 14 ms in Firefox. Large-sheet initial render measured 2.53/3.15 s in Chromium/Firefox; viewport-based net-label rendering now mounts only labels near the visible view while retaining the selected label during pan; the visible-label keyboard test passes. Local 5,000-part runs mounted 120 of 10,000 labels and reduced SVG elements to 10,682. Urgent canvas selection updates are separated from transition-priority inspector updates; selection-to-frame measured 7.7/8.1 ms median/p95 in Chromium and 3/4 ms in Firefox, with editor-ready at 1.16/1.62 s. Visible and currently focused parts remain focusable named controls; arrow-key navigation searches all parts and pans to the next one, and inspector/keyboard workflows pass. Three fresh-page repeats per browser on one i7-12700F host now measured 5,000-part selection medians of 7.9–8.1 ms in Chromium and 3 ms in Firefox; editor-ready ranges were 1.14–1.18 s and 1.64–1.66 s. This establishes same-host repeatability only. Details are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; a second hardware baseline and reliable browser/worker peak-memory measurement remain open. Component and net-label SVG subtrees are memoized; a same-host before/after in-browser click-to-next-frame benchmark measured 1,000-part selection median dropping from 107.8 to 79.5 ms in Chromium and 92 to 77 ms in Firefox, with no loss of DOM keyboard access. Routed wire SVG subtrees now use the same memoization strategy; a controlled 1,000-part/1,200-wire browser comparison reduced selection median from 1,261 to 67 ms in Chromium and 2,054 to 57 ms in Firefox, while preserving wire keyboard and drag interaction; see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). Older Playwright click timings include automation overhead and are not directly comparable. Waveform worker responses now share one axis and transfer unique typed-array buffers; UI validation scans shared vectors once. A browser MessageChannel check confirms buffer detachment and matching values for a 5.6 MB payload; Chromium also passes the starter transient and imported-IC simulation flows with the real worker. The local WASM benchmark now runs 100k- and 1M-sample transient circuits in fresh Node workers, parses their output with the production raw parser, and records payload size plus sampled process RSS; the 1M case produced 1,034,006 points, final V(out) = 1.888 V, 16.5 MB binary raw, 16.5 MB parsed vectors, 2.42 s solve/write, 17 ms parse, and about 127 MB sampled process RSS increase on the documented i7-12700F host. The prior ASCII result was 55.8 MB and 476 ms to parse. These are process-level observations, not per-worker allocations. Chromium and Firefox run 1M-sample transients through the actual browser worker, plot, and time scrubber; the latest benchmark solved 1,000,130 samples while plotting five traces in 8.00 s and 20.06 s, and recorded three cursor scrubs at 241/37/22 ms and 45/28/29 ms respectively. The transient plot memoizes SVG trace geometry so cursor-only changes do not downsample or reformat the full vectors; see `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. Chromium renderer heap was unchanged at reported precision, but this API excludes worker WASM memory; Firefox exposes no equivalent heap metric in this run. A new real-worker browser benchmark solves 24 resistor sections to produce 49 vectors and renders 40 selected traces in 716 ms in Chromium and 1,071 ms in Firefox; the one-frame-per-selection timing is recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. A combined browser stress benchmark solves 10,012 samples across 49 real ngspice vectors, renders 40 selected traces, and verifies each SVG trace stays within the 902-point sampling bound; the latest rerun measured 1,004 ms result-ready / 659 ms selection-render in Chromium and 2,006 ms / 817 ms in Firefox. Larger simultaneous sample/trace counts and reliable browser/worker peak-memory remain unmeasured. A browser benchmark now expands the two-level reusable divider to 500 instances and verifies all 502 rendered parts plus an actual 1,003-vector ngspice operating point: reload-to-render measured 656/699 ms and run-to-result 421/781 ms in Chromium/Firefox; details are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. Cursor-linked schematic animation now indexes result vectors and updates only components inside the visible viewport plus a symbol margin; offscreen parts receive the latest solved values when revealed. A 500-component/1,001-vector browser benchmark reports 25 animated components in the initial view and all 501 after Fit all; one same-host Chromium run measured 602 ms without culling and 486 ms with it, while Firefox measured 370 ms after the change. These Playwright timings are local observations, not release budgets; details are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. Fit all pan offsets now follow the SVG viewBox sign convention and center the content bounds. The combined nonlinear stress fixture now drives 24 diode branches for 100,126 transient samples, renders 40 traces, and checks 74 vectors in Chromium and Firefox; see `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. The 5,000-part selection results have three fresh-page runs per browser on this host; no second hardware baseline is available. A Chromium cross-origin-isolated memory probe was also attempted, but this browser build rejected the API as unavailable; the result is recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. Circuit Checks use per-sheet trimmed label-name sets rather than scanning every label for each net and wire; benchmark runs preserve issue counts and report both the cached-connectivity app path and standalone path. At 5,000 repeated hierarchy instances, the app-path check p95 is 20.8 ms on the documented host. Selected-part clipboard collection now indexes wire geometry for selected-pin attachments and copied-junction checks; a 5,000-part/4,999-wire stress run retained all wires with a 16.6 ms median, and the Firefox copy/paste workflow confirms only newly pasted wire IDs stay selected. Deleting wires also uses the shared spatial junction-retention helper; a 5,000-wire/2,500-junction stress run retained all live junctions at 3.8 ms median, with unit coverage for removed crossings. Project archive code is loaded only for save/open; SPICE generation loads only on Run or when opening Netlist, parameter-sweep preparation loads on Run, and CSV serialization loads on export. Live analysis-setting validation remains synchronous. Initial JS transfer is now 210.7 KiB gzip (10.3 KiB below the preceding 221.0 KiB result); the main chunk fell from 581.88 to 538.42 KB minified but still exceeds Vite's 500 KB warning. Firefox verifies lazy generator loading, edited netlist output, a real starter simulation and CSV download, and an eight-case three-parameter sweep; details are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). A fresh production build on 2026-10-14 emits a 562.59 KB minified main entry (161.18 KiB gzip), so Vite reports its 500 KB raw-chunk warning. Total initial JS+CSS is 230.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB configured budget. A Rollup module report attributes the largest rendered contributions to App.tsx, project.ts, subcircuitLibrary.ts, and componentDefinitions.ts; see PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md. The raw warning does not establish a startup regression, so compare browser startup before changing chunk boundaries. Large-selection validation now checks selected wire IDs against a memoized set rather than a nested full-project scan; Firefox Ctrl+A and group-move regressions pass. Bulk paste now appends cloned components once and assigns unique references from one prebuilt set, avoiding repeated full-array copies and reference scans; the 5,000-existing + 5,000-pasted resistor microbenchmark measured the combined array/reference assembly at 3,641.27 ms median before and 2.94 ms after, while the focused Firefox full-circuit copy/paste regression verifies unique references and selects only new objects; see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). Undo/redo history now shares immutable project snapshots instead of cloning active schematic arrays or structured-cloning the whole project at drag start; Firefox property, endpoint, and child-sheet undo/redo coverage passes. Simulation startup also now keeps the immutable project root rather than structured-cloning the full project before netlist generation; Firefox starter-run and bounded eight-case sweep checks pass, with details in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). A fresh seven-case Firefox browser benchmark run passed on 2026-10-14: the 5,000-part/4,900-wire editor rendered in 791 ms with 12/17 ms selection median/p95, 42/57 ms zoom median/max, and 18/21 ms arrow-navigation median/max; the million-sample, 32-trace, eight-window workload reached results in 11.00 s with 34.70–66.71 ms scrubs. These remain consistent with same-host history and do not establish cross-device budgets; details are in PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md. Remaining: repeat large-schematic performance on a second representative machine. The Linux-only `npm run benchmark:browser:memory -- --plot-windows 1|8` command samples Firefox plus all descendants at 100 ms intervals and requires chronological page-ready, pre-run, worker-profile, and result-ready markers. It reports startup, solver/result transfer, main-thread result validation/transfer, and plot/cursor interaction peaks, plus WASM linear-memory capacity; it holds the page open for two seconds after plot/cursor checks. The earlier three-run whole-process peaks were 969.4–1,004.1 MiB PSS (median 990.4 MiB) for one window and 1,036.2–1,081.6 MiB (median 1,046.3 MiB) for eight windows; these remain one-host whole-process observations, not app-only allocations or portable budgets. A phase-validated one-window run measured 559 MiB startup, 990 MiB during solver/result creation, 631 MiB during result transfer/validation, and 620 MiB during plot interaction. Fresh repeated profiles now cover two one-window runs and three eight-window runs; their solver/result-transfer PSS medians are 1,013.3 and 1,013.8 MiB, with overlapping ranges (994.7–1,031.9 and 1,012.2–1,079.8 MiB). This does not establish a plot-count cost. Second-hardware validation remains open. Firefox page-heap APIs remain unavailable; direct WASM linear-memory capacity is measured in Chromium and Firefox. Acceptance: publish representative benchmark circuits and budgets before optimizing; no false numeric simplifications.

M4.3 startup-bundle recheck (2026-10-24): `npm run build` passed with 234.2 KiB startup JavaScript and 14.2 KiB CSS gzip (248.4 KiB combined, below the 254 KiB guard). This leaves 4.9 KiB under the combined cap, so keep startup-bundle changes measured; the generic Vite 500 kB minified-chunk advisory is not the project transfer budget. Full output and limitations are recorded in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.
Transient data-window measurement (2026-10-12): The local performance runner now compares the same 100 ms ngspice transient with full result saving and saving from 50 ms. It verifies final V(out) is unchanged and records samples/raw/vector sizes. On the i7-12700F, retaining only the last 50 ms reduced each stored payload by about 50%; this is evidence for lower result-storage and parser/transfer cost, not a solver-runtime or browser-heap claim. The run reuses the ignored local WASM and does not rebuild it.

Large-selection update (2026-10-12): Schematic selection checks use prebuilt ID sets instead of scanning selected-ID arrays for every part, wire, label, and dragged group member. A select-all no longer forces every selected offscreen label into the SVG; the active keyboard-focused label is retained, while other labels remain viewport-culled and regain their selected state when visible. The 5,000-part/10,000-label Firefox benchmark measures Ctrl+A at 190 ms to the next frame and confirms only 105 labels are mounted after selection. A current-model wire-heavy fixture with 1,000 parts and 1,200 independent two-endpoint segments measured Ctrl+A at 125 ms. Firefox keyboard-selection, mixed-selection drag, and Fit all regressions pass. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Playback update (2026-10-12): simulation cursor changes no longer subscribe the parent schematic to every tick. Only viewport-visible animated components and a separate current-flow overlay subscribe; static wire paths remain memoized. Grounded-return and parallel-branch current-animation checks pass in Firefox. A two-run same-host comparison for the 500-component scrub benchmark measured a 9.7% lower median after this change (58.20 ms before, 52.54 ms after); the small, non-interleaved sample is suggestive rather than conclusive. Details are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Wire-flow update (2026-10-12): cursor sampling now visits only the ideal-wire networks containing visible wire segments, while preserving exact KCL flow results. On the 5,000-resistor/4,900-wire fixture, sampling 20 visible wires reduced median callback time from 25.08 ms to 0.225 ms; a three-run p95 outlier and single-network behavior remain caveats. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

View-navigation update (2026-10-12): connectivity and current-flow topology caches now depend on the circuit's parts, wires, junctions, and labels rather than the whole project object. Panning and zooming change only view data, so they no longer trigger full-circuit connectivity analysis or rebuild the current-flow graph. Circuit diagnostics and analysis-setting validation also reuse cached results across view-only commits, while still refreshing for electrical edits, hierarchy changes, model-library revisions, and simulation-setting changes. A 5,000-part Firefox browser benchmark recorded 21 ms median / 23 ms max zoom input-to-two-frames, 38 ms select-all, and 29 ms full-selection-drag input-to-frame; these are single-run local observations, not before/after claims or budgets. Focused Firefox animation and circuit-check workflows pass. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).
Large-schematic navigation follow-up (2026-10-12): The active schematic now owns live pan/zoom state and commits the viewport to project data after 200 ms idle, keeping wheel events from rerendering the full application. Pending viewport data is merged into immediate project export and autosave, and sheet changes flush the previous sheet before switching. On the 5,000-part/10,000-label Firefox fixture, one before/after run measured zoom input-to-two-frame median/p95 at 148/175 ms before and 17/40 ms after (88%/77% lower); the focused test also reloads and verifies the saved zoom/pan. Typecheck and the focused Firefox regression pass. This single local comparison is not a release budget; repeatability remains open.
Large-sheet SVG culling follow-up (2026-10-12): the schematic mounts only viewport-visible wire segments and components plus the existing component detail margin, retaining focused objects and selection state; arrow-key navigation pans to the next component before focusing it. Four same-host 5,000-part/4,900-wire runs all mounted 64 parts and 580 wires initially. Two Chromium runs measured 993/998 ms initial render and 23/21 ms median wheel-event-to-two-animation-frames, with max-of-seven 134/132 ms. Two Firefox runs measured 1,162/1,192 ms initial render; the corrected in-browser wheel timing measured 47 ms median and 74 ms max-of-five effective events. Earlier v5 timings included Playwright round-trip time and are not comparable. The current-model benchmark passes in both browsers; keyboard navigation to an off-screen part also passes. A focused ngspice/Firefox regression confirms off-screen branch wires are omitted, then regain the correct 5 mA direction/magnitude after Fit all and are culled again when zooming; it reuses the existing local runtime. Circuit Checks now loads its diagnostic module only when opened, avoiding synchronous full-circuit analysis during edits; its toolbar count is retained only for the exact immutable project snapshot that produced it. The production build moved 5.52 KiB gzip out of initial JavaScript, and the focused Firefox check workflows pass. These local observations are not budgets or cross-hardware comparisons. The latest UI review fixed browser text selection interrupting crossing-box drags and added minimum-area SVG wire hit targets; the 43-case Chromium UI smoke suite and the two focused Firefox selection checks pass. Remaining M4.3 work includes testing on a second representative machine and measuring total browser/worker resident memory; WASM linear-memory capacity is now measured directly in both supported browsers.
UI interaction work (2026-10-13): Schematic probing, simulation overlays, and wire context menus now reuse the App-level memoized connectivity result instead of running duplicate full-circuit connectivity analyses. Wire hit tests query the existing viewport spatial index, and net-label context menus use the SVG target directly instead of scanning every project label and querying the DOM per label. Sheet instance/deletion checks and transient-measurement signal choices are memoized by sheet/circuit structure, avoiding hierarchy scans and rebuilding voltage/current menus on every edit. Firefox verifies wire/component probing, net-label context actions, and transient-measurement editing. All 28 unit groups, typecheck, and the production build pass; no WASM or Pages build was run.

Project validation now parses each imported model library once, then resolves all imported subcircuit and device-model references through case-insensitive indexes; this removes repeated library parsing and linear name searches per placed IC, including nested-sheet component identity checks. The browser model-analysis and dialect caches also key on source revisions (content hash, filename, and selected sections), so editing provenance notes does not reparse library text. The project archive regression validates 400 placed ICs against a 600-definition user library.

Waveform plot preparation now resolves selected and disabled trace IDs with sets and maps trace IDs to palette indexes once per result. Plot visibility, color assignment, and cursor-readout preparation therefore avoid repeated list membership and `indexOf` scans as vector counts grow. AC plots also memoize their bounded sample indexes by visible frequency window and magnitude/phase mode, so vertical-axis zoom and panel resizing remap only the sampled points instead of rescanning every source sample. The focused Chromium AC cursor/wheel-zoom workflow waits for debounced project persistence and reads both shared and auxiliary-panel ranges; it passes against the existing local runtime. Typecheck and all 28 local unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild is needed for these UI-only changes.

Mixed-selection dragging no longer searches the full project arrays once per selected object on every pointer-move event. The production `moveSelectedSceneObjects` helper uses the selected immutable drag-start snapshot and updates arrays in linear time. Its 5,000-part/4,900-wire local benchmark measured 1.146 ms median / 2.383 ms p95, excluding React/browser interaction and persistence; details are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). The domain regression verifies immutable input and unchanged unselected parts, wires, and labels; the focused Firefox regression confirms mixed component/wire/label movement still shares the same grid delta.

Large group drags now preview by transforming the original visible SVG nodes directly and commit project data once on release. This keeps pointer capture stable and avoids rerendering the whole React schematic for every pointer event; objects outside the viewport update at commit. Firefox measured 49 ms input-to-next-frame for dragging a selection of 5,000 parts plus 10,000 labels, compared with the earlier 428 ms per-move React update path on the same host. Group movement, wire selection, and mixed component/wire/label regressions pass. This is a local observation, not a cross-machine budget; see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

WASM memory benchmark follow-up (2026-10-13): The local Node benchmark worker now captures the ngspice WebAssembly memory export and reports its committed linear-memory high-water mark separately from sampled process RSS. The i7-12700F run measured 19,660,800 bytes for 103,406 transient samples and 63,242,240 bytes for 1,034,006 samples; these are solver WASM linear-memory figures, not browser total-memory figures. The full performance benchmark passes against the existing local runtime without rebuilding WASM. A second representative hardware baseline and browser/WebView memory measurement remain open; see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Large-circuit benchmark repeat (2026-10-14): `npm run benchmark:performance:quick` on the documented i7-12700F host measured the 5,000-part/4,900-wire fixture at 34.2/46.6 ms connectivity median/p95 and 40.1/56.3 ms netlisting median/p95; cached-pin connection-dot computation was 7.8/19.9 ms for 9,800 dots. For 5,000 hierarchy instances, connectivity was 76.5/88.7 ms and netlisting 111.1/135.1 ms median/p95. Engine and transient benchmarks were skipped by the quick command. These repeat the existing same-machine profile; the second-hardware and in-browser total-memory measurements remain open.

Firefox UI/performance follow-up (2026-10-14): The full 101-case `@ui-smoke` suite passes in Firefox, including mixed component/wire copy-paste selection and recent schematic interaction regressions. The production build passes its 230 KiB initial-JavaScript gzip budget at 210.7 KiB and the 15 KiB CSS budget at 12.9 KiB; Vite still reports the main JavaScript chunk above its 500 KiB uncompressed advisory threshold. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire editor benchmark rendered in 774 ms and mounted 64 parts plus 551 wires; selection was 8 ms median / 16 ms p95, select-all was 75 ms input-to-frame, and zoom was 39 ms median / 50 ms maximum over seven samples. The existing local ngspice runtime solved 1,000,130 samples with 32 traces across eight waveform windows in 11.06 s; three cursor scrubs measured 49.91, 33.04, and 32.06 ms, and sampled maximum frame delay during plot validation was 13 ms. These single-host observations do not establish cross-machine limits, but do not support a speculative renderer rewrite. The full M4.3 follow-up still needs a second-hardware profile and browser/WebView total-memory measurement.

Visible current-flow overlay (2026-10-14): During pan/zoom, the schematic already computes the visible wire list, but the animation layer was scanning every project wire to find that same subset. It now consumes the visible list directly, avoiding an additional full-wire filter on each viewport update. Firefox/ngspice verifies parallel-branch current animation against the existing local WASM runtime. The engine-free 5,000-part browser benchmark passes, but does not exercise active current-flow overlays, so no animation speedup is claimed.

UI and browser-performance audit (2026-10-14): TypeScript, all 34 unit groups, all 98 Chromium UI-smoke workflows, the production web build, and all 14 Chromium/Firefox browser-performance checks pass. The cursor-scrub benchmark fixture was corrected to place its pulse edge inside the measured interval; its cursor update then passed five repeated Chromium runs. In one performance-suite run, the 5,000-part wire-heavy schematic rendered in 0.67 s Chromium / 0.76 s Firefox, mounting 64 parts and 551 of 4,900 wires; zoom input-to-two-frame median was 39/38 ms, with sampled maxima of 70/55 ms. The million-sample transient with 32 traces across eight plot windows completed in 4.95 s / 11.0 s and warmed scrubs measured 32–51 ms / 44–50 ms. These local results include browser/worker scheduling and, for result-ready timings, the solver; they are observations, not budgets or cross-machine guarantees. No safe production UI optimization was supported by this pass; large-wire zoom and multi-trace plot refresh remain candidates for focused profiling. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

Schematic playback render follow-up (2026-10-14): SVG symbol primitives are now memoized individually, so cursor updates rerender animated marks while static strokes keep their existing React subtree. The capacitor charge animation passes its focused Firefox/ngspice workflow against the existing local runtime. TypeScript and the production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 227.9 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM compilation or Pages workflow ran.

Firefox UI audit (2026-10-14): The current 107-workflow engine-independent `@ui-smoke` suite passes in Firefox after the schematic playback render change. It covers project recovery, lessons, keyboard/focus behavior, selected-wire copy/paste and mixed movement, model controls, hierarchy, responsive guidance, lazy UI modules, and accessibility names. The focused capacitor-animation and waveform rerun/zoom workflows also pass against the existing local runtime. No WASM compilation or Pages workflow ran.

Bulk selection notification allocation (2026-10-24): `updatePartUiState` now notifies changed part rows directly from the old/new selected sets instead of allocating a third Set solely to deduplicate changed IDs. A 5,000-ID regression verifies selected, deselected, focus-changed, and unrelated rows receive the correct single notification. The focused Firefox 5,000-part/4,900-wire benchmark passes; its single-run Ctrl+A measurement was 76 ms before and 74 ms after, which is not enough evidence to claim a timing improvement. The change removes one O(n) temporary Set allocation. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Imported device-model picker indexing (2026-10-14): Imported `.MODEL` cards are grouped once by device type and VDMOS channel for the inspector, so each App render no longer filters the full imported-library model list to build one selected component’s dropdown. Firefox verifies native R/C/L model selection and an N-channel VDMOS import, selection, and solve; TypeScript passes. No WASM compilation or Pages workflow ran.

Large-circuit benchmark recheck (2026-10-16): `npm run benchmark:performance:quick` reran on the documented i7-12700F host without building or invoking WASM. At 5,000 resistor sections, connectivity/netlisting measured 39.6/47.8 ms and 54.3/67.1 ms median/p95; at 5,000 parts with 4,900 independent wires, they measured 35.9/39.6 ms and 45.8/53.6 ms. The 5,000-instance hierarchy measured 78.9/92.9 ms connectivity and 122.7/145.0 ms netlisting. Cached-pin computation of 9,800 connection dots measured 6.0/15.7 ms median/p95. These remain same-host observations without before/after claims; the results do not justify a risky renderer or geometry rewrite. Second-hardware profiling and browser/worker total-memory measurements remain open under M4.3.

Schematic component render follow-up (2026-10-23): `CircuitPart` now reuses an immutable empty sheet-port layout for ordinary parts, preserving its memoized pin-count calculation across playback/selection rerenders. Imported symbol bounds are memoized by symbol identity so cursor-driven animations do not recalculate unchanged imported geometry. Typecheck, all 38 unit groups, production build, and whitespace checks pass. The Firefox UI suite could not start because the sandbox denied its local web-server listen (`EPERM`); no browser-level gain is claimed. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

## 4. Legal and open-source release policy

> **Owner decision (2026-10-12): out of scope.** The owner will build the ngspice WASM locally and is not preparing a public app/runtime release. Do not spend feature-work time simplifying, auditing, or changing license handling, and do not make these checks a gate for roadmap work. Keep existing notices and checks untouched unless the owner explicitly reopens distribution work.

This is an engineering risk assessment, not a legal opinion. The project can be open source under this approach, provided the exact distributed code and assets are audited.

| Material | Current finding | Required action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ngspice | Project states most code is modified BSD, with exceptions. Its manual identifies LGPL-covered portions and other provenance. | Pin source and build flags; audit compiled files and transitive code; preserve notices and satisfy any LGPL/source obligations for the actual bundle. Do not label the entire binary simply “BSD”. |
| CircuitJS/Falstad | CircuitJS1 states GPL-2.0-or-later. Ideas, workflows, and independently implemented behavior can guide ELSim; copied code/art/assets trigger its license conditions. | Build original code, symbols, examples, and wording. If incorporating CircuitJS code, revisit whole-app licensing first. |
| LTspice application/library | LTspice is free software to use but is not presented as open-source software; its EULA is installed with the product. Model and symbol rights vary by supplier. | Support user-provided files. Do not commit or package LTspice binaries, built-in libraries, demo circuits, fonts, icons, or third-party models until the specific rights are verified. Record provenance and license per bundled model. |
| User imports | A user may possess a file without redistribution rights. Browser-local import is different from publishing that file in this repository or on a server. | Keep imports local by default; show sharing/export warning if project contains restricted or unknown-license assets; require explicit user action for sharing. |
| Dependencies | Web UI, plot library, icon set, fonts, and desktop shell each have licenses. | Maintain lockfiles, SBOM, notices, and automated license checks, followed by human review of exceptions. |
| Marks | Falstad, CircuitJS, LTspice, and vendor product names identify other projects. | Describe compatibility factually; use original ELSim branding; avoid names/logos that imply endorsement. |

Do not claim “legal to use” for arbitrary manufacturer models or encrypted files. A compatibility parser is a separate deliverable from permission to redistribute source files or models. Before the first public binary, have a qualified open-source/IP reviewer inspect the chosen ELSim license, exact ngspice source set, bundled dependencies, model assets, and distribution notices.

## 5. Agent operating instructions

Use this section as the goal prompt for AI agents:

> Build ELSim according to the active milestones in `ROADMAP.md`; implement remaining app functionality without reopening the deferred license/release review. The owner builds the ngspice WebAssembly runtime locally; do not rebuild it during ordinary app work. Work in small, reviewable changes. Preserve architecture boundaries and the versioned project schema. Every new component must include electrical pin order, netlist generation, properties/units, original symbol rendering, optional animation mapping, and a numeric reference circuit. Every import path must produce a loss/diagnostic report. Update the roadmap's decision log and compatibility matrix when engineering evidence changes. Report completed acceptance criteria and focused test results with each change.

### Fast feedback rules

- For routine changes, run only the directly relevant unit test or focused browser test, plus TypeScript checking when source types change. The 28-group `npm test` suite is also short enough to use when a change crosses shared domain/engine code; do not automatically add full Playwright runs.
- For UI changes, run the smallest matching Playwright case in Firefox. Add an ngspice browser case only when the change affects simulation or result behavior; use the already-built local runtime.
- Do not rebuild ngspice WASM unless changing its build/runtime integration, testing a changed engine source, or the existing local runtime is absent or known stale. `npm run benchmark:performance` reuses the local runtime and is reserved for performance changes.
- Do not run or poll GitHub Pages as a development test. The Pages workflow is only the owner’s preview deployment; it builds the editor without ngspice.
- Run broader UI/engine suites only for milestone acceptance, a suspected cross-feature regression, or an explicit request. Report what was run and any skipped gate; a focused check must not be described as broad acceptance.

Suggested agent ownership once M0 contracts are agreed: engine/WASM; document/netlister; editor/design system; waveforms/animation; interoperability; release/legal inventory. Agents may prototype independently, but a maintainer resolves schema/adapter changes before merging.

### Definition of done for any issue

1. Acceptance criteria demonstrated with a reproducible example.
2. Relevant unit/integration tests and, for electrical behavior, comparison to pinned native ngspice.
3. Failure paths and user-facing diagnostics covered.
4. Documentation, compatibility matrix, and project migration updated if applicable.
5. Relevant technical dependencies and asset provenance recorded; license review is deferred by owner decision above and is not a feature-work gate.
6. Browser performance and accessibility checked for user-visible changes.

## 6. Decision log and open questions

| Decision | Current position | Evidence needed to finalize |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Runtime distribution | Generated ngspice WASM remains a local developer build and is not part of Pages or project binary releases | Reopen distribution review only if the project scope changes to ship generated runtime or third-party model assets |
| Browser engine | Single-thread ngspice WASM in Web Worker | M0.3 benchmarks and API viability |
| Parameter stepping | Generate bounded `alter`/`run` sequences and preserve one result dataset per value combination | Verify output parity and memory use for larger transient cases; ngspice does not offer a native `.step` command |
| Transient measurements | Named `.meas` scalar operations and delay, optional FROM/TO windows, parameter-sweep labels, source-current raw-sample calculations, and validated native ngspice scalar expressions | Add broader qualifiers only with fixtures; expression measurements are not exported to LTspice yet |
| Initial conditions | Support labeled-node voltage, capacitor-voltage, and inductor-current conditions. Element conditions emit device `IC=` values and automatically enable `uic` on `.tran`; node conditions emit `.ic` directives. | Existing netlist tests, browser flows, and pinned-WASM transient fixtures verify capacitor voltage and inductor current initialization. Add broader startup-condition cases when expanding analysis support. |
| Server backend | No required server for MVP | Revisit only if measured workloads require it |
| Desktop wrapper | Defer choice | M4.2 platform prototype |
| Plot renderer | Canvas first, internal trace API | Performance/accessibility benchmark |
| LTspice export | Implemented for a tested subset, including hierarchy, measurements, and capacitor voltage / inductor current initial conditions | Native LTspice open/simulate verification, broader format parity, and additional device mappings |

### Implementation evidence update (2026-10-09)

M0.3 has a reproducible **partial pass**. The original optimized build used Emscripten's 64 KiB default stack; checked execution showed `inp_fix_inst_calls_for_numparam` exhausting it while reserving four 10,000-entry local arrays. A clean optimized build from pinned ngspice 47 with a 2 MiB stack runs RC transient, diode operating point, RLC AC, and `.SUBCKT` fixtures in a Node worker. A fresh native build from the same commit matches every printed numeric row within `1e-7`. The build script and fixtures live under `tools/` and `engine/wasm/`; the generated wasm was 4.5 MiB and its Emscripten JS glue 76 KiB. The build enables XSPICE but skips Verilog/VHDL adapters and does not package or validate XSPICE `.cm` model modules, which remain unsupported.

The browser now runs the local ngspice WASM in a Web Worker for transient, DC operating-point, DC voltage-source sweep, and AC frequency-sweep analyses. Chromium verified the starter RC transient run, 1,037 time points, voltage/current vectors, wire voltage probing (`V(vin)`), component current probing (`I(R1)`), shared mixed-unit cursor readout, CSV export, stale-result indication, and rerun after edits. It also verified the DC operating point table against the browser worker (`V(vin) = 5 V`, `V(vout) = 5 V`, and probed `I(R1) = 0 A` for the open-at-DC capacitor circuit). AC run returned 101 complex frequency samples, displayed magnitude/phase, and exported selected vectors. DC sweep returned 51 points from 0 to 5 V, plotting the divider output against `V1`. Chromium exercised plot zoom, fixed-step pan, reset, and cursor readouts on transient, AC, and DC sweep plots. Chromium browser tests verify cancellation of an active long transient and recovery of the Run control. The focused AC source validation test passes against the browser WASM runtime. The new `.elsim` archive was downloaded in Chromium, reopened in the app with DC sweep settings preserved, and independently passed `unzip -t`; archive tests cover deterministic output, CRC failures, path traversal, compression rejection, and size limits. Automated probe hit-test coverage checks labeled wires, component bodies, rotation, pins, and rejects ground/background points. On 2026-10-10, Chromium passes all 22 engine-independent UI tests and Firefox passes the same ten engine-backed workflows as Chromium against the local WASM runtime. Still needed: engine memory/performance benchmarks and browser profiling of large real ngspice traces, broader upstream regressions, robust diagnostics coverage, true multi-panel docking, and final source/license audit. Do not claim Verilog/VHDL or full XSPICE external-module support.

### Compatibility follow-up (2026-10-11)

LTspice current-source `SINE(... Ncycles)` is now recognized as an unsupported finite-cycle waveform and skipped with a source-specific warning; the exact imported `.asc` text remains in project provenance. This avoids silently changing a finite burst into a continuous sine. A focused importer regression verifies omission, warning text, and source preservation. The behavior is recorded in `LTSPICE_COMPATIBILITY.md`. Local verification: `npm test` passes all 25 suites and `npm run typecheck` passes; `git diff --check` passes.

### Measurement compatibility follow-up (2026-10-11)

Trigger/target transient delay thresholds now accept SPICE SI suffixes and optional voltage/current unit letters across LTspice import, project validation, generated ngspice measurements, and source-current sample interpolation. Netlist generation strips unit letters and converts the value to SPICE scalar form; project data retains the imported/user-entered spelling. `npm test` passes all 25 suites, `npm run typecheck` passes, and `git diff --check` passes. GitHub Pages and Actions were not checked.

### Delay measurement follow-up (2026-10-11)

Trigger and target delay measurements now support independent `RISE=n`, `FALL=n`, and `CROSS=n` counts from 1 to 9999. LTspice import preserves counts greater than one, the delay editor exposes both counts, ngspice command generation emits the matching edge/count syntax, and source-current sample calculations locate the requested crossing sequence. Count fields are optional in the project format so older project data retains its existing shape and defaults to one. `npm test` passes all 25 suites, including shared threshold/count validator cases; `npm run typecheck` passes, both JSON schemas parse, and `git diff --check` passes. Inline threshold and crossing-number errors use the same rules as project validation, with an accessible browser regression added. The focused Playwright regression could not run here because the sandbox rejects Vite binding its local test port (`listen EPERM`).

Implementation update (2026-10-11): Educational NPN/PNP forward beta is checked consistently in circuit diagnostics and netlist generation (whole number, 1–1000). Invalid live edits stop before a model card is generated, select the transistor, and focus its beta field. Domain tests, the TypeScript check, and a focused local Chromium browser regression pass.

Ground preflight now follows only the active sheet and its instantiated child-sheet hierarchy, and requires an actual non-ground component terminal on node `0`. Since SPICE node `0` is global, a connected ground inside a used child sheet satisfies the check; an isolated ground symbol/label or a ground in an unused sheet does not. Domain regressions cover these cases.

Model dependency and recursive-chain checks now use that same active simulation scope, so unused sheet definitions no longer produce blockers for the circuit currently being checked. A domain regression covers an unused sheet whose imported IC model references a missing child model.

When a missing dependency or recursive model is inside a nested sheet, the finding now selects its visible top-level block instance in the active sheet. Direct and nested model dependency selection targets are covered by domain tests.

Imported `.SUBCKT` and `.MODEL` names duplicated case-insensitively across selected libraries are retained and reported with every source filename and line, whether imported separately or alongside an LTspice schematic. Each definition opens at its original source line. Circuit checks also flag conflicts used by the active hierarchy and select the affected top-level component, so users can resolve ambiguity before trusting a run. Domain, archive, and local Chromium regressions cover preservation, reporting, source review, and component selection.

Active conflict checks include device models referenced inside imported subcircuits and select the visible top-level block. Unused-sheet definitions do not produce active-circuit conflict warnings.

LTspice four-pin voltage-controlled `S` devices now produce a specific import warning naming their model and explaining that ELSim's manual switch has different behavior; the original schematic remains preserved. Importer regression verifies the warning and source preservation.

Nonlinear `POLY` forms of LTspice `F`/`H` current-controlled sources now receive a specific unsupported-form warning rather than a generic missing sensing-source message. The original schematic is preserved; a hand-written importer fixture verifies this diagnostic.

Exclude the entire M3 milestone and LTspice-only import/export work from current-goal progress estimates; do not count its completion as required for the active app goal. The generated ngspice WASM is local-only and its distribution review is not an active gate. Safari is outside the supported-browser scope. Refresh implementation estimates from active milestones only; do not count paused LTspice scope.

Tests cover native pin alignment, the starter circuit, legacy/manual off-grid project normalization, and browser placement/viewport interactions. Local typecheck, 26 test groups, and production build pass. Firefox browser automation was not rerun in this change; Pages/Actions were not used for testing.

GitHub Pages and Actions were not checked.

Transient delay follow-up (2026-10-11): Delay results are now computed from the solved voltage/current traces so the target crossing count is measured after the selected trigger crossing, rather than accepting ngspice's independently counted target event (which can produce a negative delay). Added a regression with a deliberately negative ngspice scalar result and corrected the counted-crossing WASM fixture expectations. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, and `npm run test:wasm` pass; WASM numeric comparison against native ngspice was skipped because no native executable was supplied.

Transformer follow-up (2026-10-12): The built-in coupled-inductor transformer now exposes its magnetic coupling coefficient in the inspector. A value of 1 keeps ideal coupling; lower positive values let learners model leakage. The coefficient persists in project data, is validated in the range (0, 1], and is emitted in the ngspice K statement. Domain tests cover the emitted value, invalid bounds, and component-definition default. This extends M1.2 without requiring a WASM rebuild.

Potentiometer library slice (2026-10-12): Added a native three-pin linear potentiometer with named upper, wiper, and lower pins; total resistance and wiper position persist in project files and validate before netlisting. The ngspice adapter emits two series resistors, reports internal-name conflicts, and exposes upper-to-wiper current; wire animation receives both section currents. The palette and inspector explain the adjustable divider, and the guided lesson solves 5 V at 50% and 7.5 V at 25%. It clarifies that position is measured from the upper terminal, predicts the new output while the prior operating-point result is stale, offers a direct rerun, compares the prediction with ngspice, and suggests the complementary 75% position. LTspice export gives this component an explicit blocker while compatibility remains paused. Unit tests, typecheck, production build, and the focused browser simulation pass against the existing local WASM runtime.

### UI interaction rendering follow-up (2026-10-13)

Pointer-cancellation and no-op edit handling: schematic `pointercancel` and unexpected pointer-capture loss roll back in-progress part, wire, label, endpoint, group, pan, box-select, and wire-creation previews instead of entering the successful pointer-up commit path. Preview transforms/attributes and hidden current-flow/connection-dot overlays are restored, a cancelled wire is not added to the project, and a cancelled edit restores the pre-gesture undo/redo history and refreshes the Undo/Redo toolbar state. Successful pointer-up adds an undo snapshot only when a move actually changes grid coordinates; clicking an object or dragging less than one grid step no longer creates a phantom undo entry. A Firefox regression confirms a selection click after Undo preserves Redo. The complete engine-independent Firefox UI suite passed 141/141 before the no-op-history refinement; focused Firefox interaction tests and TypeScript pass after it, covering component click, part/endpoint/group/wire-creation cancellation, box selection, copy/paste, independent wire movement, and endpoint dragging. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Single-object component, wire-segment, and net-label dragging uses an SVG transform preview, while wire-endpoint dragging previews line, hit-target, and handle geometry directly. Both paths commit the scene change on pointer release instead of rewriting project state for every pointer event. Current-flow and connection-dot overlays are hidden during the relevant preview and restored afterward. Common schematic actions now resolve component, wire, and label IDs from the existing memoized indexes. Seven focused Firefox drag, keyboard, hover, and connection-dot regressions, all 29 unit groups, TypeScript, and the local production build pass; the build remains within its initial download budgets. This is a code-path optimization; no end-to-end latency gain is claimed from a before/after measurement. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Waveform cursor notification follow-up (2026-10-13): the cursor snapshot changes immediately to the latest solved sample, but React subscribers for toolbar, schematic animation, and plots now receive at most one notification per display frame. This coalesces bursty scrub events without interpolating sample times. A unit regression verifies immediate exact reads and single-frame notification; Firefox confirms wire motion continues during playback. The full unit suite and production build pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Wire animation follow-up (2026-10-13): the requestAnimationFrame loop now iterates only wire segments whose solved current is nonzero, rather than scanning every visible segment and skipping idle ones on every frame. The active-flow set is updated with each current sample and cleaned up when paths unmount; unit coverage checks simultaneous flowing wires and confirms idle paths are not written. All 116 non-engine Firefox UI cases, both focused Firefox current-animation playback/reduced-motion cases, all 30 unit groups, typecheck, Chromium UI smoke, and production build pass. This reduces frame-loop work in large schematics without changing current magnitude, phase, or dot appearance; it does not yet claim a measured end-to-end frame-rate gain. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Connection-dot recomputation follow-up (2026-10-13): schematic pin locations are now reused by wire connection-dot generation instead of being recalculated on each edit. The 5,000-part benchmark still produces all 9,800 expected dots; paired measurements were 10.361 ms median / 29.885 ms p95 without pin reuse and 8.257 ms / 10.089 ms with it. Geometry regressions verify identical dot membership and coordinates. This removes duplicate pin-position work from the UI update path; it is not a frame-time claim. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild was needed.

Floating waveform render isolation (2026-10-13): plot windows and their result plots are memoized behind stable per-window callbacks. Schematic selection/property updates no longer recreate each plot's React tree when its result, traces, range, and panel geometry are unchanged; editing one window still updates that window independently. Firefox verifies all 116 non-engine UI cases and the two solver-backed probe/window workflows for adding traces, preserving panel geometry/colors, resizing, and rerunning. Unit tests, typecheck, and production build pass. This is a render-boundary improvement; a before/after browser timing has not yet been measured. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Modal keyboard handling follow-up (2026-10-13): modal Tab trapping is shared by the app dialogs and keyboard-shortcut dialog instead of maintaining duplicate implementations. Closing the generated-netlist dialog with Escape or its close action now restores focus to the control that opened it; Firefox covers this alongside the Examples, Circuit Checks, and sheet-settings focus loops. All 52 Firefox UI smoke cases and all 7 Firefox browser performance benchmarks pass, including 5,000-part selection/navigation and large waveform workflows; no WASM rebuild was needed. Manual screen-reader review and broader focus-indicator review remain open under M4.1.

Waveform resize rendering audit (2026-10-14): transient, AC, and DC plots now memoize their waveform paths against the rounded drawing bounds rather than every raw ResizeObserver width/height update. Tiny sub-0.01 px size changes still refresh plot axes/layout but skip remapping each trace’s sampled points; data ranges, zoom, and meaningful plot-size changes remain exact invalidation inputs. The Firefox UI sweep exposed stale accessible-name and file-input selectors in the large imported-IC regression; both now match the current symbol importer, and the isolated Firefox test passes. Unit tests, typecheck, production build, and the focused Firefox regression pass. The full engine-independent Firefox suite ran 136/137 before those selector fixes; it has not been rerun in full. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Focus-indicator contrast review (2026-10-13): Firefox now checks the 2 px focus outline's computed style and at least 3:1 contrast against the rendered surface for component cards, search/analysis controls, inspector/PWL fields, context-menu actions, and the keyboard-opened Examples dialog. This exposed that dialog close buttons receive programmatic focus without a visible ring; modal close controls now show the accent ring when focused. The focused regression, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass at 206.6 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip. Floating waveform title, plot, and zoom controls retain their existing contrast check. Manual screen-reader review and focus states outside these samples remain open.

Model-section dialog focus follow-up (2026-10-13): opening a multi-section model library records the actual picker trigger. Escape, Cancel, backdrop dismissal, and successful import return focus to that trigger. Firefox verifies cancellation restores focus, the dialog’s visible keyboard controls have accessible names, and a subsequent section selection still imports only the chosen model; all 53 Firefox UI smoke cases, 30 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. The build is 206.5 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Clipboard and large-schematic UI audit (2026-10-14): Ctrl+C now includes net labels explicitly selected together with components and wires, so copied mixed selections retain the full circuit annotation and pasted labels remain selected and movable with the copied circuit. Clipboard label lookup uses a `Set` instead of repeated array searches. All 122 engine-independent Firefox UI tests and all 30 unit-test groups pass, as do TypeScript and the production size-budget build (209.2 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip). The 5,000-part Firefox benchmark mounted 64 parts and 551 wires; selection median/p95 was 11/20 ms, select-all input-to-frame was 62 ms, and zoom input-to-two-frames median/max was 40/54 ms. These are current local observations, not before/after claims. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Document-switch interaction reset (2026-10-14): Replacing a document now clears pending paste selection, per-sheet selection memory, hover targets, and schematic-local drag, preview, label-draft, and context-menu state. The SVG canvas subtree is recreated for a new document so direct DOM drag previews and hidden current-flow overlays cannot leak into the replacement. Its resize observer reattaches, and Firefox verifies the view responds to resizing after replacement. The containing schematic stays mounted so a delayed viewport commit from the old document cannot run during unmount and write into the replacement document. The in-memory clipboard is preserved deliberately: users can copy a hierarchical block or imported IC and paste it into a new project, carrying its nested definitions and model assets. A Firefox regression confirms a context menu from the old schematic closes on replacement, while existing cross-project clipboard regressions protect intentional copy/paste. TypeScript, the focused Firefox regressions, and `git diff --check` pass; the broader UI audit continues. No WASM or Pages build was run.

UI-wide audit (2026-10-16): The transient, AC, and DC waveform size observer now watches the actual SVG plot instead of its parent and coalesces resize notifications to one layout read per animation frame. This keeps both plot geometry and sampled SVG paths synchronized when a floating scope or browser viewport resizes. The focused Firefox regression verifies viewBox and trace-path changes for keyboard resize, pointer resize, and phone viewport. All 194 engine-independent Firefox UI tests, 14 Chromium/Firefox browser performance benchmarks, all 34 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. The benchmark records current local observations rather tha before/after claims: Firefox's 5,000-part editor mounted 64 parts and reached the first measured editor-ready point in 652 ms with select-all-to-frame at 23 ms; its 5,000-part/4,900-wire schematic mounted 64 parts and 551 wires, rendered in 786 ms, and measured select-all-to-frame at 78 ms. The full-circuit clipboard regressions also pass for marquee copy, immediate mixed-group movement, exact cloned-wire selection, and rapid repeated paste. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This audit covers automated UI behavior and benchmarked workloads; it does not replace manual Firefox interaction or assistive-technology review.


Native imported-library `.TEMP` cards (2026-10-14): M2.4 now preserves top-level ngspice temperature cards, warns about their circuit-wide effect, and lets a non-default project temperature override an imported card by placing it later in the generated netlist. Parser/source-map tests and two existing-runtime WASM fixtures verify both default inheritance at 125 °C and override to 85 °C. Firefox verifies importing the library, seeing the warning, simulating with the override, and checking netlist order. All 34 unit-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M2.4 remains partial for broader model-library dialect coverage.
## 7. Primary references checked

- ngspice source: https://sourceforge.net/p/ngspice/ngspice/ci/master/tree/ (M0.3 spike pinned to `a80f6e3e95d51534905b1f23410a951802666656`)
- ngspice licensing overview: https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/devel.html
- ngspice manual, license and shared-library API: https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf
- ngspice shared-library overview: https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/shared.html
- CircuitJS1 repository and GPL notice: https://github.com/sharpie7/circuitjs1
- LTspice product and library information: https://www.analog.com/en/resources/design-tools-and-calculators/ltspice-simulator.html
- Analog Devices on third-party models: https://www.analog.com/en/resources/technical-articles/ltspice-how-to-import-third-party-models.html
- Analog Devices LTspice EULA location: https://support.analog.com/en-US/knowledgebase/article/ka-16982
- KiCad LTspice format import documentation: https://dev-docs.kicad.org/en/import-formats/ltspice/
- Emscripten pthread deployment requirements: https://emscripten.org/docs/porting/pthreads.html
- Emscripten worker APIs and deployment tradeoffs: https://emscripten.org/docs/api_reference/emscripten.h.html#worker-api
- LTspice arbitrary behavioral source syntax: https://ltspicehelpmanual.azurewebsites.net/barbitrarybehavioralvoltageorcur.htm
- Current EasyEDA ngspice-WASM build notes (candidate evidence only): https://github.com/easyeda/eext-simulation-with-ngspice/blob/main/NGSPICE_WASM_BUILD.md

Waveform probe follow-up (2026-10-11): Right-clicking a wire offers its voltage relative to ground. Right-clicking a two-terminal component offers its pin-to-pin voltage (including capacitors, with signed polarity) and current; multi-pin components allow choosing a positive and negative pin. Component voltage is derived by subtracting solved terminal node vectors, including sweep and complex AC results. Right-click menus also offer net-label add/edit on wires, component properties, and interactive switch toggling. Schematic current movement dots are rendered only on wires, based on solved branch currents; component symbols never receive moving dots.
