Tapped conductors split into sections with separate magnitudes derived from the solved terminal currents on each side, so parallel branches animate their own current while the shared trunk shows the summed current. Conductor loops with ambiguous ideal-wire current division are omitted. Dot phase advances continuously from signed current, so changing magnitude smoothly adjusts speed and reversing current changes travel direction without flipping the wire path. Playback sample changes update the current promptly, while dots move between samples without waiting on React renders. Capacitors retain solved-current `+`/`−` pin marks; result-derived glow/field indicators remain on applicable symbols, while branch flow itself is shown by wire dots. Capacitor plate glow extends and brightens with solved stored charge `Q = C × V` as results are played or scrubbed. Wire animation speed, dot size, and dot spacing controls are user-adjustable and saved locally. A saved Show wire current animation toggle skips flow calculation and cancels dot animation loops for large schematics. `npm test`, `npm run typecheck`, production `npm run build`, and `git diff --check` pass. Focused browser tests for probing, animation, and schematic viewport/zoom pass locally. GitHub Pages and Actions were not used for testing.

License and Pages packaging follow-up (2026-10-11): The static web artifact now includes ELSim's MIT `LICENSE`, the checked npm dependency inventory, and third-party notices; the offline app shell precaches them. The ngspice review template now includes a hash slot for the generated linked-object inventory, matching the release checker's required artifact list. The notice records the CC BY-SA 4.0 manual content included in ngspice's complete source archive. Local `npm run check:npm-licenses` passes for 74 locked packages. GitHub Pages itself remains gated: no artifact-bound ngspice approval exists, and this workspace does not have Emscripten or the pinned source checkout to regenerate and inspect the exact engine, linker map, and relinking archive. Do not mark the runtime approved or deploy it until that review is completed.

Pages WASM workflow fix (2026-10-11): The Pages workflow now invokes `tools/build-ngspice-wasm.sh` after checking out the pinned source and setting up Emscripten, so a fresh GitHub runner actually generates the runtime, upstream/toolchain notices, source archive, relinking objects, linker map, linked-object inventory, and checksums before the release/package checks. This fixes a missing build step; deployment remains blocked until a qualified reviewer approves the exact generated outputs. The release still needs review of the pinned ngspice source mix (Modified BSD default; LGPL numparam; excluded GPL table, KLU, OSDI, and Tcl paths as configured), Emscripten and runtime components, and CC BY-SA manual material in the source archive. Configure repository Pages publishing source as **GitHub Actions**. No hosted Pages or Actions run was used to verify this change.

Waveform workspace decisions (2026-10-11; preserve during future UI work): Each probe opens a floating waveform window. The probe flow offers a new window or adding the trace to an existing window. A window displays only traces the user selected; each selected trace can be hidden/shown or removed, and the full simulator vector inventory is not listed in a bottom panel or “Add traces” picker. Keep a reserved trace-control row so adding or toggling traces does not move the graph; do not show a “Positive component…” polarity helper or an empty-state prompt to choose traces. Put transient play/pause, rewind, and the simulation-time scrubber in the main toolbar. Show stale-result warnings in the main workspace toolbar, never inside the waveform window. Keep the plot window focused on the graph, its selected-trace controls, cursor readout, and relevant measurements/export. Resizing the window must resize and redraw the graph, axes, labels, and values together. Mouse-wheel zoom acts on the horizontal time/frequency axis around the pointer. These choices supersede the earlier waveform follow-up description of a collapsed “Add traces” picker; do not reintroduce that picker or the bottom trace list.

Waveform probe test migration follow-up (2026-10-11): Removed the empty waveform prompt and remaining unused `.trace-show-more` style. Browser helpers now create voltage/current probes through schematic right-click menus and the waveform destination dialog; operating-point assertions read only the selected rows in that plot window. The current-source example exposed and fixed a DC sweep default bug when its saved source reference was missing: choosing a valid source now resets quantity-specific ranges if the previous source cannot be resolved. Local Firefox passed the right-click probe, current-source sweep, imported LTspice B-source operating-point, and user op-amp operating-point workflows. `npm test` passes 27 suites; `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` pass. The full engine-backed browser suite remains to be rerun.

UI consistency follow-up (2026-10-11): The quick tour now explains right-click waveform probes and top-toolbar playback, matching the accepted workspace layout. Removed unreachable duplicate wire-copy handling; copying a selected wire now uses the same keyboard path as other selected objects. Circuit-check actions now pan offscreen affected components and wires into view before closing the report. Updated regressions to use an isolated two-endpoint wire, current paste offset, on-screen hierarchy fixtures, and the current-vector threshold units; source waveform netlist dialogs are closed between edits. Firefox passed focused tour, wire-copy, mixed-copy, shortcut, placement, waveform-measurement, hierarchy, circuit-check navigation, and source-editor regressions. The previous full Firefox UI run reported 62/69 passing; five of its seven failures have passed focused reruns after fixes. The two remaining failures are LTspice export/import coverage, which is paused per project direction. A full rerun remains pending.

Roadmap execution follow-up (2026-10-11): The migrated waveform regressions now assert the selected-trace window workflow. Firefox verified wheel-based horizontal zoom and confirmed horizontal dragging leaves the x range fixed for transient, AC, and DC plots. Global parameter sweep editing now retains discrete values when the user edits that list and updates its derived start/stop values; this fixed the editor silently reverting to an evenly spaced sweep. PWL repeat and finite-cycle workflows now explicitly open a voltage probe instead of expecting an automatic default trace. The imported device-model sweep opens its plotted node and checks all generated per-case traces. `npm test` passes 382 tests, `npm run typecheck`, and `git diff --check` pass. The four updated Firefox cases (PWL repeat, finite PWL repeat, global parameter sweep, imported model parameter sweep) pass; transient/AC/DC wheel zoom regressions also passed in the preceding focused run. The two-source `.dc` import workflow is retained as paused LTspice-compatibility coverage and is excluded from active-goal progress. Do not treat the whole engine browser suite as passing until it is rerun.

Test-loop speed follow-up (2026-10-11): LTspice-only browser workflows are tagged `@ltspice` and excluded from the active UI/Firefox and full engine commands; `npm run test:e2e:ltspice` keeps them available for the later compatibility goal. The active full engine list is 44 tests (23 paused LTspice tests are separate), and the fast `@engine-smoke` path is 8 tests. Unit tests, typecheck, and the smoke set pass. An early full-suite run exposed an intermittent current-source worker failure, while subsequent complete runs passed all 44 tests in Chromium (1.1 minutes) and Firefox (1.3 minutes); retain the shorter smoke command for routine iteration and use the full suite at solver or milestone checkpoints. No WASM rebuild or Pages/Actions run was done.

Engine error feedback follow-up (2026-10-11): Browser worker errors now show a useful message when the browser omits `ErrorEvent.message`, include an available script location, and handle message-deserialization failures instead of rendering `undefined`. Typecheck, all 27 unit groups, the 8-case smoke suite, and the complete 44-case active engine suite pass in both Chromium and Firefox. No WASM rebuild or Pages/Actions run was done.

Firefox production-preview test follow-up (2026-10-11): The editor compatibility command now runs the 59 Firefox UI cases separately from the PWA test. The previous PWA failure used a Pages-base `/ELSim/` build at a root preview URL, which returned 404 for the JS bundle; it was test setup, not a Firefox or app-shell failure. Added `npm run test:e2e:pwa:firefox`, documented that the standard root build must precede PWA preview checks, and verified the offline editing workflow passes in Firefox against `VITE_BASE_PATH=/ npm run build`. The Firefox editor suite had 59 passing cases before this separation; its new list contains those same 59 and excludes the independently passing PWA test. No WASM rebuild or Pages/Actions run was done.

Iteration scope and plot-loading follow-up (2026-10-12): LTspice compatibility remains paused. Do not spend feature-iteration time on license simplification, license review, or preparing a generated ngspice WASM release; the user builds that runtime locally, and it is not part of the published app artifact. Preserve existing license and provenance notices. Revisit distribution checks only if the user later asks to ship the generated runtime or bundled third-party models. Waveform rendering is now a deferred `AnalysisPlots` chunk loaded when a plot window opens, rather than part of the initial editor module. Local production build reduced initial JavaScript from 204.7 to 198.8 KiB gzip and emitted a separate 6.66 KiB gzip plot chunk; typecheck, all 28 unit-test groups, and the focused waveform-window Playwright workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or hosted Pages/Actions check was run.


Fast local test command update (2026-10-12): `npm run test:e2e:ui` now runs the tagged UI smoke set; `npm run test:e2e:ui:full` retains the broader no-engine browser suite. `npm run test:e2e:engine` remains the eight-case engine smoke set for changes that need simulation, and neither command rebuilds WASM. Routine unit tests remain `npm test`.

Development loop (2026-10-12): For routine changes, run `npm test` and `npm run typecheck`, then only the relevant focused Firefox workflow when browser behavior changed. Use `npm run test:e2e:ui` or `npm run test:e2e:engine:firefox` when the change crosses a broader UI or simulation boundary. Run the full browser matrix at milestone checkpoints. `npm run dev` serves the existing local WASM runtime; none of these checks rebuilds it. Rebuild WASM only after changing the ngspice source, Emscripten build recipe, or native/WASM engine integration. GitHub Pages remains a deployment path, not a test loop.
The eight-case default UI smoke suite covers the current 28-example catalog, visible-button accessible names, and implemented transformer shortcut. All eight tests pass in Chromium (7.2 s) and Firefox (9.0 s). The suite covers the quick tour, example search, keyboard probe actions, button names in the workspace/Examples/tour/context menu, shortcuts, group selection, component navigation, and keyboard wire/label selection. License simplification and publication review are out of scope; the user builds the WASM runtime locally.

Engine smoke selection correction (2026-10-12): The `@engine-smoke` tag had drifted to 17 tests while the documented target was eight. Restore the eight representative workflows (probe flow, current flow including a parallel branch, waveform-window behavior, initial conditions, and AC/DC plot controls); keep other simulation-backed lessons and viewport coverage in the full engine suite. The list-only check confirms exactly eight smoke tests and all 28 unit groups pass. Browser execution could not be repeated in this sandbox because Vite failed to bind its local test port (`listen EPERM`); the parallel-branch workflow previously passed in Firefox but failed in Chromium, so it remains in the smoke set and still needs a Chromium run in a normal local environment. No WASM build or hosted workflow was run.

Schematic keyboard-accessibility follow-up (2026-10-12): Focused parts, wires, and net labels now advertise and respond to the standard Context Menu and Shift+F10 keys, opening the same probe/options menu as a pointer right-click. The menu receives focus, supports arrow/Home/End navigation and Escape, and Escape returns focus to the schematic object. Focused Chromium and Firefox browser regressions pass; typecheck, all 28 unit-test groups, and `git diff --check` pass. No WASM build or Pages/Actions check was run.

Wire-animation scaling follow-up (2026-10-12): Current-flow polylines are now mounted only for wire segments whose bounding boxes overlap the visible schematic viewport. Offscreen base wires remain in the document and keyboard-accessible, while offscreen animated polylines unregister from the shared requestAnimationFrame loop. This reduces active DOM overlays and animation work when zoomed into large schematics without changing solved current calculations or electrical connectivity. Geometry unit coverage verifies viewport overlap boundaries; a focused local engine workflow checks that zooming hides offscreen particle polylines while retaining all wires. No WASM rebuild or Pages/Actions check was run.

Wire playback CPU follow-up (2026-10-12): Wire current flow now builds geometric sections, connectivity adjacency, connected components, bridge sets, and spanning forests once per project/connectivity result. Each playback sample only resolves terminal currents and propagates them through the cached topology, avoiding repeated geometry scans and Tarjan bridge detection while scrubbing or animating. The legacy one-shot flow function remains as a compatibility wrapper. A unit regression samples changing currents with one precomputed sampler and checks parity with the wrapper. Typecheck and all fast unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild, full browser suite, Pages, or Actions check was run.

Visible-wire result follow-up (2026-10-12): The current solver graph still includes the entire circuit so branch-current results stay correct, but per-sample flow objects and merged SVG point lists are now produced only for wires overlapping the viewport. Panning/zooming updates that requested-wire set while reusing the same cached topology. A domain regression verifies visible-only output matches the complete flow for those wires. Routine performance work continues without compiling the user-owned local WASM runtime.

Project autosave responsiveness follow-up (2026-10-12): Full-project JSON serialization is debounced by 200 ms, and pending saves flush when the document becomes hidden or the page closes. Normal-sized projects use synchronous `localStorage`; when its quota is exceeded, the latest JSON is saved to IndexedDB and compared against the local copy during startup. Editing remains blocked behind a short recovery screen until that check completes, preventing an older local copy from overwriting a newer IndexedDB recovery. The footer distinguishes localStorage saves, recovery-store saves, and failures; tests cover quota fallback, edit/reload recovery, and the case where both stores fail. No WASM or hosted build is needed.

Imported-IC beginner path (2026-10-12): The Examples library now includes an original ELSim five-pin op-amp model and matching schematic symbol as a runnable user-subcircuit example. Its lesson exposes the ordered `INP, INM, VPOS, VNEG, OUT` pins, 0.5 V input, ±5 V supplies, and 2:1 feedback network; the local ngspice run resolves to approximately 1.5 V. This gives a beginner a direct way to inspect an imported IC model and symbol without first assembling the import workflow manually. Domain netlist validation, typecheck, all 28 unit groups, and a focused engine browser workflow pass against the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild or hosted check was used. The starter model and symbol remain source files with the same content hashes used by the example project.

Voltage-controlled switch lesson follow-up (2026-10-12): The switch inspector now exposes its stored control threshold, which had been missing despite the component model and solver supporting it; the hint now correctly describes the turn-on/off thresholds as the center threshold ± half the hysteresis. The beginner lesson predicts whether the current control trace crosses the edited threshold, shows that prediction while prior results are stale, then compares the rerun's VOUT and switch state. Firefox verified lowering/raising threshold behavior with the existing local runtime. Typecheck and all 28 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or hosted workflow was run.

Current-controlled-source lesson follow-up (2026-10-12): The example now teaches the difference between a CCCS gain and a CCVS transresistance with live values from I(VSENSE). After the initial 1 mA solve, it guides changing H1 from 2 kΩ to 4 kΩ, predicts V_H = 4 V while F1 stays at −2 V, and compares both outputs after rerunning, including while the previous result is stale. Firefox verified the full edit/prediction/rerun path against the existing local runtime; typecheck and all 28 unit-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow was run.

Transformer beginner lesson and solver fix (2026-10-12): The AC transformer lesson now predicts secondary voltage from the measured 1 kHz primary voltage and current turns ratio, shows a stale-result estimate after changing T1 from 2:1 to 3:1, and compares the rerun with ngspice. The example had an ideal 0 V source directly parallel to the ideal primary winding, which caused a singular DC operating-point matrix; a 10 mΩ source resistance removes that redundant ideal loop while retaining the documented 2:1 response. The focused Firefox workflow verifies the initial 6.02 dB output and the 3 V rerun, and the netlist test checks the stabilizing source resistance. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow was run.

Focused Firefox test-loop improvement (2026-10-12): Added `npm run test:e2e:focus:firefox -- --grep "test title"` with a dedicated simulation-enabled Firefox config. It starts Vite only when needed and reuses the standard local `npm run dev` server on port 5173, avoiding temporary config edits and keeping routine checks to the changed browser workflow. Typecheck and a focused transformer workflow pass; no full suite, WASM build, or hosted workflow was run.


Roadmap accuracy and performance check (2026-10-12): Updated the project-format references to current schema v8 while retaining v1–v7 as migration inputs. Added component-definition ID migration context. The current implementation also announces keyboard-driven transient cursor values to assistive technology; autoplay remains quiet. A fresh `npm run benchmark:performance` run on the documented i7-12700F / Node 18.20.4 host measured 5,000-section chain connectivity/netlisting at 49.9/65.0 ms p95, 5,000 hierarchy instances at 145.3/230.8 ms p95, and 1,000,000-sample transient solve/raw write at 3.19 s plus 0.49 s raw parsing. This confirms local schematic preparation is below the existing 500 ms budget at these fixture sizes; solver time dominates long transients. The run reuses the already-built local engine and does not compile WASM. These numbers are one run on one host, not a cross-machine guarantee.

Passive series-loss model follow-up (2026-10-12): Capacitors now expose optional equivalent series resistance (ESR), and inductors expose optional winding resistance; absent or zero resistance preserves the prior ideal netlist. Positive values generate collision-safe internal series resistor/node names and source-map both generated rows to the owning component. Current probes continue to measure the passive branch, while capacitor charge and plate glow subtract the ESR drop and follow the ideal capacitance voltage. Values are schema-validated as nonnegative numeric resistances, invalid simulation input selects/focuses the setting, and the UI regression checks the resulting netlist. All 28 domain test groups, typecheck, production build, and a local ngspice WASM transient smoke deck pass without rebuilding WASM. Focused Firefox execution is currently blocked because this workspace cannot start Playwright's Vite webServer (`Process from config.webServer was not able to start`); the focused test is present and listed in the Firefox config.


Transformer loss-model follow-up (2026-10-12): The built-in transformer now accepts independent primary and secondary winding resistances; zero or omitted values retain the previous ideal netlist and the coupling coefficient continues to model leakage. Netlisting inserts collision-safe series resistors while keeping the coupled-inductor cards, ordered pins, and winding-current vectors intact. Schema and project validation reject invalid values; Circuit Checks and simulation preflight identify the affected transformer and inspector field. Domain coverage verifies the exact lossy netlist and rejects negative resistance. A pinned-runtime AC fixture confirms the loaded secondary drops to approximately 1.754 V with 10 Ω primary and 100 Ω secondary losses. The existing transformer Firefox workflow also checks both inspector fields and emitted netlist; full browser execution remains limited by the local Vite port bind restriction. No WASM rebuild was run.


Transformer animation update (2026-10-12): The schematic now reads both ngspice device vectors `i(@LT1_P[i])` and `i(@LT1_S[i])` from the current transient sample. Each winding gets its own current-scaled glow, and the secondary has a direction arrow tied to its solved signed current. The Firefox transformer workflow now checks both arrows after a transient run; no solver or WASM changes are needed.

Transformer beginner workflow (2026-10-12): Added a dedicated “Transformer winding currents” example preconfigured for a 50 Hz transient, so learners can run and scrub the two solved winding-current animations without changing analysis or editing the source. After the run, its lesson explains the inverse current-to-turns relationship, displays both solved winding currents, and offers an adaptive 2:1/3:1 comparison; edits mark results stale and provide a rerun action. The current readout accepts the ngspice internal vector IDs as well as friendly plot names. The existing AC turns-ratio lesson remains separate. The focused Firefox animation workflow verifies solved-current readouts, the changed-ratio stale message, and the follow-up experiment; a domain test checks the example's analysis and source setup. No WASM rebuild or full suite is required for routine changes.

Waveform control accessibility follow-up (2026-10-12): Plot zoom/reset/value-scale buttons are now exposed as a named “Plot navigation” group in the accessibility tree. The focused Firefox transient test queries that semantic role while verifying zoom, cursor and drag behavior. The same run refreshed two stale test locators for the current terminal-voltage menu wording and for the distinct chart/window live regions. Typecheck and the single Firefox/ngspice workflow pass using the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild or full suite was run.

Hierarchy parity acceptance (2026-10-12): A focused Firefox/ngspice regression now solves the two-level reusable divider with two distinct per-instance parameter overrides, then replaces the hierarchy with an electrically equivalent flat resistor network and compares both output voltages (2.5 V and 3.333333 V). This directly verifies the M2.3 flattened-reference acceptance and repeated-instance parameter isolation. Typecheck and the single focused browser workflow pass using the existing local WASM runtime; no full suite or WASM rebuild was run.

Phone-width schematic menu follow-up (2026-10-12): Context menus now measure their rendered bounds and reposition within the schematic canvas instead of relying on a fixed width/height estimate. Long menus scroll within the available canvas height. Focused Firefox coverage opens the menu at the lower-right edge at 390×640 and verifies it remains fully visible; typecheck passes. No WASM rebuild or full suite was run.

Schematic menu resize follow-up (2026-10-12): An open context menu now tracks canvas and viewport size changes, recomputes its position, and keeps current keyboard focus. The focused Firefox edge test narrows the viewport from 390×640 to 320×500 with the menu open and verifies it stays within the canvas. Typecheck and the focused test pass; no WASM rebuild or full suite was run.

Large project-open memory follow-up (2026-10-12): The `.elsim` ZIP reader now validates CRCs and parses entries as views into the bounded input buffer instead of copying each entry into a second allocation. The existing >20 MiB project archive round-trip and corrupted/path-traversal archive tests pass with all 28 unit groups; typecheck passes. This removes the duplicate entry buffers but does not measure total browser peak memory; no WASM build was run.

Simulation-result transport follow-up (2026-10-12): The worker now requests ngspice binary raw output and parses typed numeric payloads directly. On the local i7-12700F benchmark, a 1,034,006-sample result shrank from 55.8 MB to 16.5 MB; parsing fell from 476 ms to 17 ms and sampled process RSS increase from about 237 MB to 127 MB. The ngspice solve/write phase remained about 2.42 s, so remaining long-run time is solver-dominated. Local binary transient, OP, AC, DC, and noise format checks, all 28 unit groups, typecheck, and the performance benchmark pass without rebuilding WASM. Firefox browser verification could not start Vite because this workspace denies local port binding (`EPERM`); see [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Quick-tour edit action (2026-10-12): The “Change a component value” step now has an Edit resistor value action that closes the modal, selects the starter resistor, and moves keyboard focus into its inspector field. This makes the instruction actionable despite the tour’s focus trap. A focused Firefox/ngspice workflow edits R1 from 1 kΩ to 2 kΩ, runs the changed circuit, checks the generated netlist, and opens the resulting waveform. The single engine workflow passes against the existing local WASM runtime without rebuilding it.


Engine-free preview guidance (2026-10-12): The toolbar now shows a concise missing-runtime notice with a Setup action when ngspice WASM assets are unavailable. The action explains the local build commands and ignored runtime location, while project editing and saving remain available. Firefox verified missing module/WASM responses, setup guidance, the Run explanation, and a resistor value edit. No WASM build or hosted deployment was run.

Model-library parser follow-up (2026-10-12): Section inspection now returns the logical-line records it already built to the library parser, avoiding a second full continuation-processing pass for each imported model file. Parsing semantics and physical source-line mapping are unchanged.

Large-circuit model-check follow-up (2026-10-12): Circuit Checks now parse each imported model library once per check and reuse the resulting device-model name set across root and reachable child-sheet parts. Missing-model warnings and invalid-library handling are unchanged; the previous path reparsed every library for every model-assigned component.

Shared-net check follow-up (2026-10-12): Circuit Checks now append terminal references to per-net arrays in place in both root and reachable child sheets, avoiding repeated copies when many component pins share a net. Floating-terminal and orphan-wire classification is unchanged.

Hierarchy usability follow-up (2026-10-12): Sheet settings now allow renaming the root or a child sheet. Names are trimmed, limited to the project’s 80-character schema bound, and checked for case-insensitive sibling collisions. Every placed instance updates its visible block name while stable sheet IDs and generated model names preserve circuit identity; domain and Firefox UI regressions cover propagation, netlisting, and duplicate rejection.

Hierarchy lifecycle follow-up (2026-10-12): Users can delete an unused leaf child sheet after an explicit confirmation. Deletion is blocked while any placed block instance or nested child definition refers to the sheet; successful deletion returns to the parent and updates the saved project. The action participates in project undo history. Domain and Firefox UI regressions cover dependency protection, confirmed deletion, persistence, and undo.

Hierarchy naming follow-up (2026-10-12): Adding a child sheet now chooses a numbered name above the highest numbered sibling and checks case-insensitive collisions, so renamed or deleted sheets cannot cause “Add sheet” to create an invalid duplicate. Domain and Firefox UI regressions cover gaps in the existing names.

Accessibility review (2026-10-12): The quick tour now exposes its current step as a semantic progress bar with a spoken value such as “Step 2 of 5”; decorative progress segments are hidden from assistive technology. The first-use browser regression verifies its accessible role and changing value. The visible-control-name audit now also checks the lazy-loaded keyboard-reference dialog for named buttons and form controls. These are focused improvements, not completion of the broader M4.1 accessibility review.

Keyboard discoverability (2026-10-12): Added an in-app shortcut reference beside Examples. Component placement keys come from the same registry used by the editor, with the special uppercase `C` polarized-capacitor placement listed beside lowercase `c`; editing, navigation, logic-gate, and reserved future keys are listed separately. The modal traps focus, closes with Escape, and restores focus to its trigger; a Firefox/Chromium UI regression checks representative actions, case distinction, focus return, and the 390 px no-horizontal-overflow layout. It is lazy-loaded so the reference does not inflate the initial editor bundle. This advances the M1.5 beginner-experience goal without changing key behavior.

Parallel-LED visual wiring (2026-10-12): The beginner example now renders explicit source, branch, cathode, and ground wires instead of relying entirely on net labels. This makes the two current paths visible while preserving the existing operating-point lesson and ngspice-derived branch-current comparison. Firefox verified all 11 wire segments render and the resistor-change lesson still solves; all 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. At this stage, current-flow animation was limited to transient results; the operating-point follow-up below adds steady-current animation. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow was run.

Operating-point current visualization (2026-10-12): A current, non-stale ngspice operating-point result now drives the existing wire-current animation and static component result visuals; transient playback and the time scrubber remain limited to transient results. The animation settings are available for both operating-point and transient results. Firefox verified solved current on the parallel-LED source feed, both branches, cathode path, and ground return, plus stale-result invalidation after a component edit. All 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow was run.

Rectifier schematic readability (2026-10-12): The half-wave rectifier now draws eight independent wire segments for the source feed, diode/load path, and grounded return while retaining VIN/VOUT labels for plotting. Firefox verified the segment layout, solved positive and negative half-cycles, and current flow along the feed, load, and return wires during a positive half-cycle. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Bridge-rectifier schematic wiring (2026-10-12): Replaced the net-label-only bridge topology with 28 independent wire segments for the AC feed, four diode paths, positive and negative output rails, load/capacitor, reference resistor, and grounded return. Kept only one label per named net so the schematic wires themselves establish circuit connectivity. Firefox/ngspice verified the 28 visible segments, current along the AC feed and both load connections at the positive peak, positive capacitor voltage, between-peak ripple, and reduced ripple after increasing C1. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Voltage-divider schematic wiring (2026-10-12): Replaced global-label-only connections with eight explicit feed, series, and return wires while keeping one VIN, VOUT, and ground label as probe names. Firefox/ngspice verified the 5 mA solved current appears on the feed, resistor-to-resistor link, and ground return; the equal-resistor 5 V midpoint and edited 6.667 V result remain correct. This example now demonstrates that ordinary pin-to-wire connectivity is sufficient without labels silently joining terminals. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Potentiometer schematic wiring (2026-10-12): Replaced the VIN and ground label shortcuts with explicit source-to-potentiometer supply and return wires; the wiper still terminates at a labeled VOUT probe. Firefox/ngspice verified the solved current animates through both supply rails and that moving the wiper from 50% to 25% still changes VOUT from 5 V to 7.5 V. No WASM rebuild was needed.

RLC schematic wiring (2026-10-12): Replaced the label-only series-RLC connection with a nine-segment closed loop while keeping VIN, R_L, L_C, and ground labels as readable probe names. Firefox/ngspice verified current animation on the source feed, resistor-to-inductor link, and return at 1 ms, while the underdamping lesson and C1/L1 waveform probes remain intact. No WASM rebuild was needed.

RL step-response wiring (2026-10-12): The inductor current-ramp example now uses eight explicit feed, series, and return segments; duplicate global labels no longer hide its connectivity. Firefox/ngspice verified the expected 40–60 mA rising current animates on the feed, R1-to-L1 link, and return, while the decay and edited-resistance/inductance lessons continue to pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Zener regulator schematic wiring (2026-10-12): Replaced label-only connectivity with nine explicit feed, resistor, cathode, and grounded-return segments. VIN, VZ, and ground now each have one probe/reference label; domain assertions prove the wire segments alone connect all component pins. The existing Firefox/ngspice lesson still solves near 5.1 V and verifies the resistor-change experiment; all 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Project-local SPICE include support (2026-10-12): Imported `.include`/`.inc` references resolve only to explicitly imported project assets by filename; ELSim never reads paths from model source and omits include directives from generated netlists, adding each selected asset's parsed records directly. Missing, ambiguous, absolute, and traversal references appear in the model-dependency report. Libraries containing only shared `.PARAM`, `.FUNC`, or `.GLOBAL` records are now valid. Firefox verifies a missing dependency is reported, clears after importing its file, and the generated netlist contains the shared records without filesystem includes; 28 unit groups and typecheck pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Batch custom-model import (2026-10-12): Users can select multiple related SPICE libraries in one action, so project-local `.include` dependencies resolve immediately; ordinary imports validate and save as one batch. Sectioned libraries retain the explicit section picker and are auto-selected only when imported schematic metadata identifies the needed section. Firefox verifies two dependent libraries import together, the dependency report stays clear, and both definitions plus shared parameters reach the netlist. No WASM rebuild was needed.


Offline local simulation follow-up (2026-10-12): Added `npm run build:offline` to package the developer's existing ignored `public/engine/ngspice.mjs` and `.wasm` into a local production build without compiling WASM or invoking the distribution-review gate. Default and GitHub Pages builds remain engine-free. The offline service worker now answers `HEAD` runtime checks from cached `GET` assets, and the Firefox PWA regression checks JavaScript/WASM MIME types and successfully simulates after going offline and reloading. `npm run build:offline` and `npm run test:e2e:pwa:firefox` pass.


Nested-sheet deletion follow-up (2026-10-12): Deleting an unused reusable sheet now removes its complete nested definition subtree atomically, with one Undo restoring the subtree. Deletion is blocked when any removed definition is instantiated by a sheet outside that subtree, with an actionable message. Domain coverage verifies safe deletion and external-reference protection; the focused Firefox workflow verifies the sheet tabs, deletion, and Undo. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking pass.


Reduced-motion animation-loop follow-up (2026-10-12): Wire-current animation now cancels its pending requestAnimationFrame when the OS enables reduced motion instead of polling while leaving particles static. A single MediaQueryList listener resumes the loop when the preference changes back, preserving each wire's phase. The wire-animation regression verifies cancellation, resumption, and phase continuity; typecheck and all 28 unit groups pass. No WASM rebuild or browser build was needed.


Hierarchical port-direction diagnostics (2026-10-12): Circuit Checks now groups declared output ports by connected parent-sheet net and warns when multiple reusable-block outputs share a net. The advisory explicitly allows intentional wired-OR/open-collector designs and explains that port-direction metadata does not model tri-state behavior; selecting the finding focuses the affected block. Bidirectional ports do not trigger this check. Domain tests cover both directions and the Firefox UI regression checks the warning and selection. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking pass; no WASM rebuild was required.

Hierarchical port-direction visibility (2026-10-12): Reusable-block symbols now show an arrow on every declared input/output pin, including top and bottom placements; bidirectional pins show opposing arrows. The markers follow port placement and use the same direction metadata as Circuit Checks. Geometry tests cover all four sides and all three directions, and the focused Firefox hierarchy-editor workflow verifies visible input/output markers. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Hierarchical pin accessibility (2026-10-12): Keyboard and screen-reader users now hear the names, directions, and sides of the first eight reusable-block pins from the block's accessible name; larger blocks report how many additional pins they contain. The focused Firefox hierarchy-editor workflow verifies the spoken pin summary alongside the visible direction markers. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Large-sheet current animation sampling (2026-10-12): Playback now caches the visible wire IDs' wire objects and connected graph-component set by the stable viewport-set identity. Each cursor sample still recomputes electrical injections and branch flows from the selected simulation time, while avoiding a full-project wire scan every frame. The focused wire-flow suite verifies that repeated samples with the same visible set reflect changing currents; TypeScript checking passes. The 5,000-resistor quick benchmark measured 0.037 ms median / 0.055 ms p95 for 20 visible wires with a stable set and 0.041 / 0.062 ms when rebuilding that set per sample; full-network sampling was 26.766 / 26.879 ms in this one run. This is a local observation, not a browser frame-time budget or cross-machine gain claim. No full test suite, WASM rebuild, or hosted workflow was run. `npm run benchmark:performance:quick` now skips solver and memory workloads while retaining the domain, hierarchy, plot, and large-wire measurements.

Indexed large-sheet wire culling (2026-10-12): viewport updates query a 256-unit spatial hash rather than scanning every wire. On 4,900 segments, the query returns the same visible wires in the same SVG order, including focused wires outside the viewport. Long wires use a fallback list and very large viewport queries fall back to the complete scan. Two quick-benchmark runs measured 0.021 ms median indexed queries versus 0.095/0.073 ms full scans for a 25-wire viewport; building the index cost 2.147 ms median in the second run, so this is aimed at repeated pan/zoom queries. The focused geometry regression, TypeScript check, quick benchmark, and Firefox 5,000-part workflow pass. One Firefox render observation was 1,227 ms; no before/after render gain is claimed. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Component and label viewport indexing (2026-10-12): the same stable spatial index now culls parts using each symbol's detail margin and labels using their rendered text bounds. A 5,000-part domain query returns the same 36 visible parts in original order; one local benchmark measured 0.016 ms median versus 0.128 ms for a full scan, with 1.64 ms index construction. The focused Firefox 5,000-part/10,000-label workflow passes with 64 parts and 105 labels mounted, correct select-all state for all 15,000 objects, zoom, and mixed-selection movement. The old test assumed every offscreen part remained in the DOM; it now verifies culling and all selected visible controls instead. One run measured 829 ms editor ready and 40 ms select-all input-to-frame; this is not a before/after claim. Full browser heap measurements remain open.

Large-schematic keyboard navigation (2026-10-12): arrow-key navigation finds the nearest component with one pass while preserving directional priority, distance ranking, and stable array-order ties. Its selected SVG state updates through the lightweight part-state store, with inspector state deferred; navigating also clears stale wire and label selection. The focused Firefox keyboard regression passes, and the 5,000-part benchmark verifies focus moves after ArrowRight at 42 ms to two animation frames in the normal culled viewport. This is one local observation, not a before/after gain claim.

Repeated component placement (2026-10-12): palette placement stays armed after each drop so users can place several copies without reselecting the component; new parts remain unselected, and Escape cancels the active placement tool. A focused Firefox test verifies two resistor drops and two uppercase-C polarized capacitor drops, including persistent polarity metadata, active palette state, cleared selection, and cancellation. TypeScript check passes; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Firefox engine-smoke correction (2026-10-12): The common-emitter AC workflow had two copied RLC-resonance assertions even though its lesson correctly shows generic frequency-cursor guidance. The assertions now verify the lesson’s actual AC next step. The other 15 Firefox smoke workflows passed in the same run, and the corrected amplifier workflow passes by itself with the existing local WASM runtime.

Definition-driven placement defaults (2026-10-12): Added a shared component-property-default lookup and used component definitions for new part defaults that had been duplicated in the UI, including gate levels/operation, behavioral output, potentiometer position, transformer ratio/coupling, and lamp animation power. Definition tests cover all declared properties; all 28 domain test groups, TypeScript checking, and the focused Firefox repeated-placement workflow pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Component inspector schema defaults (2026-10-12): Inspector fallbacks now read logic levels, source phase, potentiometer position, transformer ratio/coupling and winding resistance, capacitor/inductor ESR, lamp animation power, and voltage-switch resistances/hysteresis from the component definitions. New voltage-controlled switches persist those defaults in their project part. TypeScript and the focused Firefox test for placement plus inspector values pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Component palette metadata (2026-10-12): Moved palette help text into the shared component definitions and made card labels and keyboard-shortcut names read from that metadata, while retaining the established VCVS/VCCS/CCCS/CCVS abbreviations. The v1 definition schema allows optional help and palette names; domain tests and the Firefox hover/focus check cover the behavior. No WASM rebuild is needed.

Component registry follow-up (2026-10-12): Component palette subtitles and placement shortcuts now also live in the definition registry. The app builds its keyboard dispatch table from that registry, while keeping icons and future unimplemented shortcuts in the UI layer. Domain assertions protect every current palette mapping; all 28 unit groups and the focused Firefox shortcut/help workflows pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Component palette extraction (2026-10-12): Moved the palette icon map and card rendering into `src/ui/ComponentPalette.tsx`; the editor supplies filtered component kinds and placement behavior. This removes the duplicate card UI from the large editor module while leaving shortcut/help metadata in component definitions. TypeScript, all 28 unit groups, and focused Firefox help/shortcut workflows pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Component category metadata (2026-10-12): Moved Basics/Semiconductors/Controlled assignments into the component definitions and derive the filter groups from that registry. This exposed and fixed the Zener card being omitted from Semiconductors. Updated the stale library-count browser assertions; the focused Firefox category/search test, TypeScript, and all 28 unit groups pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Component registry invariants (2026-10-12): Built-in definitions now require a palette subtitle, category, and case-sensitive placement shortcut at compile time; a domain check rejects duplicate shortcut keys. Imported project blocks remain exempt from palette-only fields. TypeScript and all 28 unit groups pass.

Component connection labels and iteration loop (2026-10-12): The inspector's pin rows now come from the component-definition registry, preserving SPICE order and allowing friendly labels (including polarized capacitor terminals) without per-component JSX branches. All 28 fast domain test groups (0.76 s), TypeScript (5.5 s), diff whitespace validation, and a focused Firefox inspector workflow verifies source/resistor terminal labels plus the NPN collector-current sign hint; no WASM build, Pages build, or deployment poll was run. Keep that tiering for routine changes: run focused tests plus typecheck, use focused Firefox checks for changed UI interactions, reserve complete browser/solver suites for solver changes and milestone checks, and rebuild WASM only when its source, build configuration, or engine interface changes.

Built-in pin geometry registry (2026-10-12): Fixed pin offsets now live alongside ordered pin definitions for every built-in component; `componentPins` consumes that metadata instead of a kind-by-kind geometry switch. Imported symbol offsets and dynamically laid-out hierarchy pins keep their existing data paths. Domain coverage compares every built-in offset to the coordinates used by connectivity, and the focused Firefox transistor-drag/rotation workflow passes. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Registry-driven symbol geometry (2026-10-12): The v1 component-definition schema now supports bounded line, rectangle, circle, and path primitives. The resistor and capacitor's static leads/body/plates are the first built-in symbols migrated to definition-owned primitives and a shared SVG renderer; rendered geometry remains the same while capacitor charge/polarity effects stay result-driven. Domain coverage asserts their definitions, and focused Firefox coverage confirms both render. Broader built-in symbol migration is still open. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checks pass; no WASM or Pages build was needed.

Independent-source symbol migration (2026-10-12): Voltage and current source base symbols now use the same registry-owned primitive renderer as resistor/capacitor geometry. The current-source arrow retains its filled appearance through bounded primitive metadata; the solved-current indicator remains a separate dynamic overlay. Domain checks cover source primitive metadata and focused Firefox coverage checks the voltage source and filled current-source arrow. The 28-unit-group and TypeScript checks pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Ground and diode-family symbol migration (2026-10-12): Ground, diode, Zener, and LED static outlines now use component-definition primitives; LED brightness remains a solved-current overlay. Domain checks ensure these symbol records exist and LED/diode body geometry agrees. Focused Firefox verifies the ground symbol and diode in the half-wave rectifier example. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checks pass, without a WASM or Pages build.

Switch symbol migration (2026-10-12): Static rails, fixed contacts, and voltage-control leads for standard and voltage-controlled switches now come from component-definition primitives. The movable/open/closed lever remains state-driven. Filled contacts are represented by a bounded primitive fill option. Domain coverage checks both switch definitions; focused Firefox verifies the voltage-controlled switch contacts, control lead, and stateful lever. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checks pass; no WASM or Pages build was needed.

Inductor symbol migration (2026-10-12): The fixed leads and coil path now come from component-definition geometry. The current glow and direction arrow remain dynamic simulation overlays. Domain coverage checks the coil primitive and focused Firefox opens the inductor-ramp example and verifies the coil renders without running a simulation. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript checks pass; no WASM or Pages build was needed.

Transformer symbol migration (2026-10-12): Static transformer leads, both windings, core bars, and polarity dots now render from component-definition geometry; primary/secondary current glow and the secondary current arrow remain result-driven overlays. Registry checks assert both winding paths and static geometry, and the existing focused Firefox inspector workflow verifies the transformer example's rendered parts. All 28 unit groups (0.78 s), TypeScript checking, and the Firefox check (5.7 s) pass. No WASM, Pages, full browser, or deployment checks were needed. Routine UI/registry work should use this fast loop; keep heavyweight build checks only for changes that touch the engine/build path or a release milestone.

Lamp symbol migration (2026-10-12): Fixed lamp leads and the outer bulb circle now come from its component definition. The solved-power halo and brightness-sensitive filament remain dynamic. Registry assertions, all 28 unit groups (0.76 s), TypeScript, and the focused Firefox lamp placement/inspector workflow pass. The browser check also now waits for the normal project autosave and exits the persistent placement mode before selecting the part; no WASM or Pages build was needed.

Potentiometer symbol migration (2026-10-12): The fixed track and upper/lower leads now come from the definition registry; the wiper line and arrow remain position-driven. Registry coverage, all 28 unit groups (0.84 s), TypeScript, and a focused Firefox render test pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Transistor symbol migration (2026-10-12): NPN/PNP, NMOS/PMOS, and N-/P-JFET static leads, channels, and device arrows now live in component definitions. Solved-current indicators remain dynamic. Domain assertions cover both polarities for all three families, while the focused Firefox transistor example verifies rendered NPN geometry and its inspector labels. All 28 unit groups (0.69 s), TypeScript, and the focused Firefox workflow pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Inverter symbol migration (2026-10-12): The input lead, triangle body, and inversion bubble now use component-definition geometry; the output lead remains state-colored by the solved logic result. Registry coverage, all 28 unit groups (0.81 s), TypeScript, and focused Firefox rendering against the Logic gate basics example pass. No WASM rebuild was needed.

Dependent-source symbol migration (2026-10-12): VCVS/VCCS and CCCS/CCVS leads, diamond bodies, and internal polarity/current marks now come from component definitions; control-pin labels remain UI overlays. Registry assertions, all 28 unit groups (0.76 s), TypeScript, and a focused Firefox check across the voltage- and current-controlled examples pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Behavioral-source symbol migration (2026-10-12): The two terminal leads and diamond outline now use registry geometry; its voltage/current marking remains selected from the editable output type. Domain geometry assertions, all 28 unit groups (0.75 s), TypeScript, and diff validation pass. The focused Firefox dependent-source render test already exercises the shared renderer; no additional browser or WASM build was needed.

Logic-gate symbol variants (2026-10-12): The component-definition schema now supports bounded, immutable symbol-geometry variants. AND/OR/XOR bodies, input leads, XOR curves, and inversion bubbles for all six gates are defined in the registry; the renderer retains only the solved output lead, operation label, and accessible shape state. Domain assertions cover all six variants, and the Firefox truth-table workflow passes with the already-built local ngspice runtime. All 28 unit groups (0.71 s), TypeScript, and diff validation pass; no WASM or Pages build was run.

Logic-gate SVG cleanup (2026-10-12): The gate shape metadata now lives on the interactive component SVG node instead of an empty per-gate child group. This removes one unnecessary SVG element per gate while retaining the registry-defined symbol and simulation output indicator. All 28 unit groups, typecheck, and the focused Firefox truth-table workflow pass with the existing local runtime.

Switch symbol variants (2026-10-12): Manual switch open/closed lever geometry and voltage-controlled switch open/closed lever geometry now live in component definitions and render through the shared primitive path. The manual lever follows its saved on/off value; the voltage-controlled lever follows the displayed simulation sample. Registry assertions and two focused Firefox UI workflows pass, alongside all 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Behavioral-source symbol variants (2026-10-12): Voltage-source polarity marks and the current-source arrow now live in definition-owned variants, selected by the part's output type and drawn by the shared geometry renderer. The duplicate JSX symbol branch is removed. Registry tests cover the voltage marker and filled current arrow; all 28 unit groups and TypeScript checking pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Lamp symbol geometry (2026-10-12): The lamp filament outline moved into its component definition and shared symbol renderer; only result-dependent glow and brightness coloring remain in the editor. Registry tests and the focused Firefox lamp placement/inspector workflow verify the filament, while all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and diff validation pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Diode and LED geometry reuse (2026-10-12): The built-in diode and LED now share one registry-owned base symbol; the LED definition adds its light-ray path, with simulated forward current controlling only ray brightness and the glow halo. The LED example, registry checks, all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and diff validation pass in the focused workflow. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Logic-gate operator marks (2026-10-12): Added a bounded text primitive to component-definition v1 and moved the AND/OR/XOR operator marks for all six gate variants into the definition registry. The renderer keeps only the simulation-colored output lead and accessibility state in its gate-specific overlay. Registry assertions, the focused Firefox logic-gate truth-table workflow, all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and diff validation pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Logic output-lead geometry (2026-10-12): Inverter and all six logic-gate output leads now live in component definitions with a shared class; the editor applies solved high/low color and glow through one generic primitive style. Registry checks protect inverted-gate lead placement, and the Firefox truth-table workflow verifies the leads remain simulation-colored. All 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and diff validation pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Controlled-source pin marks (2026-10-12): VCVS/VCCS and voltage-controlled switch control-voltage +/− labels now live in their component definitions and use the shared text primitive renderer. Their existing classes and positions are preserved. Registry checks and focused Firefox amplifier/switch workflows verify the labels; all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and diff validation pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Solved-current arrow variants (2026-10-12): Forward/reverse arrows for NPN/PNP, NMOS/PMOS, N-/P-JFETs, and inductors now live in component-definition variants selected by solved current sign. The generic primitive renderer applies arrow styling; inductor and transistor glow remains simulation-driven. Domain tests cover arrow definitions/orientations; focused Firefox RLC and NPN switch workflows verify their arrows against solved results. The NPN test now waits for project autosave before directly modifying stored wires/labels and confirms the project survives reload, eliminating a race that had left the prior RC project open. All 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and focused Firefox workflows pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Capacitor current polarity marks (2026-10-12): The capacitor’s simulation-driven `+`/`−` current-flow labels now use forward/reverse definition variants with explicit pin indices. The generic text primitive renders their accessible title, polarity, and pin metadata; the separate polarized-capacitor terminal marks and charge glow remain intact. The definition schema bounds the optional text pin index to 1–64. Domain tests verify both signs, and the focused Firefox RC charge-scrubbing workflow confirms both labels render during simulation. All 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and diff checks pass; no WASM or Pages build was needed.

Definition-driven component current arrows (2026-10-12): Added bounded `symbolAnimations` metadata for named channels/states. Voltage/current sources, R, lamp, diode/Zener/LED, switches, transformer, behavioral and controlled sources, inverter, and logic gates now define forward/reverse current-arrow geometry next to their component symbols. The editor appends the selected animation primitives to the current static/state variant and draws them generically, removing the kind-based arrow-path branch. Tests cover the schema and every migrated definition; focused Firefox confirms current arrows on the source and resistor in the 1 mA load example. All 28 unit groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM or Pages build was needed.

Definition-driven lamp power glow (2026-10-12): Lamp glow fill and outline geometry now live in the component definition's `brightness.power` animation channel; the renderer supplies only the solved-power opacity and glow strength through the shared primitive path. The bounded animation schema accepts dotted channel names. Registry assertions, all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox solved-power glow workflow pass. No WASM or Pages build was needed.

Definition-driven LED glow (2026-10-12): The LED's current-powered glow circle now lives in its `brightness.current` component-definition animation; its rays remain static definition geometry so the icon is complete before simulation. The renderer supplies current-derived opacity through shared primitive styling, and the separate LED-specific JSX halo branch is removed. Definition tests and the Firefox LED current-limiter workflow verify that glow appears with solved current and dims when series resistance increases. No WASM or Pages build is needed.

Definition-driven inductor field glow (2026-10-12): The inductor's current-dependent coil glow path now lives in its `field.current` component-definition animation. The shared symbol renderer applies the existing solved-current glow strength and removes the separate inductor JSX branch. Registry checks and the Firefox inductor-ramp workflow verify the glow appears at a nonzero simulated current. No WASM or Pages build is needed.

Definition-driven transformer winding fields (2026-10-12): Primary and secondary transformer winding glow geometry now live in separate `field.primaryCurrent` and `field.secondaryCurrent` definition channels. Each visual strength continues to use its own solved winding current; duplicate transformer glow paths are removed from the component-specific JSX. Registry coverage and the Firefox transformer transient workflow verify both independent glows alongside winding-current probes. No WASM or Pages build is needed.

Definition-driven transformer secondary-current direction (2026-10-12): The secondary winding's forward/reverse arrow paths now live in the component definition and are selected from signed secondary current. The shared renderer preserves pin-3-to-pin-4/pin-4-to-pin-3 metadata, while the transformer title continues to report both solved winding currents. Registry tests and the Firefox transformer transient workflow pass; no WASM or Pages build is needed.

Definition-driven capacitor charge plates (2026-10-12): Both capacitor plate-glow lines now live in the `charge.voltage` component-definition animation. The generic line renderer derives their extension, thickness, opacity, and opposite polarity colors from the scrubbed capacitor voltage/charge sample; the separate capacitor glow JSX is removed while polarized terminal marks remain. Registry assertions, all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox charge-scrubbing workflow pass. No WASM or Pages build is needed.

Definition-driven potentiometer wiper (2026-10-12): The movable wiper line and arrow now live in the potentiometer's `position.wiper` definition channel; the shared symbol renderer positions them from the saved wiper value, removing the separate potentiometer geometry branch. The component's accessible name now announces its wiper percentage and reference terminal. Definition assertions, all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox edit/render workflow pass. No WASM or Pages build is needed.

UI interaction and waveform follow-up (2026-10-12): Preserve the new selection behavior: Ctrl/⌘-click and modifier box selection add parts, wires, and labels without starting a drag; a blank-canvas click clears all selected objects; ordinary wire selection clears stale part/label selection. Component voltage probes retain their part pins and connected nets in each waveform window, and are rebuilt after a run only when those same connections remain. Probe destination cards open on the click itself; there is no second “Open waveform” action. The component library starts at Basics, has no “All parts” tab, and search still spans all categories. Keep the component panel constrained to its full allocated width with no horizontal overflow. Plot windows use larger axis, trace, and cursor text; dragging the title must not select text behind the window. The toolbar playback selector defaults to the former playback pace at 1×, offers slower/faster scaled playback, and exposes real-time playback as a separate explicit mode at one simulated second per wall-clock second. Keep the component current-flow animation speed independent from result playback. The footer does not show “Built for curious minds.”

Stable toolbar and placement preview (2026-10-12): During drag-to-place, show the actual component symbol at the snapped placement position, continuously rotated to match the drag direction. Orient the second pin along the drag direction; for BJT/MOSFET/JFET parts this points the base/gate toward the drag endpoint (for a left-to-right drag, the base/gate is on the right and the channel faces left). Keep playback, simulation-time scrubber, animation settings, waveform actions, and other mode-dependent toolbar controls mounted in fixed slots. When unavailable, disable them visibly and explain why; do not insert or remove toolbar controls as analysis/results change. Show stale-result and missing-runtime notices beside the autosave state in a permanently reserved bottom status bar, so they cannot reflow the toolbar or cover plots. Apply this fixed-slot/reserved-notice rule to future toolbar features and status messages.

Waveform and block-tab interactions (2026-10-12): Make the three-dot menu on the active sheet/block tab functional with sheet settings, duplicate-block, and delete-block actions (root restrictions remain clear). Middle-clicking anywhere on a floating waveform window closes that window. Scope plots include a visible zero reference on each relevant value axis and keep zero in the automatic range. Mouse dragging pans scopes horizontally only; vertical mouse movement must not change value-axis ranges. Keep explicit vertical zoom/pan buttons available for deliberate value-axis adjustment.

Wire-current animation and menu polish (2026-10-12): Current markers render as round dots directly along wire segments, with solved branch-current magnitude and direction driving continuous motion; rendering does not extend the wire paths or place moving markers on component symbols. Standalone yellow component-current arrows were removed; capacitor polarity marks and result-linked component glow/current indicators remain where they convey device state. The current settings popover exposes speed, dot size (4–16), and dot spacing (10–30) with half-step size/spacing adjustment, persisted locally. Context menus and the toolbar animation-settings popover close on outside click. The setting ranges and rendered-dot behavior were reviewed against the current implementation; `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` pass. No WASM rebuild, Pages build, or hosted workflow was run.


Status placement and component menu cleanup (2026-10-12): The stale-results warning and missing-runtime notice now share the fixed bottom status bar with the autosave state; this keeps them in the main workspace, visible without covering plots, and independent of toolbar layout. The redundant component-menu “Edit properties” item was removed because selecting a component already opens its inspector. The inert three-dot “More properties” control was removed from the inspector; it had no menu or action attached. `npm run typecheck` and `git diff --check` pass. The focused Firefox UI run could not start because Playwright’s configured web servers exited before tests began; no WASM or hosted build was run.

Viewport-culling wire connection dots (2026-10-12): Connection dots now use a memoized spatial index keyed to wire geometry and render only when their positions intersect the current world-space viewport, with an 8-unit margin so visible edge dots are retained. The existing T-junction and component-pin regression now also places an offscreen pin, confirms its connection dot is culled, then fits the full circuit and confirms it appears. Firefox runs both dot culling and junction culling regressions successfully. `npm run typecheck`, all 28 unit groups, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages test was run. The current 9,800-candidate domain timing does not measure browser SVG/frame performance, so it is not reported as a UI speedup. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Viewport-culling junction markers (2026-10-12): Junction circles now use a memoized point index and only mount within the schematic viewport plus a 6-unit visual margin. The focused Firefox regression confirms offscreen markers are omitted and return after Fit All. Typecheck, all 28 unit groups, production build, and both focused Firefox culling regressions pass. No browser frame-time gain is claimed. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

Mobile toolbar overflow correction (2026-10-12): The focused Firefox accessible-controls smoke test exposed 29 px of horizontal page overflow at 390 px: the persistent simulation playback group forced its time scrubber and playback-rate selector onto one oversized row. Below 800 px, those controls now use a constrained grid with the scrubber taking remaining space and playback rate in a separate compact column. The 390 px smoke workflow passes, including accessible names and exact document scroll width; TypeScript and unit tests passed earlier in this work session, and the production build passed before this CSS-only correction. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Firefox UI smoke review and shortcut-category correction (2026-10-12): The 43-case `@ui-smoke` suite passes in Chromium, and the drag-box plus wire multi-selection flows pass in Firefox. It checks first-use journeys, accessible names and representative contrast, dialogs, keyboard selection, reusable blocks, mobile width, and editing workflows. The audit found that a shortcut could arm a controlled component while leaving the palette filtered to Basics, hiding the selected part. Placement now switches the palette to the component definition's category and falls back to Basics when category metadata is absent. The shortcut regression verifies case-sensitive keys and visible selected cards; local project-restore test setup now waits for hydration, and click-to-place coverage uses a point inside the visible schematic instead of the toolbar overlay. The current M4.1 manual review remains open for screen-reader behavior and focus-indicator contrast across states; DOM accessible-name checks are not a substitute for that review. `npm test` passes all 28 groups, and `npm run build` passes its typecheck and download-size gate. No WASM rebuild or Pages test was run.

Sheet-menu keyboard navigation (2026-10-12): The sheet tab's three-dot control had `role="menu"` but only closed on Escape. Opening it now focuses the first enabled menu item, and ArrowUp/Down, Home, and End move focus across enabled actions; Escape closes it and restores focus to the trigger. The same navigation helper is shared with schematic context menus. Firefox regressions pass for both menu keyboard flows, including focus return. TypeScript checking passes; no WASM or Pages build was run. M4.1's manual screen-reader and full focus-indicator review remains open.

Lazy examples picker (2026-10-12): The examples catalog and picker dialog are now downloaded only when the user opens Examples. This saves 2.51 KiB gzip from the initial JavaScript transfer while keeping the searchable 36-example catalog, topic filters, and filter state across close/reopen unchanged. Browser regressions verify the dialog chunk is absent at startup and requested on open, and search/filter persistence; the 45-case Chromium UI smoke suite and production size-budget build pass. The offscreen keyboard-navigation fixture now seeds its saved project before app startup so pagehide autosave cannot overwrite it. Vite's 500 KB minified main-entry warning remains open for further measured work.

Large parameter-sweep selector (2026-10-13): Eligible sweep components are memoized, imported device models use a normalized-name index, and projects with over 100 eligible components get a name/type search whose native option list is bounded to 100 matches plus the existing selection. Firefox verifies selecting a component beyond the initial list, along with the existing full-circuit copy/paste regression that checks newly pasted wires remain the only selected wires. The copy/paste regression passes; no WASM build or hosted deployment is required. This bounds a large-project UI list, but does not complete the broader M4.3 browser-memory and cross-hardware measurements.

Current-flow frame timing (2026-10-13): Moving wire dots now advance according to elapsed frame time, normalized to the existing 60 Hz behavior and clamped after long frame stalls. This avoids display-refresh-dependent speed while preserving the current × speed multiplier and uninterrupted phase between solved samples. A deterministic unit regression compares 30, 60, and 120 Hz over equal elapsed time; the Firefox real-simulation animation test passes. The full 46-case Firefox UI-smoke suite, all 28 unit groups, and the production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages build was run. The 500 KB main-chunk warning remains open, and M4.3's second-machine and memory measurements remain outstanding.

UI maintenance pass (2026-10-13): Removed obsolete styles for the retired trace chooser, old netlist panel, redundant run-details UI, and other removed controls. Fixed invalid capacitor ESR/inductor winding-resistance focus so Run directs the user to the exact field. Updated stale Firefox tests and the nested-circuit browser benchmark to account for the placement preview, viewport culling, current waveform-window flow, asynchronous local save, and current netlist identifiers. The 500-instance Firefox benchmark confirms only 10 visible parts initially mount and the operating-point run returns 1,003 vectors. `npm test` passes all 28 groups; typecheck and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

UI audit checkpoint (2026-10-13): The full Firefox `@ui-smoke` suite now passes 51 cases in 52.9 seconds; the earlier 46/48/49/50-case counts in this roadmap are historical. This includes directional drag-box selection, mixed component/wire selection, keyboard focus visibility in the editor and plot window, mobile-width layout, reusable-block editing, and latest-hover delete targeting. The waveform renderer measures its containing region with `ResizeObserver` and updates plot geometry as the window changes size. All 30 unit groups and the production build pass; the initial JS+CSS transfer is 206.2 KiB gzip against a 245 KiB budget. M4.3 still needs representative second-machine measurements and browser/worker total-memory measurement; direct WASM linear-memory capacity is available from the profiled worker. M4.1 manual screen-reader review remains open.

Schematic hover render isolation (2026-10-13): The currently hovered object is retained in a ref, while React state tracks only whether any schematic object is hovered. Moving across parts, wires, or labels therefore updates the Delete action's target without scheduling a full `App` rerender for each hover transition. A Firefox regression hovers two distinct unselected parts and verifies Delete removes the latest hovered part; the existing hovered-wire deletion regression also passes. The complete 51-case Firefox UI smoke suite, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass. Bundle size is 206.2 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip. This removes a parent render per hover-target change by construction; no wall-clock speedup is claimed.

Firefox UI maintenance follow-up (2026-10-13): Fixed the engine-test scrub helper to convert adaptive `s`/`ms`/`µs`/`ns`/`ps` labels back to seconds; the prior `parseFloat` path treated values such as `15 ms` as 15 seconds and drove LC/flyback checks to the wrong sample. Updated two assertions that incorrectly required seconds-only labels. The 49-case Firefox UI smoke suite, all 28 unit groups, and production build pass. The formerly failing AC/DC waveform interactions were a responsive layout bug at the engine suite's 854 px viewport: the nowrap waveform toolbar overflowed its window, and keyboard focus scrolled the graph horizontally out of view. The waveform title and controls now wrap within the plot window, with overflow constrained; the placement helper resizes after moving the window into view, and a regression assertion checks there is no hidden horizontal scroll. The full 17-case Firefox engine-smoke suite now passes. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Keyboard focus visibility follow-up (2026-10-13): A code audit found that inspector and PWL form controls overrode the global `:focus-visible` outline, while the analysis selector, component search, and context-menu actions also suppressed it. Added a consistent 2 px accent focus ring to those controls and the containing search/analysis controls; context-menu items keep the ring while programmatically focused as the menu opens. A Firefox regression checks computed focus-ring visibility for search, analysis, resistor properties, PWL point editing, and the keyboard-opened component menu. The full 50-case Firefox UI smoke suite passes. Manual screen-reader and broad focus-indicator review across all states remains open; this automated regression covers only representative controls.

Waveform rerun restoration indexing (2026-10-13): Replaced repeated full-trace filtering and project-part scans during saved-window restoration with run-scoped vector, part, sheet, and trace indexes; connectivity is computed at most once per referenced sheet, and legacy current-vector lookup is lazy. Ground traces are indexed once per sweep case, and selected trace deduplication uses a set. Firefox's real-ngspice multi-window rerun regression confirms component voltage/current probes and trace colors remain attached after edits and rerun. TypeScript, all 28 unit groups, and production build pass; no WASM rebuild was needed. This removes repeated scans in the restoration path; no end-to-end latency gain is claimed without a controlled browser benchmark.

Browser WASM-memory observation (2026-10-13): Development-only worker profiling now captures the exported Emscripten `WebAssembly.Memory` during initialization and reports its buffer capacity after a run; it restores the global instantiate functions as soon as the runtime is initialized, and production/normal runs do not install the instrumentation. The profile benchmark requires a positive memory measurement. Firefox and Chromium each ran the 1,000,130-sample, eight-window, 32-trace transient and reported 185,794,560 bytes (about 177 MiB) of WASM linear-memory capacity. Firefox result-ready time was 10.04 s with warmed cursor scrubs of 62.84/41.57/39.67 ms; Chromium was 3.99 s with 45.89/29.68/22.52 ms. These are same-host single runs, not a cross-browser performance claim. The WebAssembly figure is its current/high-water capacity, not total worker allocation, resident memory, or process RSS. Cross-hardware and total browser/worker memory measurements remain open; no WASM rebuild was required.

Box-selection UI optimization (2026-10-13): Drag-box selection now reads bounds from rendered component, wire, and label controls in one DOM pass instead of rescanning each project collection and running a separate selector for every item. This preserves left-to-right containment and right-to-left crossing semantics while using the rendered wire hit path. Firefox regressions pass for box selection and full-circuit copy/paste selection; all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. This reduces repeated lookup work; no end-to-end speedup is claimed without a controlled benchmark.
