Connection-dot rendering optimization (2026-10-13): The schematic now builds a compact point-specific viewport index for connection dots, avoiding generated IDs and per-dot bounds objects for offscreen dots; SVG keys are created only for visible points. On the 9,800-dot/4,900-wire fixture, index-build median was 1.453 ms versus 3.832 ms for the general object index, with equivalent visible dots/order; indexed queries were comparable at about 0.02 ms. The point index falls back to direct candidate filtering for very broad viewports. A follow-up skips repeated wire/pin checks at coordinates already confirmed as dots; a separate benchmark run measured derivation at 13.068 ms median / 33.284 ms p95, versus an earlier 15.755 ms / 36.030 ms observation. These are not interleaved before/after measurements. Geometry tests verify viewport membership/order including broad viewports; two Firefox dot-rendering workflows, all 28 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. No WASM or Pages build was run.

First-circuit and probe-inspection journey acceptance (2026-10-13): The Firefox/ngspice M0.1 browser journey loads the RC starter, edits R1 in the inspector, runs transient analysis, and inspects `V(vout)` without opening the netlist. It then adds `I(R1)` to that same window and verifies both readings, a simulation-time readout, and CSV columns with seconds, volts, and amps in the headers. The end-to-end journey passes in 2.4 seconds of Playwright test execution on this host using the existing local runtime; this is not a cross-machine performance guarantee. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Component-renderer maintenance (2026-10-13): Cached component symbol geometry, custom pin-side counts, imported-symbol bounds, and label bounds so playback updates reuse static layout calculations; pin-side counting is now a single pass. Firefox regressions pass for imported symbols, rotated placement previews, and the solved capacitor charge animation. TypeScript and the production size-budget build pass. This is a targeted renderer optimization; no end-to-end speedup is claimed without a controlled large-schematic benchmark. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Fresh-profile project round trip (2026-10-13): The Firefox/ngspice M0.1 save-and-reopen journey now starts from the UI's imported educational IC example, solves it, places `V(VOUT)` and `I(RLOAD)` in one waveform window, saves the `.elsim` through the imported-file rights review, and opens that download in a new browser context. It verifies model and symbol assets, persisted trace IDs and semantic probe references, restored window contents, and the op-amp output after rerunning ngspice. The journey passes in 5.4 seconds on this host using the existing local runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Edit-and-rerun acceptance (2026-10-13): A focused Firefox/ngspice journey first solves the RC starter and adds node-voltage plus resistor-current probes to one plot. Editing R1 to 4 kΩ marks the prior result stale in the fixed main-window status area and disables playback; the same plot window and old probes remain available. Rerunning clears the stale notice, preserves both probes and the voltage trace color, changes the cursor readings, and the generated netlist confirms the edited resistor value. The focused journey passes in 2.8 seconds using the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

UI regression audit (2026-10-13): Rebuilt the production app and re-ran all 50 Firefox `@ui-smoke` cases, all 19 Firefox `@engine-smoke` workflows, and all 28 unit-test groups. This includes the previously reported full-circuit copy/paste case, which verifies that only newly pasted wires remain selected. All checks pass. The current build reports a 494.74 kB minified main JS entry; total initial transfer is 212.0 KiB gzip (199.7 KiB JS plus 12.62 KiB CSS), within the configured budgets. This is a regression checkpoint, not evidence that every UI state has been manually reviewed. Further App/core chunk splitting and manual assistive-technology review remain open.

M4.3 Firefox benchmark maintenance (2026-10-13): Repaired the 7-case browser performance suite to assert viewport-culling behavior, seed explicit saved waveform-window traces for multi-trace plots, and wait for the starter project autosave before editing fixtures. The full suite now passes in Firefox. Latest local observations and limits are recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; no before/after performance gain is claimed. TypeScript, all 28 unit groups, and production size-budget build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Guided-lesson bundle and analysis optimization (2026-10-13): Moved example-specific prediction and solved-result analysis out of the initial editor bundle; it loads only after opening a guided lesson. This reduces initial JavaScript by 8.12 KiB gzip (191.7 KiB total JS, 204.0 KiB combined with CSS). Lesson analysis now indexes project parts once, reads only traces needed by the active lesson, finds fixed-time/frequency samples with binary search, and reruns only when parts, initial conditions, or result inputs change—not when the viewport or plot layout changes. On synthetic fixtures, 64 part lookups in a 5,000-part project fell from 0.784 ms to 0.390 ms median; six lookups in a one-million-point axis fell from 42.469 ms to 0.006 ms. These microbenchmarks do not represent full UI latency. Probe labels in the controlled-source examples were also moved from component pins to open wire endpoints so the symbols do not cover their right-click targets. The lazy-load regression, seven Firefox/ngspice lesson workflows, all 51 tagged Firefox UI-smoke cases, all 28 unit groups, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run. An initial unfiltered Firefox run found 26 failures among 65 cases; the subsequent audit below supersedes that interim count. Further App/core splitting should be benchmarked before adding more async boundaries.

Playback axis lookup optimization (2026-10-13): Scaled and real-time playback now share one lookup created per playback run. It estimates the sample index from the run's time span, resolves nearby samples in a small bracket, and falls back to exact binary search when adaptive ngspice steps lie outside that bracket; playback still selects actual solved samples and never interpolates. A synthetic one-million-sample uniform axis benchmark over 500,000 lookups measured 35.73 ms median for full binary search and 16.40 ms for the bracketed lookup (five runs; machine-local microbenchmark only). Unit tests compare uniform, cubic-adaptive, and highly nonuniform axes against a nearest-sample reference. All 29 unit groups, TypeScript, and production size-budget build pass. The focused Firefox playback browser test could not start because this workspace denied the configured local Vite server; no WASM or Pages build was run. This is one playback-path optimization, not completion of the full UI audit.

Sweep ground-probe indexing (2026-10-13): Adding voltage-to-ground after a sweep now tracks seen sweep indexes in a set instead of comparing every result trace with every earlier trace. This preserves the existing one-reference-per-sweep behavior and changes the selection pass from quadratic to linear in trace count. TypeScript and all 29 unit groups pass; browser execution remains subject to the local-server permission noted above.

Firefox UI audit regression checkpoint (2026-10-13): After the playback and ground-probe changes, all 51 Firefox `@ui-smoke` cases passed in 53 seconds. Focused Firefox checks also pass for real-result wire animation during transient playback and the reported full-circuit copy/paste selection bug (all new wires selected, no original wires selected), using the already-built local runtime. Production build remains within configured budgets at 466.20 kB main entry, 191.7 KiB initial JS gzip, and 204.1 KiB combined initial JS+CSS. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run. This validates automated browser paths but does not replace the still-open manual screen-reader/focus-indicator review or review of every UI state.

Wire selection and inspector context (2026-10-13): Selecting a wire now clears component/label canvas highlights on a plain click while retaining the last component in the Properties inspector. A no-movement click on one wire from a mixed selection selects only that wire; dragging the mixed selection still moves its explicitly selected objects together. Wire endpoint dragging also preserves inspector context. Component rotate/flip shortcuts and delete/copy actions now follow active canvas selection instead of the retained inspector context. Firefox regressions cover plain wire selection, endpoint selection, Ctrl-click mixed selection, and active-tool persistence. The 51-case Firefox UI-smoke suite, all 29 unit groups, and the production size-budget build pass; total initial transfer is 204.2 KiB gzip. This repairs a concrete selection-state issue; broader manual accessibility review and second-machine performance measurements remain open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

UI audit and Firefox regression checkpoint (2026-10-13): The full non-engine Firefox suite now passes all 116 cases, including the 51 tagged UI-smoke tests. This closes the interim 26-failure test report above: the failures were stale test assumptions about autosave timing, click-to-place behavior, toast roles, and the switch symbol’s registry path, plus one test that injected a localStorage project before the app restored its initial project. The UI code build, all 29 unit groups, and TypeScript check pass. The production app is 466.52 kB minified for its main JS entry; initial JavaScript is 191.9 KiB gzip and CSS is 12.3 KiB gzip (204.2 KiB combined, within configured budgets). The quick stress benchmark on the documented i7-12700F measures 5,000-part connectivity at 46.1 ms median and indexed visible wire-flow sampling at 0.064 ms median for 20 requested wires. These are local baselines, not cross-machine guarantees. No new app-code optimization was justified by this audit’s regressions; further App/core splitting should be driven by profile data. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine/browser-worker memory measurements remain open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Context-menu focus visibility follow-up (2026-10-13): A stylesheet audit found that schematic context-menu buttons explicitly removed their keyboard focus outline while sharing pointer-hover styling. Hover and `:focus-visible` now have separate styles, with a 2 px accent outline for keyboard focus. The focused Firefox menu-navigation regression checks the computed outline after keyboard opening, then verifies arrow-key navigation and Escape focus restoration; TypeScript and this browser test pass. This closes one concrete focus-indicator defect, not the remaining manual screen-reader and cross-state review. No WASM build was needed.

Imported-symbol parse reuse (2026-10-13): The component picker and schematic renderer previously parsed each imported symbol independently after a symbol-library change. The app now parses once in the shared project-level memo and both consumers use the same parsed object. TypeScript and the production transfer-budget build pass; focused Firefox coverage confirms imported-symbol placement and accessible ordered pin labels remain correct. This removes duplicate work on import/update without claiming a user-visible timing gain. No WASM build was needed.

Global simulation-setting error navigation (2026-10-13): When a component was selected, an invalid project-level simulation setting such as temperature could not be focused because the inspector showed only that component. Starting a run with a global setting error now clears canvas selection so the project settings inspector is shown before focus is moved to the invalid field. Firefox reproduces the selected-resistor case and verifies the project setting receives focus; TypeScript passes. No WASM build was needed.

Netlist diagnostic selection synchronization (2026-10-13): Selecting a component named by a netlist error previously updated the inspector and viewport but left the old part/wire/label multi-selection IDs intact, allowing the canvas highlight to disagree with the selected properties. Diagnostic navigation now selects only the reported visible component and clears other object selections. Firefox starts with a different component selected, injects a mapped solver error for an offscreen component, and verifies that the reported component is the sole selected part after viewport recentering. TypeScript and the focused regression pass; no WASM build was needed.

Firefox engine-smoke probe recovery (2026-10-13): The shared pointer-probe test helper now identifies a floating waveform window covering a label before rejecting that point as non-topmost, so it can move the window aside and continue the real right-click flow. This fixed the common-emitter AC lesson’s second probe, which failed consistently when the first plot covered the VIN label. The dedicated test and all 19 Firefox `@engine-smoke` workflows pass with the already-built local ngspice runtime; no app behavior or WASM artifact changed.

UI audit follow-up (2026-10-13): A local browser audit found that Ctrl-clicking a wire could be swallowed by the wire drag/pointer-capture path, leaving it hovered instead of selected. Modified wire clicks now add to selection before drag handling and consume the matching click so it cannot toggle twice. The large parameter-sweep browser fixture now waits for the Run control to become enabled after reload before sending Escape; editor shortcuts are intentionally gated until project recovery finishes. All 51 UI smoke cases pass in Chromium and Firefox; the complete 116-case Firefox non-engine suite passes in 2.5 minutes. All 29 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build also pass. That build reports a 466.90 kB main JS entry and 204.3 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip, within the configured budgets. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run. This is an automated regression checkpoint; manual review of every UI state and assistive-technology behavior remains open.

Waveform keyboard-focus coverage (2026-10-13): The interactive plot already had a visible `:focus-visible` outline, but the Firefox regression for a floating waveform window did not verify it. The test now Tabs through the plot controls until the waveform itself receives keyboard focus and checks its 2 px outline; the focused Firefox/ngspice test passes with the existing local runtime. Programmatic `.focus()` alone did not prove keyboard-indicator behavior. No app behavior or WASM asset changed. Manual screen-reader review and remaining UI states are still open.

Firefox UI audit follow-up (2026-10-13): A full 116-case non-engine run exposed a race in the component-hotkey browser test: it parsed the local project before the autosave had created the storage entry, even though the UI had placed the resistor successfully. The test now treats an absent saved value as “not yet saved” and polls until the newly placed part appears. The focused case passes, followed by all 116 Firefox non-engine browser cases. `npm run build` passes its typecheck and size budgets at 204.3 KiB combined initial JS/CSS gzip. No application behavior or WASM artifact changed. Manual assistive-technology review remains open because browser automation cannot establish screen-reader behavior.

M4.3 Firefox performance follow-up (2026-10-13): The current seven-case Firefox browser performance suite passes using the existing local ngspice runtime. A refreshed 5,000-part/4,900-wire case records selection at 13/19 ms median/p95 and zoom at 38/50 ms median/max. The 40-trace plot benchmark’s 1.3 s visibility aggregate performs 80 toggles with one intentionally awaited animation frame per toggle; four single-toggle samples took 0–1 ms synchronously and 18–39 ms through two frames. This corrects the interpretation of that aggregate: the click handler is not a long synchronous task, so the measurements do not justify a renderer change. Details are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; no WASM or Pages build was run. Second-machine timing remains outstanding.

Wire-render lookup optimization (2026-10-13): The selected/hovered wire used to be found with two full-array scans on every Schematic render, even when neither selected nor hovered IDs existed. It now resolves through a memoized map keyed by wire ID. TypeScript, three focused Firefox hover/endpoint regressions, the 5,000-part/4,900-wire benchmark, and the production size-budget build pass. A synthetic lookup microbenchmark confirms the expected linear-scan versus guarded-map complexity but is not treated as an end-to-end latency claim; details and limitations are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Large group-drag lookup optimization (2026-10-13): The first changed pointer event in a multi-selection drag previously ran one CSS `querySelector` per visible selected object. It now indexes the rendered part/wire/label SVG objects once per preview and retrieves matching elements by type and ID, while preserving viewport culling, temporary transforms, and commit-on-release behavior. Firefox mixed-object movement, group movement, and full-circuit wire-selection tests pass. The 5,000-part/10,000-label benchmark verifies a 15,000-object drag and final coordinates; the 27 ms input-to-next-frame timing is a single observation and not a demonstrated speed gain. Typecheck and production size-budget build pass; see `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Group-drag snapshot lookup (2026-10-13): Drag-start snapshot creation now resolves each selected part, wire, and net label from memoized ID maps and copies only selected objects, rather than filtering all project collections three times. This makes snapshot collection proportional to selection size after the maps are built. Firefox part-group, mixed-object movement, wire-endpoint tests, and the 5,000-part/10,000-label 15,000-object drag benchmark pass. The single 28 ms frame timing is not a before/after speed claim. Production size-budget build passes at 204.4 KiB combined initial JS+CSS gzip. Details and measurement limits are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; no WASM or Pages build was run.

Floating waveform window interaction optimization (2026-10-13): Window dragging now updates the element’s position directly during pointer movement and saves the final bounds once on pointer release, avoiding a full project update/render for every pointer event. ResizeObserver size updates are debounced until resizing settles. A Firefox regression checks pointer-drag position along with keyboard movement/resizing. A complete 116-case non-engine Firefox rerun passes after updating two stale UI tests to wait for startup readiness and use click-to-place rather than drag-to-place. TypeScript, all 30 unit groups, and the production size-budget build pass at 205.2 KiB combined initial JS+CSS gzip. This is a render-work reduction; no controlled timing gain is claimed. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Connection-dot geometry performance follow-up (2026-10-13): A 5,000-part benchmark identified repeated candidate-array allocation and pin-geometry work in connection-dot derivation. The geometry path now scans matching wire buckets without assembling temporary arrays, caches built-in pin offsets, uses exact squared-distance checks for orthogonal wires, and skips trigonometry for quarter-turn rotations. Geometry unit comparisons, focused Firefox grid/hover/dot tests, and the complete 116-case non-engine Firefox suite pass. A separate seven-run quick benchmark measured 9.97 ms median / 43.48 ms p95 for 9,800 dots versus 12.18 ms / 40.79 ms immediately prior; process-to-process spread means this is not a reliable speedup claim. Repeat/interleaved measurements remain open under M4.3. No WASM or Pages build was run.

DC-sweep cursor redraw reduction (2026-10-13): DC sweep trace colors are now memoized by the trace and saved-color inputs. Before this, every analysis-cursor update allocated a new color array, invalidating the plot-path memo and rebuilding sampled SVG polylines even though the vectors, axes, and colors had not changed. The Firefox DC-sweep cursor/zoom regression, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and production size-budget build pass. This removes an unnecessary invalidation by dependency analysis; no timing gain is claimed without an instrumented browser comparison. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Wire-current sampler render stability (2026-10-13): The cursor-to-trace lookup function now keeps a stable identity until the simulation result changes. This prevents unrelated canvas rerenders (such as hover and selection) from invalidating the visible-wire current-flow memo and recalculating its graph data. The full 116-case non-engine Firefox suite, focused Firefox playback/selection/copy-paste checks, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. This reduces avoidable React work; no timing gain is claimed without an instrumented browser comparison. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Component animation render work (2026-10-13): Visible components now memoize fixed logic thresholds, capacitor/inductor values, series resistance, and component definitions by their immutable part properties instead of reparsing or looking them up on each simulation cursor update. Firefox verifies wire playback, capacitor charge animation, and lamp glow; all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. This reduces repeated per-frame derivation; no timing gain is claimed without an instrumented browser comparison. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Million-sample Firefox memory profile (2026-10-13): The existing local WASM profile completed 1,000,130 saved samples with eight plot windows and 32 rendered traces. Firefox recorded 185,794,560 bytes (177.19 MiB) of WASM linear-memory capacity after parsing, 9,619 ms solver time, and 62 ms maximum main-thread interval delay; its JS heap API returned no reading. This reports exact WASM memory capacity/high-water, not total worker or browser peak memory, which remains open. A fresh production build on 2026-10-14 emits a 539.83 KB minified main entry (155.63 KiB gzip), triggering Vite’s 500 KB raw-chunk warning; initial JS+CSS is 225.2 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB configured budget. The earlier 471.50 KB / 205.5 KiB measurement is not current. Profile, typecheck, 30 unit groups, and production build pass; the ignored local runtime was reused without rebuilding it.

UI interaction/performance audit checkpoint (2026-10-13): Repeated the full-circuit copy/paste regression five times in Firefox and extended it to drag the pasted mixed selection, asserting every copied component and wire moves together while originals remain fixed; all focused runs pass. The complete 116-case non-engine Firefox UI suite, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. The quick 5,000-part/4,900-wire benchmark measured 41.5 ms median connectivity, 54.1 ms netlisting, and 7.7 ms median connection-dot computation with cached pin locations (9,800 dots). These are local observations, not before/after claims; this audit found no justified broad renderer rewrite. Second-machine profiling and manual accessibility review remain open. No WASM build was run.

Firefox performance audit (2026-10-13): The seven-case browser performance suite passed against the existing local runtime. On this host, the 500-part/1,000-vector scrub measured 27.9 ms; a 500-instance hierarchy reached editor-ready in 381 ms and operating-point results in 526 ms; the 1,000,130-sample transient with eight four-trace windows reached result-ready in 11.0 s, with repeated scrub samples of 67/33/31 ms and a 37 ms maximum interval delay. A 5,000-part/10,000-label editor measured 660 ms ready, 21 ms Ctrl+A to next frame, 24 ms mixed-group drag to frame, and 20 ms median zoom-to-two-frames. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire case measured 770 ms render, 12/25 ms selection median/p95, 63 ms Ctrl+A, 39/53 ms zoom median/max, and 16 ms arrow navigation. Firefox exposes neither WASM memory nor JS heap here. These single-host results are local observations; no controlled before/after improvement is claimed. Detailed context is in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`.

Wire-selection render experiment (2026-10-13): A trial external selection store and memoized visible-wire layer did not show a reliable Ctrl+A latency gain in repeated Firefox runs (43–64 ms store-only, 71–74 ms after layer memoization, versus one prior 63 ms observation). The source change was reverted; no behavior or renderer complexity was retained. Further work requires interleaved profiling with stable host conditions.

Component animation sampling follow-up (2026-10-13): Component pin nets and result-vector references are now resolved lazily once per visible part and reused through the current result; each cursor frame reads values at the supplied sample index without rebuilding vector names or repeating node lookups. The cache resets when the project connectivity or result changes, and off-screen parts allocate plans only when they enter the viewport. Firefox animation regressions for wires, capacitor charge, transformer windings, lamp glow, logic-gate outputs, and voltage-controlled switch state pass using the existing local runtime. A 500-part scrub benchmark measured 28.1 ms median across five runs versus 30.7 ms across three baseline runs, with substantial overlap; treat as an observation rather than a demonstrated gain. The browser scrub helper now waits for each cursor update before reading its time label, fixing an intermittent wrong-sample test race. TypeScript, 30 unit groups, and production build pass; no WASM rebuild was run.

Schematic hover hit-test follow-up (2026-10-13): Pointer-move hover resolution now skips repeated SVG ancestry lookups while the actual event target remains unchanged, while still resolving immediately when the pointer crosses to another object. A Firefox smoke regression instruments `Element.closest`, verifies repeated moves on one target do not repeat lookups, then confirms Delete acts on the newest hovered part. All 52 Firefox UI smoke cases, 492 unit tests, TypeScript, and production build pass; the initial JS+CSS budget is 206.2 KiB gzip. This removes redundant per-event DOM work; no wall-clock speedup is claimed. No ngspice WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Imported-model placement viewport fix (2026-10-13): The full Firefox UI audit exposed that imported subcircuits used fixed world coordinates, so at common viewport sizes the new part could render beneath the Properties panel and could not be selected. Imported subcircuits and reusable project blocks now share a bounded search for snapped candidate positions inside the visible schematic, preferring clearance from existing parts. Firefox tests cover the imported-IC workflow at 390 px and 854 px; all 117 non-engine Firefox UI tests, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. Initial JS+CSS is 206.4 KiB gzip. Placement preserves pin order and project data. No ngspice WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Reusable-block placement coverage (2026-10-13): A Firefox regression now seeds the actual two-level hierarchical divider, places a reusable child block from the library, checks its rendered bounds are inside the current schematic viewport, and selects it. The imported-IC placement regression also runs at phone width and opens its Properties panel after selection. All 118 non-engine Firefox UI tests pass in 2.5 minutes. This adds direct coverage for both consumers of the shared viewport-placement helper; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Component animation registry immutability (2026-10-13): Built-in symbol-animation maps, state maps, and drawing primitives are now frozen at the same boundary as component symbols, pins, and properties. Previously these shared nested animation objects could be mutated at runtime and change how every instance of that component rendered. The component-definition regression checks immutability at all three levels; all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production download-budget build pass at 206.6 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip. No WASM rebuild or browser test was needed.

Current-flow UI cleanup (2026-10-13): Removed the leftover global current-speed slider, stored multiplier, and prop plumbing to match the requested controls: animation on/off, dot size, and dot spacing. Low-current dot speed scales linearly with solved current, so doubling current doubles speed in the milliamp range; a fixed display calibration keeps that motion visible, while a smooth cap bounds large-current motion before the repeating dash pattern can alias. Unit regressions check both the 1 mA/2 mA ratio and nonzero bounded motion at amp-level currents. The Firefox browser regression also checks that only the size and spacing sliders remain while wire motion continues during playback. The first focused Firefox run caught barely visible milliamp motion; the fixed display calibration restores visible 1 mA movement without reintroducing a user speed control. A smooth cap bounds high-current motion before it aliases, and the cap is applied to velocity before elapsed time so 30/60/120 Hz animation remains consistent. All 30 unit groups, TypeScript, the production build, and focused Firefox animation and full-circuit copy/paste regressions pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.


Expandable-control focus coverage (2026-10-13): The shared keyboard focus ring now covers native disclosure summaries used for PWL table import, imported-asset provenance, and animation settings. The focus regression uses Tab/Shift+Tab in Firefox rather than programmatic focus, which does not activate `:focus-visible` on a summary. The focused Firefox UI case, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass. This closes the sampled focus-indicator check for disclosure controls; unsampled states and manual screen-reader review remain open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.


Schematic-object keyboard focus audit (2026-10-13): Firefox now checks that keyboard focus on canvas components, wire segments, and net labels produces the intended SVG stroke/highlight treatment, at least 2 px component/label marks, and at least 3:1 contrast against the major schematic grid color. The same workflow activates the objects and verifies keyboard selection still works. The focused Firefox regression passes; the existing selection styling needed per-object color assertions because selected wires retain their selected stroke while focus uses the brighter focus color. Manual screen-reader testing and other unsampled focus states remain open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Firefox UI regression checkpoint (2026-10-13): The complete 119-case non-engine Firefox suite passes after the current animation and keyboard-focus audit updates. Coverage includes selected mixed-object copy/paste, independent wire editing, responsive viewport behavior, waveform playback, modal focus containment, visible focus on disclosures and SVG objects, and engine-free Pages guidance. No failure required further product changes in this run. Engine-tagged ngspice scenarios, manual screen-reader use, and second-machine performance measurements are separate remaining checks. No WASM build or Pages workflow was run.

Firefox/ngspice smoke checkpoint (2026-10-13): All 21 `@engine-smoke` browser workflows pass in Firefox with the existing local WASM runtime. They cover first-run editing and solving, wire/component voltage probes, stale-result reruns, operating-point and transient current animation, capacitor and inductor lessons, imported IC save/reopen, dedicated waveform windows, and AC/DC cursor/zoom. No WASM rebuild was run. This is the focused engine smoke set; broader `@ngspice` workflows remain available through `npm run test:e2e:engine:full:firefox`.

UI performance audit checkpoint (2026-10-13): Reviewed the schematic rendering, animation, plot rendering, project startup/autosave, selection, and floating waveform-window paths. No broad rewrite was justified by the code or measurements: schematic objects and plots already use memoized viewport/sample work, visible current animation is driven by one shared animation-frame loop, and plot traces are reduced to at most 900 points before SVG rendering. `npm run benchmark:performance:quick` measured 5,000-part connectivity at 43.1 ms median, 20-wire flow sampling at 0.038 ms, and 1,000,000-sample plot reduction at 0.004 ms on the local workstation; these are local observations, not targets. Typecheck and all 30 unit suites pass. The initial full Firefox `@ngspice` run passed 64/78; the 14 reported cases were subsequently triaged and all 78 now pass in the later Firefox engine verification checkpoint. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Firefox failure triage checkpoint (2026-10-13): Fixed the interactive-switch test fixture so its pins align with its wires (the old fixture created an isolated branch and correctly triggered ngspice's singular-matrix diagnostic). Noise tests now clear stale part selection before using the simulation inspector, and their expected plot label matches the separate voltage-noise-density axis. The inductor lesson compares both inductance values at the same normalized playback position; halving inductance can yield up to a 2× early-time current ratio, so the old 1.5× upper limit was invalid. All four focused Firefox regressions pass. `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` (30 suites), and `npm run benchmark:performance:quick` pass; the isolated benchmark measured 43.9 ms median connectivity and 51.3 ms median full checks for 5,000 resistor sections; a separate 5,000-instance hierarchy measured 144.5 ms median connectivity (163.9 ms p95) and 161.7 ms median full checks. The full 78-case Firefox ngspice suite subsequently passed in one run against the existing local WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was run.

Firefox UI verification follow-up (2026-10-13): All 53 cases in `npm run test:e2e:ui -- --browser=firefox` pass after the regression fixes above. A local production Vite build also completed and remained within the configured initial-download budget (206.5/245 KiB combined gzip). These checks cover the current browser UI without compiling WASM or running Pages. The 78-case Firefox ngspice suite also passes against the existing local runtime. Manual assistive-technology testing and second-machine performance measurements remain open.


Full Firefox engine verification (2026-10-13): `npm run test:e2e:engine:full:firefox` passed all 78 cases in 3.7 minutes using the existing `public/engine/ngspice.mjs` and `.wasm`. This includes the four previously isolated failures and the full set of transient, AC, noise, DC, parameter-sweep, model, animation, and waveform workflows. Combined with the 53-case non-engine Firefox UI run, browser verification currently totals 131 passing Firefox scenarios. No engine compilation or Pages workflow ran. Manual screen-reader testing, second-machine performance measurements, and the broader app roadmap remain open.

Firefox large-circuit performance recheck (2026-10-13): All seven `npm run benchmark:browser -- --project=firefox` scenarios pass with the existing engine. The 5,000-part schematic mounts 64 visible parts and 551 visible wires with 7/11 ms median/p95 selection latency; the 1,000,130-sample run renders 32 traces in eight windows in about 10 s, with 32–54 ms measured cursor scrubs and 47 ms maximum excess timer delay. Detailed one-host measurements and caveats are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM compilation or Pages workflow ran.

Waveform rendering optimization (2026-10-13): Transient, AC/noise, and DC sweep windows now share bounded formatted-point caches keyed by immutable trace vectors, sampled indices, and view geometry. Identical windows reuse exact SVG coordinates; changing the time/frequency range, vertical scale, AC mode, or trace invalidates the relevant cached view. AC transformed extrema/ranges also share sampling across windows. Unit tests prove the cache avoids rereading immutable vectors and preserves separate output for different view transforms. Firefox verified all 77 passing cases in the full engine run; the one NPN lesson cursor readout race was fixed with an explicit wait and its isolated regression passes. Transient/AC/noise/DC focused workflows, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. The million-sample timing remains noisy (9.3–9.5 s solver; 35–50 ms max timer excess), so no percentage speedup is claimed. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM compilation or Pages workflow ran.

Large-selection drag allocation follow-up (2026-10-13): Group drag now retains the immutable drag-start records rather than cloning selected parts, labels, and wire endpoint tuples. Ctrl+A reuses the full project arrays, and the movement operation translates those arrays in one pass without first allocating selected-object lookup maps. Partial selections continue to use the existing ID-indexed path. All 119 non-engine Firefox UI regressions passed before this focused change; Firefox regressions for mixed-object movement, multi-wire movement, and full-circuit paste selection pass after it. All 30 unit groups and TypeScript pass. One 15,000-object Firefox drag-to-frame sample measured 19 ms versus a prior 28 ms sample; this is too noisy to claim a stable speedup. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). The complete seven-case Firefox performance suite also passed against the existing local WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Wire-flow lookup reuse (2026-10-13): During each animation sample, repeated ngspice vector IDs are now resolved once and shared across component-pin injections. This targets duplicate reads for two-terminal parts and paired winding pins while leaving the electrical flow calculation and playback sampling unchanged. Four focused Firefox wire/capacitor/LC/transformer animation workflows pass. Five 500-component scrub runs had a 27.58 ms median versus a previous 28.1 ms median, with overlapping ranges; no user-visible speed gain is claimed. All 30 unit groups and TypeScript pass. Details are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Imported-symbol render memoization (2026-10-13): User-provided IC artwork now renders through a memoized child keyed by the immutable parsed symbol and its visible stroke color. Cursor-driven part readouts can update without rebuilding the symbol's SVG primitives and pin labels. The focused Firefox import/accessibility workflow, all 30 unit groups, and TypeScript pass. No dedicated timing comparison is available; this is a code-path reduction, not a claimed measured speedup. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Built-in symbol render memoization (2026-10-13): Static component geometry now renders through a memoized child. Playback updates continue to refresh dynamic symbols, while static resistor and other component SVG geometry is reused when cursor samples change. Focused Firefox capacitor, transformer, and lamp animation regressions pass, along with all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production bundle. No dedicated timing comparison is available; this records fewer React element mappings during playback, not a measured speedup. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Waveform trace-control memoization (2026-10-13): Trace visibility toggles now receive stable callbacks and render through a memo boundary. Plot zoom and resize no longer rebuild the selected trace controls unless their selected probes, visibility, colors, or callbacks change. Firefox probe-window and rerun/color-retention workflows pass, along with TypeScript and the production size-budget build. No timing comparison was run, so this is a render-path reduction by construction, not a measured speedup. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

AC/DC cursor render isolation (2026-10-13): AC, noise, and DC sweep cursor state is now subscribed to by only the frequency/value label, cursor marker, and live readout components. Moving the cursor no longer rerenders the chart parent that constructs axes, grid labels, trace controls, and plot frame. Firefox AC cursor/zoom/synchronization, noise plot, and DC sweep cursor/zoom workflows pass; all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. No controlled timing comparison is available, so this is a targeted update-boundary reduction rather than a measured speedup. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Firefox UI audit regression pass (2026-10-13): After the plot update-boundary changes, the complete 119-case engine-independent Playwright UI suite passes in Firefox. It covers editor startup, component and wire selection/movement, keyboard shortcuts, hierarchy interactions, dialogs, and non-engine waveform UI. Engine-tagged tests remain covered by their focused checks and prior engine suite records. No WASM rebuild was run.

Wire-flow path-string reuse (2026-10-13): Each rendered current-flow segment now compares its sampled point coordinates with its last rendered geometry and reuses the SVG `points` string when the wire path itself has not changed. Solved magnitude and direction continue to update normally, and a moved component that changes the electrical path refreshes the geometry. Firefox playback, moved-component refresh, and transformer animation checks pass; all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production budget build pass. A single 501-component playback run measured 30.98 ms versus earlier 26–31.82 ms observations, so no performance gain is established. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Floating plot readability (2026-10-13): The plot’s key reading text has minimum sizes of 12 px for axis labels and selected trace names and 13 px for cursor values; plot controls remain compact. Firefox verifies these sizes along with plot-window movement/resizing and the phone-width workspace. Keep this typography when adjusting plot layout.

UI audit follow-up (2026-10-13): Rechecked the current UI’s schematic selection, mixed-object movement, paste-selection, inspector/tool state, floating plot, and narrow-layout paths in Firefox. All six focused browser regressions pass, including the regression that selects only newly pasted wires. TypeScript and all 30 unit-test groups pass. A full UI rewrite is not justified by these checks; continue targeted fixes against user-visible defects and preserve the existing memoized/viewport-bounded render paths. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Large hierarchy optimization (2026-10-13): Connectivity grouping no longer copies each growing net-member list or sorts all members to choose deterministic auto-net order. Netlist generation now parses imported model libraries once per artifact and indexes generated/imported `.SUBCKT` definitions once instead of reparsing the generated library for each placed block. The 5,000-instance local benchmark measured hierarchy netlisting at 107.79 ms median after the change versus 243.76 ms before both optimizations; sequential runs on one machine, not a cross-machine budget. All 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and Firefox's nested-hierarchy versus flattened-circuit simulation regression pass against the existing local runtime. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Connectivity depth hardening (2026-10-13): Disjoint-set `find` now compresses paths iteratively, keeping adversarial reverse-ordered global labels from building a recursive call chain. A 16,000-label unit regression passes; the seven-run large-hierarchy benchmark remains within the previously observed run-to-run range. This change records stack-safety, not a measured speed gain.

Imported-library netlist follow-up (2026-10-13): Netlist generation now reuses parsed `.MODEL`, `.SUBCKT`, and model-record data instead of parsing the same imported libraries again while writing source lines. The added 400-IC/600-definition benchmark retained identical deck output and reduced median generation from 7.86 ms to 5.52 ms on this workstation. All 30 unit groups and TypeScript pass. Benchmark details and limits are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md); no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Device-model library lookup (2026-10-13): Placed D/Q/J/M devices now resolve imported models through a case-insensitive index, preserving first-match behavior while avoiding a linear card scan per device. A 400-device/600-model fixture retained the same generated netlist and reduced local median generation from 18.97 ms to 5.62 ms. The 30 unit groups and TypeScript pass; benchmark caveats are recorded in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Viewport-query allocation follow-up (2026-10-13): The reusable spatial indexes now deduplicate candidates with generation marks in typed arrays instead of allocating a `Set` on each pan/zoom/render query; ordered output, focused-item inclusion, and exact bounds checks are preserved. Resistor power Basics lesson (2026-10-13): Added an editable 5 V / 1 kΩ operating-point example that teaches P = V × I = V²/R, with V(VIN) and I(R1) selected. Series-resistor Basics lesson (2026-10-13): Added a beginner walkthrough for unequal series resistors, showing equal branch current and the separate voltage drops in a 10 V / 1 kΩ / 2 kΩ circuit. Inverting op-amp experiment (2026-10-14): The Advanced inverting-amplifier lesson now calculates VOUT = −VIN × RF/RIN from the live source and resistor values, shows an updated prediction while the prior operating point is stale, and compares it with ngspice after rerun. Kirchhoff current-law lesson follow-up (2026-10-14): The parallel-resistor beginner example now plots the source current alongside both branch currents. Its guided card compares ngspice's measured `|I(V1)|` with `|I(R1)| + |I(R2)|`, updates the predicted sum when values change, and retains the equal-branch-current experiment. Domain coverage protects the selected source-current trace, and the Firefox/ngspice workflow checks both the original 15 mA sum and the edited 20 mA sum using the existing local runtime. This advances the M1.5 beginner-experience milestone; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

NPN base-drive lesson (2026-10-14): The low-side switch example now plots base-resistor and load currents alongside base/collector voltage and transistor current. Its guided explanation estimates base current from `(VDRIVE − 0.7 V)/RBASE`, compares `β × IB` with the load current required to pull the collector low, and compares those estimates with ngspice at 25 ms. Changing RBASE to 150 kΩ predicts limited base drive and a higher collector voltage; after rerun, the lesson reports solved current and collector voltage and adapts the next step. Firefox verifies the original and edited runs with the existing local WASM runtime. TypeScript and all 30 unit groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M1.5 remains open for broader beginner walkthroughs.

PNP base-drive lesson (2026-10-14): The high-side PNP example now plots RBASE and RLOAD current in addition to base/collector voltage and transistor current. Its lesson estimates active-low base current from `(VCC − VDRIVE − 0.7 V)/RBASE`, compares β × IB with the current needed to raise the loaded output, and checks both against ngspice at 25 ms. Increasing RBASE to 150 kΩ demonstrates weaker high-side drive and a lower collector output; the lesson reports the rerun and suggests reducing RBASE again. A domain regression verifies the transient deck and selected traces; Firefox verifies on/off behavior, stale prediction, and the edited solve using the existing local runtime. TypeScript and all 30 unit groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M1.5 remains open for broader beginner walkthroughs.

NMOS gate-drive lesson (2026-10-14): The low-side NMOS example now plots gate-resistor current and load current alongside gate/drain voltage and transistor current. The lesson uses the built-in Level 1 model's KP and VTO for a rough saturation-current estimate, compares it with the current required to pull the 1 kΩ load low, and explains why this educational model only partially conducts at 5 V. Raising VDRIVE to 10 V predicts increased drain current; Firefox/ngspice confirms the drain falls from about 4.90 V to 4.44 V while gate current remains zero at the 25 ms sample. Guidance adapts to suggest the reverse 5 V comparison and marks the model as educational. A domain regression protects selected traces; all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, the production budget build, and the focused browser workflow pass with the existing local runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M1.5 remains open for broader beginner walkthroughs.

PMOS gate-drive lesson (2026-10-14): The high-side PMOS example now plots gate-resistor and load current alongside gate/drain voltage and transistor current. Shared MOSFET lesson calculations use VSG for PMOS and VGS for NMOS, with output-polarity-specific load-line estimates. At VSG = 5 V, the built-in model estimates 0.09 mA against the 4.8 mA needed to raise the output near VCC; changing VDRIVE from 0 V to −5 V increases VSG to 10 V, and Firefox/ngspice measures about 0.559 mA and a 0.559 V drain output. Guidance explains the active-low direction, near-zero steady gate current, the remaining output limitation, and the need to check a real device's maximum gate rating. Domain coverage, both focused Firefox/ngspice switch workflows, all 30 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass with the existing local runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M1.5 remains open for broader beginner walkthroughs.

Voltage-controlled switch model support (2026-10-14): Built-in four-terminal voltage switches can now use an imported `.MODEL ... SW(...)` card. The editor filters the model picker to SW cards, hides its local Ron/Roff/Vt/Vh fields while a model is assigned, and drives the schematic lever from parsed numeric Vt/Vh values; symbolic or missing model parameters leave the visual state unknown rather than guessing. Netlist generation uses the selected imported card and reports missing or wrong-type assignments. Regression coverage checks correct/wrong model types and a Firefox/ngspice browser simulation whose imported threshold overrides the editable default. Native custom model-card coverage is broader; LTspice import/export remains deferred.

Parallel-current prediction (2026-10-14): The parallel-resistor beginner example now asks learners to predict the R2 branch current before simulation. Its estimate recalculates from the supply and both resistor values, explains why equal branch voltage gives different current, and checks that source current equals the KCL branch sum. Firefox coverage checks an incorrect answer, a correct answer, value-driven recalculation before running, and comparison with ngspice at both resistor ratios. M1.5 remains open for other example journeys and broader first-use review.

Loaded-divider prediction (2026-10-14): The Thevenin-equivalent lesson now asks learners to predict VOUT before the first run. It calculates the lower leg as R2 ∥ RLOAD and shows the resulting voltage-divider equation; changing V1, R1, R2, or RLOAD clears the previous answer and recalculates the target. A Firefox UI-smoke regression checks the 3.333 V default, the 4.762 V result after increasing RLOAD to 10 kΩ, and that Run stays available without an engine. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed. M1.5 remains open for further beginner journeys and first-use review.

Wheatstone-bridge prediction (2026-10-14): Before the operating-point run, the lesson now asks learners to estimate VOUT − VLEFT from both arm-divider ratios. Feedback shows each midpoint formula and the signed imbalance; editing any source or resistor clears the old answer. A Firefox UI-smoke regression verifies the initial 1.667 V imbalance, the zero-volt balanced answer after R4 changes to 1 kΩ, and continued access to Run without ngspice. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed. M1.5 remains open for further beginner journeys and first-use review.

RC filter cutoff prediction (2026-10-14): Both AC low-pass and high-pass lessons now ask learners to predict the cutoff from their current R1 and C1 values before running. Feedback shows `fc = 1/(2πR1C1)` and the ideal one-pole −3 dB relationship; changing either component resets the answer and updates the estimate. A Firefox UI-smoke regression checks both filter types, value-driven recalculation, and that the AC simulation action remains available. TypeScript and all 32 domain-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed; M1.5 remains active.

Parallel-RLC resonance prediction (2026-10-14): The AC resonance lesson now asks for the LC resonant frequency before simulation and calculates `f₀ = 1/(2π√(L1C1))` from the live inductor and capacitor values. Editing either clears the prior response and updates the target; after solving, the existing lesson feedback compares the prediction with ngspice's actual output peak. Firefox verifies the initial 10 mH/1 µF estimate plus recalculation after changing each component. TypeScript and all 32 domain-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed; M1.5 remains active.

Voltage-switch preflight diagnostics (2026-10-14): Circuit Checks now finds missing `.MODEL` assignments on voltage-controlled switches and identifies when the assigned card exists but has the wrong SPICE type. The diagnostic selects the switch and says that an SW card is required, before netlist generation. Domain tests cover missing, incompatible, and valid cards, including the selected switch ID used by the UI to focus the affected part. This closes the preflight gap alongside imported SW netlist support.

Lesson UI loading (2026-10-14): Beginner prediction widgets now load only when an example lesson needs them, keeping their interactive lesson code out of the editor's initial JavaScript download. Lesson-specific browser checks verify the widgets still appear and respond correctly.

Imported device model compatibility (2026-10-14): The component registry is now the single source of truth for which imported `.MODEL` types the UI picker offers and which types Circuit Checks accepts for each built-in device family. Coverage includes diodes/LEDs/Zeners, BJTs, JFETs, MOSFETs, and voltage-controlled switches; the project-format guide now documents the same supported set. All 30 unit groups, typecheck, production size-budget build, and the focused Firefox full-circuit copy/paste selection regression pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Reusable-block settings render cleanup (2026-10-14): Port-removal protection now checks whether any instance exists with nested short-circuit `some` calls rather than allocating a flattened array of every instance on project updates; both the settings warning and delete guard use the same boolean. Removed an explicit `disabled={false}` from the UIC checkbox. This avoids the temporary array without claiming a measured timing change. All 30 unit groups and the production build/size check pass. The focused Firefox reusable-block workflow confirms removal remains disabled while a placed instance uses the block. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Firefox UI audit and plot benchmark correction (2026-10-14): All 66 Firefox UI-smoke cases pass, covering first use, storage recovery, examples, selection/clipboard, accessibility, and reusable-sheet operations. The focused mobile stale-result and reusable-block instance-protection regressions also pass. The quick benchmark now separates uncached downsampling from cached lookup instead of warming the cache before measuring the reported reducer cost. On this host, cold sampling of 100,000 and 1,000,000 samples measured medians of 0.139 ms and 1.010 ms; cached lookup measured 0.001 ms and below 0.001 ms respectively. These are local observations, not timing guarantees. The benchmark skips ngspice/WASM. No Pages workflow ran.

Current-flow playback allocation cleanup (2026-10-14): `knownPinInjection` now uses module-level component-kind sets rather than constructing two temporary arrays and scanning them for every pin on every playback sample. Native custom symbol files (2026-10-14): Added `.elsym` v1, a documented JSON format for user-created vector symbols with ordered model pins, simple shapes/text, and bounded SVG paths. The importer rejects scripts, external references, unsupported SVG markup, duplicate/gapped pin order, oversized geometry, and invalid coordinates; original files remain hashed project assets. Custom symbols can now pair with user `.SUBCKT` models by pin order even when symbol display labels differ from model pin names. Domain tests cover parsing, rejection cases, project validation, and stored pin offsets; a Firefox workflow imports the file through the UI, places the matching five-pin model, and verifies rendered path/pins. `npm test`, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox workflow pass. LTspice interoperability remains paused; this is an ELSim-native asset format. No WASM rebuild or Pages run.

Generated native IC symbols (2026-10-14): Imported subcircuits now offer “Create simple symbol & place,” which builds a readable rectangle with the declared model name and ordered side pins, stores it as a validated `.elsym` project asset, and places the IC against that symbol. Stored symbols have a direct download action for reuse and editing. Domain coverage checks ordered pin geometry and invalid input; a Firefox workflow verifies the generated SVG, persisted content hash, attached pin offsets, and download filename. LTspice interoperability remains paused; no WASM rebuild or Pages run.

Generated IC output-pin layout (2026-10-14): The simple `.elsym` generator now recognizes common output pin names (`OUT`, `OUTPUT`, `VOUT`, `VO`, `QOUT`, including signed/numeric variants) and places recognized outputs on the right while keeping other pins on the left. SPICE order and generated component pin mapping remain unchanged; models without a recognizable output retain the balanced split layout. Unit and Firefox coverage verifies the op-amp layout, persisted pin offsets, and balanced fallback. All 527 unit tests, TypeScript, the production build/size budget, and the focused Firefox generated-symbol workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages run.

Generated IC supply-pin layout (2026-10-14): Extended generated `.elsym` layouts to recognize common positive supply names (`VPOS`, `VCC`, `VDD`, `VPLUS`, `VP`, `AVDD`, `DVDD`) and negative/ground names (`VNEG`, `VEE`, `VSS`, `VMINUS`, `VM`, `AVSS`, `DVSS`, `GND`, `GROUND`). They appear at the top and bottom of the symbol, with pin labels placed beside vertical leads. The native op-amp layout now shows inputs left, output right, and supply pins vertically while preserving declared SPICE order; unknown names and all-output names keep a balanced fallback. Both browsers passed app-shell/service-worker caching, project edit persistence across reload, offline RC simulation through cached ngspice assets, and offline example loading; the production CSP checks also passed. Project archive compression (2026-10-14): New `.elsim` files use manifest v2 and raw-DEFLATE `project.json` when it reduces size; older v1 stored archives remain readable, and unsupported compression can fall back to stored output. The reader inflates incrementally under the existing 49 MiB per-entry and 50 MiB total limits, verifies declared uncompressed size and CRC before JSON parsing, and rejects DEFLATE in v1 archives. A 21 MiB repetitive provenance fixture compresses below 2 MiB and round-trips; the focused domain suite also verifies deterministic output within the runtime, v1 compatibility, path/CRC rejection, and unsupported methods. Native project format documentation and archive schemas v1/v2 were updated.

Autosave recovery ordering (2026-10-14): IndexedDB recovery now compares the validated project `updatedAt` before falling back to the separate localStorage save timestamp. This prevents an older IndexedDB snapshot with an equal timestamp from overwriting newer local JSON when the browser closes between the JSON and timestamp writes, while retaining IndexedDB fallback when it contains newer work or local storage is unavailable. Five domain regressions cover newer-local, newer-IndexedDB, tie-breaking, missing-local, and invalid-date cases. A Firefox browser regression for the interrupted-write scenario was added; it could not be run in this sandbox because Playwright’s local web server is denied socket binding (`listen EPERM`).

Corrupt local-project recovery (2026-10-14): Startup now checks local project versions from newest to oldest and loads the newest valid copy instead of abandoning the workspace when the current version is malformed. Unreadable saved strings remain untouched until the user chooses to continue; a recovery dialog offers downloads of the original data before the currently open valid project is autosaved. Domain tests cover fallback to a valid older-format key and retention of malformed/empty values. The Firefox recovery/download/autosave regression passes; it now verifies that Shift+Tab from the autofocus Continue action reaches the download action and Tab returns to Continue, keeping focus inside the alert dialog.

Schematic render isolation (2026-10-14): The SVG schematic is memoized and receives stable event callbacks backed by current-action refs. Inspector, dialog, and other unrelated application state updates can now skip rebuilding the large schematic subtree; edits to scene, selection, tool, playback result, or viewport still refresh it. Typecheck, all 32 unit-test groups, and the production UI build pass. Browser interaction checks were not rerun because this environment denies the Playwright local-server socket (`listen EPERM`).

Example runtime guidance (2026-10-14): The Examples dialog now reflects whether the local ngspice runtime is present. Engine-free Pages users are told they can inspect and edit examples, with simulation setup instructions tied to the workspace Setup action; users with the runtime are told simulation is available in-browser. The Firefox engine-free preview regression checks the dialog does not claim simulation is available when both runtime assets return 404. This aligns first-use copy with M0.2/M4.1's local-only engine policy; no WASM rebuild or Pages test ran.

Resistor-power prediction (2026-10-14): The resistor-dissipation lesson now asks users to predict R1's power before the operating-point run, computes it from the current source and resistance, and explains both `P = V × I` and `P = V²/R`. Editing either input clears the answer; the guided lesson distinguishes electrical dissipation from resistor temperature and safe power rating. Firefox checks incorrect/correct values and the 25 mW to 250 mW change when R1 drops from 1 kΩ to 100 Ω. M1.5 remains active; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.
