Potentiometer prediction activity (2026-10-14): The Basics potentiometer lesson now checks VOUT before simulation from the live source and wiper position, explaining that the lower section is `1 − position` of the track. Changing the wiper clears the old answer and updates the expected output. Firefox verifies the 50% → 5 V prediction, recalculation at 25% → 7.5 V, and continued access to Run; the existing Firefox/ngspice workflow still confirms both solved outputs with the local runtime. All 33 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 218.1 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M1.5 remains active for broader first-use review.

Current-source prediction activity (2026-10-14): The current-source Basics example now asks for VOUT before simulation using the live source current and load resistance, then explains `V = I × R`. Editing either value clears the previous answer and recalculates the target. Firefox checks an incorrect and correct 1 V estimate, then verifies 2 V after doubling R1 and 4 V after doubling source current; the existing Firefox/ngspice workflow still verifies the solved 1 V and edited 2 V operating points with the local runtime. All 33 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 218.1 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M1.5 remains active for broader first-use review.

Diode polarity prediction activity (2026-10-14): The forward/reverse-bias diode example now asks whether current flows before simulation, derives the expected answer and explanation from V1 polarity, and resets its answer when the source value changes. A focused Firefox workflow checks forward and reverse bias; the full 82-test Firefox UI-smoke suite also passes. The existing whole-schematic copy/paste regression passes in Firefox with all copied wires selected and moving with their copied components. All 33 unit groups and TypeScript pass. The UI audit found no reproducible defect or benchmark-supported reason for a broad component rewrite; current viewport culling, memoized schematic layers, lazy editors, and shared animation loop remain. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

NPN switch prediction activity (2026-10-14): The NPN low-side switch lesson now predicts whether the base drive can pull the collector low using the approximate β×IB capacity versus load current. The estimate updates and clears its previous answer when VDRIVE, RBASE, Q1 beta, VCC, or RLOAD changes, and explains that ngspice gives the actual operating point. Firefox checks the default 10 kΩ base resistor and the limited-drive 150 kΩ experiment. Binary predictions also refuse to grade invalid source values; the diode polarity lesson has a regression for malformed V1 text. Focused Firefox workflows, all 33 unit groups, and TypeScript pass; production build passes at 218.3 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against the 245 KiB budget, although Vite's generic 500 KiB uncompressed app-entry warning remains. No WASM rebuild was needed.

PNP switch prediction activity (2026-10-14): Reused the same bounded β×IB versus load-current estimate for the active-low PNP high-side switch lesson. The prompt explains that pulling the base below the emitter should raise the collector toward VCC, while insufficient base drive limits the load current. Firefox verifies the default 10 kΩ and changed 150 kΩ base-resistor cases. Both NPN and PNP focused workflows, all 33 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 218.4 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The Vite 500 KiB uncompressed app-entry warning remains. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

MOSFET threshold prediction activity (2026-10-14): The NMOS and PMOS switching lessons now ask whether gate-to-source drive exceeds the selected model's VTO magnitude. The estimate uses numeric imported-model VTO defaults and instance overrides when available, otherwise the built-in ±2 V teaching-model threshold; unknown/non-numeric thresholds are not graded. Feedback distinguishes threshold conduction from the stronger current needed to drive the load. Firefox checks both polarity modes above and below threshold. All 33 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 218.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. Vite's generic 500 KiB uncompressed app-entry warning remains. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Waveform UI responsiveness (2026-10-14): AC/DC cursor updates now notify React subscribers at most once per display frame while snapshots still expose the newest exact cursor value. Canceling a plot drag restores the exact pre-drag range, including horizontal movement already flushed in earlier frames; pointer release commits the pan normally. Added regressions for frame-coalesced analysis-cursor notifications and Firefox cancellation during a transient plot drag. Verified with 34 unit groups, TypeScript, production build (218.6 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip), and focused Firefox plot and NMOS/PMOS lesson checks against the existing local runtime. The schematic already memoizes canvas objects and viewport-culls them; plots sample traces before SVG rendering, and heavyweight editors are lazy-loaded. Vite's existing generic 500 KiB uncompressed entry warning remains. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Half-wave rectifier prediction (2026-10-14): The beginner rectifier lesson now asks for the positive output peak before simulation using the live sine offset/amplitude and an explicitly approximate 0.7 V silicon-diode drop. Feedback contrasts that simple estimate with the nonlinear ngspice model; changing source amplitude clears the old response and recalculates the answer. Firefox verifies 4.3 V at the default 5 V input peak and 7.3 V after changing amplitude to 8 V, while Run remains available. All 34 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 218.8 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The 500 KiB uncompressed entry advisory remains. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M1.5 remains active for broader beginner journeys.

UI selection race and bridge-ripple lesson follow-up (2026-10-14): Added a bridge-rectifier prediction that estimates capacitor ripple from the live sine source, full-wave interval, smoothing capacitance, and parallel load resistance; the lesson labels its constant-diode-drop RC result as a first-order approximation. Firefox UI coverage checks the default 0.1615 V estimate, updates it to 0.07654 V when C1 changes to 470 µF, and confirms Run remains available. A broad Firefox run reached 152/153 engine-independent workflows and exposed a real rapid-input race: selecting hierarchical instance X2 and immediately pressing Ctrl+C copied the prior X1 because the shortcut read transition-delayed React state. Selection setters now synchronously update refs for parts, wires, and labels; the global copy shortcut reads those current refs and the current project before constructing its clipboard. The focused X2 copy/new-project workflow now passes, along with four other selection/copy/mixed-movement workflows. The transient measurement time-window browser test was also rewritten to use the real Add measurement flow, reveal circuit settings by clicking blank canvas, and verify edited time limits survive reload and appear in the generated netlist; its focused workflow passes. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 219.1 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The quick local benchmark measured 5,000-part connectivity at 36.95 ms median, netlist generation at 48.86 ms, and one-million-sample plot reduction at 1.02 ms; these figures do not justify a speculative UI renderer rewrite. The full 153-workflow Firefox suite has not yet been rerun after the selection-race fix. Manual screen-reader review and second-machine performance measurements remain open under M4.1/M4.3. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M1.5 remains active.

Selection and ripple verification follow-up (2026-10-14): Removed render-time overwrites of the synchronous selection refs after a ten-repeat X2 clipboard stress run reproduced the transition race intermittently; the corrected X2 copy/new-project workflow now passes 10/10 repeats. The bridge ripple browser lesson also verifies a −5 V sine offset still predicts the bridge's 8.6 V capacitor peak, with the estimate recomputed from absolute input polarity. The bridge lesson and real-UI measurement persistence workflow both pass in Firefox. The full 153-workflow suite was rerun after the last race correction and all 153 pass; all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass too. The quick benchmark measured 5,000-part connectivity at 36.82 ms median, netlist generation at 48.86 ms, 5,000-instance hierarchy netlisting at 110.0 ms, and one-million-sample plot reduction at 1.01 ms. Initial JS+CSS transfer is 219.1 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. Vite still reports a 516.9 kB uncompressed entry chunk; this is 16.9 kB over its generic advisory threshold, while the enforced compressed startup budget passes. No measured browser-rendering hotspot or reproducible UI defect was found to justify broad refactoring. Manual screen-reader review and second-machine performance measurements remain open under M4.1/M4.3. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M1.5 remains active.

First-use acceptance verification (2026-10-14): Re-ran the Firefox/ngspice first-circuit journey against the existing ignored local runtime. It edits the RC starter without opening the netlist, solves a transient, opens voltage and current probes, reads time/value units, exports a populated CSV, and completes under the five-minute target. The focused workflow passes in 4.8 seconds including browser startup; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This verifies the M0.1/M1.5 first-use acceptance path on the current UI.

Cross-browser UI performance checkpoint (2026-10-14): The focused Chromium and Firefox M4.3 browser benchmarks pass using the existing local ngspice runtime. A 500-instance hierarchical circuit renders its 14 visible parts in 375/356 ms and solves in 428/537 ms. A 5,000-part/4,900-wire schematic renders in 639/756 ms; selection median/p95 is 5.1/9.4 ms in Chromium and 9/18 ms in Firefox, while zoom and keyboard navigation reach two frames within 17–78 ms across the measured samples. These one-host results remain consistent with prior measurements and do not identify a bottleneck requiring a rewrite. Full measurements are in [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; cross-machine timing and memory review remain open under M4.3.

Toolbar outside-click hot-path cleanup (2026-10-14): The app-shell pointer-down handler no longer queries the entire document to find the single animation-settings menu; it uses a direct ref and only closes it when open. The sheet-tab menu also skips a redundant state update when already closed. Firefox verifies both outside-click dismissals and existing sheet-menu/wire-selection behavior. TypeScript, all 34 domain-test groups, production size-budget build, and focused Chromium/Firefox 5,000-part navigation benchmarks pass. After the change, selection median/p95 was 5.3/9.7 ms in Chromium and 10/16 ms in Firefox; these single-run values are consistent with the preceding benchmark and do not claim a speedup. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Model dependency report paging (2026-10-14): Imported-library reports now show missing model cards and external file references in bounded pages of 20, with “show more” controls so large dependency graphs remain navigable. Missing model cards now include a direct multi-file import action; visible counts reset when the project’s model libraries change. Firefox verifies a 25-reference report reveals all entries and opens the file picker, while the existing missing-include import-and-repair workflow still passes. TypeScript, all 34 domain-test groups, and the production size-budget build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; broader model-dialect support remains separate from this UI fix.

Playback cursor synchronization (2026-10-14): Playback already advances on `requestAnimationFrame`, so cursor-linked plots and component animations now receive playback samples synchronously in that same frame instead of waiting for a second frame. Manual slider and plot scrubbing remain coalesced to one notification per display frame. Added a unit regression proving playback cancels a queued scrub notification and does not schedule another frame. All 154 engine-independent Firefox UI workflows, all 34 domain-test groups, TypeScript, the quick performance benchmark, and the production bundle check pass. Initial JS+CSS is 219.5 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget; Vite’s advisory for the 517.6 kB uncompressed app entry remains. The UI audit found no other reproducible issue or measured bottleneck warranting a broad renderer rewrite. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; manual screen-reader review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

UI audit refresh (2026-10-14): Rechecked the current editor interactions in Firefox after playback synchronization. All 154 engine-independent UI workflows pass, including hover transitions, copy/paste of a full schematic followed by moving the copied mixed selection, mixed selection, wire endpoint editing, waveform windows, responsive layout, and lazy-loaded controls. All 34 domain-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass; the initial JS+CSS transfer measured 206.7 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. A fresh 5,000-part/4,900-wire Firefox stress test mounted 64 parts and 551 wires, rendered in 771 ms, measured 6/10 ms selection median/p95, 50 ms Ctrl+A-to-frame, 39/56 ms zoom-to-two-frames median/maximum, and 17/21 ms arrow navigation. These single-host readings are consistent with the existing M4.3 range and do not support speculative changes to interaction or rendering logic. The Vite 517.6 kB uncompressed entry advisory remains; the enforced compressed startup budget passes. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Manual screen-reader review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

Schematic gesture transform cache (2026-10-14): Pointer-down now captures the SVG screen bounds, viewBox offset, and scale once; object, mixed-group, wire-endpoint, box-selection, wire-creation, and pan gestures reuse that transform through pointer-up instead of repeatedly reading layout during pointer movement. The Firefox 5,000-part/10,000-label benchmark measured 20 ms for dragging the 15,000-object selection to the next frame, compared with 25 ms in the earlier local run; this is one comparison and not enough to claim a stable speedup. Focused Firefox regressions pass for copied-circuit selection/movement, mixed object movement, selected-wire-group movement, and wire-endpoint dragging; TypeScript passes. No WASM rebuild was needed. Second-machine profiling remains open under M4.3.

Active-gesture zoom follow-up (2026-10-14): If wheel zoom changes the SVG viewBox during an object, wire, or selection gesture, the gesture now refreshes its cached transform after the view commits; ordinary pan movement still uses its original scale cache. Firefox verifies that a component continues moving by the intended 30 grid units when zooming mid-drag. TypeScript passes; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Active-gesture viewport-resize follow-up (2026-10-14): A viewport resize now refreshes the cached SVG transform and rebases the active pointer gesture, preserving accumulated grid movement instead of making a part jump. The same refresh path handles pan and wheel-zoom view changes. Firefox verifies a component moves the intended 30 grid units across a viewport resize and that the existing mid-drag zoom workflow remains aligned. All 558 unit tests, TypeScript, and the production build pass; the initial JS+CSS transfer is 222.0 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Second-machine profiling remains open under M4.3.

Parallel-LED prediction lesson (2026-10-14): The beginner parallel-LED example now asks users to estimate the lower branch current before simulation using the editable supply and R2, with the rough 2 V LED-drop assumption stated explicitly. Feedback compares the other branch estimate and explains that each resistor independently sets its LED current. Editing either resistor or the supply resets the answer and recalculates both estimates. The 93-workflow Firefox UI-smoke suite passes, including the default 9.091 mA estimate and updated 4.412 mA estimate after changing R2 to 680 Ω; all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. The initial JS+CSS bundle remains within the 245 KiB gzip budget. No WASM rebuild was needed. M1.5 remains active.

Lamp-power prediction lesson (2026-10-14): The lamp-brightness example now asks learners to predict electrical power before simulation using the editable supply and lamp resistance. Feedback relates the calculated power to the glow illustration and clarifies that the fixed-resistance lamp has no filament-temperature or warm-up model. Editing the source resets and recalculates the estimate; Firefox verifies 250 mW at 5 V, then 62.5 mW at 2.5 V. The focused Firefox workflow, all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; the initial JS+CSS remains 220.1 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild was needed. M1.5 remains active.

Transformer turns-ratio prediction lesson (2026-10-14): The AC transformer example now asks learners to estimate secondary voltage from the editable primary AC magnitude and secondary-to-primary turns ratio. Feedback explains the ideal transformer relationship and notes how source resistance and loading affect the coupled-inductor result. The focused Firefox test verifies 2 V for a 1 V primary at 2:1, then 3 V at 3:1 and 6 V after increasing primary magnitude to 2 V; all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass. Initial JS+CSS is 220.2 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild was needed. M1.5 remains active.

Native ngspice `.OPTIONS` in imported libraries (2026-10-14): The custom-library parser now preserves `.OPTIONS` solver cards as ordinary top-level model records instead of rejecting libraries that contain them. Unit coverage checks source-line retention, and the Firefox/ngspice import workflow imports the card, confirms it appears in the generated netlist, and successfully runs the dependent subcircuit. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS remains 220.2 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild was needed. M2.4 remains partial for broader library dialect coverage.

Full UI review follow-up (2026-10-14): Rechecked the React/SVG editor, waveform/animation layers, lazy-loaded editors and dialogs, and selection/clipboard paths in Firefox. The 163-workflow engine-independent run found one stale category-count assertion after two semiconductor parts were added; the UI correctly showed 11 items. Updated both category assertions, and the focused Firefox regression now passes. The other 162 workflows passed, including full-circuit copy/paste selection and group movement. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production frontend build/size check pass; initial JS+CSS is 222.5 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The quick domain benchmark measured 0.38 ms median mixed group-drag work for 5,000 parts plus 4,900 wires, and 1.03 ms median plot reduction for one million samples on this host. Existing viewport culling, memoized SVG layers, lazy secondary UI, and the frame-bound animation path remain effective; this pass found no reproducible product defect or benchmark evidence that supports a broad rewrite. No WASM or Pages build ran. Manual screen-reader review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

Nested user-library subcircuits (2026-10-14): Native SPICE library parsing now accepts nested `.SUBCKT`/`.ENDS` blocks, validates each closing name and all unmatched blocks, retains source order/text, and assigns X-device dependencies to the innermost definition that owns them. The focused Firefox/ngspice project-local `.LIB` workflow now carries a nested `LEAF` definition inside `CORE` through the generated netlist and solves it in the browser engine. A dedicated local WASM fixture checks a nested two-level user model against a 2.5 V loaded-divider result. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the complete local WASM fixture suite pass using the already-built runtime; no WASM rebuild was needed. M2.4 remains partial for broader ngspice library dialect coverage.

Subcircuit-scoped device models (2026-10-14): `.MODEL` cards inside a `.SUBCKT` are now indexed with their owning scope. Device dependencies resolve through the current nested subcircuit and its parent scopes before falling back to global model cards; scoped names do not appear in the global component-model picker or global model-type checks. A regression covers a local `DFAST` in parent `CORE`, used from nested `LEAF`, while a separate global `DFAST` remains independently available. Firefox verifies import, clean dependency reporting, generated source, and a browser ngspice run; a local WASM fixture verifies the nested diode's numeric operating point. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the complete local WASM suite pass without rebuilding the runtime. M2.4 remains partial for other ngspice model-library dialect and scoping forms.

Native ngspice subcircuit parameter headers (2026-10-14): Imported `.SUBCKT` definitions now accept formal parameter defaults directly after the ordered pin list, with or without `PARAMS:` and with whitespace around `=`. The browser inspector still exposes defaults and emits valid instance overrides. Parser coverage checks pins and defaults for both header forms; an existing Firefox IC-import workflow now exercises the native no-marker form, and a new local WASM fixture verifies that a `GAIN=3` override produces 15 V. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the complete existing-runtime WASM fixture suite, and the focused Firefox/ngspice workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment was run; M2.4 remains partial for broader native model-library dialect coverage.

Whitespace-separated subcircuit instance parameters (2026-10-14): Dependency analysis now identifies the target `.SUBCKT` before optional instance assignments written as `gain = value`, both with and without `PARAMS:`. The parser regression resolves all three parameter-call forms, and the Firefox imported-IC workflow now instantiates a nested `USER_CORE` using `gain = {gain}`, confirms the nested dependency and parameterized solve, and keeps the 3 V output. The direct ngspice fixture verifies spaced header and call assignments produce 15 V. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the full existing-runtime WASM fixture suite, and the focused Firefox/ngspice browser workflow pass; no runtime rebuild or Pages deployment ran. M2.4 remains partial for broader native model-library dialect coverage.

Scoped ngspice `.PARAM` cards (2026-10-14): Confirmed the importer retains global and subcircuit-local `.PARAM` records in their original source scopes. A new fixture proves ngspice uses local `SCALE=2` instead of global `SCALE=10` when evaluating the imported subcircuit; the Firefox user-IC import workflow also verifies that a nested model’s local `SCALE=1` keeps a `GAIN=3` output at 3 V despite global `SCALE=10`. Parser retention, all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the full existing-runtime WASM suite, and the focused Firefox/ngspice workflow pass. No app logic needed to rewrite the model or rebuild WASM. M2.4 remains partial for broader native library constructs.

Schematic render isolation for waveform preferences (2026-10-14): Plot-window position, range, and trace-preference edits replace the project object but leave the schematic scene unchanged. The memoized SVG editor now receives a stable scene-only project snapshot and a separate simulation-current flag, so project-level waveform preference edits no longer invalidate the entire schematic render. The dependent domain helpers now accept only the project fields they actually read. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, production build/initial gzip budget (220.9/245 KiB), and focused Firefox waveform-window, copy/paste, and mixed-selection regressions pass. This removes a redundant render by construction; no before/after frame-time claim is made. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Native BJT dependency resolution (2026-10-14): Imported subcircuit libraries now resolve ngspice Q-device model names after optional substrate and VBIC junction-temperature nodes, including when the model card is in another imported file. Missing-card diagnostics retain the final positional token as the unresolved model name. Unit coverage checks default, substrate, substrate-plus-temperature, instance options, and missing cross-library models; `npm test` and TypeScript pass. Syntax follows the [ngspice 46 manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf), section 7.3. Broader native model-library constructs remain open under M2.4.

Native ngspice VDMOS components (2026-10-14): Added distinct N-channel and P-channel three-terminal power MOSFET parts with D-G-S order. Their inspector requires an imported matching `.MODEL ... VDMOS` card; N-channel is the ngspice default and P-channel models must declare `Pchan`. The native netlister rejects missing or wrong-polarity models, and component current probes use the drain-current vector. Browser result instrumentation now recognizes VDMOS model cards and omits unavailable four-terminal MOSFET current vectors. Unit coverage checks parser polarity, schema and symbol pin counts, D-G-S netlisting, mismatch diagnostics, and result-vector selection; the focused Firefox browser run verifies importing/using an N-channel VDMOS project with the existing WASM runtime, and the local WASM numeric fixture checks N- and P-channel operating points. All 34 domain-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed. M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library support.

Waveform resize redraw regression (2026-10-14): The Firefox waveform workflow now verifies the waveform SVG viewBox and plotted trace coordinates update after both keyboard resizing and dragging the floating plot’s native resize edge. This guards against a stale trace with only grid/labels resizing. The focused run passes with the existing local WASM runtime; the plot reuses sampled extrema and recalculates screen coordinates, so no additional solver or WASM work was needed.

Eight-window waveform performance recheck (2026-10-14): Firefox solved 1,000,130 samples and rendered 32 traces across eight windows in 10.07 s using the existing runtime. End/Home/End scrubs measured 53.31/48.71/36.57 ms, with 7 ms maximum excess over a 16 ms timer during cursor scrubbing. These readings are within the established same-host range; no new bottleneck or reason for speculative plot changes appeared. The Firefox process did not report heap or WASM-memory metrics. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.


Conditional model runtime scope (2026-10-14): Added an existing-runtime WASM fixture for `.IF/.ELSEIF/.ELSE/.ENDIF` branches containing duplicate `.MODEL` names inside a user `.SUBCKT`, complementing top-level branch coverage. Both fixtures select the configured `Is=1n` branch and produce 4.602852 V; no WASM rebuild was needed. M2.4 remains partial for broader native model-library dialect coverage.

M4.3 quick domain profile (2026-10-14): Repeated the `--skip-engine` benchmark on the documented i7-12700F host. At 5,000 elements, resistor-chain connectivity/netlisting p95 were 42.33/57.22 ms, wire-grid p95 were 46.64/49.59 ms, and 5,000-instance hierarchy connectivity/netlisting p95 were 87.37/133.34 ms. Full circuit-check p95 values were 48.82/54.40/104.11 ms respectively. These remain below the 500 ms domain targets; the run did not isolate a new optimization target. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`.


Conditional-model sweep defaults (2026-10-14): When a shared model parameter has different defaults across mutually exclusive `.IF` branches, imported-model stepping now labels it as branch-varying and identifies the first-branch value used to initialize the editable range. Parameters absent from any branch remain excluded; equivalent numeric defaults with different SI notation are treated as equal. Domain coverage checks both cases, and a focused Firefox UI regression verifies the label in an imported NPN model. All 34 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox test pass. M2.4 remains partial for broader native model-library dialect coverage.

Full Firefox UI audit and editor profile (2026-10-14): The 164-workflow Firefox suite passes against the local Vite app, including editor selection/copy/paste, wire movement and hover, waveform controls, dialogs, accessibility names/focus, and importer flows; all 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript also pass. A fresh 5,000-part/4,900-wire Firefox profile rendered in 761 ms, mounted 64 parts and 551 wires, measured selection median/p95 at 6/18 ms, select-all-to-frame at 46 ms, zoom-to-two-frames median/max at 40/57 ms, and arrow-navigation median/max at 18/21 ms. These same-host measurements are consistent with the existing range and show no specific renderer hotspot. No speculative UI rewrite or WASM rebuild was justified. The full suite excludes only engine-tagged workflows; the engine-dependent workflows were not part of this audit.

Wire-flow SVG association fix (2026-10-14): Each solved wire-current segment is now grouped beneath an SVG element carrying its source wire ID. The existing per-wire selectors exposed that this association was missing: parallel-branch current values were correct, but Firefox could not find the rendered flow by wire ID. The parallel 1 kΩ branch regression now passes, and all nine focused Firefox ngspice smoke workflows pass against the existing local runtime. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

Native passive and transmission-line model references (2026-10-14): Imported library dependency analysis now recognizes native ngspice model references on R/C/L devices (with or without explicit values), O/LTRA transmission lines, and U/URC distributed RC lines. Resolved references retain the source file dependency so required-library export closure includes model definitions imported from other files; unresolved references remain visible in the dependency report. Parser/domain tests and a Firefox import workflow cover these forms. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the 97-workflow Firefox UI-smoke suite, and production build pass. The build remains within the 245 KiB initial gzip budget; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Broader native library dialect coverage remains open under M2.4.

Parameterized passive model references (2026-10-14): R/C/L dependency parsing now retains braced or quoted value expressions as a single SPICE field, so the model name following values such as `{RBASE * 2}` is resolved instead of being mistaken for the expression. Tests cover spaced expressions, cross-file model closure, and an unresolved model after an expression. The Firefox library-import workflow also exercises a parameterized R-model reference. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the focused Firefox importer workflow, and the production build pass; the initial JS+CSS transfer is 222.7 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.4 remains active for broader native library dialect coverage.

Imported passive model-family diagnostics (2026-10-14): Circuit Checks now identifies a used IC whose native ngspice R/C/L/O/U device references a model card of the wrong family (R/C/L/LTRA/URC). Resolved dependency records retain their target card type; findings select the top-level affected IC and identify the source library line. Domain coverage checks both a subcircuit-scoped wrong card and a cross-file wrong card, then verifies matching R/L cards clear the warnings. Firefox verifies the actionable warning and component selection. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, focused Firefox workflows, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 222.9 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.4 remains active for broader native library dialect coverage.

Native imported-IC model-family diagnostics (2026-10-14): Extended the previous R/C/L/O/U check to ngspice D, Q, J, M, S, W, and Z devices. The accepted `.MODEL` families follow the ngspice 46 [device/model table and device-line forms](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf), including W/CSW current-controlled switches and Z/NMF/PMF MESFETs. Wrong-family findings identify the original library line and select the affected top-level IC. Circuit Checks now computes a transitive definition closure once per distinct placed model and reuses an instance-by-definition index for missing, recursive, conflicting, and incompatible findings. Unit coverage checks all seven families and confirms matching card families clear the warnings; Firefox verifies the W/CSW report and selection flow. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, production build, and the focused Firefox workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.4 remains active for broader model-library dialect coverage.

Wire context-menu selection (2026-10-14): The wire context menu's `Select wire` action now updates the mixed-object selection, returns keyboard focus to the wire, and leaves the Select toolbar tool active. A focused regression passes in Firefox and Chromium. The engine-independent 99-workflow UI suite passed in both browsers before this focused test was added; the existing 5,000-part Firefox profile measured 761 ms to render, 6/10 ms selection median/p95, and 17/21 ms keyboard-navigation median/max, with viewport culling mounting 64 parts and 551 of 4,900 wires. TypeScript, the production build, and all 34 unit-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

UI code audit follow-up (2026-10-14): Reviewed the schematic render/culling path, canvas selection and paste reconciliation, plot sampling and pan lifecycle, floating plot window resize/drag lifecycle, simulation cursor notifications, and current-flow animation scheduling. The existing code already uses viewport culling, memoized canvas objects, sampled plot paths, visible-network current-flow sampling, and frame-batched cursor updates. No WASM rebuild or GitHub Pages workflow ran; second-hardware and total-memory profiling remain open under M4.3.

Imported user-defined `.FUNC` parameter calls (2026-10-14): Imported subcircuit defaults and per-instance overrides may call bounded, arity-checked functions declared at library top level or in a uniquely resolved project-local include chain. The inspector, project validator, and netlist preflight share the same function scope; unrelated library functions and unknown or wrong-arity calls remain rejected. Expressions are preserved for ngspice and are never evaluated by the UI. Parser, project/netlist, include-scope, and Firefox inspector regressions pass; a focused fixture solves both a `.FUNC`-based default (20 V) and override (30 V) with the existing WASM runtime. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Native sheet parameter `.FUNC` calls and UI audit (2026-10-14): Reusable ELSim sheet block defaults and per-instance overrides now accept imported project `.FUNC` calls with a unique, validated arity; the inspector, project validator, and netlist generator use the same project function map. Imported IC parameter scoping remains limited to that IC's library/include closure. Domain and focused Firefox coverage verify a function-backed default and override, reject unknown functions, and preserve the calls in the emitted netlist. An existing-runtime WASM operating-point fixture independently verifies the sheet default produces 20 V and its instance override produces 30 V; no WASM rebuild was needed. A fresh Firefox run passed all 169 engine-independent UI workflows, including mixed selection/movement, copy/paste, wire editing, viewport behavior, plots, dialogs, and accessibility focus. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; production build passes at 224.7 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The uncompressed 538 KiB Vite entry advisory remains, but no profiled interaction bottleneck supports a broad renderer rewrite. No GitHub Pages workflow ran; M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.


UI health follow-up (2026-10-14): All 103 Firefox `@ui-smoke` workflows pass, including clipboard/mixed-selection movement, waveform windows, resize behavior, keyboard access, and dialogs. The focused imported `.MODEL`/`.FUNC` netlist workflow also passes. TypeScript, all 34 unit groups, and production build pass. No reproducible UI defect surfaced. The current build emits a 539.83 KB raw main entry with a 500 KB Vite warning, while initial JS+CSS remains within its gzip budget; profile before changing chunk boundaries. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

Native slash-delimited model identifiers (2026-10-14): Native library parsing, cross-library dependency analysis, saved-project validation, and parameter-sweep validation now accept ngspice model names containing slash, such as `LF356/NS`; pin names and node labels retain their separate grammar. Domain regressions cover `.SUBCKT` and `.MODEL` names, dependency resolution, and saved-project validation. Firefox imports a slash-named IC and verifies its generated X instance. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the focused Firefox workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M2.4 remains partial for broader native-library dialect coverage.

Imported definition ID collision fix (2026-10-14): Component-definition IDs retain their existing value unless two model names in one library collapse to the same display slug; colliding names receive stable escaped-name suffixes. This fixes picker/renderer map collisions such as `LF356/NS` versus `LF356.NS`, while existing projects with the old ambiguous ID are upgraded during validation. Unit tests verify collision separation, legacy project ID upgrade, and archive round-trips. The focused Firefox importer verifies unique IDs for both colliding names and places the slash-named model successfully. TypeScript and all 34 unit groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Native ngspice `.ENDS` name handling (2026-10-14): The imported-library parser now follows ngspice: an optional subcircuit name on `.ENDS` is advisory and does not have to match the innermost `.SUBCKT`; one `.ENDS` still closes one active definition. Extra `.ENDS` arguments and unmatched openings/closings remain errors. Domain coverage checks nested mismatched names and source ranges, and a new fixture runs the same nested model on the already-built ngspice WASM runtime to verify a 5 V loaded-divider result. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. See the [ngspice manual, section 2.6.2](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

UI audit continuation (2026-10-14): The Firefox compatibility suite passes all 186 engine-independent workflows, including wire selection, copy/paste, mixed-group drag, zoom, viewport resizing, and placement. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire benchmark mounted 64 parts and 551 wires and measured 752 ms initial render, 12/19 ms selection median/p95, 57 ms select-all-to-frame, 39/59 ms zoom-to-two-frames median/max, and 19/33 ms arrow-navigation median/max. A separate 5,000-part run measured 635 ms editor-ready time and 6 ms selection-to-frame median. These local measurements remain within the established interactive range and do not justify a renderer rewrite. Heavy plots, examples, lessons, and advanced editors remain lazy-loaded; the schematic remains viewport-culled. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the production build pass; the build stays within the initial-download gzip budget (229.6/245 KiB), with a 555.5 KiB raw entry-chunk advisory. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Broader manual accessibility and second-hardware review remain open under M4.

Native `.CSPARAM` model-library records (2026-10-14): The imported-library parser now preserves top-level ngspice `.CSPARAM` constant-vector records in the generated netlist and rejects them inside `.SUBCKT` blocks rather than silently dropping them. The ngspice manual describes `.CSPARAM` as experimental, so ELSim preserves the source and leaves evaluation to ngspice; ELSim does not treat the constant as an editable component value. A parser regression, Firefox library-import/netlist workflow, and existing-runtime WASM fixture verify retention and that ngspice evaluates a test constant to 3. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; the focused Firefox flow and WASM fixture pass without rebuilding the runtime. M2.4 remains partial for broader model-library dialect coverage.

Native ngspice `.SAVE` cards in imported libraries (2026-10-14): Top-level `.SAVE` vector selections are now retained in imported model-library text and flagged as circuit-wide output configuration. The generated simulation's later `.save all` request coexists with an imported `.SAVE V(vin)` under the bundled ngspice runtime; numeric coverage confirms output-voltage and resistor-current vectors remain available. The shared warning panel is labeled “Imported model compatibility warnings” and describes both native ngspice library effects and dialect mismatches accurately. Parser/source-line tests and focused Firefox import-and-simulate workflows pass, along with all 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript. No ngspice rebuild or Pages workflow was needed. M2.4 remains partial for broader native model-library dialect coverage.

UI list and hierarchy indexing (2026-10-14): Symbol-to-IC suggestions are now grouped by pin count once per filtered library, and symbol usage plus symbol assets are indexed once per project structure. The project-block list computes reverse hierarchy reachability once instead of recursively scanning the hierarchy for every candidate sheet; reopening a sheet validates saved selections with ID sets instead of rescanning every part, wire, and label for each selected item. Firefox passes the 52-IC/device-model search, symbol-match cap and placement workflow, plus reusable-block visibility and sheet-menu workflows. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 225.5 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed.

Model dependency report rendering (2026-10-14): Recursive model-cycle findings now use the same 20-item paging as missing model references, so a large imported dependency graph does not mount every cycle in the report at once. The “import referenced files” button condition is memoized against the include findings rather than rescanning them on every parent render. Firefox verifies 25 independent cycles render in pages of 20 and 5, alongside the existing 25 missing-model references. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 225.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed.

UI derived-data reuse (2026-10-14): Imported-symbol pin mismatch descriptions are now computed with the filtered library matches and reused across unrelated app renders. Reusable-block settings derive available port names once per active-sheet label/port update using a set, rather than scanning all existing ports for every label on each render. Firefox passes the large imported-library symbol-search/placement workflow and reusable-block settings workflow; TypeScript and the production build pass. Initial JS+CSS is 225.7 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed.

Sheet-tab parent labels (2026-10-14): Sheet tabs now resolve their parent label from the existing sheet-ID map instead of searching the sheet array once per tab. Project validation caps the sheet count at 64; the Firefox reusable-block placement and sheet-menu workflows confirm hierarchy labels and navigation remain intact. TypeScript and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed.

Duplicate-model diagnostic paging (2026-10-14): The imported-library conflict panel now mounts at most 20 duplicate model-name conflicts at a time, with an expand control for additional findings. Each displayed conflict still lists its source files and retains direct source-line review. Firefox verifies 26 distinct conflicts across two valid imported libraries, expansion from 20 to 26, and source navigation. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 225.8 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed.

Full UI audit follow-up (2026-10-14): Rechecked the previously reported intermittent whole-circuit paste selection in Firefox. The complete engine-independent Firefox suite passed 172/172, including the 104 core `@ui-smoke` cases; all 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript passed, and the quick performance benchmark completed. Large schematic Firefox recheck (2026-10-14): The current 5,000-part/4,900-wire editor benchmark mounted 64 parts and 551 wires and rendered in 763 ms. Selection measured 7 ms median/12 ms p95, Ctrl+A to the next frame 46 ms, zoom to two frames 40 ms median/53 ms max, and arrow navigation 18 ms median/31 ms max. This single-host run is consistent with the existing 2026-10-14 range and does not justify a speculative canvas rewrite. The focused Firefox performance test passed without loading or rebuilding WASM; second-machine and peak-memory baselines remain open under M4.3.

Imported-library terminal probing (2026-10-14): User-provided model libraries can now preserve top-level native ngspice `.PROBE` cards. The compatibility panel warns that terminal-current probes can insert current-sense branches across the circuit and increase stored-vector memory; a `.PROBE` inside a `.SUBCKT` is rejected because the supported ngspice behavior targets top-level devices. The pinned existing WASM runtime verifies `.PROBE I(R1)` yields an accessible current vector while retaining transient voltage samples; Firefox verifies importing, warning, generated-netlist retention, and a complete simulation. All 34 unit groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. See the [ngspice manual, section 11.6.5](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf) for `.PROBE` semantics. M2.4 remains partial for broader library-dialect coverage.

Paste-selection race hardening (2026-10-14): Pasting now switches the synchronous selection refs to the new part, wire, and label IDs immediately, and the wire validity pass preserves pending clone IDs until the project records arrive. This prevents a split React commit from leaving the original wires in the drag snapshot. Firefox regressions verify the pasted mixed selection and a rapid double-paste select and move only the latest clone; both pass. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Imported ngspice measurement cards (2026-10-14): User libraries can now retain top-level `.MEAS`/`.MEASURE` cards for transient, AC, and DC analyses. The library compatibility report explains that these measurements run over the full circuit, rejects cards inside a subcircuit, and associates returned values with their library source line. Native measurement results appear alongside waveform results for each analysis type. Unit tests cover transient, AC, and DC scalar output parsing, analysis-specific filtering, and sweep labels; pinned existing-runtime WASM fixtures verify native AC/DC measurement values coexist with saved waveform vectors, while Firefox verifies imported transient, AC, and DC measurements appear in results windows with the correct source lines and only for the matching analysis. Results are matched only against the currently selected analysis, so other valid library measurements do not produce false missing-result warnings. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; broader library-dialect coverage remains under M2.4.

UI audit recheck (2026-10-14): Re-ran the full 173-case engine-independent Firefox Playwright suite; all passed, including clipboard/mixed selection, wire editing and movement, viewport gestures, modal focus, responsive layouts, lazy-loaded tools, and waveform controls. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the local production build also pass.
