Imported measurement name conflicts (2026-10-14): Dependency analysis now identifies duplicate `.MEAS`/`.MEASURE` names when they target the same analysis and links each imported card to its source line; same names for different analyses and mutually exclusive top-level conditional branches are not reported as conflicts. The worker suppresses ambiguous scalar values when duplicate imported cards or ELSim transient-measurement names collide, while retaining source-mapped diagnostics in the run record. Pinned-runtime WASM coverage records ngspice’s duplicate-name output, unit coverage verifies ambiguous values are omitted, and Firefox verifies the conflict report and source review. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

UI health audit (2026-10-14): The complete 173-workflow engine-independent Firefox suite passes, along with nine real-ngspice Firefox smoke workflows and the focused duplicate-imported-measurement simulation. The full suite covers project recovery, schematic selection/copy/paste/mixed movement, wire endpoints/hover/junctions/current animation, component placement and rotation, waveform windows/cursors, imported models, lessons, keyboard operation, focus/accessibility names, dialogs, and narrow layouts. The live engine test exposed a bad node name in the new regression fixture (`V(out)` instead of the starter's `V(vout)`); the source-line expectation was corrected and the end-to-end duplicate-name run now passes. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and production build pass; startup JS+CSS is 226.8 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The quick benchmark stays within current domain targets; one-million-sample plot reduction is 1.019 ms median. No reproducible UI runtime defect or new hot path was found, so no broad rendering rewrite was justified. `App.tsx` remains a large maintainability hotspot (4,809 lines, including the 1,050-line `Schematic` and 371-line `startSimulation`); extract those responsibilities incrementally when a behavior change or profiling result gives a clear boundary, without treating file splitting as a runtime optimization. Manual screen-reader review and a second-machine performance baseline remain open under M4.1/M4.3. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Imported `.FUNC` collisions and wire Ctrl-click race (2026-10-14): Imported-library conflict analysis now reports duplicate top-level `.FUNC` names with source lines, while mutually exclusive `.IF` definitions and subcircuit-scoped records are excluded. The import panel provides direct review actions; a Firefox test covers two libraries with different same-arity bodies, and a domain test protects conditional-branch behavior. The full Firefox UI-smoke suite found an intermittent Ctrl-click wire-selection failure: a zero-delay reset could clear the wire's click-consumption flag before Firefox dispatched the synthetic click, toggling the wire back out of the mixed selection. Moving the fallback reset to the next animation frame fixes the event-order race. The selection regression passes 10/10 repeats, the complete 105-workflow Firefox UI-smoke suite passes, and the duplicate-measurement real-engine workflow passes. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and production build pass; startup JS+CSS is 226.9 KiB gzip against 245 KiB. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

UI review and large-schematic profile (2026-10-14): The Firefox engine-independent UI suite ran 106 workflows; 104 passed, and its two failures were stale checks that still treated lowercase `a` as a future shortcut after adding the built-in educational op-amp. The shortcut reference and selection checks now describe the actual `Idealized op-amp` command; both focused Firefox regressions pass, as does the new op-amp simulation/probe workflow on the existing WASM runtime. TypeScript, all 34 unit groups, and the local production build pass. The built app transfers 227.9 KiB gzip initially (215.0 KiB JavaScript plus 12.9 KiB CSS) against the 245 KiB limit; Vite’s raw main-chunk advisory remains, while the enforced compressed budget passes. Firefox’s 501-component/1,001-vector benchmark reports 42.95 ms for result scrubbing, 19 visible animated components before fitting, and all 501 after fitting. Existing viewport culling and lazy loading are working; no measured UI hot path justifies a broad renderer rewrite. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

Built-in op-amp lessons (2026-10-14): Inverting and non-inverting gain lessons now use the ELSim-authored built-in five-pin op-amp without imported IC files. Firefox verifies negative-feedback outputs near −1 V/−2 V and non-inverting outputs near 1.5 V/2.5 V through the existing browser WASM engine. A convergence issue in the initial hard-clamped behavioral source was fixed with a finite-gain controlled source and diode rail clamps; focused WASM fixtures verify output polarity and internal current direction. The two focused Firefox/ngspice lessons and both pinned-runtime WASM fixtures pass. No WASM compilation or Pages workflow ran.

Copied-scene selection report (2026-10-14): Added a Firefox regression for hovering a wire, marquee-selecting the full schematic, copying/pasting, and dragging the pasted selection. It verifies that only new wire IDs are selected and moved, while original wires remain unchanged. The targeted test passes four repeated Firefox runs; the two existing whole-circuit copy/paste regressions passed six repeats each (18 total). The reported mix of old and copied wire selection did not reproduce in this checkout. Both locally available DDC schematics also passed the same select/copy/paste check after temporary copies were normalized to the current project schema; the source archives were not changed. No application behavior was changed without a failing case. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.


Subcircuit-local `.FUNC` scope (2026-10-14): Imported libraries now resolve functions declared inside a `.SUBCKT` for that subcircuit’s parameter defaults and local `.MODEL` expressions; nested children inherit parent functions, child declarations can shadow them, and siblings cannot access them. Project validation, the IC inspector, and netlist preflight use the same lexical function scope for instance overrides. Domain tests cover forward declarations, inheritance, shadowing, sibling isolation, local model expressions, and project/netlist behavior. A focused Firefox workflow verifies import, the displayed default, a valid function-based instance override, and generated netlist preservation. The focused existing-WASM fixture confirms a scoped function produces 20 V without rebuilding ngspice. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, `git diff --check`, and the production size-budget build pass. The focused Firefox run needed temporary local-server permission; the full UI suite was not run. M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

UI derived-work reuse (2026-10-14): The component shortcut help data is now built once at module load instead of being reconstructed on every App render, and the Examples dialog memoizes its topic/query filtering and order. TypeScript, unit tests, and the production size-budget build pass; the focused Firefox scoped-function inspector workflow also passes. No change to UI behavior is intended.


Firefox UI audit and current-host benchmark (2026-10-14): All 180 engine-independent Firefox workflows pass, including schematic copy/paste and mixed movement, wire editing, responsive panels, dialogs/focus, waveform controls, and imported subcircuit-local `.FUNC` inspector validation. The category-search regression exposed a stale expected card count; Controlled now correctly includes the voltage-controlled switch, and the test asserts it explicitly. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 228.7 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget, while the raw 551.7 KB main chunk still produces Vite’s advisory. The quick synthetic benchmark measured 5,000-wire connectivity at 32.1/41.5 ms median/p95, netlisting at 44.1/53.7 ms, mixed group drag data transformation at 0.376 ms median, and million-sample plot reduction at 0.999 ms median. These are current-host measurements; no new UI bottleneck warrants a renderer rewrite. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

Imported `.FUNC` scope lookup optimization (2026-10-14): project validation, netlist generation, and the imported-model picker now create one indexed resolver per parsed library set. Asset include-chain functions and each requested subcircuit’s lexical scope are memoized, avoiding a full library index rebuild for every subcircuit. Domain regressions confirm cached reuse, parent inheritance, and sibling isolation; the focused Firefox imported-subcircuit workflow, all 34 unit-test groups, typecheck, production build, and performance quick benchmark pass. The local ngspice WASM was not rebuilt. The full Firefox UI suite was not rerun because the preceding audit already passed it and this change had a focused browser regression.

Imported model include indexing (2026-10-14): Dependency analysis now indexes project-local asset basenames once and caches target section lists, so each `.include`/`.LIB filename section` no longer scans the whole asset set or reparses the target's section declarations. Domain coverage checks nested path basenames, duplicate filename ambiguity, repeated references, and unselected sections; Firefox passes the multi-file import flow. The version 11 quick benchmark resolves 500 shared section references across 501 libraries in 9.33 ms median / 9.67 ms p95 on the documented i7-12700F host. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; broader native model-library dialect coverage remains open under M2.4.

Conditional model-library directive validation (2026-10-14): Imported ngspice `.IF` blocks now accept nested conditionals, device records, and `.MODEL` variants only; unsupported dot directives such as `.FUNC`, `.MEAS`, `.GLOBAL`, and `.OPTIONS` receive a source-line error instead of being passed through or accidentally treated as unconditional. This prevents conditional function definitions from leaking into global validation scope. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass, and Firefox verifies supported conditional `.MODEL` parameter variants still import and expose branch-varying defaults. Constraint follows the [ngspice manual, section 2.15](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Imported subcircuit ground-pin validation (2026-10-14): The model-library parser now rejects node `0` in a `.SUBCKT` external pin list with the source line and model name; ngspice requires subcircuit external nodes to be non-ground (manual §2.6.1). A domain test checks the error, and a focused Firefox import test confirms the user sees the diagnostic and the invalid asset is not retained. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass. See the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Imported IC polarity pin names (2026-10-14): Imported `.SUBCKT` pin validation now accepts ngspice polarity suffixes such as `IN+` and `IN-`, and project schemas v2–v8 preserve these names. Domain coverage validates parsing and project loading; Firefox verifies the importer places an IC with the exact ordered pin labels and retains them after reload. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass. See the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Full engine-independent Firefox UI audit (2026-10-14): `npm run test:e2e:compat` passed all 182 workflows against the current development UI and local preview. Coverage includes autosave recovery, onboarding/examples, importer diagnostics, mobile layout, waveform controls, wire and mixed-selection editing, copy/paste, hierarchy, focus/keyboard behavior, and project archive flows. No reproducible UI regression was found. No WASM rebuild or GitHub Pages workflow ran.

Ngspice library comments and dollar identifiers (2026-10-14): The shared model-library scanner now recognizes documented `$`, `;`, and `//` end-of-line comments only outside quoted text and at token boundaries. It no longer truncates valid identifiers such as `D$FAST`, `OPA$1`, or pins `IN$+`/`IN$-`. Unit coverage checks parsing, retained source records, and comment-only compatibility text; Firefox imports and places a dollar-named IC with ordered dollar-containing pins. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass, as does the focused Firefox workflow. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows [ngspice manual §2.4.4](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf); broader M2.4 dialect work remains.

Ngspice double-backslash model continuations (2026-10-14): Imported libraries now join physical lines ending in the native ngspice `\\` continuation marker, including a following `+` line, while retaining the original physical records and line numbers in generated netlists. The parser rejects continuation markers on `.TITLE`, `.LIB`, and `.INCLUDE` records, and reports a marker with no following line. Parser tests cover `.MODEL` parameters, `.SUBCKT` defaults, source preservation, forbidden directives, and incomplete records; Firefox verifies import, IC placement, and generated-netlist preservation. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the focused Firefox workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows [ngspice manual §2.4.5](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf); broader M2.4 dialect work remains.

Native ngspice model-parameter scale suffixes (2026-10-14): The safe imported-IC parameter validator now accepts ngspice's `mil` and atto (`a`) scale factors and trailing alphabetic unit text, including in bounded arithmetic expressions. This prevents valid `.SUBCKT` defaults and per-instance overrides from being rejected before reaching ngspice. Domain coverage verifies imported defaults and suffix expressions; Firefox verifies the inspector accepts an atto override and emits it in the X instance netlist. A fixture on the existing WASM runtime evaluates `mil`, `a`, and unit-suffixed defaults and solves to 5 V. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, production build/download budget, and focused Firefox flow pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Syntax follows [ngspice manual §2.1.4.2–2.1.4.3](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Numeric-leading imported IC identifiers (2026-10-14): The library parser and project validator now accept ngspice subcircuit names such as `74HC00m` and ordered pins such as `1A`, while retaining stricter node-name checks elsewhere. Domain and Firefox tests verify import, placement, ordered pins, and netlist output; an existing-runtime WASM fixture checks a numeric-leading model name and pins solve correctly. This follows ngspice manual §2.1.3.4 and its native `74HC00m` example. M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Slash-delimited imported IC pins (2026-10-14): The `.SUBCKT` parser and project validator now accept node identifiers such as `IN/P` and `OUT/P`, matching ngspice batch-mode node-name rules. Parser/project tests cover round-trip validation, Firefox verifies imported symbol placement and emitted pin order, and an existing-runtime WASM fixture confirms ngspice solves the slash-named interface to 10 V. This follows [ngspice manual §2.1.3.4](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains partial for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Ngspice `.LIB` section identifiers (2026-10-14): M2.4 import compatibility accepts ngspice section names as single tokens rather than C-style identifiers, including numeric-leading and punctuation-rich names. The scanner preserves a leading `$` when it is a section token while still recognizing `$` comments after the filename and section arguments; project v7/v8 schemas use the same token constraints. Parser, project, and Firefox picker tests cover `$cash` and a section containing mixed punctuation, and seven existing-runtime WASM fixtures select and solve names with numeric, dot, slash, dollar, plus, and broader punctuation. This follows the `.LIB filename libname` syntax in [ngspice manual §2.10](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M2.4 remains active for broader native library dialect coverage.

Native MESFET components (2026-10-14): Added N-channel and P-channel MESFETs to the component catalog, v8 project schema, palette, model-family checks, probes, wire-current balance, and native netlist generation. They use ngspice Z devices with ordered Drain, Gate, Source pins and require matching imported NMF/PMF models. Because ngspice does not expose a Z-device drain-current vector, netlists add a zero-volt drain sense source; this makes the plotted current use the conventional positive-into-drain direction. The existing bundled WASM solves both NMF and PMF test circuits and verifies their sense-current vectors without rebuilding ngspice. Domain tests cover both model families and wrong-polarity diagnostics; Firefox verifies the palette, imported NMF model, simulation, and drain-current waveform. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass; no Pages workflow ran. This follows [ngspice manual §7.5](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains active for broader native model-library coverage.

Firefox UI audit follow-up (2026-10-14): The full 118-workflow engine-independent Firefox UI suite exposed two shortcut/property defaults that the prior checks missed. Lowercase `w` now reliably activates Draw wire; the current-controlled switch uses uppercase `W`, matching the case-sensitive shortcut scheme. Placing a current-controlled switch auto-selects its sole available voltage source, while circuits with zero or multiple voltage sources still require an explicit choice. The affected flows pass in Chromium and Firefox, and the full Firefox UI smoke suite passes 118/118. The four whole-circuit paste/move regressions also pass in Chromium, including rapid repeated paste. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; initial JS+CSS is 230.5 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB startup budget. The raw application chunk still triggers Vite’s size advisory but its compressed startup budget passes; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

UI audit recheck after toolbar notice placement (2026-10-14): All 188 Firefox engine-independent browser workflows pass, alongside the 119-case Chromium UI-smoke suite, TypeScript, and all 34 unit groups. Coverage includes the desktop stale-results overlay staying out of toolbar layout, the mobile notice strip, full-circuit copy/paste selection, wire editing, responsive panels, and keyboard/focus behavior. No reproducible UI failure surfaced. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine performance measurements remain open under M4.1/M4.3. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Native ngspice node-token compatibility (2026-10-14): Imported `.SUBCKT` pins now accept documented batch-mode node tokens such as `IN!`, `IN:1`, and `OUT#1`, while rejecting whitespace, ngspice-forbidden delimiters, control characters, oversized names, and ground aliases as imported interface pins. Project schemas and component-definition schemas use matching rules; native project hierarchy retains its existing ground-port behavior through an explicit internal parser option. Parser, saved-project, Firefox import/netlist, and existing-runtime WASM tests pass. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows [ngspice manual §2.1.3.4](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains active for broader native model-library coverage.

SPICE model-name schema parity (2026-10-14): Project schemas v2–v8 now accept the same numeric-leading and slash-delimited imported model identifiers as runtime validation, including names such as `74HC00m`, `LF356/NS`, and `DIODE/FAST`, while still rejecting empty slash segments and whitespace. A schema regression checks both subcircuit and device model fields in every supported project schema version. All 34 unit groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This closes a serialization/schema mismatch found during M2.4 import review.

Passive model-picker coverage (2026-10-14): The focused Firefox workflow now exercises imported R, C, and L model selection, including placing a new inductor, selecting its L-family model, confirming the model-default inspector state, and checking the generated instance references the selected `.MODEL`. This closes the prior E2E coverage gap for inductors under M2.4; the existing local-runtime fixture continues to cover solver behavior without rebuilding WASM.

Imported ground-pin guidance (2026-10-14): When an imported `.SUBCKT` declares `0` or `GND` as a formal pin, the import error now explains that ngspice treats it as global ground rather than a distinct connectable IC terminal and recommends keeping ground internal or renaming a signal pin. The parser regression checks numeric zero and case-insensitive GND spellings. Existing-runtime ngspice behavior was checked directly; no WASM rebuild was run.

UI loading audit (2026-10-14): The model-dependency report is now code-split and only requested when missing, recursive, or unresolved model dependencies exist; ordinary projects do not load its module. Production build confirms it is a separate lazy chunk and initial JS+CSS is 230.0 KiB gzip against the existing 245 KiB budget. The 120 engine-independent Firefox UI smoke tests passed, including model dependency actions, wire/component selection and paste, responsive layout, keyboard behavior, and lazy-loaded views. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow ran. The raw main chunk still emits Vite’s size advisory; keep investigating through measured, isolated code-splitting work rather than broad UI rewrites.

Large-schematic playback sampling (2026-10-14): Visible ordinary parts no longer subscribe to every playback sample unless selected or hovered for a live value tooltip. Result-driven symbol animations and state indicators (capacitor, inductor, transformer, lamp, LED, switches, logic, and transistor families) remain sample-synchronized. The Firefox 501-part/1,001-vector browser benchmark now measures both the default viewport and a fitted full sheet: full-sheet cursor scrub measured 80–88 ms after the change versus 123 ms before it in single local runs; offscreen parts remain culled. Focused Firefox tests pass for capacitor, transformer, lamp, voltage-switch animation, reduced-motion, component hover (including diode current), and mixed selection/paste interactions. TypeScript, all 34 unit groups, and production build/startup budget pass. The raw app chunk warning remains; the attempted model-parser split was reverted because core project modules import it statically and Vite confirmed it would not leave the initial graph.

Playback follow-up (2026-10-14): Wire-flow sampling is skipped when a project has no wires, and the transient value readout subscribes to playback frames only while at least one trace is visible; the plotted cursor marker and simulation-time label keep their own live updates. The Firefox 501-part/1,001-vector benchmark passed at 40.3 ms for the default viewport and 78.7 ms for the fitted sheet (local single-run measurements; compare with care). Focused Firefox waveform cursor/readout tests pass, as do all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build. Initial JS+CSS is 230.1 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Waveform performance follow-up (2026-10-14): A focused Firefox run of the 40-trace/10,012-sample plot measured individual visibility updates at 24–33 ms through two frames, with plotted traces capped at 452 points. This remains consistent with the preceding single-host profile and does not justify a speculative plot rewrite; cross-hardware profiling remains open under M4.3. No WASM rebuild was run.

Batch model-library section selection (2026-10-14): Importing multiple user libraries now opens one section picker when any selected library has multiple named `.LIB` sections and the schematic does not identify which one is needed. The dialog shows every selected file, requires at least one section from each multi-section library, and commits all files together so project-local include dependencies resolve in one operation. Single-file section selection remains unchanged. Focused Firefox tests cover selecting one of two device sections alongside its parent library, along with the existing single-file picker and keyboard-focus behavior; the batch workflow then saves and reopens a portable `.elsim` archive and verifies both original library texts and the selected `fast` section survive. All 34 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This advances M2.4 custom user-library workflows; broader dialect coverage remains open.

Repairing an imported model section (2026-10-14): The model-dependency report now offers a direct section picker when an already-imported library contains the referenced `.LIB` section but that section was not selected. The action updates the existing project asset, retains other selected sections and recorded provenance, and keeps the original library text intact. A Firefox regression imports dependent libraries with the wrong section selected, repairs it from the report, verifies the dependency clears and project data is preserved, then successfully runs the project in browser ngspice against the existing local WASM runtime. The full 191-case engine-independent Firefox UI suite, the focused engine-backed workflow, TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and production build pass; initial JS+CSS is 230.9 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. Vite still reports the raw entry chunk above 500 KiB; compressed startup remains within budget. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow ran.

XSPICE model-module diagnostic (2026-10-14): Imported-library warnings now state that an XSPICE A-device cannot load its required `.cm` code-model module in this browser build. The build configuration omits the XSPICE module directory, and ELSim does not import `.cm` files; the previous “unverified” wording understated this known limitation. The diagnostic offers a plain-text `.SUBCKT` alternative and opens the exact source line. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the focused Firefox UI-smoke test pass; no WASM rebuild or hosted workflow ran.

UI health and performance audit (2026-10-14): The full engine-independent Firefox UI suite passed all 192 cases, covering selection and clipboard behavior, wire/component editing, keyboard and focus, responsive layout, dialogs, model workflows, and project save/load. A quick domain benchmark measured about 6.5 ms connectivity, 9.1 ms netlisting, and 7.9 ms full circuit checks at 1,000 parts; at 5,000 parts these were about 40 ms, 52 ms, and 46 ms. Plot reduction of one million samples took about 1 ms in the local Node benchmark. Review confirmed the UI already uses viewport culling, memoized spatial indexes and geometry, lazy-loaded advanced editors, and frame-bound playback updates. No additional UI defect or low-risk optimization with a measured benefit was found, so the large `App.tsx` was not mechanically rewritten; any future extraction should target a measured render bottleneck. No WASM rebuild or GitHub Pages workflow ran.

Native ngspice quoted parameter expressions (2026-10-14): User model imports now preserve and validate single-quoted parameter expressions, including spaces, for subcircuit defaults, model-card values, and instance overrides. Validation still accepts only the existing bounded arithmetic and allowlisted function grammar. Unit tests, the focused Firefox import/editor/netlist workflow, and an actual browser-WASM ngspice fixture pass; the fixture checks a quoted default evaluates to 5 V and a quoted override to 6 V. This follows ngspice manual §2.11.1. The existing local runtime was reused; no WASM rebuild or hosted workflow ran.


Native ngspice BSIM3 model binning (2026-10-14): The imported-model compatibility checker no longer labels `.MODEL` bin cards using native BSIM3 `LEVEL=8` or `LEVEL=49` as unsupported LTspice syntax. The existing browser-WASM runtime selected `NCH.1` from an unsuffixed `NCH` device using its L/W bounds, and the fixture verified the expected drain current; an alternate bin has a distinct threshold to detect wrong selection. Dependency analysis resolves an IC’s internal unsuffixed model reference across bin cards and retains every matching library asset, even when bins are split across files. Unit and focused Firefox import-warning tests confirm BSIM3 bins stay warning-free while unsupported level-1 bins retain their warning. This follows [ngspice manual §3.2.3 and §7.6.3.3](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). The existing runtime was reused; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Full UI audit recheck (2026-10-14): The complete engine-independent Firefox suite passes all 193 workflows, including whole-circuit and rapid repeated paste, mixed wire/component/label selection and movement, independent wire editing, viewport and resize interactions, focus/keyboard behavior, responsive layouts, floating waveform controls, and model-library UI. All 34 domain test groups and the production build pass; initial JS+CSS transfer is 231.2 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The raw entry chunk still triggers Vite's advisory, but no interaction failure or measured bottleneck in this pass justifies a broad App.tsx rewrite. No ngspice/WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

Standalone BSIM3 bin-family selection (2026-10-14): Imported standalone NMOS/PMOS pickers now offer an unsuffixed BSIM3 base name when complete ngspice `LEVEL=8`/`49` bin cards share that family; the option reports the number of bins. Electrical checks and netlisting resolve the base name while leaving each original `.MODEL` card untouched, and project asset-use tracking retains every library that contains one of the selected family's bins, including when they are split across files. A real base-name collision with an explicit standalone card suppresses the synthetic family option. Domain tests, the focused Firefox picker/netlist workflow, and the existing-runtime ngspice bin-selection fixture pass; all 34 domain test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. Startup JS+CSS is 231.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.4 remains partial for broader native model-library dialect coverage.

Standalone MOSFET geometry for BSIM3 bins (2026-10-14): Native NMOS/PMOS parts now have optional channel-length and channel-width fields. Positive SPICE dimensions are validated, saved in project v8, and emitted as `L=`/`W=` instance parameters; blank fields preserve ngspice defaults. This makes the imported unsuffixed BSIM3 bin-family option usable with the model's L/W ranges. The domain test covers valid dimensions, invalid zero size, and metadata on an incompatible part; the Firefox workflow imports a two-bin library, selects its family, saves both dimensions, and checks the generated M-device line. The pinned existing-runtime ngspice bin fixture, all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, Firefox workflow, and production startup-size build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.4 remains partial for broader native library dialect coverage.

BSIM3 bin-range preflight (2026-10-14): Circuit Checks now warns when explicit MOSFET L/W dimensions fall outside every complete numeric range in a selected imported native BSIM3 bin family, and lists each bin's covered geometry. This remains advisory; ngspice is still the model-selection authority, and incomplete or expression-based ranges are not guessed. Dimension parsing follows ngspice suffix semantics (`m`/`M` is milli; `meg` is mega), distinct from ELSim's general component-value convention. Domain tests cover in-range/out-of-range selection and suffix interpretation; Firefox verifies the warning from the imported-bin inspector workflow. All 34 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production startup-size build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual §3.2.3](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-43-manual.pdf). M2.4 remains active for broader native model-library coverage.

UI audit and BSIM3 warning readability (2026-10-14): Re-ran the complete 194-workflow engine-independent Firefox UI suite; all passed. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass, with initial JS+CSS at 232.0 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. Circuit Checks now displays imported BSIM3 L/W bin limits in the model's original units and suffixes (for example, `0.5u–2u`) instead of raw meter decimals. The full UI review found no reproducible defect or measured hot path supporting a broad rewrite; prior viewport culling, memoized layers, lazy-loaded tools, and frame-bound playback remain. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

Custom-symbol path bounds and viewport culling (2026-10-14): Native `.elsym` path bounds now follow relative SVG segments and Bézier control points instead of treating every numeric token as an independent coordinate; malformed paths and cumulative coordinates outside the symbol limit are rejected. Viewport culling uses each imported symbol's actual conservative bounds, so artwork extending beyond the old fixed 100-unit margin remains rendered when only part of the symbol is in view. Domain tests cover relative paths, curves, malformed commands, and limits; Firefox verifies a long path remains visible while its anchor is outside the viewport. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the focused Firefox workflow, and the production build pass; startup JS+CSS is 232.9 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This advances M2.4 custom-symbol support.

SVG arc-bound validation follow-up (2026-10-14): Custom-symbol path analysis now bounds rotated elliptical arcs using their corrected radii and ellipse extents, including the radius scaling required when the supplied radii cannot span the endpoints. It also rejects paths without an initial move-to, malformed comma separators, and arc flags other than 0/1. Domain tests cover a rotated eccentric arc and the malformed cases. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the production build, and the focused Firefox custom-symbol workflow pass; startup JS+CSS is 233.4 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

UI audit and imported-symbol fit bounds (2026-10-14): Ran all 194 engine-independent Firefox UI workflows and all 125 tagged UI-smoke workflows; both suites pass, including copy/paste selection, wire editing, waveform controls, responsive layout, accessibility interactions, and custom-symbol import. `npm test`, TypeScript, and the production build pass; startup JS+CSS is 233.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The audit found that long custom-symbol text and pin names could extend beyond the old culling/fit bounds; their rendered extents now contribute to viewport culling and Fit all. No ngspice/WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Existing viewport culling, memoized schematic layers, lazy-loaded editors, and single-frame animation remain in place; no broad rendering rewrite was justified by the passing regressions or measured bundle budget. A five-run Firefox 500-component benchmark measured 43.99 ms median viewport scrub-to-readout and 75.10 ms after Fit all reveals 501 parts; instrumented repeats found only two component groups mutated per scrub, not a 501-part React rerender. Both timings include Playwright input/assertion and mutation-observer overhead, so a browser-frame profile is still needed before changing cursor-update behavior (see PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). Manual assistive-technology review and a second-machine performance baseline remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

UI code audit recheck (2026-10-14): Re-ran all 125 tagged Firefox UI-smoke workflows; all pass, including mixed selection and full-circuit paste/drag, focus and keyboard interactions, plot windows, mobile/desktop layouts, and imported-model UI. `npm test` passes all 34 groups, TypeScript passes, and the production build passes its startup transfer budget at 233.6 KiB gzip (220.7 KiB JS + 12.9 KiB CSS, against a 245 KiB combined limit). Reviewed the current editor, plot, cursor, palette, and animation paths; no new reproducible defect or measured optimization target surfaced. The raw 572.2 kB entry-chunk warning remains a Vite advisory; compressed startup remains within budget, and no evidence supports a code-splitting or renderer rewrite in this pass. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine performance baseline remain open under M4.1/M4.3. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Native ngspice `.TITLE` cards (2026-10-14): Imported model libraries now retain `.TITLE <text>` records and show a source-line compatibility notice that the card replaces the generated deck heading without changing electrical behavior. Empty title cards and `.TITLE` inside a `.SUBCKT` are rejected with line-specific guidance because the directive acts on the whole deck. Domain tests, a Firefox simulation against the existing local WASM runtime, all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Syntax and global title behavior follow the [ngspice manual §2.4.1](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

Native ngspice `.GLOBAL` nodes (2026-10-14): Imported model cards now validate `.GLOBAL` node tokens in both top-level and subcircuit scope, retain scoped records, and display a source-line warning that those nodes connect across the complete circuit hierarchy. Firefox imports a subcircuit-scoped `.GLOBAL`, runs the project through the existing local WASM runtime, and confirms the card remains in the generated deck. Domain tests cover preserved scope, warning location, and malformed node lists; all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual §2.7](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which defines `.GLOBAL` nodes as available across circuit and subcircuit blocks.

UI code and interaction audit (2026-10-15): The 125-case UI-smoke run exposed a test fixture that placed the standalone BSIM3 MOSFET outside the initial canvas viewport, where the properties panel intercepted the click. The fixture now places the part inside the visible canvas; its focused workflow passes in Chromium and Firefox. The remaining 124 UI-smoke workflows passed on the first run. A quick performance benchmark and source review confirmed that memoized schematic layers, viewport culling, lazy advanced editors, and frame-bounded cursor updates are already in place; no additional measured hot path or reproducible product defect justified speculative UI rewrites. Six focused Firefox regressions for mixed part/wire selection, whole-circuit clipboard movement, wire movement, and zoom pass. All 34 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

Transient dual-unit scales (2026-10-15): Voltage and current traces now use independent automatic and manual vertical ranges, with volts labeled on the left and amperes on the right. The ranges are symmetric around zero, keeping the 0 V and 0 A references aligned while allowing milliamp waveforms to remain readable beside volt-level signals. The shared time axis, horizontal navigation, and saved trace colors are unchanged. The Firefox waveform-window workflow compares the rendered V(R1) and I(R1) vertical spans, checks that both unit scales and zero lines are present and aligned, and retains the vertical zoom/pan checks. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Mixed-unit analysis plot audit (2026-10-16): AC noise and DC sweep plots now give each visible unit its own zero-centered vertical range, with aligned zero references and left/right labels for secondary units. A reserved right-axis gutter keeps those labels inside the responsive plot instead of clipping them. Firefox verifies voltage/current noise labels, aligned zero references, and simultaneous voltage/current DC sweep traces; the DC fixture's resistor current is effectively constant, so the test checks the plotted vector and unit scales instead of claiming a visible current swing. All 125 engine-independent Firefox UI smoke workflows pass, along with both focused ngspice plot workflows, all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build. Initial JS+CSS remains within its 245 KiB gzip budget at 234.0 KiB. No WASM or Pages build was run.

Imported model `log10()` defaults and overrides (2026-10-16): Bounded imported `.SUBCKT` parameter expressions now recognize ngspice's built-in base-10 logarithm, and `.FUNC log10(...)` is correctly rejected as a redefinition of a reserved function. Domain/parser regressions cover both paths; Firefox verifies the imported default and editable `log10(1000)` instance override are preserved in the generated netlist. A focused fixture passes against the existing local WASM runtime: the `log10(100)` default produces 2 V and the `log10(1000)` override produces 3 V. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This advances M2.4 native model-library coverage.

UI audit and quick performance recheck (2026-10-16): All 194 engine-independent Firefox workflows pass, including selection/copy-paste, wire topology and hover, drag/resize/zoom, responsive layouts, accessibility focus, dialogs, imported-model editing, plot controls, and archive/export flows. The version-11 quick domain benchmark (two warmups, seven measured runs on the documented i7-12700F host) reports 5,000-resistor chain connectivity/netlisting/full-check p95 of 52/71/61 ms; 5,000-resistor/4,900-wire grid 51/73/66 ms; and 5,000-instance hierarchy 97/150/124 ms. The million-sample plot reduction p95 is 1.08 ms. All stay within current domain budgets; the benchmark skips solver and browser rendering. No reproducible UI defect or measured UI hot path was found, so no broad rendering rewrite was made. Manual assistive-technology review and second-machine profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

Imported SW/CSW parameter stepping (2026-10-16): Numeric parameter sweeps now include imported voltage-controlled `SW` and current-controlled `CSW` switch models. Model-default parsing accepts ngspice engineering suffixes with unit decorations such as `IT=1mA`, and the parameter editor derives a useful doubled endpoint while retaining its displayed unit. Domain tests verify both model families generate `altermod` cases; Firefox with the existing local WASM runtime imports a CSW card, sweeps IT from 1 mA to 8 mA, runs both transient cases, and confirms distinct result labels. This follows [ngspice manual §3.3.16](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf). All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the focused Firefox/ngspice workflow, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Firefox engine-suite follow-up (2026-10-16): The full existing-WASM Firefox run passed 105 of 110 workflows. Four failures were outdated lesson assertions that expected prediction feedback before the learner answered; the tests now follow the prediction interaction and check the revealed calculation. One RLC cursor-readout assertion also needed to wait for the chart's accessible numeric readout after focus. The corrected resistor-power, transformer, lamp-power, and RLC workflows pass in focused Firefox runs; the PWL sweep failure from the full run passed when isolated. The unrelated full suite has not yet been rerun. No application behavior, ngspice binaries, or Pages build changed.

Firefox engine-suite completion (2026-10-22): Fixed two remaining test setup races: the hierarchy view assertion now waits until persisted zoom agrees with the visible zoom control, and the current-source PWL sweep fixture is installed after the app's initial project hydration. The full local Firefox engine suite now passes 110/110 using the existing ngspice WASM. No application runtime behavior, WASM build, or Pages workflow changed.

Firefox UI audit and 5,000-part performance repeat (2026-10-22): All 125 tagged engine-independent Firefox UI-smoke workflows pass, along with all 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build. Initial JS+CSS is 234.0 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. Three repeats of the 5,000-part/4,900-wire Firefox benchmark measured selection median/p95 of 8–13/18–24 ms, select-all-to-frame of 51–55 ms, zoom median of 35–50 ms, and keyboard navigation median of 18–21 ms. Render time was 914–993 ms versus a prior 761 ms single run while the host load average was 8.02, so the difference is inconclusive; repeat under idle or second-machine conditions before changing rendering. No reproducible UI defect or measured interaction hot path was found. M4.1 manual assistive-technology review and M4.3 second-machine profiling remain open. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

Native ngspice subcircuit parameter operators (2026-10-22): The bounded imported `.SUBCKT` default/override validator now accepts ngspice arithmetic, comparison, logical, and ternary operators, including `**`, `%`, integer division `\\`, `&&`, `||`, `!`, and `condition ? yes : no`. Expressions remain size/depth limited and are preserved for ngspice evaluation; the UI never evaluates them. Unsupported word aliases and malformed operator sequences are rejected. Domain tests cover the grammar and a focused Firefox/WASM circuit verifies a conditional parameter override produces the expected 2 V operating point. Syntax follows the [ngspice manual, §2.9.5](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; M2.4 remains active for broader imported-library dialect coverage.

Firefox UI audit and large-schematic profile (2026-10-23): All 125 tagged engine-independent Firefox UI-smoke workflows pass. The run covers selection and clipboard movement, wire interactions, plot-window actions, focus/keyboard behavior, responsive layout, and import/model dialogs. Two Firefox large-project performance workflows pass: for 5,000 parts, editor readiness was 635 ms and selection median/p95 was 13/16 ms; for 5,000 parts and 4,900 wires, render readiness was 755 ms, selection median/p95 was 6/10 ms, select-all to frame was 44 ms, and zoom to two frames was 41 ms median/57 ms maximum. Firefox UI re-audit (2026-10-23): All 125 `@ui-smoke` workflows pass in Firefox, including the reported whole-circuit copy/paste selection and mixed group-movement cases. Firefox UI maintenance recheck (2026-10-23): Re-ran the 125 `@ui-smoke` workflows on Firefox; all passed, including selection/copy-paste, mixed group movement, responsive layouts, plots, dialogs, keyboard/focus, and imported-model controls. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the quick performance benchmark pass. Imported stochastic subcircuit overrides (2026-10-23): When an imported `.SUBCKT` uses a random function (`gauss`, `agauss`, `unif`, or `aunif`) as a formal default and an instance explicitly overrides that parameter, the netlister now emits a private instance-specific copy with that override promoted to the copied model's default. The user-owned model source remains intact, generated source mappings point back to original model lines, and instances without such overrides continue using the original model. Conditional-branch model declarations are left unchanged because moving them would change their scope. Domain tests cover specialization and source mapping; the existing local ngspice WASM fixture verifies the explicit 2 kΩ override produces 1.666667 V despite the random 1 kΩ default; a Firefox browser regression exercises the same flow through the editor. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. M2.2 continues; imported library dialect coverage remains incomplete.

Hierarchical stochastic override follow-up (2026-10-23): Tracing the previous fix through the reusable-sheet netlist flattener showed that child-sheet-generated model copies were omitted from the flattened model library, leaving those `X` instances with undefined model names. The flattener now carries generated imported-model copies into the root deck and preserves their source filename/line mapping. A hierarchy netlist regression checks generated X/model names and source mapping; a second fixture confirms ngspice solves the child-sheet override to 1.666667 V. Both stochastic-override fixtures pass against the existing local WASM runtime; all 34 unit groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild, Pages workflow, or full browser suite was needed.

Generated-model name collision guard (2026-10-23): Private stochastic-subcircuit copies now reserve names against imported `.SUBCKT` declarations as well as device models, references, and nodes. A netlist regression imports a user-defined subcircuit matching the generated base name and verifies ELSim uses a unique suffixed name while preserving the user definition. The WASM operating-point fixture now includes that same name collision and still solves to 1.666667 V. TypeScript, all 34 unit groups, and both stochastic override fixtures pass against the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Plot and playback cursor teardown (2026-10-23): When the last plot or schematic cursor subscriber unmounts while a frame-batched cursor notification is queued, the analysis and shared simulation cursor stores now cancel the pending animation frame and discard its stale notification. If another cursor subscriber remains, the shared update stays scheduled. Unit regressions cover both teardown and remaining-subscriber behavior. TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass; Firefox UI execution was blocked in this restricted shell because Vite could not bind its local test port. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

Firefox UI audit follow-up (2026-10-23): With local loopback access granted, all 125 `@ui-smoke` workflows pass on Firefox against the current app, covering editor selection/clipboard, model and symbol import, lessons, hierarchy editing, keyboard/focus, phone layout, and deferred editors. This closes the earlier test-environment restriction for the UI audit; no additional reproducible UI defect surfaced. The two cursor-store teardown fixes remain covered by unit tests. No WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

No WASM rebuild, Pages workflow, or broad UI rewrite was needed.

M4.3 Firefox worker-memory profile (2026-10-23): Repeated the million-sample Firefox transient with `VITE_ELSIM_PROFILE=1` and the existing WASM runtime. Browser result-ready time was 11,027 ms. Firefox page-heap values remain unavailable, and this capacity is not a peak browser-process measurement; the second-hardware baseline and peak-memory evidence remain open. No WASM rebuild, Pages workflow, or app-code change ran.


M4.3 Firefox memory sampling tool (2026-10-23): Added `tools/benchmark-firefox-memory.py` and `npm run benchmark:browser:memory`. The Linux-only runner reuses the existing ngspice WASM, launches the million-sample Firefox benchmark with worker profiling, and samples Firefox plus descendants from `/proc` every 100 ms. It reports separate pre-worker-profile and post-worker-profile PSS/RSS maxima, WASM linear-memory capacity, and explicitly states what each phase includes. An opt-in two-second test hold keeps the plot UI alive for the post-solve samples; ordinary browser benchmark tests are unchanged. Two phase-sampled validation runs reported 990.4/1,081.6 MiB PSS pre-profile and 627.8/687.0 MiB post-profile for one/eight plot windows respectively. The sample supports repeatable measurement but not attribution or cross-hardware acceptance. Python syntax, TypeScript, and the focused profile runs pass; no WASM build or Pages workflow ran.

M2.3 quoted sheet-parameter values (2026-10-23): Reusable ELSim sheet parameter defaults and per-instance overrides now have Firefox and domain coverage for double-quoted string values as well as numeric and expression values. The inspector saves the quoted override and generated `.SUBCKT`/`X... PARAMS:` cards preserve the text and whitespace. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and three focused Firefox runs pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 imported-subcircuit inspector lookup (2026-10-23): Selecting an imported IC now resolves its model through a memoized, case-insensitive name map instead of scanning the complete subcircuit array on every App render. The index preserves the previous first-match behavior for duplicate names. All 34 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox IC inspector workflow pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages run.

UI performance audit follow-up (2026-10-23): Reviewed the main UI render paths and existing profiling evidence before making changes. Imported voltage/current switch animation sampling previously scanned each selected model's parameter list to read threshold and hysteresis values; it now uses a memoized case-insensitive model-parameter index, avoiding repeated linear scans during cursor-driven rendering. The focused Firefox switch animation workflow passes, along with the full 130-case Firefox UI-smoke suite, all 34 unit-test groups, and TypeScript. The quick 5,000-part/4,900-wire domain benchmark reports 0.017 ms median wire viewport query, 0.015 ms median visible-network current-flow sampling, and cached plot reduction lookups below 0.001 ms median after their initial reduction. These measurements do not justify speculative changes to established viewport, plot, or wire-animation architecture. No WASM rebuild, production build, or GitHub Pages workflow ran; cross-device profiling remains open under M4.3.

M2.4 imported current-switch animation acceptance (2026-10-23): Added a Firefox/ngspice workflow that imports a user CSW model with a 6 mA threshold, assigns it to the example switch, runs the circuit, and scrubs solved samples. The example's sensed current is 5 mA, so its state must stay open under the imported threshold; the regression would fail if animation fell back to the built-in 2 mA value. It passes against the existing local ngspice WASM runtime without rebuilding the engine. The imported SW threshold workflow and all 130 Firefox UI-smoke cases also pass on this revision; M2.4's broader native model-library coverage remains active.

M4 UI accessibility and performance audit (2026-10-23): Extended Firefox focus coverage to keyboard-tabbed wire segments, net labels, and draggable wire endpoints, checking their SVG-painted focus indicators. The full 199-case engine-independent Firefox suite passes, including copied-group selection/movement, wire tools, plot windows, responsive layout, and dialog workflows; the focused focus-indicator regression, TypeScript, and all 34 unit groups pass. The quick 5,000-part/4,900-wire benchmark reports 0.016 ms median viewport wire query (0.018 ms p95), 1.039 ms median reduction for a million-sample plot (1.137 ms p95), and 1.516 ms p95 for a full 5,000-part/4,900-wire group-drag model update. Together with the recent Firefox large-schematic browser profiles, these results do not show a stable editor bottleneck warranting a broad rewrite. No app runtime code changed and no WASM or Pages build ran; manual screen-reader review and second-machine/browser-memory profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

M2.4 native XSPICE transfer-form diagnostics (2026-10-23): Imported native ngspice subcircuit libraries now receive source-line warnings when E-source LAPLACE or FREQ forms require XSPICE `s_xfer`/`xfer` modules omitted from ELSim's browser runtime. Comments and ordinary E-source VALUE forms do not trigger the warning; original source is retained, and the UI links directly to the affected source line. Domain and focused Firefox import regressions cover the forms and no-false-positive cases. This documents an engine support boundary; the simulation engine and WASM artifact are unchanged. See the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf) for the documented transfer-model forms.

M4.3 Firefox large-result memory comparison (2026-10-23): Repeated the existing 1,000,130-sample Firefox benchmark with eight plot windows/32 rendered traces and one plot window/four traces. Both runs pass. WASM linear-memory capacity is 177 MiB in both; whole Firefox process-tree peak PSS is 1.03 GiB with eight windows and 1.09 GiB with one. Result-ready times are 11.0 s and 10.0 s. These are single-run measurements and the initial sampler did not isolate the solve phase, so they do not implicate plot-window count as the memory source. ELSim-only allocation attribution, phase-specific repeat-run distributions, and a second hardware profile remain open under M4.3. The existing local WASM runtime was reused; no build or Pages deployment ran.

M4.3 Firefox memory sampler phase attribution (2026-10-23): Updated the Linux Firefox sampler to require chronological markers for page startup, pre-run, ngspice worker profile, and result-ready. A validated one-window million-sample run recorded 559 MiB startup PSS, a 990 MiB peak during solver and result transfer, 631 MiB during main-thread result validation/transfer, and 620 MiB during plot checks and cursor scrubbing; WASM linear-memory capacity was 177 MiB. The run completed in 11.0 s and passed with no missing phase markers. This isolates the high peak to the solve/result-creation interval rather than multi-window rendering, but PSS still includes Firefox/Playwright and does not attribute allocation to ELSim or ngspice internals. Python syntax, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox benchmark pass. Repeat-run distributions and second-hardware profiling remain open; no WASM rebuild or Pages run occurred.

M2.4 native E-source logic-gate diagnostics (2026-10-23): Imported ngspice subcircuit libraries now receive source-line warnings for E-source `AND`, `OR`, `NAND`, and `NOR` forms, which ngspice rewrites through the XSPICE `multi_input_pwl` code model omitted from ELSim's browser runtime. Comments and the native E-source `TABLE` and `VALUE` forms are not flagged; original source is preserved, with an action to review each affected line. Unit and focused Firefox importer regressions cover all four gates and the no-false-positive cases. The solver and WASM artifact are unchanged. See the [ngspice manual section on E-source logic gates](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf) for the documented rewrite.

M2.4 native digital U-device import guidance (2026-10-23): Imported ngspice model libraries now flag recognized digital U-device primitives (gates, arrays, flip-flops/latches, delay lines, LOGICEXP, and PINDLY) with their source line. The manual requires these devices to be inside a `.SUBCKT` and ngspice PS compatibility mode; ELSim's worker does not select PS mode for native library imports. URC transmission-line records and comments are not flagged. Unit and focused Firefox import regressions verify primitive recognition, no URC false positive, and source-line navigation. This is compatibility guidance rather than a claim that these digital devices are simulated in the browser build; source and WASM are unchanged. See the [ngspice manual's U-device requirements](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

UI interaction and performance audit (2026-10-23): Audited plot pointer handling and completed all 202 engine-independent Playwright workflows in Firefox, plus the three focused Firefox ngspice plot workflows using the existing local runtime. Plot drag-pan cancels and restores its starting range when the browser loses focus, drops queued movement on cancellation, flushes the final frame-batched movement on pointer release, and clears drag state, listeners, and pending animation frames on unmount. A focused Firefox/ngspice regression verifies blur cancellation and that a later drag starts cleanly. TypeScript, all 34 unit-test groups, and the production build pass; initial JS+CSS transfer is 236.0 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The quick benchmark passes; at 5,000 wire-grid sections, cached plot reduction p95 is 1.143 ms, connectivity p95 33.764 ms, netlisting p95 58.032 ms, and full circuit-check p95 55.991 ms on the documented host. Measurements did not identify a safe broad UI rewrite target; focused changes remain preferable to speculative restructuring. No WASM rebuild, Pages deployment, or GitHub Actions run was needed. Manual assistive-technology coverage and second-machine/browser-memory profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

Three focused Firefox workflows verify path order, steps 1→2, cancellation preservation, successful continuation, selection reset, and visibility without horizontal overflow at 390 px; TypeScript and all 34 unit-test groups pass. M0.3 native parity command (2026-10-23): Added `npm run test:wasm:parity`, a required-native gate for the four named M0.3 reference circuits: RC transient, diode operating point, RLC AC, and a small `.SUBCKT`. It requires `NGSPICE_NATIVE`, verifies the executable responds to `--version`, and invokes the existing numeric row comparison for only those fixtures; the gate cannot silently skip parity. Unit coverage checks the required executable and exact fixture set. All 36 unit groups and the four local WASM fixtures pass. Firefox UI audit (2026-10-23): The complete engine-independent `@ui-smoke` selection passes all 134 workflows in Firefox. The focused `@engine-smoke` selection passes all 14 workflows against the existing local ngspice runtime, without rebuilding WASM. Five repetitions each of the three full-schematic copy/paste and immediate mixed-group drag workflows pass (15/15), with assertions that selected wire IDs are only the pasted IDs and that copied wires move while originals stay fixed. M4.3 ngspice result-file lifetime (2026-10-23): The browser worker now unlinks each binary raw result from Emscripten's virtual filesystem immediately after copying it into a standalone buffer for parsing. Parameter-sweep files are released one at a time instead of remaining resident through parsing of every case. This reduces overlap between raw-file storage and parsed typed vectors; it does not change result vectors, plots, solver behavior, or the WASM binary. The raw-file cleanup unit regression, all 36 unit-test groups, all local WASM fixtures, TypeScript, the production bundle and a focused Firefox/ngspice first-circuit workflow pass. No controlled whole-process memory comparison has been run, so no numeric reduction is claimed. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 memory-profile phase accuracy (2026-10-23): The Linux Firefox process sampler now takes a sample at each logged phase boundary, so short result-validation phases no longer disappear when multiple markers arrive between 100 ms polls. A million-sample Firefox run after raw-file cleanup completed with 1,000,130 samples and a 177.2 MiB WASM memory capacity; process-tree PSS peaks were 1,039.1 MiB during solver/result transfer, 656.7 MiB during validation/transfer, and 630.1 MiB during plotting/cursor interaction. This single run verifies the phase recording and workflow, not an ELSim-only memory reduction. Details and limits are recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; second-hardware and controlled before/after profiles remain open.

M1.4 large waveform CSV responsiveness (2026-10-23): CSV download now formats selected traces in 8,192-row chunks and yields to the browser between chunks. It passes the chunks directly to `Blob` instead of retaining a per-sample row-string array and joining a second full-file string. The app announces preparation for large exports. Exact CSV output is preserved for shared and distinct axes, mixed units, and complex AC vectors; Firefox's first-circuit journey verifies the actual download. All 36 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production size-budget build pass. Chunking reduces temporary row-object accumulation but the final CSV still occupies browser-managed Blob storage; no peak-memory benchmark is claimed. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.1 installed-app update flow (2026-10-23): When a new service worker has installed while an older worker controls the page, ELSim now shows an accessible update notice with Reload to update and Later actions. Accepting sends the explicit skip-waiting message and reloads once on controller change; the built worker retains the previous cache until activation. The app checks for updates on focus, when connectivity returns, when the tab becomes visible, and hourly while visible. The local offline PWA browser test changes the served worker script, triggers the focus check, accepts a real waiting-worker update, verifies the app reloads, and confirms the saved RLC project remains open. Chromium and Firefox pass the production offline workflow, including offline simulation and update activation. No WASM compile, Pages test, or hosted deployment ran.
M4 waveform vertical-view persistence (2026-10-23): Floating waveform windows now save manual vertical axis ranges in project plot settings alongside horizontal ranges. Transient voltage/current axes persist independently; AC magnitude/phase and noise axes, and DC sweep unit axes, also restore their saved view. Reset returns axes to automatic range and removes their persisted overrides. Existing saved projects remain compatible because the new field is optional. Firefox regressions verify transient axes both persist across reruns and reset together, and AC magnitude/phase keep separate ranges when switching modes; both pass against the existing local WASM runtime. The review caught and fixed two state issues: simultaneous transient axes updates now merge by unit, and AC mode changes no longer discard saved scales. Project v8's schema now describes the optional axis ranges, while runtime validation rejects unknown analysis keys, oversized unit maps, non-finite bounds, and unordered ranges. All 36 unit-test groups (641 assertions), TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and the production build pass (237.3 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against the 245 KiB budget). The full 134-case Firefox UI-smoke suite passes. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4 UI audit and engine-smoke stabilization (2026-10-23): Re-ran the full 134-case Firefox engine-independent UI-smoke suite; all pass, including selection/copy-paste wire regressions, responsive layouts, floating plots, keyboard controls, dialogs, and model inspectors. The quick performance benchmark and production build pass; initial JavaScript/CSS transfer is 237.4 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The 14-case Firefox engine-smoke run passed 13 workflows and exposed a test setup race in AC plot persistence: the test edited/simulated the starter before waiting for its initial autosave, then dereferenced an absent storage key. M4 UI keyboard-handler state audit (2026-10-23): The global shortcut listener also captured asynchronous simulation state through `startSimulation()`. M2.2 native behavioral PWL coverage (2026-10-23): Native ngspice `pwl(input, x0, y0, ...)` is now accepted in bounded schematic B-source expressions, including declared parameter names among its points. Parser and generated-netlist regressions cover the dialect boundary and untouched native syntax. The existing local WASM fixture verifies interpolation using `.param` point values and lower-end extrapolation numerically; all 36 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass without a runtime rebuild. M2.2 behavioral-source inspector guidance (2026-10-23): The B-source inspector now shows a copyable native `pwl(V(vin), 0, 0, 1, 5)` example and explains that ngspice extends the end segment beyond listed points; it distinguishes imported LTspice `table()` endpoint clamps. A focused Firefox UI-smoke regression verifies that guidance is visible when editing a behavioral source. The workflow passes against the local dev server; no WASM or Pages build ran.

M2.2 native Boolean expression semantics (2026-10-23): Native B-source validation now accepts ngspice `&&`, `||`, and unary `!`, passes them through unchanged, and rejects LTspice-only single-character `&`, `|`, and unary `~` in the native dialect. Imported LTspice Boolean operators retain their 0.5-threshold translation. Generated ideal logic-gate expressions now use native `&&`/`||`/`!`. Unit tests cover dialect separation; the existing local WASM fixture confirms ngspice treats a nonzero 0.4 V value as true, unlike a 0.5-threshold LTspice boolean; Firefox solves all four input truth-table states through the app. All 36 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused solver/browser tests pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages build ran.

M4 selection-store fast path and regression coverage (2026-10-23): The large-schematic per-part selection/focus store now returns before allocating a replacement `Set` when a render effect reports the same selected IDs and roving-tab-stop ID (for example, after wire-only selection changes). Direct tests verify only rows whose state bits change are notified, identical updates stay silent, and unsubscribed rows receive no callbacks. The store is compiled and tested by the standard runner; all 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM or browser build ran.

M4 engine-free preview setup guidance (2026-10-23): Replaced the missing-runtime toast fragments with a keyboard-accessible dialog explaining the GitHub Pages/editor-only behavior, local runtime prerequisites, pinned ngspice checkout, copyable build command, output location, reload step, and the full build guide. Run, status-panel, and mobile/desktop Setup actions share the dialog and restore focus on close. The engine-free Firefox smoke regression checks the guidance and both entry points. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, and the production web build pass; the focused browser smoke test could not start because this environment rejects local TCP binding with `listen EPERM`. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M4.3 current quick profile (2026-10-23): Re-ran the engine-free domain/plot benchmark on the documented host and added version-11 measurements to `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire model update is 1.29 ms p95, one-million-sample plot reduction is 1.04 ms p95, and full circuit checks are 61.5 ms p95; these remain under the current 500 ms domain budget. The viewport wire-animation sampler processes 20 visible wires in about 0.015 ms median versus 16.6 ms for all 4,900 networks. No broader editor rewrite is indicated by these domain results; browser rendering and second-hardware verification remain separate M4.3 work. No WASM workload ran.

M4.1 Firefox UI smoke verification (2026-10-23): Ran the full 139-case engine-independent `@ui-smoke` suite in Firefox against the current worktree; all 139 pass, including first-use/onboarding, local-runtime guidance, schematic selection and wire editing, copy/paste, responsive layout, imported-library dialogs, keyboard/focus accessibility, and advanced-editor lazy loading. M4.1 local-runtime dialog keyboard and phone-layout follow-up (2026-10-23): Extended the engine-free preview Firefox regression to verify the setup dialog's first focus target, Tab/Shift+Tab containment, Escape dismissal, and focus restoration to desktop Setup, Run, and phone-width Setup triggers. At 390 px, the dialog stays inside the viewport and the page has no horizontal overflow. The focused Firefox workflow and TypeScript pass.
