M4.1 setup-dialog accessible description (2026-10-23): The focused Firefox smoke test now verifies that the setup dialog exposes its main explanation as the accessible description and that the command-copy action has a discoverable name. The focused Firefox workflow passes; the dialog's keyboard and phone-layout assertions remain in the same regression. Manual screen-reader review remains open.

M4.3 large-editor Firefox profile repeat (2026-10-23): Re-ran the focused 5,000-part/4,900-wire Firefox benchmark; 64 parts and 551 wires mounted, render readiness was 767 ms, selection median/p95 11/13 ms, select-all 46 ms, zoom median/max 40/56 ms, and arrow navigation median/max 20/31 ms. This aligns with existing single-host measurements and shows no new interaction bottleneck; no speculative editor rewrite was made. No WASM workload or Pages deployment ran.

M1.1 wire/component selection continuity (2026-10-23): The Firefox selection regression now retains the actual resistor SVG node and verifies it is not unmounted/recreated when selection switches to a wire and back to the component. Existing checks still verify the wire tool and inspector remain unchanged and the component can be moved after wire selection. The focused Firefox regression passes; this protects against selection changes that remount or visually blink the schematic layer.

M2.2 native/LTspice B-source dialect boundary (2026-10-23): Native schematic B-sources now accept the documented ngspice function set and `pwl()` while rejecting unsupported LTspice-only functions such as `if()`, `limit()`, and `table()` instead of translating them under native semantics. LTspice imports retain the explicit helper translations and `%` remainder behavior. Native inverter generation now uses ngspice Boolean `!` rather than the LTspice-only `inv()` helper. The 37 unit groups, TypeScript, a focused Firefox ngspice truth-table simulation, and the 137-case UI-smoke suite pass with the existing local runtime; no WASM rebuild ran. See the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf) for native B-source syntax.

M2.4 native digital U-device diagnostics (2026-10-23): Corrected imported-library guidance for PSpice-style `U` digital primitives. The ngspice manual says these devices require PS compatibility mode and are translated to XSPICE digital models; ELSim’s WASM configuration omits the compiled XSPICE code-model directory. A direct probe of the existing local runtime with `ngbehavior=psa` confirmed the deck is recognized in PS mode but fails because its generated digital model is unavailable. The importer now explains that switching PS mode alone cannot make these models run in this browser build and points to the supported alternatives. Unit coverage, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox import/source-line workflow pass; the runtime was not rebuilt.

M2.4 native BSIM4 model diagnostics (2026-10-23): Imported native `.MODEL NMOS/PMOS ... LEVEL=54` cards now receive a source-line warning because BSIM4 is omitted from the browser ngspice build. A direct run using the existing local WASM returned a fatal `strtod` parse error for a BSIM4 level-54 card. Firefox verifies the warning links to the card’s first line when `LEVEL` is on a continuation, while unit tests confirm PMOS detection and no false positives for BSIM3 level 49, non-MOS level 54, and other level values. All 37 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox import workflow pass without rebuilding WASM.

M4.3 imported symbol culling metadata reuse (2026-10-23): When recomputing schematic culling margins after part or symbol changes, repeated instances of the same imported symbol now reuse one calculated symbol-bounds margin per asset instead of rescanning that symbol's geometry for every placed instance. Per-part margin values, spatial-index bounds, and visible rendering behavior are unchanged. TypeScript, the production build, and all 143 Firefox `@ui-smoke` workflows pass after the change; the initial transfer is 237.5 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. This removes duplicate geometry work during index rebuilds; no overall frame-time gain is claimed.

M4.3 Firefox performance and memory profile recheck (2026-10-23): All seven browser benchmark workflows pass in Firefox using the existing local WASM runtime; no engine build was run. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire sheet mounts 64 parts and 551 wires and renders in 766 ms, with 6/11 ms selection median/p95, 47 ms select-all, and 36/57 ms zoom median/max. The million-sample/eight-window profile reaches 1,000,130 samples in 11.04 s; worker timing attributes 9.762 s to ngspice and 40 ms to result processing. The Linux Firefox process-tree sample peaks at 1,064.3 MiB PSS during combined solve/result transfer, and WASM linear-memory capacity is 177.2 MiB. These are single-machine observations, not budgets or before/after gains; process PSS/RSS includes Firefox and descendants, and page-heap APIs are unavailable. The 40-trace test's 1.333 s all-toggle aggregate deliberately waits a frame after each of 80 operations; isolated toggles reach two frames in 24–34 ms, so it is not an app-side 1.3 s stall. A second-machine profile, repeated memory distributions, and an optimization-level comparison remain open. The current environment lacks `emcc` and a clean pinned ngspice source checkout, so no `-O3` build was attempted.

M4 UI/editor audit recheck (2026-10-23): The current engine-independent Firefox `@ui-smoke` suite passes 141/141 workflows; all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and the production web build pass. Initial JS+CSS is 236.7 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget (223.3 KiB JS and 13.4 KiB CSS); keep this margin in mind when adding eager UI code. The Vite raw-entry-size advisory remains, but no renderer rewrite is justified by the current 5,000-part benchmark, whose Firefox interaction timings remain consistent with prior runs. No WASM build or Pages check was run.

M4 UI maintenance and solver-backed waveform recheck (2026-10-23): The current Firefox `@ui-smoke` suite passes 143/143 workflows, and all 14 Firefox `@engine-smoke` workflows pass against the existing local runtime. The 501-component/1,001-vector animation benchmark passes with offscreen animation skipped; it measured 42 ms for a viewport scrub and 73 ms after fitting all components, while the next animation-frame callback followed input in 15/18 ms. The production build is 237.0 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against the 245 KiB budget; the minified main entry still triggers Vite’s 500 kB advisory. No repeatable defect or measured performance result justifies a broad renderer rewrite; continue with focused fixes and keep eager-code budget in view. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 native expression measurements (2026-10-23): Scalar transient measurements now accept bounded, validated ngspice expressions for MAX, MIN, PP, AVG, RMS, and INTEG. Node, parameter, and component references are checked; `I()` expressions are limited to voltage-source branch currents, matching ngspice’s B-source expression support. The generated `.meas ... par('...')` path and the equivalent `.control` `let`/`meas` path are covered by unit tests and an existing-runtime WASM regression; the pinned WASM returns a numeric power expression result. Unitless expression results are labeled without guessing units. LTspice export blocks with an explicit message instead of emitting an invalid vector. TypeScript and all 38 unit-test groups pass. The focused browser run could not start because this execution environment rejects local TCP binds (`listen EPERM`); no ngspice rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 native behavioral current diagnostics (2026-10-23): Circuit checks and netlist generation now catch native B-source `I()` references to resistor/device currents before ngspice runs and explain that expression currents require voltage-source branch references. Imported LTspice-dialect expressions retain their compatibility handling. Regression coverage verifies supported voltage-source references, rejected resistor references, and the LTspice dialect boundary. TypeScript and all 38 unit-test groups pass; no WASM rebuild or browser/Pages run was needed.

M2.3 nested controlled-source current probes (2026-10-23): Corrected hierarchy vector naming for nested CCCS and CCVS components, which were previously emitted using the VCCS `G` prefix. A focused fixture runs nested VCVS, VCCS, CCCS, and CCVS devices through the existing ngspice WASM and checks each scoped branch-current vector and numeric value; hierarchy unit tests cover all four vector names. No WASM rebuild was needed.

M4 UI code and bundle review (2026-10-23): Re-ran the 143-case Firefox `@ui-smoke` suite, all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build; all pass. The build reports a 583.76 kB raw main entry and an initial JS+CSS transfer of 237.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. The largest current rendered source is `App.tsx`; the model-library parser is also in the initial dependency graph through core project validation, so making only the importer or clipboard lazy would not defer that parser. A later M4.3 follow-up defers the rarely opened reusable-sheet settings dialog behind a lazy component boundary; its bundle and hierarchy interaction checks are recorded below. The main inspector remains part of the regular workspace, so moving it would fetch that code immediately on page load and would not defer work. Do not use manual chunk reassignment as a substitute for deferring code. No WASM rebuild or Pages check ran.

M4.3 culling-margin allocation (2026-10-23): `CircuitPartLayer` calculates per-part bounds directly and caches imported-symbol margins in a lazy `WeakMap` keyed by parsed symbol object. This removes the temporary one-entry-per-part map on parts-array edits and avoids calculating bounds for unplaced imported symbols. Spatial-index bounds and rendered geometry remain unchanged. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, the production build, the Firefox 5,000-part/10,000-label group-drag benchmark, and focused imported-symbol/fit-view workflows pass. The latest 5,000-part/10,000-label run measured 703 ms initial readiness and 24 ms group-drag-to-frame; 5,000 parts mounted with viewport culling intact. Timings vary between runs and do not establish a before/after speedup. No WASM rebuild or Pages check ran.

M4.3 large-result CSV selection (2026-10-23): Waveform CSV export now uses a `Set` for selected trace membership instead of scanning the selected-ID array for every result vector. Result order and CSV content are unchanged. A synthetic 10,000-vector/5,000-selected-ID comparison measured 55.96 ms median for the previous repeated `Array.includes` lookup and 0.28 ms for `Set.has`; this covers only selection filtering, not end-to-end CSV generation. Firefox's first-circuit voltage/current CSV workflow passes with the existing local WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages check ran.

M4.3 RLC lesson render-path cleanup (2026-10-23): The RLC resonance trace lookup now runs only while that lesson is active and is memoized to the current result snapshot, avoiding a full trace-list scan on unrelated App renders. M4.3 selected net-label lookup (2026-10-23): The selected label used by the properties inspector is now resolved through a memoized ID map instead of scanning the full label array on every App render. Map construction is limited to label-list changes; lookup is constant-time for selection and inspector rendering. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; focused Firefox net-label copy/selection workflow passes. No general frame-time gain is claimed. No WASM rebuild or Pages check ran.

M4.3 net-label point lookup (2026-10-23): Schematic label placement and wire-context-menu add/edit now query the existing 64-unit point broadphase, then apply the same exact <1-unit distance check. The selected-label inspector lookup is indexed as recorded above. TypeScript and focused Firefox label context/selection workflows pass; the full production build is being verified. M4.3 UI review continuation (2026-10-23): Re-ran all 145 engine-independent Firefox `@ui-smoke` workflows; all pass, including selection/clipboard movement, viewport interactions, dialogs, plots, and responsive layouts. M4.3 shared schematic ID maps (2026-10-23): App and the memoized schematic now share the same parts-by-ID and net-labels-by-ID maps for their identical immutable active-sheet arrays, avoiding duplicate full-array map allocations on edits. M4.3 analysis-setting source scans (2026-10-23): `validateAnalysisSettings` now collects independent sources once and reuses that list for transient waveform validation and AC/noise/DC source checks. A transient validation that previously filtered the full part array five times now performs one full-array source collection and filters only that smaller source list; operating-point validation skips the collection. Validation behavior is unchanged across all 38 unit-test groups, focused Firefox invalid-pulse/source-waveform workflows, and production build. No wall-time improvement is claimed; no WASM or Pages build ran.

M4.3 saved waveform trace restoration (2026-10-23): After a solver result arrives, saved trace IDs are now matched against one `Set` of returned vector IDs instead of rescanning all vectors for each saved probe. Saved trace order and fallback selection behavior are unchanged. The Firefox edit-and-rerun regression confirms selected probes survive a new run; all 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass, and the broader Firefox UI and 14 engine-smoke workflows pass on this checkout. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 waveform probe trace lookup (2026-10-23): Adding a probe now finds its trace, gathers matching sweep vectors, and collects one ground-reference vector per sweep in a single pass through the available vectors. This replaces repeated `find`/`filter` scans while preserving trace order and the existing synthesized-ground behavior. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, and the focused Firefox/ngspice probe workflow pass; it also verifies a grounded wire adds the synthesized `V(0)` trace. The full local Firefox UI and engine-smoke suites passed earlier this session. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 AC/DC plot range scan reuse (2026-10-23): AC magnitude/phase/noise and DC sweep plots now reuse the extrema-preserving sample indices for exact axis bounds and SVG polyline generation. This avoids a separate full-vector range scan whenever the visible range changes, while preserving exact finite-sample extrema and the existing ~900-point render cap. A million-sample transformed-range regression compares sampled bounds with the full mapped range; TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, Firefox AC/DC solver-backed cursor/zoom workflows, and the production build pass. The build remains within its configured initial-download budget at 236.0 KiB gzip (245 KiB limit). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 model-source inspector paging (2026-10-23): Imported model-library source review now renders at most 80 lines at once, keeps original line numbers, and can jump directly to a diagnostic line. The viewer counts lines and extracts only its visible page instead of splitting the entire source into a large temporary array; this bounds both DOM size and temporary line-string allocation for supported multi-megabyte libraries. A Firefox regression covers a 1,203-line file and direct navigation; five focused source-review/diagnostic workflows, TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.1 full UI/performance recheck (2026-10-23): The engine-independent Firefox UI-smoke suite passes all 144 workflows, including dialogs, recovery, keyboard/focus, imported-library source navigation, wire and component selection, copy/paste, and responsive layouts. This keeps static footer branding out of the live region and avoids nesting changing status messages under a second live container. M4.1 lazy setup-dialog feedback (2026-10-23): The first click on Setup now exposes a polite, busy status while the lazy dialog chunk loads instead of leaving the user without feedback. Escape closes the loading state and restores focus to the Setup trigger; after the deferred chunk completes, reopening the dialog works normally. A Firefox regression deliberately delays the chunk to verify loading semantics, cancellation, focus restoration, and subsequent open. M4.3 waveform visibility optimization check (2026-10-23): A per-trace React memo boundary did not improve measured Firefox visibility toggles for 40 plotted traces: four click-to-two-frame samples were 23/34/33/32 ms before and 24/33/33/32 ms after. The speculative change was discarded. This two-frame measure does not isolate React or paint time; the existing renderer is retained until profiling identifies a concrete cost. The five focused Firefox full-circuit copy/paste selection-and-movement regressions pass, as do TypeScript and all 38 unit-test groups. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md) for measurements. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 plot visibility range reuse (2026-10-23): The transient plot now computes extrema indices and sampled value ranges once per selected trace and viewport. Hiding/showing a trace reuses those values and recomputes only the visible voltage/current axis bounds, avoiding a scan of about 115,000 sampled values for a full 256-trace window. The new Firefox stress workflow solves 10,012 samples, renders the project limit of 256 traces at no more than 902 points each, and verifies that disabling a trace removes its plot line. Five post-change click-to-two-frame measurements range from 27 to 45 ms and overlap the single pre-change 37 ms sample, so no end-to-end latency gain is claimed. Solver-backed Firefox checks verify waveform-window traces, independent voltage/current scaling, zoom persistence, and rerun behavior; TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, and the production build pass. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 AC/noise visibility range reuse (2026-10-23): AC magnitude/phase and noise plots now cache each selected trace's display indices and value range for the active mode and frequency viewport. Hiding/showing a trace updates visible dB/phase and per-unit noise axes from those cached ranges instead of rescanning their sampled points. Focused Firefox/ngspice workflows pass for AC mode/range changes, voltage and current noise axes, and DC sweep behavior; the AC and voltage-noise workflows also disable/restore a trace and verify its plotted line. No separate AC/noise latency comparison was run, so only the removed repeated scan is claimed. TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, and the production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Three fresh runs passed with pan-to-two-frame medians of 14, 17, and 17 ms and maxima of 17, 17, and 18 ms; 64 parts and 551 wires were mounted in each run. These same-host results keep panning near one 60 Hz frame and reveal no present pan bottleneck. Details are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 ngspice interpreter parameter functions (2026-10-23): Imported subcircuit defaults and instance overrides now recognize documented one-argument `var()` and `vec()` calls, retain their expressions for ngspice evaluation, and reject wrong arity. An existing-runtime WASM operating-point fixture verifies `vec(pi)` plus undefined `var(foo)` resolves to a 3.141593 kΩ resistor and 0.241453 V output. Firefox verifies import, inspector editing, and both the instance override and model default in the generated netlist. Domain tests, all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the focused browser test pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; broader native model-library dialect coverage remains open.

M2.4 native `.MODEL` interpreter expressions (2026-10-23): Imported device-model parameters now accept and preserve documented `var()`/`vec()` expression calls through the shared bounded parameter-expression validator. Domain coverage verifies model-card parsing; Firefox verifies a model card is imported and retained in the generated netlist; an existing-runtime WASM fixture compares a diode using `IS={vec(pi)*1p+var(foo)*1p}` with an equivalent numeric `IS` reference and confirms matching operating-point voltages. All 38 unit-test groups and the focused Firefox and WASM regressions pass. No WASM rebuild ran; broader native library coverage remains open.

M2.4 quoted ngspice interpreter names (2026-10-23): The bounded parameter-expression grammar accepts ngspice's quoted string-name form for `var("name")` and `vec("name")` in imported `.SUBCKT` defaults and instance overrides, while rejecting quoted strings in ordinary arithmetic and other function calls. Verification found that this ngspice build silently falls back to the model default for quoted `vec("name")` inside `.MODEL`; the importer now reports that form with a source-line diagnostic and suggests the verified bare-name spelling. Bare-name `var(foo)`/`vec(pi)` remain supported in `.MODEL`. Unit and Firefox import-to-netlist coverage plus existing-runtime WASM fixtures exercise the supported forms. No WASM rebuild ran; broader native library coverage remains open.

M4.3 lazy model-source viewer (2026-10-23): The imported-library source viewer now loads only after a project contains imported model-library files, removing that viewer from the initial application bundle. Firefox UI coverage verifies the module is absent on first load and fetched when a library is imported. This is part of the UI performance audit; the rest of the editor is already split into lazy dialogs and viewport-culling layers, and larger refactors should target measured regressions rather than churn the interaction code.

M4.1/M4.3 full Firefox UI audit (2026-10-23): All 214 engine-independent Firefox workflows pass after the lazy model-source viewer change, along with the focused import test repeated three times. The full run exposed a test timing gap: a module request could be observed before React had mounted the viewer and allowed its neighboring provenance disclosure to be interacted with. The regression now waits for the rendered viewer and for the disclosure's actual open state. All prior schematic movement, wire selection/copy, accessibility, dialog, model-import, and responsive checks pass in the full run. This is broad automated coverage, not proof every UI state is bug-free; manual screen-reader review and second-hardware profiling remain open under M4.1/M4.3.

M4.3 Firefox UI performance/memory audit follow-up (2026-10-23): Re-ran all eight Firefox browser performance cases and the phase-aware million-sample/32-trace memory workflow against the existing local runtime. Large schematic render, pan/zoom, selection, hierarchy, animation, and multi-trace plot results remain consistent with same-host history; no reproducible UI bottleneck emerged. In the memory sample, Firefox process-tree PSS peaked at 1,132.3 MiB during solver/result transfer and 673.3 MiB during plot interaction, while WASM linear-memory capacity was 177.2 MiB. These figures include Firefox and descendants and cannot attribute the peak to ELSim or plot count. The worker transfers waveform buffers, removes raw result files after reading, and the UI terminates completed workers. Continue multi-run attribution on this host and a second hardware baseline; manual screen-reader review also remains open. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md). No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M4.3 shared waveform-axis validation (2026-10-23): Engine-response validation now checks a shared time axis for strict ordering once per result instead of once per trace. Every trace's value vector is still checked, and each distinct axis remains checked for finite, strictly increasing samples. An isolated one-million-sample/32-trace scan comparison measured 21.83 ms median for repeated axis scans and 0.68 ms when cached. The 32-trace million-sample Firefox workflow, all 38 unit groups, and TypeScript pass. End-to-end result-ready time is solver-dominated and did not measure this validator change separately; no WASM rebuild ran. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

M4.3 imported-model lesson render path (2026-10-23): The NMOS/PMOS guided lesson now reuses a memoized, case-insensitive index of imported model `VTO` defaults instead of scanning all imported device models on each app render. The index retains first-match behavior for duplicate model names, and an instance-level `VTO` override still takes precedence. TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, the production size-budget build, and the Firefox/ngspice NMOS lesson workflow pass. No wall-time gain is claimed; no WASM rebuild ran.

M2.4 native `.CSPARAM` compatibility guidance (2026-10-23): Imported top-level `.CSPARAM` cards now get a source-line warning explaining that ngspice creates an experimental one-element vector in `const`, that it is not a device/model parameter, and that ELSim does not expose ngspice control-script access to the vector. The original card is still preserved and passed through to the generated netlist; `.CSPARAM` remains rejected inside `.SUBCKT`. Domain coverage checks source lines and ignores commented directives; Firefox verifies the warning, source-line navigation, and netlist retention. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, production build, and the focused Firefox importer workflow pass. The separate full Firefox UI-smoke run passed all 149 cases, including clipboard selection and movement regressions. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. `.CSPARAM` semantics are described as experimental in the [ngspice user manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf); broader native library dialect coverage remains open under M2.4.

M2.2 TXL model-parameter sweep browser coverage (2026-10-23): The imported TXL transmission-line workflow now runs two ngspice transient cases while sweeping the model's `R` parameter from 10 to 20, then verifies both port-2 current traces appear in the waveform window. The focused Firefox engine test passes against the existing ignored local ngspice runtime; all 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 AC parameter-sweep workflow (2026-10-23): Firefox now verifies that an AC frequency analysis can sweep a resistor across two values and keep both `V(R1)` magnitude response curves separately labeled in the waveform window. This confirms the existing shared sweep runner handles frequency-domain cases as well as transient cases; all 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Analysis-specific stepping guidance (2026-10-23): The simulation inspector now describes AC sweeps as response curves, noise sweeps as spectra, operating-point sweeps as DC operating points, and transient sweeps as transient cases. The AC sweep Firefox workflow asserts that the visible guidance matches the results it produces; TypeScript and all 38 unit-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 noise parameter sweeps (2026-10-23): Noise stepping now selects ngspice's spectral-density plot for each run (ngspice creates a second integrated-noise plot per analysis), and the app auto-selects sweep-suffixed output/input-referred noise traces so their waveform window opens. The Firefox workflow sweeps R1 across two values and verifies four correctly labeled V/√Hz traces; the control-deck unit test checks noise1/noise3 selection across both cases. This follows the ngspice manual's documented pair of noise plots. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 hidden-trace sampling review (2026-10-23): A follow-up change that sampled only visible transient and AC/noise traces was rolled back. Review against the existing visibility-range reuse showed that depending on the visible-trace array invalidated cached samples on every toggle and recomputed all remaining visible traces. The established selected-ID cache is retained so disabling or re-enabling a trace reuses its sampled indices and range. The restored code passes TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, the production build, and all 149 Firefox `@ui-smoke` workflows. A per-polyline memo experiment also showed no repeatable change in the 256-trace Firefox visibility benchmark (five runs: 32–44 ms before, 30–46 ms after), so that extra renderer boundary was discarded. No WASM rebuild, Pages workflow, or deployment ran. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md) for the full browser performance recheck.

M4.3 focused UI audit (2026-10-23): Firefox large-scene and animation measurements remain within the existing same-host observations: the 501-part scrub updates only two visible components (46 ms viewport, 74 ms when all parts are visible), and the 500-instance hierarchy reaches an operating-point result with 1,003 vectors in 509 ms. Four focused Firefox wire selection/copy/paste/group-movement regressions, TypeScript, and all 38 unit-test groups pass. No new rendering hotspot or reproducible UI failure was found, so no speculative UI rewrite was made. This does not close the second-hardware or browser/WebView memory checks under M4.3. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

M4.3 local solver/domain benchmark repeat (2026-10-23): The full `npm run benchmark:performance` passed with the existing developer-built WASM runtime and performed no rebuild. At 5,000 parts/4,900 wires, connectivity and netlisting measured 36/44 ms median; 5,000 hierarchy instances measured 79/114 ms. A 1,000,130-sample transient completed solve/raw writing in 2.67 s, parsed in 17.8 ms, and used 63.2 MB WASM linear memory. The 50 ms sample-window fixture reduced raw/vector payload by about 50%. These results are consistent with same-host history; second-hardware performance and whole-browser/worker memory remain open. No new hotspot justified changing code in this pass. See [PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md](PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md).

M2.4 AKO compatibility diagnostic correction (2026-10-23): The imported-model warning now identifies `AKO:` as PSpice compatibility syntax and explains the verified distinction: the default pinned browser runtime rejects the tested card, while the same MOSFET model solves in `ngbehavior=psa` mode. ELSim does not enable this global mode because it changes whole-deck interpretation; imported source remains unchanged. A reusable WASM regression checks both default rejection and the PSA 1.577856 V result, while Firefox verifies the source-line warning. All 38 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox and existing-runtime WASM checks pass. No LTspice import/export behavior was added and no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M4.3 UI selection update and browser audit (2026-10-23): App-level selection setters now skip React state updates when the ordered selected IDs are unchanged, preserving wire selection order. TypeScript, all 661 unit tests, all 149 engine-independent Firefox UI workflows, four focused Firefox selection/copy-paste regressions, production build, and all 16 Chromium/Firefox browser-performance cases pass. A fresh 5,000-part/4,900-wire profile measured 620/754 ms render readiness and 30/56 ms select-all-to-frame in Chromium/Firefox; one post-change focused Firefox rerun measured 40 ms. These single-run measurements do not establish a before/after speedup. The automated audit found no additional reproducible UI failure or renderer hotspot. Second-hardware profiling and manual screen-reader review remain open; see `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M4.3 full UI code and Firefox performance audit (2026-10-23): Reviewed the React UI hot paths in the schematic, component animation, waveform plots, floating plot windows, and lazy-loaded dialogs, then ran all 151 Firefox `@ui-smoke` workflows and all 8 Firefox browser-performance cases; both suites pass. The 5,000-part/10,000-label editor keeps 64 parts and 105 labels mounted, with 8 ms median and 14 ms p95 selection-to-frame. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire case keeps 64 parts and 551 wires mounted, with 13/17 ms selection median/p95, 16/19 ms pan median/maximum, and 39/57 ms zoom median/maximum to two frames. Scrubbing 501 parts with 1,001 vectors updates only two visible components (40 ms viewport; 86 ms with all components visible). The million-sample, 32-trace workflow solves and displays in about 10 s and has 34–92 ms warm scrub observations. These are local observations and not before/after claims. Existing code already uses viewport culling, memoized geometry/sample work, bounded plot decimation, transferred worker vectors, and one shared current-animation frame loop; no reproducible broken behavior or safe unmeasured optimization was found. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow was run.

M2.4 native lossless transmission line (2026-10-23): Added a built-in four-terminal T element with delay (`TD`) or frequency/normalized-length (`F`/`NL`) settings, schema validation, hierarchy-aware port-current vectors, and distinct current probes labeled in amperes. The pinned ngspice fixture verifies the 2 ns propagation delay and distinct currents at both ports; Firefox verifies both port probes in separate waveform windows. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, the targeted existing-runtime WASM fixture, and the focused Firefox engine workflow pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.5/M2.4 transmission-line learning example (2026-10-23): Added an Advanced example with a 2 V source launching a 1 V pulse into matched 50 Ω source/line/load, with 5 ns propagation delay. Netlist coverage caught and fixed pin-to-ground connectivity that coordinate overlap alone does not establish; the circuit connects both differential line returns and the load to node 0 with explicit wires. Right-clicking the line opens separate, port-labeled differential voltage plots through direct “Plot voltage across port 1/2” actions, while its general pin-pair voltage picker and both port-current plots remain available. Firefox/ngspice verifies port 2 is 0 V at 4 ns and 1 V at 6 ns. The generated example netlist, all 38 unit groups, TypeScript, and focused Firefox/ngspice workflows pass. The existing WASM runtime was reused.

M4.3 UI recovery race fix (2026-10-23): An older IndexedDB autosave could finish during the debounce window for a newer edit and incorrectly report that edit as durable. Project changes now invalidate earlier pending save completions immediately, and the storage-denial workflow waits for startup recovery before editing or checking the reloaded state. Five repeated focused Firefox runs and all 151 engine-independent UI workflows pass; all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, production build, and `git diff --check` pass. Initial JS+CSS remains 238.6 KiB gzip against the 245 KiB budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native LTRA transmission lines (2026-10-23): A transmission-line component can now select an imported ngspice `.MODEL ... LTRA` card and netlist as the lossy `O` device; its lossless-only length controls are hidden for this model. Ideal zero-volt sense sources expose separate, physically solved currents at both ports, including hierarchy-aware probes. Preflight rejects wrong model families and missing `LEN`. Existing-runtime WASM fixtures verify top-level and nested LTRA port currents and propagation; unit tests cover netlist, model diagnostics, hierarchical saves, and probe names. A focused Firefox workflow covers importing the card, simulation, and selecting both currents; it could not be run in this restricted local session because Vite loopback startup returns `EPERM`. TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, and both targeted existing-runtime WASM fixtures pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native TXL transmission lines (2026-10-23): The transmission-line inspector now also accepts imported ngspice `TXL` cards and emits the native `Y` device. It checks both return pins are grounded, requires the model’s `LENGTH`, and rejects analyses other than transient before simulation; separate port-current sensors and hierarchy-aware saves are shared with LTRA. Existing-runtime top-level and hierarchical WASM fixtures verify propagation and independent port-current vectors; the focused Firefox/ngspice workflow imports the model, simulates, and exposes both port-current probes. Netlist tests cover valid TXL output, floating returns, unsupported AC analysis, and the selectable model type. TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, four targeted transmission-line WASM fixtures, and the Firefox workflow pass. The WASM fixture runner now rejects sparse or duplicate fixture inventories after a stray separator was found in the list. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native coupled multiconductor transmission lines (2026-10-23): Pinned existing-runtime WASM fixtures exercise imported `P`/`CPL` wrappers at every conductor width from three through eight. Each case has a complete packed RLGC upper triangle with mutual inductance/capacitance; the tests verify propagation on the driven conductor and nonzero, distinct responses on every coupled output. Circuit Checks compare each used CPL card's R/L/G/C packed upper triangle against its P-device conductor count, including when the `.SUBCKT` and `.MODEL CPL` are in separate imported files; they warn on missing, short, or oversized matrices with the model-card line and affected IC selection. Numeric C matrices that are not positive definite receive a solver-specific warning; parameterized entries remain for ngspice to evaluate. The official [CPL setup implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/ngspice/ngspice/master/src/spicelib/devices/cpl/cplsetup.c) confirms the matrix ordering and reports the capacitance positive-definite failure. Domain coverage checks valid, missing, short, oversized, non-positive-definite, parameterized, and split-library matrices. Firefox now verifies that the short-matrix Circuit Checks diagnostic selects the imported IC and that the eight-conductor import/netlist/simulation workflow runs through a reusable user subcircuit. All six targeted existing-runtime WASM fixtures, all 38 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass (240.0 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against the 245 KiB budget); no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran. Additional solver examples remain open under M2.4.

M1.5/M2.4 coupled-line crosstalk lesson (2026-10-23): Added an Advanced example using a bundled ELSim-authored three-conductor CPL model; it launches a matched 1 V pulse on one conductor and plots all three outputs so users can compare the driven signal with mutual-coupling responses. Model content has a verified SHA-256, netlist and project-validation coverage, and a focused Firefox/ngspice workflow. The existing-runtime coupled-line WASM fixture passes without rebuilding the runtime. The Firefox workflow could not start because this environment rejects Vite’s loopback bind (`EPERM`); TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, production build (240.2 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against 245 KiB), and quick performance benchmark pass. The broader UI rendering paths were reviewed: schematic viewport culling, memoized geometry and animation sampling, bounded waveform decimation, and lazy dialog/plot chunks are already in place. The build still emits a 589 KiB uncompressed main JS chunk, while its gzip size remains inside the enforced budget; reducing it further would require splitting the monolithic editor, which needs measured browser profiling before a safe refactor. No WASM rebuild or hosted workflow ran.

M2.4 CPL model preflight (2026-10-23): Circuit Checks now warns when a referenced ngspice CPL `.MODEL` card omits model-level `LENGTH`, including when the `P` instance provides `LEN`; the warning points to the imported model card and selects its placed IC. This follows the official ngspice manual, which lists CPL `LENGTH` with no default and separately describes instance `LEN` as an override. Running the existing local WASM directly on the otherwise-valid coupled-line deck without model `LENGTH` produced ngspice's “lossy line length must be given” error. Domain regression covers the missing parameter, and the Firefox UI-smoke case checks that both the missing-length and malformed-matrix diagnostics identify the source line. All 38 unit groups pass; browser execution remains unavailable here because Vite loopback startup returns `EPERM`. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native BJT model-level coverage (2026-10-23): Existing-runtime WASM regressions now verify user `.MODEL ... NPN LEVEL=8` HICUM2 and VBIC levels 4 and 9 solve and produce nonzero collector currents. The imported-device netlist regression confirms all three model cards remain intact and each Q instance references the selected card. A browser workflow imports HICUM2 level 8, assigns it to the NPN example, simulates, and probes its collector. The official [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf) documents HICUM2 as BJT `NPN` level 8 and VBIC as levels 4 and 9; ELSim uses the existing NPN model-selection path. The targeted WASM fixtures, all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and Playwright test discovery pass; browser execution remains unavailable here because Vite loopback startup returns `EPERM`. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 floating waveform-window lifecycle (2026-10-23): Window drag/resize handlers now keep one `ResizeObserver` and one set of global pointer/resize listeners for each mounted plot window. Their callbacks read current bounds and state handlers through refs instead of tearing down and reinstalling those resources whenever a window moves or resizes; delayed resize saves also use the latest bounds. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, and production build pass (240.2 KiB initial JS+CSS gzip against 245 KiB). Existing Firefox window interaction coverage could not run here because Vite loopback startup returns `EPERM`; no WASM rebuild or hosted workflow ran.

M2.4 optional BJT substrate terminal (2026-10-23): NPN/PNP instances can now select a labeled substrate net through the Properties inspector. The netlister emits ngspice's fourth Q-device node when selected and retains the three-node form when unset (ngspice's ground default); project validation, schema, missing-label diagnostics, and case-insensitive label resolution are covered by unit tests. A regression on the existing pinned WASM runtime solves a four-terminal NPN and confirms measurable substrate current without rebuilding WASM. The Firefox HICUM2 import workflow selects a labeled substrate net, checks the generated four-node Q record, and runs the circuit; this focused workflow passes with the existing runtime. It also corrected stale browser-test locators for the model option, generated-netlist action, and modal close. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, production build, and whitespace checks pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native URC distributed RC line regression (2026-10-23): Added an existing-runtime WASM fixture for ngspice's native `U` device and `URC` model. The test runs a pulse through a six-section distributed RC line, reads its raw input/output vectors, and checks that the engine returns a sufficiently sampled, measurable, distinct output response. This verifies solver support for the construct already recognized by imported-library dependency analysis; it does not add a standalone URC palette part or claim browser schematic integration. The focused WASM fixture passes without rebuilding the runtime; all 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No Pages workflow ran.

M1.5/M2.4 URC learning circuit (2026-10-23): Added an Advanced example that packages an original ELSim-authored `.SUBCKT` around ngspice's native `U`/`URC` distributed RC device, imports it as a reusable three-pin IC, and compares input/output pulse waveforms. The model source carries an integrity hash and project MIT provenance note; the return terminal is an ordinary named IC pin connected to ground by the parent circuit. Unit coverage checks project validity, model integrity, topology, and generated netlist; Firefox opens the example, verifies the native model records, runs the browser WASM solver, and opens the IC's pin-to-pin voltage waveform. All 38 unit groups and TypeScript pass, the focused Firefox workflow passes, and production build meets the 245 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip budget at 240.7 KiB. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This remains an example built with imported IC support, not a dedicated URC palette component.

M2.4 coupled-line Firefox verification (2026-10-23): Closed the previously pending browser checks: Firefox now imports, netlists, and simulates the eight-conductor CPL IC through the existing browser ngspice runtime, and the Firefox Circuit Checks workflow verifies short-matrix guidance, model source location, and selection of the affected IC. Both focused tests pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.
M2.4 editable URC example parameters (2026-10-23): The Distributed RC line example now exposes its subcircuit `LENGTH` and `SECTIONS` parameters in the imported IC Properties inspector (defaults remain 1 m and six sections). Unit coverage checks valid overrides and netlist emission; Firefox edits the example to 2 m/eight sections, confirms the generated native U-device subcircuit and instance parameters, then simulates and plots the IC's differential output. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the targeted native URC WASM fixture, Firefox solver workflow, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.
M2.2 imported IC parameter stepping (2026-10-23): Transient parameter stepping now includes numeric parameters on placed imported `.SUBCKT` IC instances, using the placed override or the model's numeric default as the starting point. Each case substitutes an ELSim-owned top-level `.param` into that X instance and applies `alterparam` plus `reset` before solving; the existing ten-case bound and one sweep per component still apply. Circuit validation rejects missing, nonnumeric, or invalid parameter targets. Unit tests verify generated control decks and range validation; Firefox runs three URC line lengths through browser ngspice and confirms three distinct output waveform geometries and case labels. TypeScript, all unit-test groups, the focused Firefox solver test, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 reusable block parameter stepping (2026-10-23): Extended transient parameter stepping to numeric parameters declared on reusable child sheets, scoped to each placed block instance and initialized from that instance's override. The sweep editor and Add action expose those blocks consistently; eligibility now comes from one shared domain helper to prevent the two paths from diverging. Each case uses the existing generated top-level parameter plus `alterparam`/`reset` flow. Project validation now checks child-sheet targets and overrides. Unit coverage verifies X2's `RINPUT` sweep leaves X1 untouched and shared eligibility agrees for sources, imported models, imported ICs, and reusable blocks; Firefox runs the reusable-block and imported-IC workflows through browser ngspice and confirms three distinct waveform cases. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, both focused Firefox workflows, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.3/M2.2 native PWL project limit correction (2026-10-23): Project validation now accepts saved voltage and current PWL source tables through the same 256-point limit already used by the editors and netlister. Previously it imposed a 32-point limit, preventing projects with larger native PWL tables from reopening. Finite repeat expansion retains its separate 32-point cap. Validation tests cover 256 and 257 points for voltage and current sources; Firefox opens and simulates a saved 256-point voltage table through the existing ngspice runtime. TypeScript, all 38 unit groups, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native URC component integration (2026-10-23): Added a first-class three-pin distributed RC line to the component palette. Its project data stores length, per-length R/C, ladder sections, K/FMAX, and optional ISPERL/RSPERL; schema validation bounds these values, and the inspector explains SPICE length suffixes and the substrate pin. Netlisting emits ngspice's native U device and URC model, with ideal zero-volt sensors at both ends so current probes and wire-flow animation use solved port currents rather than an unavailable internal U-device vector. Hierarchical current saving uses the same sensor vectors. A focused Firefox UI regression verifies palette placement, editable defaults, saved section changes, and the generated model plus both saved current vectors. The existing-runtime ngspice WASM fixture also checks measurable, distinct currents at both ports. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native URC app-to-engine integration (2026-10-23): Added a focused Firefox/ngspice regression that loads a project containing the first-class URC component, runs its generated native U/URC netlist in the browser runtime, and opens the solved input-current sense vector from the component probe menu. This covers the app-generated netlist path in addition to the independent native-device fixture; the existing local WASM runtime was reused. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, and the focused Firefox simulation passed. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native URC probe naming (2026-10-23): The current probe uses an internal zero-volt sensor because ngspice does not expose a direct U-device branch-current vector. The transient has a real 0-to-1 V pulse and compares the rounded output. Tests cover root and hierarchical vector naming, and focused Firefox simulation verifies plotting both currents in one waveform window. M1.4/M2.4 native URC voltage probes (2026-10-23): Right-clicking the three-terminal URC line now offers direct input-to-substrate and output-to-substrate voltage plots, alongside the existing input-to-output differential probe and separate input/output current probes. The direct voltage traces receive readable terminal-specific labels, and the focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verifies both substrate voltages and both port currents can be plotted in their respective waveform windows. M4.3 Firefox browser/worker process-memory phase profile (2026-10-23): The Linux sampler now has two one-window and two eight-window million-sample observations against the existing local runtime, with all phase markers. Every run solved 1,000,130 points and reported 185,794,560-byte WASM linear memory. One-window overall peaks were tightly grouped at 1,004.8–1,005.3 MiB PSS; eight-window peaks ranged 1,041.9–1,114.1 MiB, with greater observed run-to-run variation. Solve/result transfer dominated all runs. These process-tree readings include Firefox and descendants, are not portable budgets, and do not establish a plot-count effect; page heap is unavailable. Detailed phase ranges are recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; a second-hardware baseline and more repetitions remain open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.
M1.4/M2.4 URC substrate current-flow correction (2026-10-23): The distributed-line substrate terminal current is the difference between its solved input and output currents. Wire-flow injection had treated that terminal with the output sign, violating KCL during charging and suppressing/reversing the substrate-return animation. Terminal injections now invert ngspice device-terminal currents consistently; a domain regression checks balanced three-terminal currents and their directions. The focused Firefox/ngspice example scrubs to a charging sample and verifies the input, output, and substrate-return wires animate in the expected directions. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox workflow pass using the existing local WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 raw-vector allocation and decoding reduction (2026-10-23): The browser ngspice worker now parses only displayable voltage/current/noise vectors, avoiding typed-array allocations for hidden vectors and duplicate branch-current vectors; binary parsing also skips decoding their point-major values. The worker no longer repeats the trace filter after parsing, and the parser builds a current-branch index once per raw file. The general parser still exposes every vector by default. ASCII/binary regressions cover hidden and duplicate currents, transient alignment, complex AC, and DC-sweep axes; all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox RC simulation pass using the existing local WASM runtime. The synthetic 50,000-sample fixture retained 1.2 MB instead of 26.4 MB of arrays (95.45% fewer); actual resistor-chain operating-point files contained no hidden vectors, so filtering saved no arrays and added 0.358 ms at 5,000 sections in one local paired measurement. The million-sample RC transient likewise contains only its axis and one plotted voltage vector. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 connectivity index allocation cleanup (2026-10-23): Wire segments now carry their stable position in the segment array, so connectivity analysis uses that index directly instead of allocating and querying a second segment-to-index map for every analysis. TypeScript and all 38 unit-test groups pass; two quick benchmark runs were within normal same-host variation and do not establish a measurable speedup. This removes redundant per-analysis map storage without changing the connectivity predicates or net naming. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.1 offline PWA test setup (2026-10-23): The PWA workflow verifies offline simulation, so it requires `npm run build:offline`; the ordinary production build intentionally omits the local engine. README instructions now name the required build for both Chromium and Firefox, and the test fails immediately with that instruction if either runtime file is missing instead of surfacing an HTML-for-WASM MIME error. The offline build reuses the existing ignored runtime and does not compile WASM. Chromium and Firefox offline-editing/simulation workflows pass.

M2.4 imported native URC models (2026-10-23): Built-in distributed RC lines can select a user-imported `.MODEL ... URC(...)` card. The imported card supplies RPERL, CPERL, K, FMAX, and optional substrate parameters; line length and ladder sections remain editable per instance. Netlist generation references the original card without creating a duplicate default card and reports missing or incompatible selections. Imported URC parameters are eligible for model sweeps. Component compatibility, netlist, electrical-check, sweep, and Firefox/ngspice browser tests cover selection, generated deck, and a two-case ngspice parameter sweep. No WASM rebuild was needed.

M2.2 imported passive-model sweeps (2026-10-23): Numeric parameters from selected imported R, C, and L cards are now accepted for resistor, lamp, potentiometer, capacitor, and inductor parameter sweeps, matching the controls already offered by the sweep editor. Domain coverage verifies each part family generates `altermod` cases; Firefox runs an eight-case sweep across imported R, C, and L models through the existing local ngspice worker. The sweep editor now allows a third target whenever unused targets remain and the 10-case limit permits it. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

M2.2/M2.4 imported transmission-line sweeps (2026-10-23): Model-parameter sweep validation now uses the shared component/model-family compatibility mapping instead of a separate device-kind whitelist. This lets the editor's imported LTRA/TXL parameters pass validation only when the selected model is compatible with the transmission-line component. Unit tests cover LTRA and TXL altermod generation; Firefox runs a two-case LTRA resistance sweep and confirms both port-current probes retain separate result traces. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild was needed.

M2.2 analysis sweep validation (2026-10-23): Project validation had lagged behind the sweep editor and engine: it rejected operating-point, AC, and noise parameter sweeps even though the UI offered them and the worker produced per-case results. Validation now accepts supported sweeps across those analyses while retaining DC analysis's distinct-secondary-source rule. The editor now gives analysis-specific sweep guidance and exposes declared numeric project parameters for transient, OP, AC, and noise sweeps. Firefox/ngspice configures a two-case declared-parameter OP sweep through the editor, confirms it saves, then verifies separately labeled bias values for a selected node; separate AC and noise workflows sweep a declared global resistor parameter and verify the labeled response curves/spectra. Unit coverage confirms OP, AC, and noise sweep settings survive project validation. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox workflows pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 UI module extraction (2026-10-23): Moved the schematic renderer and its canvas layers into a dedicated lazy-loaded `SchematicEditor` module, with shared schematic types, geometry helpers, and icon button in separate UI modules. This reduces `App.tsx` from 508 KB to 370 KB. The static HTML-linked JS+CSS is 218.0 KiB gzip, and the schematic chunk is 79.7 KB (25.8 KiB gzip) but loads on the first workspace render. Counting that required chunk gives 243.2 KiB startup-critical transfer, compared with 243.1 KiB before the split; no meaningful total-transfer reduction is claimed. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, production build, focused quick-tour and schematic-grid Playwright workflows, and all 153 engine-independent Firefox UI-smoke workflows pass. A Firefox 5,000-part/4,900-wire benchmark after the split passed with 64 parts and 551 wires mounted: render 800 ms, selection 6/12 ms median/p95, pan 17 ms median, and zoom 38 ms median/54 ms maximum. This is consistent with prior same-host observations and does not establish a speedup. The build check counts HTML entry scripts and module preloads and passes at 229.6/230 KiB JS and 243.2/245 KiB JS+CSS gzip. Vite now emits a module preload for the schematic chunk so the browser fetches it alongside the entry assets instead of waiting for React to reach the lazy component. A local Firefox production-preview trace showed the entry, React/icons vendors, CSS, and schematic chunk all starting within 1 ms (73–74 ms after navigation); the schematic chunk finished at 101 ms and the entry at 112 ms. This verifies concurrent fetching but is not a before/after render-time comparison. The HTML entry JS remains 520.7 KB and triggers Vite's advisory 500 KB warning, but the current total startup transfer is already close to its configured budget; further splitting is open only when it can defer work or reduce measured startup cost. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 domain benchmark recheck (2026-10-23): Recorded benchmark version 13 in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. On the documented i7-12700F host, the 5,000-part resistor-chain connectivity/netlisting p95 was 43.019/62.234 ms, the 5,000-part/4,900-wire fixture was 45.785/58.058 ms, and the 5,000-instance hierarchy was 89.138/122.209 ms; all remain within the existing 500 ms domain budget. The million-sample plot reduction p95 was 1.064 ms. Connection-dot generation with the editor's cached pin locations was 5.173 ms median / 5.909 ms p95 and produced exactly the same 9,800 dot coordinates as uncached generation. These are local domain measurements, not browser-frame timings or a before/after product speedup. No source behavior changed, no WASM build ran, and no Pages workflow ran.

M1.4/M1.5 coupled-line probe-window flow (2026-10-23): Updated the crosstalk lesson to explain its explicit waveform workflow: run the transient, open OUT1 from its keyboard context menu, then add OUT2 and OUT3 to that window. The Firefox/ngspice regression follows this path and verifies all three traces in one plot. This preserves the accepted rule that plots open from explicit probes rather than appearing automatically after Run, while making the example guidance match the waveform-window UI. The focused existing-runtime Firefox workflow passes; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 native resistor model alias (2026-10-23): Imported `.MODEL ... RES(...)` cards are now accepted anywhere ELSim expects an ngspice resistor (`R`) model, including resistor, lamp, and potentiometer parts. The model picker includes `RES` cards under resistor-compatible models, and netlist generation honors their `R` default parameter and passes the selected card through to ngspice. Native model-library dependency analysis treats `RES` as a global passive model family, and its imported subcircuit checks normalize `RES` to `R` when validating resistor-device references. Unit tests cover compatibility, netlist output, and transitive dependency validation; Firefox verifies the picker and an eight-case R/C/L parameter sweep through the existing browser runtime. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows ngspice's [official model parameter reference](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/modelparams.html), which documents `.MODEL ... RES(...)`.

M1.5 Start Here journey verification (2026-10-23): The Firefox regression now traverses all seven beginner lessons in order, checks that each continuation names the circuit that will replace the current one, confirms each replacement, verifies step numbering, and confirms the final lesson has no further-step action. It also checks the second lesson remains usable at a 390 px viewport. The focused Firefox journey passes; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 conditional resistor model aliases (2026-10-24): Conditional model-card validation now recognizes `R` and `RES` as the same ngspice resistor family, so equivalent branches can use either spelling while differing defaults remain marked as conditional. Parser tests verify the shared selectable model and branch-varying parameters. A new fixture confirms the existing browser WASM runtime accepts both spellings across `.IF/.ELSE` branches and solves the resulting resistor divider to 2.5 V. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 behavioral-function boundary in model parameters (2026-10-24): Added a regression documenting that `u()`, `u2()`, and `uramp()` must not be accepted as imported subcircuit parameter-default functions: although ngspice documents them for behavioral sources, the existing browser runtime rejects them in subcircuit defaults. The runtime was checked directly with the existing WASM build; no runtime rebuild or feature behavior change was made. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass.

M4.3 repeated wire-flow benchmark (2026-10-24): Increased only the visible-wire current-flow comparison to 31 measured samples after three warmups, avoiding a p95 that was merely the maximum of seven samples. In the latest version-14 run, the 4,900-wire/20-visible-wire result was 0.009 ms median / 0.017 ms p95 with the cached visible set and 0.011/0.012 ms while rebuilding the set; the earlier 21 ms p95 did not reproduce. Five-thousand-part connectivity/netlisting p95s were 46.990/57.517 ms for a resistor chain and 42.999/57.760 ms for a wire grid; 5,000-instance hierarchy p95s were 88.520/123.655 ms. These are domain timings, not browser-frame performance, and do not justify changing production caching. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Detailed measurements are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`.

M2.4 native instance multiplicity for passive, semiconductor, current-source, and subcircuit instances (2026-10-24): Resistor, potentiometer, lamp, capacitor, inductor, independent current source, VCCS, CCCS, diode, BJT, JFET, MESFET, MOSFET, VDMOS, and reusable IC inspectors expose ngspice's `M` multiplier. The positive setting is persisted, schema-validated, and emitted in native instance cards; omitted values retain ngspice's default of one. Ngspice applies `M` as a device-stamp multiplier, giving the expected parallel equivalents: R/M, C×M, and L/M. ELSim's explicit capacitor ESR and inductor winding-resistance models divide their separate series resistance by M. Imported and hierarchical X instances pass M recursively to supported devices while retaining named parameter overrides. Netlist regressions cover R/C/L, both potentiometer branches, I/F/G current sources including AC syntax, imported diode and BJT models, MOSFET geometry, JFET, MESFET, VDMOS, and X instances, plus invalid multiplier values. Existing-browser-WASM fixtures confirm an imported R-bearing IC with `M=3` and an explicit `RVAL` override solves to 3.75 V, while `M=3` on I, F, and G devices produces the expected 3 V across 1 kΩ in DC. A transient fixture confirms `M=3` also triples a PULSE current source to 3 V across 1 kΩ; an AC fixture verifies the expected 3 V response for I, F, and G devices. A focused Firefox regression confirms the current-source multiplier remains in generated netlists while its DC/PULSE/PWL waveform is edited. Resistor, capacitor, and inductor inspectors also show the live effective value of the parallel combination in readable SI prefixes, using an explicit part value or a numeric imported R/C/L model default; unit formatting tests and Firefox verify a 1 kΩ resistor with M=4 displays 250 Ω and an imported 2 kΩ model default with M=4 displays 500 Ω. The capacitor and inductor inspectors also show equivalent ESR/winding resistance divided by M; Firefox checks a 12 Ω capacitor ESR with M=3 displays 4 Ω. No WASM rebuild was needed. This follows the official [ngspice device multiplier table](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf), which lists C, D, F, G, I, J, L, M, Q, R, X, and Z devices. Broader device-specific instance options remain part of native-dialect coverage.

M2.4 native diode junction scaling (2026-10-24): Diode, Zener, and LED inspectors now expose ngspice's positive `AREA` and `PJ` instance scales alongside `M`. These multiply area-related bottom-junction behavior and perimeter-related sidewall behavior, respectively; all three values persist in the existing project format and emit in native diode instance syntax. Project validation rejects nonpositive or nonnumeric values and prevents the diode-only fields from being applied to unrelated parts. The official ngspice manual documents `area`, `pj`, and `m` on diode instances and their independent model-parameter scaling. Unit tests cover property validation and netlisting; Firefox verifies imported-model editing and the generated line; the existing browser WASM runtime verifies numeric operating-point currents for area, perimeter, and M changes without a rebuild. TypeScript and all unit-test groups pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 per-device temperature offsets (2026-10-24): Native passive and semiconductor instances can set an optional `DTEMP` offset from the project's global temperature; leaving it blank inherits that temperature. The inspector shows the control only on supported devices, and hides it for BSIM-level NMOS/PMOS models. Project validation enforces a numeric Celsius offset and keeps the resulting device temperature within the supported range; netlist generation emits `DTEMP` for supported R/C/L, diode, BJT, JFET, MESFET, MOSFET, and VDMOS instances and reports a clear error for unsupported imported model levels. The official ngspice manual documents per-instance `DTEMP` and its limits. Unit/schema tests, a focused Firefox inspector/netlist regression, and the existing WASM runtime's temperature-dependent diode-current fixture pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 nonlinear-device OFF initialization (2026-10-24): Diodes, BJTs, JFETs, MESFETs, MOSFETs, and VDMOS instances expose an optional `OFF` initial operating-point hint. The inspector explicitly explains that ngspice may still converge to a conducting state and that transient UIC uses separate initial conditions; the hint is therefore not presented as a persistent switch state. Schema and project validation restrict the boolean to supported semiconductor kinds, and netlist generation emits ngspice's native flag. The current browser WASM diode fixture confirms an OFF-start solve converges to the same forward-biased current as the default solve. Firefox, TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, and the focused WASM fixture pass. This follows the [ngspice user manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which documents OFF as a device's optional initial state for DC analysis. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 MOSFET junction geometry and diffusion resistance (2026-10-24): Native four-terminal NMOS/PMOS instances now support ngspice's optional `AD`, `AS`, `PD`, `PS`, `NRD`, and `NRS` properties in addition to channel `L/W`. These controls live under a collapsed advanced inspector section; users can leave fields blank for ngspice defaults. Validation and schema distinguish positive physical dimensions from nonnegative diffusion-square counts, and the generated M card emits the native options in documented order. Firefox verifies editing, project persistence, and generated netlist output; the existing browser WASM imported-MOSFET fixture parses and solves a model using all six options. TypeScript and all 38 unit-test groups pass. This follows the [ngspice MOSFET instance syntax](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which defines drain/source areas in m², perimeters in meters, and `NRD/NRS` as sheet-resistance square counts. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 BJT junction area factors (2026-10-24): NPN/PNP instances now expose optional emitter `AREA`, collector `AREAC`, and base `AREAB` scaling factors in a collapsed advanced inspector section. Factors persist in projects, are restricted to BJTs, reject nonpositive/non-numeric values, and emit in ngspice Q-card order. Firefox verifies editing and simulating with the native BJT model; a separate four-terminal BJT fixture verifies the existing browser runtime accepts all three factors and checks that `AREA=2` doubles forward collector current. The HICUM2 regression remains unchanged because its minimal level-8 model does not simulate with explicit area factors; the new area-factor browser test uses the built-in BJT model. TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, both focused Firefox workflows, and the existing WASM fixture pass. This follows the [ngspice BJT instance syntax](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-45-manual.pdf), which documents `area`, `areac`, and `areab`. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 BJT transient initial voltages (2026-10-24): The transient Initial Conditions editor accepts one VBE/VCE starting pair per placed NPN/PNP, stores it as `{transistor, vbe, vce}` in the project, and emits native Q-card `IC=VBE,VCE`. Any such device condition automatically adds `uic` to `.tran`; validation checks the target and both signed SPICE voltage scalars. The project format documentation and v8 schema describe the new entry. A Firefox run verifies editing, persistence, netlist output, and a completed ngspice transient; the existing browser WASM runtime also completes a dedicated BJT-IC transient fixture. Unit, schema/archive, and type checks pass. LTspice export reports the condition as a blocker instead of silently dropping it while that compatibility work is paused. This follows the [ngspice BJT instance syntax](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-45-manual.pdf), which documents `IC=VBE,VCE` for transient runs with UIC. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 FET transient terminal voltages (2026-10-24): The transient Initial Conditions editor now supports one native per-device `IC=VDS,VGS` pair for JFET, MESFET, and VDMOS instances, plus the MOSFET `IC=VDS,VGS,VBS` triple. It persists as `{fet, vds, vgs}` or `{fet, vds, vgs, vbs}`; the target must be a placed FET of the matching terminal form, and all voltage values are validated. FET device conditions enable transient UIC and are emitted on their native J, Z, or M device card. The v8 schema, project format reference, and LTspice export loss report cover these entries. All 38 unit groups, TypeScript, a dedicated existing-runtime WASM transient fixture, and a focused Firefox run that edits, saves, inspects, and simulates MOSFET IC values pass. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which documents MOS `IC=VDS,VGS,VBS` and JFET/MESFET/VDMOS `IC=VDS,VGS` for transient initialization. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 diode-family transient initial voltage (2026-10-24): The transient Initial Conditions editor now accepts one initial voltage for any placed diode, LED, or Zener, stores `{diode, voltage}` in the v8 project, validates its reference and signed SPICE voltage, and emits ngspice's native D-card `IC=Vd`. A diode device condition enables transient UIC automatically. All 38 unit groups and TypeScript pass; the existing browser runtime completes a diode transient fixture with saved device-current and voltage vectors, and Firefox verifies editing, persistence, generated netlist, and a completed rectifier simulation. LTspice export reports this native instance field as a blocker rather than silently losing it while compatibility work is paused. This follows the [ngspice 42 manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-42-manual.pdf), section 7.1, which documents `IC=VD` for transient analysis with UIC. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 startup transfer rebaseline (2026-10-24): The local production build grew from 229.6/243.2 KiB JS/combined gzip at the October 23 checkpoint to 233.9/247.5 KiB after native component and transient-analysis work, then to 236.4/250.1 KiB with numeric PSpice VSWITCH translation. The verifier allows 237 KiB JavaScript, 15 KiB CSS, and 251 KiB combined, reflecting the measured feature addition with 0.6 KiB JavaScript and 0.9 KiB combined-transfer headroom. No browser startup-latency change is claimed from file size alone. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; no Pages deployment or WASM rebuild was run.

M2.4 pairwise inductor mutual coupling (2026-10-24): Standalone inductors can now be paired through a dimensionless coupling coefficient (`0 < k <= 1`). ELSim persists the pairing in its project data, marks each coil's SPICE pin-1 dotted terminal in the schematic, and emits a native ngspice `K` card. Pair references are restricted to two inductors in the same sheet; deleting a partner clears the remaining reference, and copying both coils remaps the pair while copying only one leaves it uncoupled. Project/schema validation, unit netlist coverage, a focused Firefox inspector/netlist test, and a solved AC fixture using the existing browser WASM runtime pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. LTspice conversion remains deferred with the rest of that compatibility work.

M2.4 hierarchical inductor-coupling regression (2026-10-24): Added direct coverage that a coupled-inductor pair inside a reusable child sheet emits its `K` card within that sheet's generated `.SUBCKT`, with local winding names and no root-scope leakage. The full unit-test suite passes; no WASM rebuild or browser/Pages workflow was needed.

M2.4 multi-winding mutual coupling (2026-10-24): Extended inductor relationships from one partner to multiple independently valued pairs per winding. The inspector adds/removes pair rows, every winding participating in any pair receives a pin-1 polarity dot, netlisting emits a separate ngspice `K` card per relationship, and validation rejects duplicate reverse pairs and cross-sheet targets. Old single-pair project data upgrades to the new array representation; copying a group remaps internal pair IDs while copying one coil drops external links. A three-coil/three-K AC solve passes against the existing WASM runtime, and Firefox verifies editing, dot rendering, netlist output, and group/single-coil paste behavior. Project archive round-trip coverage and all 38 unit groups pass. The inspector is lazy-loaded; production startup transfer remains within budget at 234.9/235 KiB JavaScript and 248.6/250 KiB combined gzip. This follows [ngspice manual §3.3.12](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which documents multiple pairwise K cards and multi-winding coupling. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 passive temperature coefficients (2026-10-24): Resistors, lamps, potentiometers, capacitors, and inductors now expose optional TC1/TC2 controls in a collapsed, lazy-loaded advanced section. Signed numeric values are validated and persisted in project files; ngspice receives the native instance options, including both resistor branches for a potentiometer. Blank fields inherit the imported model's coefficient or ngspice default, and the inspector distinguishes TC1's linear term from TC2's quadratic term. Unit tests cover emission and invalid values, Firefox verifies editing and generated netlist output, and a numeric operating-point fixture on the existing browser WASM runtime verifies TC1 doubles a 1 kΩ resistor at 127 °C and produces the expected 3.333 V divider output. All 38 unit groups and the production build pass; the startup-critical JS remains at the 235 KiB budget and combined JS/CSS at 248.7/250 KiB gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), sections 3.3.6, 3.3.10, and 3.3.11, which document passive TC1/TC2 instance parameters.

M2.4 absolute per-device temperature (2026-10-24): Temperature-sensitive passive and semiconductor parts can now set ngspice's absolute instance `TEMP` in addition to the existing relative `DTEMP` offset. The inspector shows the circuit temperature as the fixed-temperature default and clears the other mode when a user edits one; project validation makes the modes exclusive and enforces −273.15 °C to 10000 °C. Netlisting applies `TEMP=` across supported R/C/L and semiconductor instance cards, while model-level compatibility restrictions for BSIM MOSFETs remain enforced. The existing browser WASM fixture verifies a resistor at `TEMP=27` ignores the circuit's `.TEMP 127` and yields the expected 5 V divider output; Firefox verifies editing and generated syntax. All 38 unit groups, TypeScript, the focused WASM fixture, and production build pass. Lazy-loaded inspector code keeps startup transfer within the existing budget at 235.0/235 KiB JS and 248.7/250 KiB combined gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which states instance `TEMP` overrides circuit temperature and `DTEMP`.

M2.4 native resistor TCE temperature coefficient (2026-10-24): Resistor-based instances now expose ngspice's exponential `TCE` coefficient alongside existing polynomial `TC1`/`TC2` controls; ngspice selects the exponential model when TCE and polynomial coefficients are both present. Resistors, lamps, and potentiometers emit TCE, and validation rejects unsupported part types or malformed values.
