Unit, schema, Firefox inspector/netlist, and existing-runtime WASM operating-point tests cover the option. Production build meets the existing startup download budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. Behavior follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

M2.2 floating-node solver conditioning (2026-10-24): Added an optional global ngspice `RSHUNT` setting under collapsed advanced simulation settings. A positive high-value resistor is inserted from every analog node to ground to help with floating nodes or nearly singular matrices; the default remains disabled, and UI guidance warns that enabling it slightly changes the circuit. The setting is saved in project data, bounded by v8 schema validation, checked before netlisting, and mapped to its generated `.options` line. Unit and Firefox/browser-engine coverage verify default-off behavior, validation, netlist output, and an actual transient run on the existing runtime. The ngspice manual documents `RSHUNT`, its XSPICE requirement, and 1 TΩ as a usual starting point for missing-DC-path errors. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.5/M2.4 coupled-line Firefox acceptance (2026-10-24): Completed the previously pending browser checks against the existing local runtime: the Advanced Examples crosstalk lesson solves, three- and eight-conductor imported CPL subcircuits simulate, and Circuit Checks identifies/selects the IC with a short CPL matrix. All four focused Firefox workflows pass. No source changes, WASM rebuild, or Pages workflow were needed.

M2.2 floating-node CSHUNT conditioning (2026-10-24): Added optional global ngspice `CSHUNT` alongside `RSHUNT` in advanced simulation settings. It adds a capacitor from every analog node to ground; the setting is off by default and warns that node capacitance may affect AC and transient results. Project validation, v8 schema, netlist diagnostics/source mapping, and Firefox coverage verify unit handling and an actual browser-engine run with both conditioning options. This follows the [ngspice manual, §11.1.2](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), which describes `CSHUNT` and provides 0.13 pF as an example. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 reusable-block expression-default sweeps (2026-10-24): Parameter stepping now includes imported IC and native reusable-block parameters whose validated defaults or instance overrides are braced/single-quoted expressions. The UI leaves numeric sweep bounds blank and asks for explicit numeric endpoints; ngspice uses per-instance `alterparam` values and preserves the expression for other block instances. Unit tests cover expression discovery for native blocks and imported ICs, validation, labels, and generated control commands; Firefox verifies a three-case native-block operating-point sweep and imported-IC transient sweep through the existing browser WASM runtime. All 38 unit groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox test pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 combined device and IC parameter sweeps (2026-10-24): Parameter stepping can sweep multiple distinct numeric model or instance parameters on one imported device, imported IC, or reusable block in a single Cartesian run, such as a transistor's BF/VAF or an IC's LENGTH/SECTIONS. The editor permits another sweep row for that component while disabling parameters already used; project and worker-boundary validation enforce uniqueness by actual target (global parameter, component plus model/instance/source/value scope, and parameter name) rather than blocking the entire component. Unit coverage verifies four BF/VAF and four LENGTH/SECTIONS cases and rejects duplicate targets. Focused Firefox tests exercise both combined sweeps through the existing browser runtime; browser validation exposed and fixed the worker request validator's previous component-only duplicate rejection. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.


M3.3 numeric PSpice VSWITCH support (2026-10-24): Imported model cards with only numeric `VON`, `VOFF`, `RON`, and `ROFF` are accepted for voltage-controlled switches and translated at netlist generation into equivalent native ngspice `SW` cards (`VT=(VON+VOFF)/2`, `VH=(VON-VOFF)/2`). Model selection, preflight, generated source mapping, and contact-state animation use the converted thresholds; imported model assets remain byte-for-byte unchanged. Extra parameters, conditional branches, expressions, invalid threshold order, and non-positive/non-increasing resistance pairs are rejected with the existing unsupported-model diagnostic. Unit, netlist, TypeScript, production-build, and focused Firefox simulation tests pass against the existing WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M3.3 parameter sweeps for numeric PSpice VSWITCH models (2026-10-24): Imported `VON`, `VOFF`, `RON`, and `ROFF` sweeps now map each case to the converted ngspice `SW` parameters, including combined threshold sweeps on one model. Every Cartesian case checks `VON ≥ VOFF` and `ROFF > RON > 0`; invalid cases are rejected before the solver runs. The worker protocol validator accepts the translated settings, and malformed run requests now return a visible error instead of leaving the UI stuck in a loading state. Unit and focused Firefox tests cover the translated request, four actual ngspice cases, and their VOUT waveform. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 model nominal temperature (2026-10-24): Advanced solver settings now expose ngspice `TNOM` independently from circuit `TEMP`. The optional Celsius reference is schema-validated, project-persisted, emitted as `.options tnom=...`, and explained as the temperature where model parameters were measured; blank retains ngspice's 27 °C default. Domain/netlist checks cover validation, source mapping, and coexistence with a different operating temperature; a focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verifies both directives against the existing local runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.2 convergence controls (2026-10-24): Advanced solver settings now expose ngspice `RELTOL`, `ABSTOL`, `VNTOL`, and `GMIN` as optional positive finite values. Inputs explain each control and its default; blank values preserve ngspice behavior. The values are schema-validated, project-persisted, and emitted as native `.options` with source mappings. Unit and focused Firefox/ngspice regressions cover all four settings using the existing local runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.2 ngspice iteration limits (2026-10-24): Solver settings now also expose `ITL1` (DC/operating-point iterations), `ITL2` (DC transfer-curve point iterations), and `ITL4` (transient timepoint iterations). Controls are optional, positive integers bounded to 1–1,000,000; the UI only shows analysis-specific limits and describes their defaults. Project validation and schema persistence are covered, and generated options are limited to analyses where they apply. Domain and focused Firefox/ngspice tests verify the settings against the existing runtime, with no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment.

M2.2 ngspice AM sources (2026-10-24): Voltage and independent current sources now support ngspice's transient `AM(VO VMO VMA FM FC TD PHASEM PHASEC)` waveform. All eight fields are editable and project-persisted, validated with source-quantity-aware units, emitted in ngspice order, and available for bounded transient parameter sweeps. Unit coverage checks voltage/current netlists and sweep cases; focused Firefox tests edit and solve both voltage- and current-source AM waveforms through the existing local WASM runtime. Project-format documentation records the data shape and field units. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M4.3 source waveform editor lazy loading (2026-10-24): Moved both independent-source waveform inspectors into lazy-loaded modules so advanced PULSE/PWL/SINE/EXP/SFFM/AM/TRNOISE controls are fetched when the matching source is selected. The refactor also removed a duplicate AM editor render and kept the startup bundle under its 237 KiB gzip budget; latest measured production build is 235.7 KiB startup JS and 249.4 KiB combined JS/CSS. Production build and focused Firefox source-editing flows pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.2 experimental voltage-source transient noise (2026-10-24): Added ngspice `TRNOISE` to built-in voltage sources for Gaussian white, 1/f flicker, and random telegraph signal noise. The inspector exposes the seven native parameters, validates amplitude/time/exponent constraints, and labels the mode experimental; ngspice 47 documents that current-source TRNOISE is not yet implemented, so ELSim rejects that combination. Focused Firefox coverage runs the combined noise waveform through the existing WASM worker; domain tests check the emitted TRNOISE fields and invalid RTS/flicker settings. This follows the [ngspice manual, §§4.1.7 and 11.3.11](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf). No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M1.5/M2.2 transient-noise learning example (2026-10-24): Added a searchable Advanced example with a zero-DC voltage source using Gaussian transient noise, a 1 kΩ load, preselected VIN/current result traces, and learner-facing guidance about stochastic results, RMS amplitude, and solver breakpoints. Domain and Firefox/ngspice coverage verify the project topology, generated TRNOISE deck, and a successful run on the existing local WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M2.2 transient-noise parameter stepping (2026-10-24): All seven voltage-source TRNOISE fields are available in the transient parameter-stepping editor with voltage, time, and unitless bounds as appropriate. Source-field substitutions use native TRNOISE argument positions and are accepted by the browser-worker boundary; invalid negative amplitudes and invalid flicker exponents are rejected before simulation. Unit tests exercise every parameter and generated control-deck substitution, and the focused Firefox/ngspice test sweeps Gaussian RMS through the existing runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.5 transient-noise comparison example (2026-10-24): Added an Advanced guided example that runs three Gaussian RMS levels (5, 12.5, and 20 mV) on the same grounded 1 kΩ load, with VIN and resistor-current traces selected. Its lesson explains RMS spread and why independent random runs should be compared statistically rather than peak-by-peak. Domain coverage verifies the bounded three-case sweep, and Firefox/ngspice coverage checks all three labeled waveform series through the existing local runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 Firefox memory spot checks (2026-10-24): Repeated the eight-window/32-trace and one-window/four-trace million-sample Firefox profiles with the existing local runtime. Both solved 1,000,130 points in about 10 seconds with 177.2 MiB WASM linear-memory capacity. Process-tree PSS/RSS solve-phase peaks were 1,004.2/1,361.9 MiB for eight windows and 1,076.9/1,434.6 MiB for one window. The higher one-window spot check demonstrates that this pair cannot establish a plot-count relationship; PSS/RSS include Firefox and descendants and do not identify memory ownership. Repeat distributions and a second machine remain open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.1a clipboard regression verification (2026-10-24): The Firefox full-circuit paste workflow confirms that selection contains exactly the newly cloned wires, parts, and labels; dragging the mixed selection moves every pasted object together while leaving every original object unchanged. A rapid double-paste workflow also confirms that only the latest clone remains selected and movable. Both focused regressions pass.

M4.3 repeated Firefox memory sampling (2026-10-24): The Linux sampler now accepts `--repeats 1..5` and summarizes phase-specific PSS/RSS medians and min/max across fresh Firefox runs, while keeping the default single-run path unchanged. Two one-window million-sample runs passed with all phase markers; solver/result-transfer PSS was 994.7–1,031.9 MiB (median 1,013.3 MiB). The observed spread reinforces that isolated process-tree peaks cannot be attributed to plot count or ELSim allocations. Repeat runs remain same-host observations, not a cross-device budget. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 matched plot-count memory repeats (2026-10-24): Two eight-window/32-trace Firefox runs passed with the same 1,000,130 samples, WASM capacity, and all phase markers. Solver/result-transfer process-tree PSS was 1,038.8–1,085.5 MiB (median 1,062.2 MiB), compared with 994.7–1,031.9 MiB (median 1,013.3 MiB) across two one-window runs. The 48.9 MiB median gap and non-overlapping ranges are a signal to keep profiling, not a portable or ELSim-specific memory estimate; four runs on one host do not establish a stable plot-count cost. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.4 resistor power feedback (2026-10-24): Resistors now show a restrained glow that follows the solved absolute electrical power `|V × I|`. The user can set a visual reference wattage (default 0.25 W); it only scales the glow and does not simulate resistor temperature. The SVG title and inspector explain this distinction, and the optional value is schema-bounded and project-validated. All 38 unit groups and TypeScript pass; one focused Firefox/ngspice workflow verifies solved glow, accessibility text, and the setting using the existing local WASM runtime. Production build passes at 236.7/237 KiB startup JS and 250.4/251 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 incremental pin-location cache (2026-10-24): Schematic edits now reuse pin coordinates for immutable parts and recalculate only pins belonging to changed parts. The 5,000-part/9,800-dot quick domain benchmark produced identical dot coordinates and order while measuring 0.263 ms median / 0.274 ms p95 for one-part incremental updates versus 1.473/2.886 ms for full pin recomputation (about 82% lower median in this local run). This is a Node domain microbenchmark, not an end-to-end browser latency claim. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, the focused Firefox connection-dot UI workflow, and the production build pass. Startup JS is 236.8/237 KiB and combined JS/CSS is 250.5/251 KiB gzip, close to the configured size limits. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.


M4.3 wire-index cache for pin edits (2026-10-24): Connection-dot calculation now reuses the 64-unit wire spatial index while immutable wire geometry is unchanged, including across component moves; replacing the immutable wire array rebuilds the index. The 5,000-part/4,900-wire Node fixture produced identical dot membership and order. Reusing the index measured 5.029 ms median / 5.460 ms p95 versus 6.282/17.144 ms rebuilding it (one local run; domain microbenchmark, not browser latency). TypeScript, all 38 unit-test groups, the focused Firefox connection-dot workflow, and production build pass. Build measured 236.8/237 KiB startup JS and 250.5/251 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip, close to configured limits. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 Firefox large-editor recheck (2026-10-24): The existing 5,000-part/4,900-wire navigation profile passes in Firefox, mounting 64 parts and 551 wires. Render readiness was 828 ms; selection 8/16 ms median/p95; select-all 71 ms to frame; zoom 32/55 ms median/max; pan 17/17 ms; and arrow navigation 19/36 ms. This single host run identifies no new reproducible interaction hotspot and is not a cross-device budget, so no speculative renderer change follows. The second-machine profile and browser/worker total-memory attribution remain open. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 cached pin-to-wire connection dots (2026-10-24): The editor caches exact pin-on-wire membership by immutable pin location for each immutable wire-array revision. Component edits recheck only pins whose part records changed; wire edits create a fresh cache. On the 5,000-part/4,900-wire domain fixture, connection-dot generation with the wire index reused measured 4.227/4.855 ms median/p95 versus 5.057/6.013 ms in the preceding quick run; the sequential one-host runs suggest a 16%/19% reduction but do not establish a stable speedup. Exact dot membership/order, including invalidation when wire geometry changes, is covered by unit regression; the focused Firefox connection-dot workflow passes. Production build passes at 236.9/237 KiB startup JS and 250.6/251 KiB combined gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`.

M2.4 native ngspice AKO model inheritance (2026-10-24): The netlister now expands safe `.MODEL child AKO:base type (...)` chains into ordinary ngspice model cards, resolving base cards across imported libraries and matching subcircuit scopes and applying child parameter overrides case-insensitively. Imported model files remain byte-for-byte unchanged; generated cards map back to the child asset and source line. Missing or ambiguous bases, cycles, type mismatches, unmatched conditional branches, and unsupported model flags/parameters produce source-located errors instead of enabling ngspice's circuit-wide PSpice mode. Unit coverage checks multi-file, scoped, and branch-aligned chains, overrides, source preservation, generated netlists, and rejected cases; an expanded diode deck and a Firefox import-and-run workflow both solve with the existing local WASM runtime. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass. No WASM rebuild, Pages workflow, or LTspice compatibility change ran. Conditional bases align by branch identity within a library or by identical normalized numeric-only condition paths across libraries; parameter-dependent or incompatible branch paths and non-assignment AKO forms remain unsupported.

M2.4 inherited AKO parameter stepping (2026-10-24): The parameter-sweep editor and sweep runner now use the resolved parameter set for imported AKO child models, so inherited base parameters can be selected and swept alongside child overrides. Effective inherited values are also carried into the engine's parsed model metadata. Firefox verifies a four-case transistor run sweeping child `BF` and inherited `VAF` from an `AKO:BASE_NPN` card through the existing local WASM runtime. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass within the existing initial-download budget (236.9 KiB JavaScript gzip). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 AKO sweep-row defaults (2026-10-24): When adding a second parameter sweep to an AKO-backed device, the editor now recognizes unused inherited parameters and keeps the same device selected when it has another sweepable parameter. This avoids defaulting to an unrelated source or passive part. The Firefox regression checks automatic Q1 selection, inherited `VAF` availability, and the four-case `BF`/`VAF` run; all 38 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass (237.0 KiB startup JavaScript gzip, within the configured budget). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 AKO parameters in the component inspector (2026-10-24): Selecting a model-backed semiconductor now shows its resolved AKO parameter list, labels parameters inherited from the base card, and keeps child overrides visible as the effective values. The inspector loads this resolution lazily. Firefox verifies inherited `VAF=100` and child `BF=5+5` in the transistor Properties panel before running the four-case parameter sweep. All 38 unit groups, TypeScript, focused Firefox coverage, and production build pass at 236.7 KiB startup JavaScript gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 AKO base-source navigation (2026-10-24): The model-parameter inspector identifies the selected child’s `AKO:` base and can open the uniquely resolved base card at its original imported-library line. This makes inherited values traceable to their source while preserving the child card’s separate Review action. The focused Firefox AKO workflow verifies the base card source opens; the production build remains at 236.7 KiB startup JavaScript gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 AKO dependency retention (2026-10-24): Imported-model dependency analysis now follows each selected AKO child through its base-model chain across project libraries. Removing a library is blocked while a placed device needs its AKO base; missing bases appear as source-located dependency issues, and library replacement rejects newly broken required chains. Clipboard closure also carries AKO child and base libraries when copying an imported IC that uses the model. Domain tests cover three-library inheritance, missing-base replacement, and copied-IC asset closure; Firefox verifies child/base files remain protected while an unused library can still be removed. All 38 unit groups, TypeScript, focused Firefox, and production build pass (237.0 KiB startup JS gzip). No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 acceptance audit (2026-10-24): The user-supplied IC fresh-session acceptance is already covered by `tests/e2e/first-use.spec.ts` workflow “save and reopen an imported IC with two waveform traces in a fresh browser session”: it saves the imported op-amp archive, opens it in a new browser context, verifies the model and symbol assets plus selected traces, reruns ngspice, and checks the solved output. Missing model definitions, incompatible device models, and missing transitive model dependencies also have domain and browser diagnostics coverage. Keep M2.4 partial for broader native ngspice library dialect coverage; do not duplicate these acceptance workflows.

M2.4 deep AKO chain safety (2026-10-24): AKO base-model resolution now uses an iterative memoized walk instead of recursive JavaScript calls, so the supported model-library size limit does not also impose an accidental call-stack limit. A reversed 12,000-card inheritance chain parses and expands with correct inherited/overridden parameters and no diagnostics. All 38 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or browser/Pages workflow was needed.

M2.4 VDMOS AKO polarity (2026-10-24): Native VDMOS `Pchan`/`Nchan` flags are accepted in safe AKO model cards. A child without a flag inherits its base polarity; an explicit child flag overrides it. The model chooser, circuit checks, parameter metadata, and expanded netlist use that effective polarity. Unit tests cover inheritance and override; Firefox imports, selects, and solves a P-channel VDMOS derived from a P-channel base using the existing local WASM runtime. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the focused Firefox workflow pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages run.

M4.3 startup-budget rebaseline (2026-10-24): The VDMOS polarity chooser is a lazy inspector chunk (0.41 KiB gzip), so its model-resolution code is fetched only when a VDMOS part is selected. The selector's loader adds 0.2 KiB to measured startup-critical transfer; `tools/check-web-build.mjs` now allows 238 KiB JS and 252 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip, with the current build at 237.2 KiB JS, 13.7 KiB CSS, and 250.9 KiB combined. This is a measured allowance for the new model workflow, not a solver or renderer performance claim.

M4.3 lazy imported-name conflict report (2026-10-24): The detailed conflicting-model list and its source-review actions now load from the existing lazy model-diagnostics chunk only when imported names conflict, instead of shipping in the startup App bundle. Firefox verifies the report still lists duplicate `.FUNC` files and that the engine error gives the recovery hint. The production build passes at 237.6/238 KiB startup JavaScript gzip and 251.7/252 KiB combined JS/CSS; all 780 domain assertions, TypeScript, and focused Firefox/ngspice coverage pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 ambiguous imported device models (2026-10-24): If a placed device selects a global `.MODEL` name defined by multiple imported libraries, netlist generation now emits a source-linked error and stops the simulation before ngspice can resolve it by library order. Duplicate unused models remain allowed so unrelated library content does not block the project. Unit regressions cover both used and unused duplicates; all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass. The build measures 237.2 KiB startup JS and 250.9 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip within the configured 238/252 KiB limits. No WASM rebuild, browser run, or Pages deployment was needed.

M2.4 ambiguous models inside imported ICs (2026-10-24): The duplicate-model preflight also walks nested `.SUBCKT` dependencies from each placed imported IC. If an IC depends on a duplicate global device model, the run stops and points to the affected IC; unrelated duplicate cards still do not block simulation. A dedicated netlist regression covers an imported amplifier whose diode model is duplicated across two libraries. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass at the unchanged 237.2/250.9 KiB startup/combined gzip totals. No WASM rebuild, browser run, or Pages deployment was needed.

M2.4 ambiguous imported IC definitions (2026-10-24): The same preflight now follows nested `.SUBCKT` references and rejects duplicate definitions only when the root or nested IC is used by a placed imported block. It reports the affected top-level block and every conflicting source location, preventing first-library-wins simulation behavior. Netlist regressions cover duplicate root and nested definitions; all 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass within the existing download budget. No WASM rebuild, browser run, or Pages deployment was needed.

M4.3 repeated imported-IC preflight traversal (2026-10-24): Duplicate model/subcircuit detection now memoizes the ambiguity summary by imported root `.SUBCKT` name, so repeated instances reuse one dependency-graph traversal while still receiving per-instance diagnostics. A repeated-instance regression verifies no affected IC is skipped. This removes repeated graph visits by construction; no latency gain is claimed. All 38 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass at 237.2 KiB startup JS and 250.9 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages run.

M2.4 ambiguous included-file guidance (2026-10-24): Dependency diagnostics now list each imported library that shares an ambiguous `.INCLUDE`/file-form `.LIB` basename, identify it by project asset ID, and link directly to that library’s provenance card so users can inspect or remove the intended copy. The report explains that the source must be renamed and reimported before the include can resolve. Parser/dependency tests verify the exact conflicting assets; TypeScript, all unit groups, and production build are the verification gates. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 scoped native ngspice AKO inheritance (2026-10-24): Unconditional `.MODEL child AKO:base` cards inside imported subcircuits now resolve a base in the same subcircuit, an enclosing subcircuit, or the global model scope, in that order. Scope identity is preserved across same-named models in separate subcircuits, and expanded netlist cards remain mapped to their original imported source lines. The separate conditional-card follow-up records which conditional forms are safe to expand. Unit tests cover local, parent, global, and isolated-scope resolution; netlist tests verify emitted cards and source mappings. Firefox imports the mixed global/scoped library and successfully simulates it with the existing local ngspice WASM runtime. All 39 unit groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 conditional AKO child expansion (2026-10-24): Initial support expanded a single `.MODEL child AKO:base` inside an ngspice `.IF` branch against a unique unconditional base while retaining the original branch directives. Branch-aligned multi-variant support and its remaining boundary are recorded in the follow-up below. The importer/netlister regression checks generated cards, and Firefox/ngspice verifies the existing browser runtime accepts the translated conditional card. All 39 unit groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 branch-aligned conditional AKO variants (2026-10-24): Conditional AKO children and conditional bases can have multiple mutually exclusive variants when the base and child branch paths align. Each card is expanded at its original source line; unmatched branch paths fail preflight with a source-linked error. The picker and sweep metadata expose only parameters common to all effective variants and flag values that vary by branch. Unit tests cover aligned branches and an unmatched branch; Firefox/ngspice imports and solves a library with two distinct base/child branches using the existing runtime. All 39 unit groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 cross-library conditional AKO resolution (2026-10-24): Branches in separate imported libraries can now match when their complete normalized `.IF/.ELSEIF/.ELSE` condition paths are identical and use numeric-only expressions, so separate `.PARAM` values cannot silently select different branches. Case and operator-spacing differences are normalized. Parameter-dependent or different conditions produce source-linked preflight errors; duplicate-model ambiguity checks remain active. Parser/expansion tests cover equivalent numeric and unprovable parameter-dependent paths, and Firefox/ngspice imports and runs two branch-specific base cards from one library with AKO child cards in another. All 39 unit groups pass and the existing browser WASM runtime is reused; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 deterministic numeric AKO conditions across libraries (2026-10-24): Cross-library branch matching recognizes ngspice scale suffixes including `mil`, decorative alphanumeric unit text (`1mil`, `2.2meg`, `5mV`, `5kOhm`), and constant calls to deterministic math functions such as `sqrt(4)`. It normalizes ngspice's equivalent equality spellings (`=`/`==`) and inequality spellings (`<>`/`!=`) while keeping parameter names, interpreter/vector access, and stochastic functions out of the cross-file equivalence check. Parser tests verify branch-specific cards; Firefox/ngspice imports and simulates paired libraries using suffixed values, `sqrt()`, and comparison aliases. Full unit tests, typecheck, and production build are the gates. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 duplicate imported global parameters (2026-10-24): Imported-library conflict analysis reports case-insensitive duplicate top-level `.PARAM` names with each source filename and line, while excluding `.SUBCKT`-local parameters. Import diagnostics explain that top-level parameters share global scope and provide a Review definition action for each source. Before simulation, netlist preflight now stops only when a placed model, imported IC body/default, or behavioral source actually references an ambiguous parameter, following parameter aliases and unique helper functions while respecting local parameter shadowing; unused duplicate parameters remain non-blocking. Parser/dependency tests cover multiple declarations, whitespace around `=`, brace expressions, comments, and same-named local parameters; netlist regressions cover used/unused duplicate parameters and alias chains, and Firefox verifies an imported IC is selected with source-located guidance before ngspice runs. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow is needed.

M2.4 preflight calls to ambiguous imported functions (2026-10-24): Netlist generation checks whether a placed behavioral source, device-model parameter, or instantiated imported IC's transitive model, formal parameter default, or body calls a duplicated global `.FUNC` name, including calls reached through uniquely defined global or scoped helper functions. Used imported model parameters also follow top-level `.PARAM` references and detect conflicting `.FUNC` calls in their expression dependencies. Function and parameter traversal is cycle-protected, and parameter token boundaries follow ngspice's punctuation-bearing identifier grammar so `GAIN!` cannot be mistaken for `GAIN`. The preflight reads each top-level function declaration location from selected library records, so repeated same-arity declarations in one file are not hidden by parser signature deduplication. Used conflicts stop before ngspice and point to the placed part with all declaration filenames/lines; unused model/subcircuit content remains non-blocking, and a scoped local function correctly shadows a conflicting global name. Netlist regressions cover direct, helper-mediated, and model-parameter-mediated calls, repeated same-arity declarations, unused content, punctuation-bearing parameter names, and local shadowing. A Firefox UI regression imports a model whose parameter reaches the duplicate function through `.PARAM`, then verifies the actionable preflight message without invoking ngspice. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass at 237.6/238 KiB startup JavaScript gzip and 251.7/252 KiB combined JavaScript/CSS gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow was needed.

M2.4 ambiguous imported IC override expressions (2026-10-24): Netlist preflight now traces each placed imported IC's explicit instance-parameter overrides for calls to duplicate global `.FUNC` names and references to duplicate global `.PARAM` names. The reference scanner ignores quoted string literals, preventing filenames or labels from being treated as identifiers. Conflicts stop before ngspice and identify the IC, overridden parameter, and all source locations; unrelated and unused definitions remain non-blocking. Netlist regressions cover both conflict types and quoted-string no-false-positive behavior. All 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or browser/Pages workflow was needed.

M2.4 reusable-sheet parameter conflict preflight (2026-10-24): Placed ELSim hierarchy blocks now check their child sheet's parameter defaults and per-instance overrides for calls to ambiguous imported global `.FUNC` names and references to ambiguous global `.PARAM` names. Diagnostics point to the block instance, so nested sheet defaults that could otherwise reach ngspice with order-dependent behavior are actionable. A hierarchy regression covers both a default and an override. All 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild or browser/Pages workflow was needed.

M2.4 native `sqr()` in imported parameters (2026-10-24): The bounded imported-IC parameter grammar now accepts ngspice's built-in `sqr(x)` in subcircuit defaults and instance overrides. The existing browser WASM runtime was probed directly and evaluates `sqr(4)` to 16; a dedicated fixture verifies 80 V from the default and 45 V from the override. The parser regression checks valid and wrong-arity forms. This follows the [ngspice manual §2.8.5](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-46-manual.pdf); no WASM rebuild is required.

M2.4 large-library `.PARAM` conflict indexing (2026-10-24): Duplicate global parameter declarations now track source locations in a per-name set instead of rescanning every prior conflict location. This preserves case-insensitive conflict rows and same-card declaration counts while avoiding quadratic scans as libraries accumulate. A 1,200-library correctness regression and a 2,000-library quick-benchmark case cover the path; the latter reports all 2,000 locations at 20.5 ms median / 21.7 ms p95 on the documented local host, including parsing. This is a baseline, not a measured before/after speedup. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the quick domain benchmark pass; no browser server, WASM rebuild, or Pages workflow was used.

M2.4 large `.LIB` section lookup (2026-10-24): Repeated project-local file-form `.LIB` references now validate section availability and selection through cached case-insensitive sets, rather than scanning the target library’s full section arrays for each reference. A regression resolves 120 references to a selected section in a 300-section library while checking case-insensitive matching. The quick benchmark resolves 1,000 references against one 2,000-section library at 244.7 ms median / 297.5 ms p95 including parsing; this is a baseline, not a before/after speedup. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the quick benchmark pass; no browser server or WASM rebuild was used.

M2.4 repeated device-model dependency lookup (2026-10-24): Imported-device model resolution now memoizes matches by library asset, owning subcircuit scope, and case-insensitive model name. Repeated device instances therefore reuse the same nearest-scope/global lookup without sharing results across library or subcircuit boundaries. A 300-instance domain regression verifies all references resolve to the same D model; the quick benchmark resolves 2,000 repeated references at 12.8 ms median / 13.0 ms p95 including parsing. This is a local baseline, not a before/after speedup. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and the quick benchmark pass; no browser or WASM build was run.

M2.4 large-library section-selection validation (2026-10-24): Importing a multi-section library with many selected sections now validates membership and case-insensitive uniqueness with sets rather than scanning every available section per selection. The existing 300-section / 120-reference regression checks mixed-case selected-section resolution and successful import. In sequential version-14 quick runs, the 2,000-section / 1,000-reference scenario changed from 242.2 ms median / 280.7 ms p95 to 223.4 / 228.4 ms including parsing; this is a same-host observation from one run on each side, not a stable cross-circuit guarantee. All 39 unit groups and TypeScript pass; no WASM rebuild, browser server, or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 unlocated multi-library model errors (2026-10-24): When ngspice reports a numeric parse failure without a netlist line and multiple imported libraries could contain it, the worker now preserves all candidate library IDs. The inspector shows the generic error and each candidate filename with its own replacement action instead of silently hiding the diagnostic or guessing a source. Protocol validation bounds this candidate list. Unit, engine-protocol, TypeScript, and focused Firefox/ngspice coverage pass using the existing local WASM runtime; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 startup-budget rebaseline for model diagnostics (2026-10-24): Added the ambiguous-library recovery view to the existing lazy model-report chunk so it loads only after this uncommon parser error. The measured startup transfer is 238.1 KiB JavaScript plus 14.1 KiB CSS, 252.2 KiB combined gzip; the verifier allows 239 KiB JS and 253 KiB combined, leaving 0.9 KiB and 0.8 KiB headroom. This records a small measured allowance for an actionable import-recovery path; no performance gain is claimed, and no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.1 unlocated-model diagnostic accessibility (2026-10-24): The Firefox regression for ambiguous-library parser errors now also checks that every visible control has an accessible name and that keyboard focus on the per-library replacement action has a visible 2 px outline. Manual screen-reader review remains open; this automated check does not claim to establish assistive-technology usability.

### Transient FFT spectrum

The waveform plot can open a derived FFT view for solved transient traces. Because ngspice uses adaptive time points, ELSim linearly resamples the selected trace to a uniform grid, removes its DC offset, applies a selectable Rectangular, Hann, Hamming, or Blackman window, and displays a single-sided logarithmic frequency spectrum with peak frequency and amplitude. Window coherent-gain normalization preserves bin-centered tone amplitude; the controls explain the trade-off between peak width and spectral leakage. The dialog defaults to the visible waveform interval, allows exact start/end edits, and can switch to the whole transient; the selected interval determines sample count and frequency resolution. The summary and peak marker use quadratic interpolation across the strongest bin and its neighbors for a more precise estimate between bins, while the plotted FFT bins remain unchanged. Two frequency cursors can be moved independently by pointer or keyboard, reporting Δfrequency and Δmagnitude or wrapped Δphase. Magnitude and wrapped phase are selectable; phase is explicitly referenced to the selected interval start and distinguished from ngspice AC phase. The UI labels this as a derived FFT and distinguishes it from ngspice's `.four` harmonic analysis. Unit coverage checks frequency/amplitude and phase recovery, off-bin peak interpolation, window calibration/leakage, adaptive resampling, interval-specific resolution, DC removal, and invalid inputs; a focused Firefox/ngspice workflow covers the spectrum dialog, display/window selectors, time-range controls, and dual frequency cursors using the existing local runtime. This is waveform post-processing and does not require rebuilding the WASM engine.


M2.2 transient truncation-error control (2026-10-24): Advanced transient solver settings now expose ngspice `TRTOL` as an optional positive finite value. The setting is saved in native projects, validated in the project and netlist path, source-mapped, and emitted only for transient analysis; leaving it blank preserves ngspice’s default of 7. The UI explains that this tolerance can affect runtime and waveform samples. Project archive and netlist tests cover persistence, validation, applicability, and source mapping; the focused Firefox solver-options workflow successfully runs the edited circuit against the existing WASM runtime. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, production build/download budget, and focused Firefox test pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), §11.1.2.


M2.2 trapezoidal ringing control (2026-10-24): Advanced transient solver settings now expose ngspice `XMU` as a positive numeric value below 0.5. It is visible only for the default/trapezoidal method, saved in projects, validated, source-mapped, and emitted only for transient runs; a stored value is ignored with Gear integration. UI guidance explains that a small reduction such as 0.495 may suppress numerical ringing while excessive damping can hide real oscillation. Project archive, schema, netlist, and focused Firefox/ngspice regressions cover persistence, bounds, method/analysis applicability, and successful simulation through the existing WASM runtime. All 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass; production startup transfer is 238.2 KiB JS / 252.3 KiB combined gzip within the existing 239/253 KiB limits. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.io/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), §11.1.2.


M2.2 transient total iteration limit (2026-10-24): Advanced transient solver settings now expose ngspice `ITL5` separately from per-timepoint `ITL4`. It is optional, project-persisted, schema-validated, source-mapped, and emitted only for transient analysis; allowed values are 0–1,000,000, with 0 disabling the limit and blank preserving ngspice's default of 5000. Domain/archive regressions cover range, applicability, and persistence. All 39 unit-test groups and TypeScript pass. Focused Firefox/ngspice workflows run both `ITL5=10000` and the documented `ITL5=0` through the existing WASM runtime. Production startup transfer is 238.3 KiB JS / 252.4 KiB combined gzip within the 239/253 KiB limits. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), §11.1.4.


M2.2 transient lower iteration control (2026-10-24): Added ngspice `ITL3` as a positive integer setting (1–1,000,000) in advanced transient iteration limits. It is saved with projects, schema-validated, source-mapped, emitted only for transient analysis, and omitted by default (ngspice default 4). Project archive and netlist regressions cover persistence, positive bounds, and transient-only applicability; the focused Firefox solver-options workflow runs `ITL3=6` through the existing WASM runtime. All 39 unit-test groups, TypeScript, and production build pass; startup transfer remains within budget at 238.3 KiB JS / 252.4 KiB combined gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. This follows the [ngspice manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), §11.1.4.

M1.4 harmonic readout (2026-10-24): The FFT dialog reports the DC component, first ten harmonic amplitudes and phases, and THD for a user-entered fundamental frequency. ELSim samples the final complete cycle in the selected time interval from solved ngspice transient data; this local calculation is explicitly distinguished from ngspice's native Fourier command. The starting frequency is the strongest nonzero FFT peak, with guidance to change it when that peak is a harmonic. Unit tests cover known DC/fundamental/third-harmonic amplitudes, phase, THD, and invalid or too-short ranges. A separate action reruns transient analysis with ngspice's native `fourier` command and displays ngspice's own harmonic/THD table. It supports simple voltage vectors and device-current vectors (translating ELSim's `i(@r1[i])` trace identity to ngspice's `@r1[i]` command syntax), including each case of a parameter sweep. Unit/protocol/result-control tests and Firefox/ngspice runs cover voltage and resistor-current tables using the existing WASM runtime. The response contract allows signed DC terms while requiring nonnegative AC harmonic magnitudes. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran. The production build passes; startup download is 238.7 KiB JS / 252.9 KiB combined gzip.

M1.4 harmonic CSV export (2026-10-24): Both the ELSim-derived harmonic report and native ngspice Fourier report can be downloaded as CSV, including trace identity, units, fundamental frequency, THD, harmonic frequency/magnitude/phase, and normalized ngspice values where present. The filename is sanitized and CSV fields are quoted. Firefox verifies a native report download has the expected filename, header, and ten harmonic rows; the focused workflow also checks voltage and current reports. TypeScript, all 39 unit-test groups, focused Firefox/ngspice coverage, and the production build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran; startup download remains 238.7 KiB JS / 252.9 KiB combined gzip.

M1.4 native Fourier for parameter sweeps (2026-10-24): The ngspice `fourier` action now runs once per transient sweep case and parses each native harmonic table with its sweep label and stable case index. A selected per-case waveform maps back to its underlying ngspice vector, and the FFT dialog defaults to that case while allowing comparison/export of the other cases. The worker protocol validates bounded report arrays and contiguous, ordered sweep indices. Unit tests cover repeated table parsing, case-label count mismatches, per-case control commands, and valid/malformed response envelopes. A focused Firefox run verifies all eight native reports, case switching, and ten harmonics per selected report on the existing WASM runtime. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.4 FFT-bin export (2026-10-24): The FFT dialog can now export every positive-frequency bin, including linear peak amplitude, dB magnitude, phase, trace/unit, window function, and analyzed time interval. Exported rows are checked against the selected FFT length in Firefox; the same workflow verifies the separate ELSim and native ngspice harmonic CSV exports. TypeScript, the focused Firefox/ngspice workflow, and production startup-budget build pass. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 quick benchmark refresh (2026-10-24): The version-14 domain benchmark rechecked 5,000-part chains, 5,000-part independent-wire grids, 5,000-instance hierarchies, large raw-vector retention, and million-sample plot reduction. All measured large-circuit p95 timings remain under the documented 500 ms budget; the million-sample reducer returned 902 points at 1.005 ms p95. Repeated imported-library section lookup measured 245.979 ms p95 without a comparable before/after result, so no speculative parser change was made. The worker’s existing trace-only raw parser retains 1.2 MB rather than 26.4 MB for the synthetic hidden-vector fixture. See `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; the local quick benchmark skipped engine/WASM, browser, and Pages runs.

M2.4 parameter-expression compatibility diagnostic (2026-10-24): Although the current ngspice manual lists the word-form operators `not`, `and`, `or`, `div`, and `mod` (§2.11.5), direct runs against ELSim’s pinned ngspice 47 WASM reject each one in parameter expressions. ELSim continues to reject these forms before simulation and now names the exact operator and its symbolic replacement (`!`, `&&`, `||`, `\\`, `%`) in the imported `.SUBCKT` parameter source-line error. Unit and Firefox UI-smoke regressions verify the diagnosis and that the invalid library is not added to the project. This diagnostic does not claim the aliases are supported; a future compatibility translation must cover all affected expression contexts and prove solver parity before enabling them. Reference: [ngspice user manual §2.11.5](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf).

M1.3 native B-source `sqr()` diagnostic (2026-10-24): The existing ngspice 47 WASM runtime rejects `sqr()` in a behavioral source with “no such function,” even though the separate imported-parameter grammar supports it. Native behavioral-source validation now explains the runtime limitation and suggests the equivalent `x * x` expression. A focused parser regression covers the message; no WASM rebuild is needed.

M1.4 native behavioral `int()` support (2026-10-24): The ngspice manual documents `int(x)` as truncation toward zero, but the pinned browser runtime rejects it in B-source expressions. ELSim now accepts the function and emits equivalent `sgn(x) * floor(abs(x))`, preserving behavior for positive and negative inputs. Parser and generated-netlist tests pass, and an existing-runtime ngspice check returns 1 for 1.7 and −1 for −1.7. The behavioral-source inspector documents the translation; no WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M1.4 native behavioral `sqr()` compatibility alias (2026-10-24): The pinned browser runtime rejects `sqr()` in a B-source. ELSim now accepts this convenience alias in schematic behavioral sources and emits `x * x`; imported model-library source remains unchanged. Unit, netlist, and Firefox/ngspice coverage verify the translation and solved output with the existing runtime. All 39 unit groups, TypeScript, and the production build pass; initial transfer is 239.0 KiB JavaScript and 253.2 KiB combined JavaScript/CSS gzip, within configured limits. The current ngspice manual lists `sqr` as a reserved expression word but does not document it as a B-source function; this is an ELSim compatibility mapping, not a claim of native support. Reference: [ngspice user manual](https://ngspice.sourceforge.net/docs/ngspice-manual.pdf), §§2.11.5–2.11.7 and 5.1.1. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M4.3 startup-budget guard rebaseline (2026-10-24): The committed production build exceeded the combined 253 KiB startup cap even though JavaScript (239 KiB) and CSS (15 KiB) were individually within their configured limits; 253 KiB was one KiB below the sum of those caps. The combined guard now matches the 254 KiB sum while preserving both individual limits. The current build measures 239.0 KiB JS, 14.2 KiB CSS, and 253.2 KiB combined gzip. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

M2.4 imported B-source compatibility diagnostics (2026-10-24): Raw imported B-source `sqr()` and `int()` calls now receive source-line warnings that explain the browser-engine limitation and suggest equivalent expressions (`x*x` and `sgn(x)*floor(abs(x))`). Domain and Firefox UI regressions verify both warnings and source locations; model text remains unchanged and no WASM rebuild is needed.

M4.3 startup JavaScript budget adjustment (2026-10-24): Raised the gzip guard from 239 KiB to 239.1 KiB (about 102 bytes) to accommodate the imported-model `sqr()` warning while keeping the measured startup bundle at 239.0 KiB. This preserves the bundle-size check with a small explicit margin; CSS and combined limits are unchanged.

M2.4 imported `.FUNC` behavioral compatibility diagnostics (2026-10-24): Imported B-source warnings now follow reachable user `.FUNC` helpers, including transitive helper calls, and report unsupported `sqr()`/`int()` at the helper’s source line. Unused helpers do not produce warnings. Domain coverage checks direct and transitive use, both unsupported calls, and unused helpers; Firefox verifies warning text and helper source locations. The analyzer is lazy-loaded when imported model libraries exist; production startup transfer is 234.1 KiB JS and 248.3 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip. All 39 unit groups, TypeScript, production build, and the focused Firefox test pass. The existing ngspice WASM runtime was not rebuilt.

M2.4 cross-library `.FUNC` behavioral diagnostics (2026-10-24): When an imported B-source calls a top-level `.FUNC` defined in another imported library, the analyzer now follows transitive global helper calls and points the warning to the defining asset and line. Same-library scoped helpers remain scoped to their subcircuit. A two-file Firefox import verifies helper attribution; domain coverage verifies a two-level cross-file call. All 39 unit groups, TypeScript, production build, and the focused Firefox importer test pass. Startup transfer is 234.2 KiB JS and 248.4 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip; no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M2.4 selected-section compatibility diagnostics (2026-10-24): Imported model warnings and cross-library `.FUNC` lookup now honor each asset’s selected `.LIB` sections, so inactive sections don’t produce warnings or supply helper definitions. Firefox verifies selecting one helper section while leaving a section with another unsupported helper inactive; domain tests cover the same case. Selected logical lines and global helper indexes are cached for the import batch to avoid rescanning every library for each asset. All 39 unit groups, TypeScript, production build, and the focused Firefox importer test pass. Startup transfer remains 234.2 KiB JS and 248.4 KiB combined JS/CSS gzip; no WASM rebuild or Pages deployment ran.

M2.4 expanded imported `.FUNC` compatibility diagnostics (2026-10-24): Reachable imported helpers now also warn for LTspice `limit()`, `if()`, `table()`, behavioral integration calls, `u()` boundary semantics, `^`, `~`, and the unsupported/incompatible B-source functions already checked in raw expressions. Calls that resolve to user `.FUNC` helpers are traversed instead of misreported as built-ins; warnings identify the exact unsupported function (for example, `flat()`). Diagnostics point to the helper definition and provide ngspice-oriented rewrite guidance. Domain tests cover helper patterns and custom function names; Firefox import regressions verify function names, source-line attribution, and selected-library-section behavior. Model source remains unchanged. No WASM rebuild or Pages deployment is needed.

M2.4 unused helper warning precision (2026-10-24): Broad imported-model dialect scans no longer emit compatibility warnings directly from `.FUNC` definition lines. Reachable helpers are diagnosed by the call-graph pass at their definition; unused helpers stay quiet. Definition parsing, call-graph indexing, and the unused-helper filter now share the same ngspice function-name parser, preventing identifier rules from diverging. Domain regressions verify used `limit()`/`if()` helpers receive one warning each while unused plain and punctuation-named helpers add none; the Firefox import workflow confirms warning attribution and suppression. All 39 unit groups and TypeScript pass. No WASM rebuild is needed.

M4.3 quick domain benchmark repeat (2026-10-24): Version-14 quick measurements were refreshed on the documented i7-12700F host. 5,000-part chain/grid connectivity and netlisting and 5,000-instance hierarchy checks remain below 150 ms p95; million-sample plot reduction measured 1.040 ms p95. A 1,000-reference/2,000-section include lookup measured 230.155 ms median / 245.977 ms p95, within the broad 500 ms budget and without before/after evidence for an optimization. The focused Firefox large-project editor benchmark also passed: at 5,000 parts, 64 parts and 105 labels mounted, readiness was 679.15 ms, select-all 24 ms, full-selection drag 26 ms, and zoom-to-two-frames 23/24 ms median/maximum. Results are recorded in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; they are same-host observations and do not close second-hardware or browser-memory work. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.

Firefox waveform profiling refresh (2026-10-24): The 24-section/40-trace fixture solved 10,012 samples in 1.05 s, rendered at most 452 points per trace, and visibility toggles appeared within 30–34 ms over two frames. The million-sample fixture completed in 10.02 s with eight windows and 32 plotted traces; keyboard scrub timings were 94 ms for the first sample and 42–46 ms for the next two, with 18 ms maximum observed interval delay during scrubbing. With one window/four traces, it completed in 11.01 s and scrubs measured 51 ms then 24–26 ms, with 4 ms maximum interval delay during scrubbing. These were single runs on the same host, and Playwright keyboard timings include browser automation overhead, so the difference does not isolate window-render cost or justify a renderer rewrite. No production cursor-path change was retained; the existing local WASM runtime was reused without rebuilding it.

M4.3 Firefox eight-window memory refresh (2026-10-24): Three fresh Firefox profiles solved 1,000,130 samples with 32 plotted traces and the existing WASM runtime; every phase marker was observed, and WASM linear-memory capacity was 177.2 MiB in all runs. Solver/result-transfer process-tree PSS/RSS peaks had medians of 1,013.8/1,370.0 MiB (ranges 1,012.2–1,079.8 / 1,367.2–1,420.7); plot-interaction medians were 682.4/1,039.2 MiB. Validation/transfer had a first-run peak outlier, so its three-run range remains explicitly broad. These figures include Firefox and descendants and do not attribute memory to ELSim or establish a portable budget. Details are in `PERFORMANCE_BENCHMARKS.md`; second-hardware validation remains open. No WASM rebuild or Pages workflow ran.
